These prescriptions define generic actions with values in the history construction on $G$. For a rank-two word over a globular arity its raw source tree locates the steps at its edge leaves as in Response 4. Every evaluation at the generic variables is a shuffle of the lists at these leaves. All such shuffles give one class: at a binary node each step belongs to one of the two branches, with the stationary arrow equal to the current end arrow in the opposite branch. Exchanges move the left steps first, then induction orders each branch internally. A stationary branch contributes no steps. This establishes (i) for any raw parallel 2-words with equal ordinary strict image. At rank one it holds by strict category composition. For (ii) at rank three the two generic histories therefore have **the same normal representative**. Top uniqueness in the free Gray-category makes the chosen 3-cell over the strict identity the identity on that representative. At rank three (i) follows from the same top uniqueness at the common generic faces. At lower ranks (ii) uses identities at a vertex or an empty history. Substitution as proved above validates the equations with arbitrary inputs. The ordered extension by thin 4-cells works exactly as for $K(G)$: the two projected operations on the truncated arity have common history boundaries and ordinary strict value, hence are equal by top uniqueness, and substitution of their top labels is monotone. In higher dimensions all parallel cells coincide. This gives a model $E(G)$ for the entire binary word surface.

It remains to relate its interpretation of **old terms** to that in $K(G)$. Use a third globular set with the same objects and arrows. A 2-cell is a triple $(u,z,j)$ where $u$ is a history class, $z$ is a parallel 2-cell of $G$, and $j:r(u)\cong z$ is an invertible Gray 3-cell. Between two triples take pairs of 3-cells $(b,c)$ such that $c j=j' b$. Give them the order of either projection: conjugation by the two fixed isomorphisms preserves and reflects it. Extend by thin 4-cells. Both projections lift cells at specified faces. In dimension two use $z=r(u)$ in one direction, or the single atom labelled $z$ in the other. In dimension three solve the conjugation equation; in dimension four use order reflection.

Both projections are **kernel** maps for a common kernel action on the triples. For a generic 2-head choose the two projected values as above. Its comparison $j$ first undoes sorting, then applies the biased Gray paste to the input isomorphisms. The column order of that paste is the order chosen in $K(G)$; structural 2-cells used there to fit weak 1-boundaries become identities in $G$. For a composite raw 2-expression the comparison is obtained by composing these isomorphisms. At a generic 3-head, the parallel diagrams asserting $c j=j'b$ on the generic variables have the same strict value, hence agree by Gray top uniqueness. After labelling, the comparison still commutes: expansion and sorting give precisely the conjugation formula above and changing the 2-labels by their $j$'s uses naturality, checked on 3-generators and interchangers. Thus the two projected 3-operations give a cell of triples. Rank four then follows from the order and all higher ranks from thinness. This constructs the claimed span $K(G)\leftarrow D\to E(G)$.

In the preceding proof lift the old assignment in $K(G)$ along $D\to K(G)$ and interpret the proposed surface 4-term in $E(G)$ using its other projection. The 3-boundaries are already lifted old terms in $D$. An inequality in $E(G)$ at this pair gives the inequality in $K(G)$ by conjugation. Continue along $q,v$ and the kernel cylinder exactly as before. This proves the strengthened dimension-four result. In particular the proposed failure of $\{U,I\}$ for ordinary CaTT, if it occurs, must first occur at dimension **at least five**. ∎

### Insertion through dimension four

The ordinary Gray model $K(G)$ with the column order above also interprets $\equiv_{sua}$. Consequently **$\mathrm{Catt}_{sua}$ is conservative over ordinary CaTT through dimension four in every finite context**. This uses the untagged column-order model, since insertion can change the binary addresses.

Disc removal returns the labelled generator. Endo-coherence removal returns the identity: in dimension two an identity strict value has no events, and in dimension three use top uniqueness on the generic arity at the equal endpoints. For insertion check the rule first over the inserted tree, applying the exterior substitution to the outer head. In rank one, splicing lists of arrows preserves their composite. In rank two a tree is a list of columns, each with an ordered list of 2-events. The column-order operation executes those lists consecutively, with the completed arrows to its left and the initial arrows to its right. There are only two possible branch depths.

- At depth one (a branch $[k]$ in the paper), the whole selected column is a disc. If it bears a 2-event, its replacement executes all the columns of the inserted tree as one contiguous block. These are exactly the columns spliced in at position $k$. The exterior whiskers append the same completed and initial arrow paths as in the enlarged tree. Insertion at a 1-edge can introduce only a path of 1-arrows and contributes no 2-event.
- At depth two the branch selects an event within one column. The trunk-height condition requires the inserted tree to have one outer column. Insertion therefore splices a vertical list at that event without changing the other columns. The initial and final arrows of the block are precisely the images of the old boundary arrows under the exterior substitution. Identity blocks have an empty event list.

Thus one obtains literally the same ordered whiskered 2-path. This also covers identity-headed cases, using the padded standard composite of the inserted tree. For a rank-three insertion both generic terms over the enlarged tree have the same interpreted 2-faces (the exterior substitution on the head type) and the same ordinary strict value by associativity of substitution of pasting diagrams. Top uniqueness in the ordinary Gray operad gives equality of the 3-operations. In rank four and higher, equality follows from the common boundary and thinness. Finally substitute the inserted labelling; the exterior and interior substitution identities give the original two sides of the rule. This proves soundness also under conversion inside a head or its arguments. The syntactic support side condition in the insertion theory gives the same ordinary generic arity here: inductively the support of a head application is the union of the supports of its argument cells, since its full generic strict cell visits every slot of its arity. On a globular arity this is the support of their strict paste, by positional substitution. The operation and coherence tests on supports thus give respectively the usual total and full identity. [Finster–Rice–Vicary, Definitions 19–23 and Lemma 27](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303#page=17)

Apply the ordered strictification span with this surface action on $K(G)$. The return at the original 4-boundary uses the same order reflection and cylinder lifting as above. For lower ranks one can use ordinary strict interpretation and Known 2 directly. ∎

**Validation.** Direct enumeration on a one-object category with two arrows and nonunique factorizations checked 57,800 composable event pairs. Each of the 8,912 possible directed exchanges preserved its two end arrows. Both three-swap routes gave identical histories, including stationary arrow labels, at all 80,512 overlapping peaks through binary depth three. On all height-four pasting trees through eight edges, all 43,037 strict 4-cells had the predicted slot endpoints, with 10,661 forward slot steps. The insertion check compared full whiskered event sequences on 360 whole-column and 27 within-column insertions, including empty blocks. [Exchange checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_histories.py) · [Slot, insertion and raw-term checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_order.py)

For a check at a composite attaching type, take a scalar $a:1_x\to1_x$, four 3-endomorphisms $r,s,t,d$ of $a$, and a 4-generator $c:((r *_2 s)*_2 t)\to d$. Associativity converts it to $r *_2(s *_2 t)\to d$. Agda 2.8.0 with the repository's safe CaTT checker verified that composing inverse reassociation with $c$ has exactly this old type. Its reverse is impossible: in the ordinary strict interpretation the number of occurrences of $d$ increases by one at $c$ and is unchanged by identities, so can never decrease along a 4-cell. These checks exercise directionality and the finite combinatorics; the arbitrary-context arguments are the proofs above.

This settles the ordinary CaTT half of Q3 for all eight subsets. For the directed Gray kernel the cases containing $I$ already fail in dimension three, and the empty subset is covered in all dimensions. The remaining universal dimension-four cases there are $\{A\}$, $\{U\}$ and $\{A,U\}$.

## Researcher Response 6

**Answer to Q3.** Through dimension four, in every finite context, ordinary CaTT is conservative for every subset. The directed Gray kernel is conservative exactly for the four subsets omitting $I$. Here is the remaining affirmative argument. It uses the repository's directed realization theorem, including its identification of the realized fixed homs. A shift of dimension matters: the ordinary prescribed-boundary result needed in a fixed hom stops at **local dimension three**.

### Strictifying the fixed homs

Let $X$ be the raw free directed algebra on the computad $\Gamma$. For two of its objects $x,y$, let $H=X(x,y)$ be its ordinary weak hom algebra. There is a canonical strict functor
$$
             S H\longrightarrow \mathfrak F(\Gamma)(x,y),                 \tag{1}
$$
where $S$ is ordinary algebraic strictification. It sends a weak hom cell to the strict Gray image of the corresponding global cell. **This functor is a folk weak equivalence of strict ω-categories.** Its source retains distinct objects for different raw writings of a global arrow. We prove the homotopical assertion, including the compatibility of (1) with the actual attaching maps.

Write $\mathcal W$ for the coinductively complete homotopy theory of ordinary weak ω-categories and $\mathcal C$ for the folk homotopy theory of strict ω-categories. There is a derived adjunction $s:\mathcal W\rightleftarrows \mathcal C:N$. At the algebraic level the left adjoint interprets ordinary weak operations in the strict theory. The right adjoint equips a strict category with these operations. It preserves trivial fibrations, since both tests lift globes at a prescribed boundary. It preserves fibrations: the equifibration tests lift a coinductively invertible cell at a given source, in every fixed hom. In the underlying weak algebra of a strict category the coinductive invertibility predicates agree, since their defining witnesses have the same strict composites and identities. Thus this is a Quillen adjunction for the ordinary model structure used in Response 3. In particular $s$ on a free weak computad is computed by $S$ without replacement. [Ordinary model-structure and realization inputs](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-16) · [Fujii–Hoshino–Maehara](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09849)

Equip both sides with the **lax Gray tensor**. Then $s$ is strong monoidal. On a Gray cube $Q$, the derived counit $sNQ\to Q$ is an equivalence: realize its strong Steiner presentation by a weak computad. The comparison to the strict cube is a globular trivial fibration, and strictifying this cofibrant presentation gives the cube itself. The semantic cube represents $NQ$ by the same strong Steiner realization theorem. These counits are natural on the full cube category. Tensor powers of cubes on the strict side compute derived powers, by the pushout-product theorem and cofibrancy. The canonical strict and semantic tensors of cubes are the same cubes, on maps as well as objects. Now cubes are dense on the weak side; density persists under coinductive localization since the cubes are local. Both functors $(A,B)\mapsto s(A\boxtimes B)$ and $(A,B)\mapsto sA\otimes sB$ preserve colimits separately. Their identifications on pairs of cubes therefore extend uniquely as natural equivalences, and the unit and associativity diagrams extend in the same way from tuples of cubes. [Strong Steiner comparison, §3](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r10-2026-11-21.md) · [Campion, cube density](https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.09376) · [Campion, Gray tensor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.00205) · [Ara–Lucas, folk pushout-product theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.13564)

Consequently change of enrichment $s_*$ applies $s$ on homs and is left adjoint to change along the lax monoidal right adjoint. This follows by taking the adjunction on each string of inputs in the enriched composition operations. One may keep the object set fixed while forming free attachments of positive-dimensional generators; passing to complete enriched categories preserves the homs at the displayed objects. The raw directed realization has hom $\Phi H$ at $x,y$, with $\Phi$ the ordinary realization equivalence. On a raw globular arity $P=\bigvee_i\Sigma P_i$ the realized forward hom is the strict Steiner tensor of the traversed factors. Strictifying it by $s$ gives that same strict hom, naturally on raw substitutions: the arity comparison is strict interpretation, and tensoring the preceding counits gives its identification in $\mathcal C$. Point homs and empty homs obey the same assertion. [Directed realization, Responses 15–16, including the maps of arity charts](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-16)

For clarity, the comparison at a general free computad is the following map. Use the chart-colimit description of directed realization in Response 16 there, apply $s_*$, and send the chart of each label $P\to X$ to its strict label in $\mathfrak F(\Gamma)$. The cocone respects substitutions by strict evaluation. On a fixed hom, restriction to a suspended chart is precisely the derived ordinary strictification of the label followed by (1). Ordinary realized homs are colimits of these charts, by Response 15 there. This identifies the fixed-hom component of the comparison, without making choices of weak representatives for strict attaching maps.

Both sides of this comparison compute a cellular enriched colimit. On the source this follows from the directed realization theorem for computad-cofibrant algebras and the fact that $s_*$ is a left adjoint. On the target we need that **strict free enriched attachments compute the derived ones**. Here are details avoiding any model structure assumption on arbitrary strict Gray-enriched categories.

Fix the object set. Start from its discrete enriched category; all its homs are cofibrant. To attach one generating globe at poles $i,j$, use the free enriched extension of the previously attached category $A$ along the map of hom globes $K=\partial D^m\to L=D^m$ (for a global arrow use $m=0$). In the strict pushout filter each hom by the number $r$ of new letters. At stage zero it is $A(a,b)$. For $r\ge1$ the new word object is
$$
 A(a,i)\otimes L\otimes A(j,i)\otimes L\otimes\cdots\otimes L\otimes A(j,b)
$$
with $r$ occurrences of $L$. Attach it along the punctured cube replacing at least one $L$ by $K$, using the attaching map and old composition at the shortened words. This word filtration is valid with repeated objects and with units in any intervening hom. Its boundary map is a cofibration between cofibrant objects: insert the cofibrant old hom factors in the iterated pushout-product of $K\to L$, or equivalently take pushout-products also with their initial maps. Thus each stage is a homotopy pushout and its homs stay cofibrant. [Haugseng, non-symmetric free-algebra pushout filtration and enriched graphs, §§3–4](https://arxiv.org/abs/1312.3881)

Here is a verification that the filtration has the desired **enriched** derived universal property. Resolve $A$ under the free category on the graph $K_{ij}$ by the simplicial free-forgetful resolution under that object. The relative free functor on a graph $V$ is the free category on $K_{ij}\amalg V$. The augmentation is split on underlying graphs. All levels have cofibrant homs, since the free-category formula uses coproducts of tensors of cofibrants. Their free enriched categories also represent the free objects after localization, by the same tensor-word formula. At each free level the pushout is the free category on $L_{ij}\amalg V$, the required derived pushout. Realization of this simplicial pushout thus computes the derived pushout under $A$: underlying graphs create sifted colimits for the associative algebra monad because tensors preserve colimits separately. Compare it to the strict pushout via the word filtration. At stage zero the comparison on homs is an equivalence by the split augmentation. At each subsequent stage, realization of the word objects and of their punctured cubes gives the word objects and punctured cubes of $A$ in $\mathcal C$. Indeed realization is sifted, all tensor factors are cofibrant, and the cubes compute homotopy colimits by the pushout-product argument. Induction on the homotopy pushouts, then passage to their sequential colimit, gives equivalence on all homs. This proves the attachment assertion using only cofibrant homs.

Now induct on the declarations. The comparison is an equivalence on a globe and its boundary: the latter is the cellular union of its two lower globes at their common boundary (starting with the empty boundary); a globe is a raw arity. New objects are discrete point summands. In a positive-dimensional attachment the boundary map on the target is exactly the strict evaluation of the two specified attaching terms by the chart cocone. The two pushout descriptions and the inductive comparison give an equivalence after attachment. Thus the chart comparison is an enriched equivalence and hence a folk equivalence on each displayed hom. Finally $H$ is computad-cofibrant: take as its ordinary generators every raw term in the hom whose outer constructor is not the suspension of an ordinary head application, including the global variables and all global arrow writings regarded as local objects. For a suspended head, its two pole arguments are fixed, and its remaining arguments are uniquely the labelling of the ordinary head in this hom. Parsing recursively gives an inverse to evaluation from the ordinary free algebra on these generators. Source and target declarations are parsed in lower dimension; the suspended head typing rule is exactly the one-factor rule. Since the kernel has literal syntax this is an isomorphism. In particular its ordinary derived strictification is $SH$, completing the identification and the proof of (1).

### Returning at a weak boundary

For $I\notin S$ the strict enriched category $\mathfrak F(\Gamma)$ interprets the surface as in Response 2. Consider an inhabited old 4-type with parallel raw 3-faces $u,v$ and poles $x,y$. Its strict interpretation gives a **local 3-cell** between the images of the local 2-cells $u,v$. By (1) the specified parallel cells $\pi u,\pi v$ of $SH$ admit a strict 3-cell in the same direction. This is the local essential-surjectivity clause in the folk characterization of a weak equivalence: one can lift the target cell up to a reversible comparison at the next dimension, with the source and target in the domain prescribed. Inhabitation needs only the resulting domain cell. [Folk weak equivalences and reversible cells](https://arxiv.org/abs/0712.0617)

Apply ordinary prescribed-boundary lifting **through local dimension three** to the free ordinary hom computad. Explicitly, realize a strict 3-paste using its generating 3-cells, identities and binary composites; at interfaces and the outside boundary use the identity-image comparisons in dimensions one and two of Known 2. This is the usual finite-dimensional globe-lifting implication of ordinary relative coherence. The hom computad can be infinite, but the strict paste, the two raw boundary terms and their iterated attaching data use finitely many declarations; include the finite equational derivations of any strict identifications at their interfaces as well. The finite-context theorem therefore applies. The result is a local weak 3-cell of $H$ between the **original** local 2-cells. By definition of the hom algebra it is a raw global Gray 4-term $u\to v$. Responses 1 and 2 cover lower dimensions. For every $S$ containing $I$ the old reverse-square type already proves universal failure at dimension three. Together with Response 5 this gives the claimed complete fallback classification. ∎

### Two consequences in unbounded dimension

For **full Gray-image conversion**, ordinary inhabitation conservativity at the particular context $\Gamma$, with no dimension bound, is equivalent to ordinary CaTT inhabitation conservativity for **full ordinary-image conversion in every fixed hom computad $H$ just constructed**. In one direction start with a positive-dimensional global strict cell at an old boundary, of global dimension at least two. Apply (1) to get a local strict cell at the prescribed strict faces. In full ordinary-image conversion every strict paste is realizable at prescribed old faces: spell its generators, identities and binary cuts, converting at each equal strict interface. Local conservativity gives the required raw term. Global objects and arrows lift directly. Conversely a local derivation with full ordinary-image conversion translates to a full-image global derivation. Send the hom generators to their raw defining global terms, suspend the ordinary heads, and evaluate under this assignment. Every local strict equality maps to a global Gray equality under (1); on generic suspended heads the strict test is the one-factor test. Any resulting raw global filler of the old translated type parses back to a raw filler in $H$. This is an inhabitation criterion. It does not require specifying which reversible comparison in the target of (1) accompanies an individual lift.

There is also a transfer in the direction relevant to the **binary** conjectures. If an ordinary CaTT word surface fails in $\Lambda$, then the Gray word surface for the same laws fails in $\Sigma\Lambda$, one dimension higher. An ordinary derivation suspends: inside one hom all its binary operations are available and the arity test agrees, so both families of word equations suspend. It suffices to see that raw existence in the hom at the two poles reflects existence in $\Lambda$. The canonical ordinary map from the free algebra on $\Lambda$ to that hom is a weak equivalence. Under realization it is the unit comparison from the realized ordinary algebra to the forward hom of its enriched bipointed suspension. On a globular arity this is the one-factor identification. On a general computad use the cellular presentation: enriched bipointed suspension is left adjoint to taking the forward hom, so it preserves these globe attachments. The attaching maps are the suspended raw terms, by naturality on the arity charts. The bipointed suspension has the original object as its forward hom, so the comparison for the cellular colimit is an equivalence, also after object-space completion. The ordinary realization comparison detects the weak equivalence. Its local essential-surjectivity test therefore reflects existence at any already parallel pair of raw cells. This proves the transfer without assuming conservativity of full-image conversion in the hom. [Suspension, fixed poles and directed realization](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-16)

**Validation.** For the non-globular attachment $a:f\Rightarrow ff$ on a loop, the two ends of the attaching interval have different path lengths. The $r$-letter word object over the discrete old hom has an $r$-cube for each choice of $r+1$ old path gaps. Independently enumerating rewrites from $f^n$ for $1\le n\le6$ through four events gave 6,937 paths. The 805 executions on distinct original edges matched precisely the 112 corresponding cube charts, including the shifts in whisker position after earlier doublings. The other 6,132 paths act on an output edge at least once and are vertical composites using successive charts. Direct Steiner tableaux of the 3-cube gave the two distinct pastes of elementary squares at the two ends of its top cell. This checks the filtration on a composite attachment and retains the directed higher boundary. The argument for arbitrary attachments is the enriched colimit proof above. [Reproduce the hom calculation](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_hom.py)

## Researcher Response 7

**An exact context criterion for Gray interchange on finite 2-computads.** Suppose all declarations of $\Gamma$ have dimension at most two and $I\in S$. Write $i\leadsto j$ for reachability, including the empty path, in the graph of generating 1-arrows. Call an ordered pair of generating 2-cells $b,c$ separated if $t_0 b\leadsto s_0 c$. The two occurrences may be of the same generator. Then the Gray surface is conservative in $\Gamma$ in every dimension **if and only if there is no separated pair**. In particular a smallest failing context, by number of declarations, is the point with one scalar $a:1_x\Rightarrow1_x$. Its first failure has dimension four. Arbitrary parallel 1-terms are allowed as attaching data in the criterion.

### A raw four-dimensional obstruction at one scalar

For spatially composable 2-cells $b,c$ put
$$
 R(b,c)=(s_1 b\triangleleft c)*_1(b\triangleright t_1 c),
 \qquad L(b,c)=(b\triangleright s_1 c)*_1(t_1 b\triangleleft c).
$$
Let $J(b,c):R(b,c)\to L(b,c)$ be the substituted directed square. These formulas specify binary bracketings throughout. The two generic words are parallel and $I$-equal. Consequently (i) equates their instances and (ii) equates $J(b,c)$ with $1_{R(b,c)}$, also when its arguments are composite.

Take a third 2-cell $z$ to the right. Put $u=R(b,c)$, $v=L(b,c)$ and $h=J(b,c)$. The two mixed whiskerings
$$
 R(h,z)=(s_1 u\triangleleft z)*_1(h\triangleright t_1 z),
 \qquad L(h,z)=(h\triangleright s_1 z)*_1(t_1 u\triangleleft z)
$$
go from $R(u,z)$ to $R(v,z)$ and from $L(u,z)$ to $L(v,z)$, respectively. Thus the following are parallel **kernel** 3-terms from $R(u,z)$ to $L(v,z)$:
$$
 P=J(u,z)*_2 L(h,z),\qquad Q=R(h,z)*_2 J(v,z).
$$

Interchange conversion inhabits both $P\to Q$ and $Q\to P$. Indeed $u\approx v$ and all four exterior 2-corners are equal in the surface. Replace each of the three occurrences of $J$ (inner, lower exterior, upper exterior) by its identity using (ii). To contract the whiskered identities, use the following *kernel 4-cells in both directions*. Over a 2-disc followed by a 1-edge compare $1_d\triangleright f$ with $1_{d\triangleright f}$. Over a vertically composable pair of 2-discs compare $1_d *_1 e$ and $d *_1 1_e$, each with $1_{d *_1 e}$. Over the single 2-disc compare $1_d *_2 1_d$ with $1_d$. In each pair the raw boundaries agree literally and the common Gray image is an identity with full support over the displayed arity. Whisker the first comparison by the stationary factor, apply the appropriate second comparison, then compose at cut two with the remaining identity and apply the disc comparison. This gives 4-cells both ways between each of $P,Q$ and the identity at the common converted corner. Composition at cut three gives the asserted inhabitants using $I$ alone.

Now specialize $b=c=z=a$ in the scalar context. Compute its strict Gray hom at $(x,x)$ by the word filtration of Response 6. The old hom is the tensor unit. The new letter is an interval whose two endpoints attach to the same old object. In filtration degree $r$ the word object is the $r$-fold tensor cube, attached on the union of all its proper faces. Each face map evaluates the selected endpoint to the tensor unit and omits that coordinate. The strict cube is freely generated by its faces, with one top atom, so this gives a strict polygraph with one local generator in each positive dimension. In particular its local 1-cells are words $a^n$. Its generating local 2-cell is the crossing $a^2\to a^2$. At width three the two elementary crossings are $1$ and $2$ according to their temporal position in the word; local 2-cells there are strings in those two letters. Strict interchange only commutes crossings in disjoint substrings, and there are no disjoint pairs at width three.

The unique local 3-generator has boundary $121\to212$. To see both the words and their orientation, start with event order $z,c,b$. Moving $z$ past the block $c,b$ and then exchanging $c,b$ gives positions $1,2,1$, the image of $P$. Exchanging $c,b$ first and moving $z$ past the ordered block gives $2,1,2$, the image of $Q$. Here the block exchange is the tensor of a two-step path with the interval. Its subdivision into two squares follows by composing the two adjacent interval-square charts. For the sign, the negative faces of the oriented tensor cube fix coordinates $1,2,3$ respectively at $0,1,0$ in the original spatial order $b,c,z$ (from $\partial(e\otimes e\otimes e)$). These are precisely the three squares of $P$; the other three give $Q$. Folding the endpoints preserves the crossing positions.

Every strict local 3-cell at this width factors into applications of that generator in contexts: decompose a strict paste into one generating step at a time. Each step preserves width. The generator uses all three strands, so it has only empty exterior strand contexts. Prefixes and suffixes of crossings may still be added. Thus the number of letters $1$ decreases by one at every generating step. Higher generators do not impose relations on lower cells of a free polygraph. This excludes a local 3-cell $212\to121$, hence excludes a raw global 4-term $Q\to P$. In contrast, through dimension three every exchange edge at this context is a loop on its list $a^n$, so the return-path test of Response 2 holds. Contexts with fewer declarations are empty or a single point, covered by the arity lifting argument (the empty context has no closed object). This proves minimality in number of declarations.

### Necessity on arbitrary 2-computads

If $t_0 b\leadsto s_0 b$, whisker $b$ on the right by a writing of the return path when it is nonempty. This gives a 2-term composable spatially with itself. Use it in all three slots of the preceding construction. There is a strict enriched map from $\mathfrak F(\Gamma)$ to the scalar example sending every vertex to $x$, every generating edge to $1_x$, and every generating 2-cell to $a$. The two strict attaching paths always map to $1_x$. The map sends the three inputs to $a$, so a raw reverse 4-cell would give the impossible strict reverse at width three.

Otherwise suppose $t_0 b\leadsto s_0 c$ and no generator has a return path to its own source. Then $b,c$ are distinct. Whisker $b$ by the intervening path if necessary and use the basic square on the resulting pair. Its forward move takes the temporal list $c,b$ to $b,c$. A directed return would have to exchange those same two occurrences as its first step: there are no declared 3-generators, and the list has length two. To exchange them from temporal order $b,c$, the occurrence of $c$ must lie spatially to the left of $b$. This would give $t_0 c\leadsto s_0 b$. Concatenating the given path, an attaching path of $c$, and this last path would give $t_0 b\leadsto s_0 b$. Therefore the reverse 3-type is empty in the kernel by Response 2, and inhabited by conversion.

### Sufficiency in all dimensions

Assume there is no separated pair. For vertices $p,q$, form the graph whose vertices are edge paths $p\leadsto q$ and whose arrows are the contextual single rewrites $(l,b,r)$ of Response 2. Let $C(p,q)$ be its free ordinary category. In the free ordinary strict image the fixed hom is precisely $C(p,q)$ with higher identities. Indeed a 2-paste is a string of these rewrites modulo exchanges of separate occurrences. Such an exchange would give a separated pair. There are no higher generating cells. This strict image interprets every binary surface by projection as in Response 1.

It remains to reflect inhabitation at the *original* directed boundaries. The canonical evaluation from the realized raw fixed hom to $C(p,q)$ is an equivalence in the ordinary weak homotopy theory used in Response 6. Here the equivalence can be computed before applying the strictification functor of that response. Start with the generating 1-graph. Its enriched homs are the discrete sets of edge paths, by the tensor-word formula for the free category. Adjoin the generating 2-cells as interval attachments in the semantic enriched category of the directed realization theorem. Suppose the fixed homs so far are the free categories on the previous contextual rewrites. To attach $b$ at $i,j$, the word filtration in the weak monoidal category has no stages with two or more new letters, since the intervening hom from $j$ to $i$ is empty. At the single-letter stage the outside homs from $p$ to $i$ and from $j$ to $q$ are discrete. Indeed a contextual rewrite in either would give a previously attached generator separated from $b$, by its right or left whisker, respectively. Tensoring these discrete coproducts of units with the interval therefore attaches one ordinary interval, along its two endpoints, for each pair of outside edge paths. The endpoints in the old hom are the two specified contextual paths, by evaluation of the attaching terms. This is the enriched free-extension filtration applied in the colimit-preserving semantic tensor, as in Response 6. [Haugseng, free associative algebra extensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/1312.3881)

Freely attaching such intervals to a free graph category gives the free category on the enlarged graph also in the weak theory. For detail, the free weak category on a graph of discrete arrows is its ordinary path category: view the graph as enriched in weak categories one dimension down, take the free enriched category, and use the coproduct over strings of products of the arrow objects. Every nonempty summand is the terminal object, once per path. Equivalently the Segal spine of each path supplies its composite with a contractible space of choices. Mapping out is consequently a choice of vertices and arrows of the target with those endpoints. This universal property also gives the attachment assertion when the two endpoints of an interval coincide. Under these identifications the comparison map is evaluation on the actual path and rewrite generators. Induction gives the claimed equivalence for the whole computad.

By the fixed-hom identification of directed realization, this comparison represents the strict evaluation from the ordinary raw hom algebra to the underlying weak algebra of $C(p,q)$. A weak equivalence of these algebras reflects existence of a cell between prescribed parallel domain faces whose images admit a cell, by local essential surjectivity. Apply this to the ordinary strict value of a surface inhabitant at any old boundary of global dimension at least two. The resulting raw hom cell is a directed kernel inhabitant at that boundary. At dimensions zero and one vertices and edge paths lift directly. This proves sufficiency, in fact for all eight surfaces. ∎

**Validation.** The checker below emits the two specialized terms and checks their common explicit type using the certified directed kernel. It also checks both orientations of the four generic identity comparisons and computes the two signed boundaries from the Steiner cube. The global impossibility claim uses the width-three polygraph argument above. [Reproduce the raw terms and cube calculation](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_scalar_cube.py)

The all-dimensional context criterion above assumes generation through dimension two. With higher declarations the scalar assignment used in the necessity proof need not extend, and there can also be return paths using declared 3-cells. Q3 is settled by Responses 5–6. The unbounded universal binary questions still open here are the three nonempty interchange-free Gray surfaces and the seven nonempty ordinary surfaces. The arbitrary-context insertion question also remains open.

### First failing dimension and the smallest dimension-three example

The same hypotheses ($I\in S$, generation through dimension two) give the following exhaustive alternatives. Here a *scalar rewrite* means a generator whose two flattened attaching paths are empty.

| Condition on the context | First failing type dimension |
|---|---|
| No separated pair | Conservative in all dimensions |
| There is a separated pair, and every separated pair consists of scalar rewrites joined only by an empty path | Four |
| Some separated pair uses a nonscalar rewrite or admits a nonempty intervening path | Three |

To prove the last two entries it suffices, by Response 2, to characterize the two-step elementary exchanges that admit a return. Write the initial path as $l\,s(b)\,m\,s(c)\,r$, with $b$ to the left of $c$. Along every directed move on a two-step list there are two monotone integers. The start position of the *first* temporal step in the initial path decreases by $|s(b)|+|m|$. The start position of the *last* temporal step in the final path increases by $|t(b)|+|m|$. These assertions follow by reading the two whisker prefixes before and after exchange. Measuring the second position in the final path accounts for arbitrary changes of length in either rewrite.

On a directed cycle both integers are constant. Thus every move in it has an empty gap and a scalar rewrite on its spatial left. In the list immediately after the first move the temporal order is $b,c$. A subsequent move must use $c$ as its spatial left rewrite. No other kind of move exists on this list, since no 3-generator is declared. If the first move alone is a loop, its two labels are equal, so scalarity of $b$ gives scalarity of $c$ as well. Conversely, for two scalar rewrites at the same cut there is a return by applying the generic directed square with the spatial roles reversed. Both steps leave the path unchanged, so the two reverse whisker positions are exactly the original ones. This proves the characterization. Return paths extend under exterior whiskers and temporal prefixes and suffixes. For the middle row of the table either member of a separated pair is a scalar at a vertex, so the preceding width-three cube proof supplies the dimension-four failure. All conditions in the table are finite directed-graph tests; a nonempty return to a vertex is detected by an outgoing edge followed by ordinary reflexive reachability.

The signature $(x,f:x\to x,b:f\Rightarrow f)$ attains the dimension-three failure with three declarations. On $ff$, the two occurrences of $b$ have different positions, so their exchange has no return. With at most two declarations, either there is no generating 2-cell or there is only a single 2-cell over a point. In the latter case it is a scalar rewrite at the only cut. No such context can fail in dimension three, even if the second declaration is allowed arbitrary dimension: with a higher generator there is no generating 2-cell to exchange. This gives both minima, two declarations for unrestricted failure and three for failure in the earliest possible dimension.

The accompanying checker also compared the return criterion with directed reachability on 8,100 exchanges. It allowed repeated labels, both empty and nonempty gaps, and all source and target lengths from zero to two on a generating loop with initial path lengths through five. [Two-step reachability check](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_scalar_cube.py)

## Researcher Response 8

**All eight Gray cases on every finite 2-computad, in every dimension.** For $I\notin S$ the surface is conservative on every such computad, even for conversion by *full Gray-image equality*. For $I\in S$ the exact answer and first failing dimension are those of Response 7. For full Gray-image equality the generator dimension bound is sharp: the suspended scalar of Response 1 has a 3-generator and an unfillable old 5-type. We also prove below that deciding unbounded Gray conservativity at an individual finite computad is undecidable for each $I\in S$.

### A cubical presentation of the fixed hom

Write $W(p,q)$ for the following precubical set, using directed cubes with face maps only. An $r$-cube is a spatial string
$$
       w_0\,b_1\,w_1\cdots b_r\,w_r
$$
from $p$ to $q$, where $w_i$ is an edge path and $b_i$ a generating 2-cell. Both end paths of a mark $b_i$ go between its two objects. A face evaluates one mark at its flattened source or target path and concatenates the two adjacent gaps with this path. Disjoint evaluations commute with reindexing, so these are cubical face maps. Equal faces and empty paths are allowed. The zero-cubes are precisely the edge paths.

Let $|W|_s$ be the strict colimit of the directed tensor cubes over their faces with these labels. Let $|W|_w$ be the corresponding colimit of semantic cubes in the ordinary weak homotopy theory of Response 6, attaching each cube on the union of its faces by a homotopy pushout. There is a canonical equivalence
$$
                      |W|_w \longrightarrow N|W|_s .                 \tag{2}
$$
Here $N$ regards a strict category as weak. To see this, regard the directed interval as an acyclic atom. Tensor powers are acyclic atoms with the usual strict Steiner cubes as their categories of molecules. Extend the face diagram of $W$ to directed complexes with acyclic atoms by gluing one representable atom for each cube on its full face boundary. This is a presheaf construction. It permits arbitrary identifications by the attaching maps, including the two vertices of a loop interval. Its strict molecule category is $|W|_s$, since the molecule functor preserves the cellular pushouts and agrees with the strict tensor on the atoms. Chanavat's Corollary 3.27 and Proposition 3.28 identify the semantic realization of these attachments with the same strict category. More explicitly each boundary inclusion is a monomorphism of presheaves; the pushout adding a cell computes the homotopy pushout of the corresponding discrete presheaves pointwise. The left Kan extension of the semantic atom realization therefore computes these very attachments. Sequential unions use the same pointwise argument. The comparison is the map induced by the strict characteristic maps of the cells. Apply coinductive completion to the equivalence in flagged categories. Completion preserves the colimits used here and gives the weak image $N$ of the same strict target. [Chanavat, §§1.2, 3.3, in particular Corollary 3.27, Proposition 3.28 and Theorem 3.29](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.22242#page=41)

We identify the two sides of (2) with the homs required by the question. Initially the free enriched category on the 1-graph has discrete edge-path homs in both theories. Attach the 2-generators in sequence. When adjoining $b$ at $i,j$, filter the newly allowed spatial strings by the number $r$ of occurrences of $b$. At stage $r$, cut at those occurrences. This gives exactly one older cubical hom in each gap and one new interval in each marked place. Their tensor here is the ordered cubical product with dimensions added (on representables, concatenate coordinates). Its attaching subobject is the union where at least one of the $r$ selected coordinates is an endpoint. Its attaching map evaluates endpoints to the prescribed old paths. Cutting is unique even if all the gaps are empty or representatives of several faces coincide. Conversely every cube in the word object outside its attaching subobject gives one new cube by concatenation. This identifies the pushout in each degree with the stated filtration of $W$.

Strict realization sends ordered cubical products to strict Gray tensors: the two constructions agree on representable cubes and extend by separate colimit preservation. The strict filtration is therefore the free enriched attachment of Response 6. In particular $|W(p,q)|_s=\mathfrak F(\Gamma)(p,q)$. Weak realization likewise sends these word objects to the semantic tensors. One can compute both sides by their separate cellular colimits: tensoring two cubes concatenates their coordinates, and the semantic tensor preserves colimits in each variable. The punctured word objects have the same identification by taking their unions of faces. Each such union embeds into its word object; after semantic realization its pushout is the homotopy pushout attaching the indicated cells. This proves by induction on $r$ and the declarations that the weak filtration computes the enriched semantic free extension: it is the non-symmetric free-algebra word filtration on these same old homs, intervals and attaching maps. The construction applies also when $i=j$. By the directed realization and fixed-hom theorem used in Response 6, the left side of (2) is the realization of the raw hom algebra. Its comparison is strict evaluation: it evaluates each 1-path at its edge word, each new interval at its declared generator, and all word charts by enriched composition. This specifies the enriched comparison throughout the free attachments. [Haugseng, free-algebra attachment filtration](https://arxiv.org/abs/1312.3881) · [Directed realization on charts and on free attachments](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-16)

Consequently strict evaluation from the raw fixed hom to its strict Gray hom is an ordinary weak equivalence. This conclusion concerns the raw hom itself; the equivalence from its *strictification* in Response 6 would not suffice. Local essential surjectivity now reflects existence in every dimension at prescribed parallel raw faces. For inhabitation, the weak equivalence gives a domain cell at the prescribed faces as soon as a strict cell exists there, whether or not that strict cell has a nontrivial equivalence to the image of the lift. Full Gray-image conversion interprets in the strict target, as do all binary surfaces omitting $I$. Dimensions zero and one lift using vertices and edge paths. This proves the assertion about 2-computads.

### Higher attachments represented by acyclic atoms

There is a useful extension in arbitrary generator dimensions. Continue the construction with enriched homs in the presheaf category of directed complexes with acyclic atoms and its Day tensor. Suppose that for a new global $(m+1)$-generator in the hom $(i,j)$ there is an acyclic $m$-atom $U$ and a map $\partial U\to W(i,j)$ taking the two globular boundary pastes to the prescribed strict images of its attaching terms. Then adjoining $U$ on this boundary in that enriched hom preserves the conclusion about strict evaluation. This is a sufficient condition at each attachment.

Indeed the strict atom category freely adjoins its one top generator to the category of its proper faces, with the indicated source and target pastes. Its cellular globe pushout is also a semantic homotopy pushout by the homotopy-polygraph theorem on this atom. Thus the relative attachment of the atom gives precisely the required globe attachment in both enrichments. The inductive comparison evaluates the actual raw attaching maps; restricting to a boundary globe evaluates its two faces, with their common subboundary given by the same chart. This identifies the map of the boundary sphere with the specified strict boundary map. To extend to all homs, use the word filtration with $\partial U\to U$ in each new slot. Ordered tensors of representables are representables on acyclic tensor atoms; the union with a boundary in one of the new slots is a subpresheaf of the word object. For detail, each old hom is cellular on atoms. Filter the old factors by their cells as well: the tensor of characteristic atoms has the product face poset, and a boundary in any selected coordinate is the subcomplex of its proper faces in that coordinate. The complement consists of the product interiors with all selected coordinates interior. Gluing over old faces identifies only those old faces. This proves the subpresheaf assertion also for non-injective old labellings. Strict and semantic realizations handle these word pushouts as above, using Chanavat's monoidality theorem. The enriched homs remain presentations by directed complexes with acyclic atoms, so (2) applies again.

In particular, **all finite 3-computads for which the source and target lists of each 3-generator are both nonempty** admit every full-Gray-image lift of *inhabitation* in every dimension. Construct their two-skeleton first. Take the oriented polygon whose two paths have the lengths of the two lists. Both paths have at least one edge and have disjoint interiors before labelling. This is an acyclic 2-atom: each input edge points to the top face and that face points to each output edge. Within either boundary path the oriented Hasse edges run from its initial vertex toward its terminal vertex; the two paths meet only at those extremal vertices. A directed cycle is impossible. Label each edge by its contextual single rewrite, which is an interval in $W(i,j)$. The path vertices then have the prescribed edge-path labels. These define the required map of the whole polygon boundary even when some labels coincide. The strict images of the two attaching terms are exactly these two paths by Response 2. This includes composite, unequal-length and repeated-letter rule sides.

### No decision algorithm for the all-dimensional individual-context question

Fix any $S$ containing $I$. Use the finite three-object computad of Response 2's string-rewriting reduction, with nonempty sides on every string rule. Write $L,R$ for the two contextual letters across the two columns and $j:RL\to LR$ for their directed square. Only the long hom from $x$ to $z$ has nonidentity local 2-cells. Its strict category $C$ has one object, the free monoid of words on $L,R$ and the extra letters at local dimension one, and the following free local 2-generators: $j$, the string rules, and the two marker rules. Indeed before adjoining the extra letters and rules the cubical hom has just the two intervals and their square, with endpoints folded. Each column can be used only once along the path of objects. The extra declarations in the long hom have no nonidentity exterior spatial whiskers. Their extension adds exactly the listed generators. The polygon result applies, so strict evaluation from the raw hom reflects inhabitation at any old parallel boundary.

We need that imposing $j=1$ does not equate two different *parallel* positive 2-diagrams of $C$. Here is a strict model that proves this. Take signed planar diagrams on this directed word presentation, with formal inverses for every local 2-generator and equivalence by dipole cancellation. Call the resulting monoidal groupoid $G$. Its objects are words, its arrows are diagrams and its tensor is juxtaposition. Empty words have only the empty diagram to themselves, since all rule sides are nonempty. The map from the positive diagram category of $C$ to $G$ is faithful. Indeed positive pastes are planar diagrams modulo exchange of disjoint vertices: the possible readings of one diagram are the linear extensions of its finite order of cells, related by adjacent incomparable swaps. A positive diagram has no inverse pair, so is already reduced. Uniqueness of reduced diagrams then gives faithfulness. Each declared rule retains its own name, even if the opposite orientation of its boundary is declared separately. [Guba–Sapir, Theorem 2.5](https://www.math.vanderbilt.edu/~msapir/ftp/pub/2comp/dc.pdf#page=7)

The subgroupoid $J$ generated by contextual $j$'s and their inverses is thin. Direct each swap $RL\to LR$ toward sorting, with every other letter a barrier. The number of inversions in the intervening two-letter blocks decreases, and normal forms sort each block uniquely. Any two possible first swaps are disjoint, since $RL$ has no proper self-overlap. Their diamond commutes by exchange of disjoint diagram cells. Induction on inversion number shows that all directed sorting diagrams starting at a given word are equal: commute two different first steps to their common successor and use the induction hypothesis on both successors. Writing $d_w$ for the resulting sort, every directed or inverse swap is $d_v^{-1}\circ d_w$ between its end words $w,v$. This proves thinness, with whiskers included.

Identify words connected by $J$ and transport arrows along its unique isomorphisms at either end. The resulting groupoid $\bar G$ has a strict monoidal product induced by juxtaposition, since $J$ is closed under juxtaposition and word whiskering. More explicitly the arrows between two word classes are diagrams with any representatives as endpoints, identified by pre- and post-composition with arrows of $J$. Uniqueness of the intervening $J$-arrow defines composition, and also proves that two arrows at the same original endpoints can be identified only if equal in $G$. Thus $C\to\bar G$ is faithful on parallel local 2-cells and sends every contextual $j$ to an identity.

Make a strict ordinary higher-category interpretation with this monoidal groupoid as the one-object strict 2-category in the long hom. Use the original one-generator path categories in the two short homs, points at identities and empty backward homs. Spatial composition is the functor from the product of the two short homs that sends the pair of powers $(a^k,b^l)$ to the word class of $L^kR^l$. It preserves their product law because $L,R$ commute in the word classes. There are no further nontrivial spatial composites. The extra long-hom letters and rules are interpreted by their own word classes and diagrams. In this ordinary strict model every surface equation holds by ordinary projection. Its evaluation on raw terms factors through the strict Gray evaluation and the map just constructed: they agree on generators, and the square across the columns goes to the identity. On a generic Gray head in an ordinary strict target the value is its projected strict paste, as in Response 1.

Now take an inhabited old global 4-type in the surface. In this model its two local 2-faces have equal images, since the model has only identities in local dimension three. Their original strict images are already parallel in $C$ and hence equal by faithfulness. The strict identity between them lifts in inhabitation at the raw faces by the polygon result. The same reasoning in short and identity homs is immediate. At global dimension at least five any parallel strict faces are already equal, since all original strict homs have only identities above local dimension two. Again lift at the raw boundary. Thus **on this family every surface is conservative in dimensions four and higher**, regardless of reachability in the given string system. This statement concerns each old type in those dimensions and is compatible with failure at a lower dimension.

By Response 2 the same context is conservative through dimension three precisely when $u\to^*v$ in the chosen string system. Consequently its conservativity in *all* dimensions is equivalent to that reachability instance. Finite semigroup presentations with both directions of each relation give the undecidable word problem. This excludes a decision algorithm for the full individual-context question already on these finite 3-computads with an acyclic object graph and positive rule sides. ∎

**Validation.** On mixed cubical words with insertion, deletion, rewriting and a scalar, direct contextual rewriting agreed with both signed walks of 1,776 square boundaries. Evaluation verified 153,280 cubical face identities and 7,465 folded faces of the word attachment. For every word of length at most seven on the two exchange letters and a barrier, the checker retained individual wire identities and the order of all their crossing partners. All 8,890 complete directed sorting paths gave the same diagram at each of the 3,280 starting words. For two long-hom generators with two- and three-step attaching paths, Agda 2.8.0 accepted both directed kernel 4-terms exchanging the two local rewrites at their literal raw boundaries. The all-dimensional comparison uses the acyclic-atom theorem and the undecidability proof uses faithfulness of positive diagrams. [Cubical attachment and shuffle checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_cubical_homs.py)

## Researcher Response 9

**Triple exchanges and a dimension bound for Q2.** Three generating 2-cells are spatially composable if one can insert edge paths between the target object of each and the source object of the next. Occurrences may repeat. If a finite Gray context admits such a triple, every surface containing $I$ fails in dimension four whenever the context has no 4-generators. The conclusion still holds with 4-generators if all of their ordinary strict sources are nonidentity 3-cells, or if all of their ordinary strict targets are nonidentity 3-cells. There is no restriction on the 3-generators, or on declarations above dimension four.

For the positive 3-computads of Response 8 (the two ordered attaching lists of each 3-generator are nonempty), this gives a uniform reduction of **unbounded conservativity to conservativity through dimension six**. Under the additional hypothesis that no 3-generator and 2-generator are spatially composable in either order, there is an exact test in terms of directed rewriting: conservativity for $I\in S$ holds iff there is no spatially composable triple of 2-generators and every elementary exchange has a directed return path. The test permits arbitrary graphs of objects and composite, length-changing attaching paths.

### Support in the ordinary strict projection

Let $F\Gamma$ be the free ordinary strict category, with a Gray attaching term first projected to ordinary strict syntax. For a strict expression record the set of declared generators it uses, including recursively the generators in their boundaries. Take unions at composites and preserve the set at identities. This gives a well-defined support of a strict cell: associativity, the identity-of-composite equations and exchange preserve the unions. For the unit laws the support of either boundary of a cell is contained in that of the cell, by induction on strict expressions. Thus inserting the identity at a composable boundary also preserves the union. In particular a factor in a strict paste can use only generators in its support.

For a *raw* ordinary or Gray term $t$ in any computad, this support of its ordinary strict projection equals the dependency closure of the variables actually used in $t$. To verify the substitution step, let $d$ be a strict cell on a globular arity and let $\sigma$ label that arity by strict cells. Then $\operatorname{supp}(d[\sigma])$ is the union of the supports of $\sigma(v)$ for $v\in\operatorname{supp}(d)$. This follows by strict expression induction; including faces of a generating variable adds only subsets of the support of its label. Every generic raw head projects to an ordinary cell with support its entire arity, by the head calculation in Response 4. Applying the substitution formula uses every argument of the raw head. Induction on declarations takes care of the attaching types of variables, and induction on term syntax now proves the assertion. One consequence is literal strengthening: if the strict support of a raw filler lies in a dependency-closed subcontext, the filler itself types over that subcontext. The generic head and its side condition are independent of the ambient context, and its arguments and their typing derivations restrict recursively.

Apply the construction of $P,Q$ from Response 7 to the three generating 2-cells, absorbing each intervening nonempty path into a right whisker. In the two-skeleton the strict enriched map to the scalar example sends all edges to the identity and all 2-generators to the scalar. It sends $P,Q$ to $121,212$ respectively. Thus there is no raw filler of $Q\to P$ over the two-skeleton. The surface proof of the reverse from Response 7 applies under this substitution in the original context.

Suppose a raw filler exists in the larger context. Its ordinary strict value is a 4-paste with source and target supported on the two-skeleton. With no 4-generators it must be an identity. More generally factor any ordinary strict 4-paste into its top sequence of whiskered generating 4-cells. Such a factorization follows by expanding each lower cut by strict exchange, executing its factors one at a time with stationary whiskers. If there is any generating step, its source at the first step contains the ordinary strict source of that 4-generator in its support. A nonidentity strict 3-cell contains a 3-generator, since a paste built only from lower cells at that dimension is an identity. This contradicts the support of $Q$ under the source hypothesis. The last step and $P$ give the same contradiction under the target hypothesis. Hence the strict value is an identity supported on the two-skeleton in each of the stated cases. Support reflection puts the raw filler there too. This proves the obstruction. In particular no extension of a scalar context using only declarations of dimension three can repair its missing cube reverse.

The obstruction can first appear in dimension four even on an **acyclic object graph with positive attaching paths and a return for every elementary exchange**. Use the linear graph on three edges $f_1,f_2,f_3$, with a generating 2-endomorphism $a_i$ of each edge. For each of the three pairs of positions adjoin a 3-generator from the output of the corresponding basic square to its input, using $a_1\triangleright f_2$ and $a_3$ for the nonadjacent pair. Every generating exchange on any path is one of these with exterior whiskers, and temporal prefixes and suffixes preserve the returns. Response 2 gives conservativity through dimension three. The triple gives failure in dimension four by the support argument, although a strict map sending the three stipulated returns to reverses of the generic directed square would not be available. This example uses no identity as the ordinary strict source or target of any generator.

### At most two active factors

First assume a positive 3-computad has no spatially composable triple of 2-generators. Each interval used in the construction of Response 8 occupies the object interval of a generating 2-cell. Each polygon occupies the object interval of a 3-generator. Its boundary contains a nonempty path of contextual single rewrites. Choosing one such step gives an underlying 2-generator with exterior edge paths connecting its poles to the poles of the polygon. Therefore three spatially composable positive-dimensional factors, intervals or polygons, would provide a spatially composable triple of 2-generators. A characteristic atom in a fixed hom has at most two such factors: in the word filtration its atoms are tensors of old atoms and the newly adjoined atoms, and cutting the spatial word recovers these individual factors, with paths in the gaps. Each has dimension at most two. The strict fixed hom thus has no generators above local dimension four, even when the object graph admits arbitrarily long edge paths. It has only identities above that dimension.

For an old type of global dimension $n\ge7$, its parallel boundary faces in a fixed hom have local dimension $n-2\ge5$. Two parallel identities are equal. The identity between their strict images lifts in inhabitation at the original faces by Response 8. Thus in this case every such type is already inhabited in the kernel. In the complementary case of a spatially composable triple, any $S$ containing $I$ already fails through dimension four. For $I\notin S$ the positive 3-computad comparison of Response 8 applies in all dimensions in either case. This proves the dimension-six reduction, without asserting that six is sharp.

Now impose also the stated exclusion of a 3-generator alongside a 2-generator. The preceding argument excludes a polygon alongside another polygon as well. In a fixed hom the strict image is a free ordinary **2**-category $C$: its 0-cells are edge paths, its 1-generators are contextual rewrites, and its 2-generators are contextual polygons (declared rules) and contextual interval squares (directed exchanges). Indeed the only positive-dimensional tensors left are single intervals, single polygons and pairs of intervals. Every 2-generator has nonempty path on each side, including the two-step sides of a square. In the remainder we prove that conversion is conservative at every old boundary of dimension at least four on these no-triple contexts. Response 2 will then give the exact return-path criterion.

### Faithfulness after identifying the two orders

For each of the present many-object fixed homs, start with the signed diagram groupoid on its directed 2-complex. Concretely its objects, for fixed exterior local vertices, are paths of contextual rewrites, and a signed 2-diagram may use formal inverses of the generating rules or squares. The positive diagrams of $C$ embed faithfully by uniqueness of the reduced diagram. This version allows arbitrary directed graphs as 1-skeleta. Concatenating paths and juxtaposing diagrams between successive local vertices is well defined; the nonempty rule sides also ensure the empty path has only its identity positive diagram to itself. [Guba–Sapir, §2, especially Theorem 2.5](https://www.math.vanderbilt.edu/~msapir/ftp/pub/2comp/dc.pdf)

The subgroupoid generated by contextual exchanges and their inverses, including exchanges in temporal contexts, is thin. No 2-generator has a return path to its own source object, since it would give three composable occurrences of itself. Hence each has nonempty source and target edge paths. For any temporal list form the vector of starting positions of its steps, measured successively in the evolving edge path. A forward exchange reduces this vector lexicographically. Indeed the new first step of the pair starts $|s(b)|+|m|$ positions earlier, where $b$ is the spatially left rewrite and $m$ the intervening path. Previous steps are unchanged. The length of the vector is fixed, so reduction terminates.

For a given neighboring pair of steps there is at most one forward exchange: the positions, the two nonempty attaching segments and the specified contextual labels fix the exterior paths and the intervening gap. Two forward exchanges cannot overlap temporally in exactly one step. If both $a,b$ and $b,c$ were forward moves, the spatial ordering would give $t_0 c\leadsto s_0 b$ and $t_0 b\leadsto s_0 a$. This is the excluded triple. Two distinct first moves therefore act on disjoint pairs in the temporal list. Their common successor is obtained by making both swaps, and the two diagrams to it are equal by strict exchange *inside the fixed hom*. This argument permits both variable path lengths and multiple named rules with the same boundaries. Well-founded induction on the position vector gives unique normal forms and equality of all directed sorting diagrams to them. As in Response 8 every signed exchange path factors as the sort from its source followed by the inverse sort from its target. This proves thinness.

In each hom now identify lists in the same exchange component and transport signed diagrams along the unique exchange isomorphisms at their ends. The resulting strict 2-category has the same vertices as $C$, and the map from $C$ is faithful on parallel 2-cells: at fixed endpoints a transport to themselves is the identity. Juxtaposition in the hom descends because the exchange subgroupoids are closed under it. The quotient also has ordinary strict spatial composition across the object graph. On paths it is edge concatenation. On rewrite lists do the left list first and then the right, with the appropriate stationary edge paths. The two possible orders represent the same class by exchanges. This makes the action on lists strictly functorial for temporal composition, associative and unital: any two interleavings of spatially separate blocks preserving each block order are connected by adjacent exchanges.

For clarity, on a pair of local 2-cells there is no additional interchange relation to impose on a nonidentity signed diagram. Every nonidentity signed diagram has a representative using a generating polygon or square; if the other spatial factor contributes any rewrite, the poles and the outside paths would exhibit a polygon alongside a 2-generator or a triple of 2-generators, respectively. This applies also to a rewrite occurring inside that other factor's diagram. Consequently whenever a nonidentity diagram occurs the other factor is an identity on a stationary edge path. Spatial composition then just appends or prepends that path to every contextual label in the diagram. This action preserves dipoles, commutation of disjoint moves, and contextual exchanges, so respects both quotients. It respects vertical and temporal composition; for lists of identities the requisite law is precisely the independence of the two orders just proved. Successive exterior path whiskers concatenate strictly. These formulas define the ordinary strict target with the displayed fixed homs. Its evaluation agrees on raw terms with strict Gray evaluation followed by the hom maps: it sends the generating squares to identities, sends each contextual rule to its labelled diagram, and respects the free enriched attachments.

A surface inhabitant of an old global 4-type gives equality of its two local 2-faces in this target, which has only identities in local dimension three. Faithfulness at parallel 2-cells gives equality in $C$, hence a strict filler. At global dimension at least five the strict faces are already parallel identities in $C$. In both cases Response 8 lifts existence at the raw boundary. For dimensions at most three Response 2 proves that conservativity for $I\in S$ is equivalent to a directed return for every exchange. Combining this with the triple obstruction proves the stated exact test. The undecidable family of Response 8 satisfies both spatial exclusions, so even this criterion on positive 3-computads need not be decidable. ∎

### Inhabitation can be conservative even when EL(2) fails

Keep just the first two edges of the preceding linear example, their two 2-endomorphisms, and the single declared reverse $k:L\to R$, where $R,L$ are the two layerings and the generic square is $R\to L$. For each $I\in S$ the surface is conservative in every dimension by the exact test: the long hom has the one exchange with its stipulated return. Yet EL(2) fails at precisely this pair. Any raw reverse $e:L\to R$ must use $k$. Otherwise it strengthens to the two-skeleton, whose exchange graph on the two distinct positions has just the forward edge. Its ordinary strict image therefore has $k$ in its support. The ordinary strict image of $1_L$ has no $k$, and ordinary strict projection respects both surface clauses, including under substitution and conversion. Thus no raw reverse can satisfy $e\approx_S1_L$.

In particular the surface identity converted to this reverse type has no definitionally equal raw representative at that type, even though the type has a raw inhabitant. This separates the two conclusions of interest in these very theories. A criterion demanding identity-equal lifts in every conservative context would exclude this example. The conditional equivalence in Known 1 requires its extra hypotheses.

**Validation.** Agda's certified directed checker accepted the three independently declared reverse-square types, both cube terms at the same raw boundary, and the old reverse 4-type in that context. The support proof excludes its inhabitant. A second check enumerated 1,101 well-typed temporal lists in a five-edge graph with overlapping contractions and expansions. Every one of the 957 directed exchanges preserved its final path and strictly reduced its position vector. All 212 possible pairs of distinct first moves formed disjoint diamonds.
