## Researcher Response 10

**Ordinary CaTT is conservative on every finite 2-computad, in all dimensions, for all eight binary surfaces and for insertion $\equiv_{sua}$ (also $\equiv_{su}$).** This includes the scalar context, composite bubbles, and any mixture of generators with empty inputs and empty outputs. In fact *every well-formed old type of dimension at least five* over a 2-computad is already inhabited. The dimension-four part uses Response 5 and is essential: a planar diagram can have nontrivial braid loops, as the scalar in Response 1 illustrates. Together with Responses 7–8, this settles both kernels on contexts generated through dimension two. In an arbitrary-context counterexample to ordinary conservativity, the boundary of the first failing type must itself involve a generator of dimension at least three in its dependency closure.

### The planar diagram spaces are aspherical

We prove that each component of the space of 2-cells at two prescribed 1-arrows in the free weak category on a 2-computad is a 1-type. We use the planar tier presentation from the repository's homotopy-polygraph theorem. Here are the model details that matter when a connected component of a diagram floats free of the boundary.

Mark the individual generating vertices of a planar diagram $G$, retaining ordered wire ports, external boundary data, region incidences and labels. Its complex $K_G$ has a vertex for each serial execution (one generating event per height). A cell specifies successive tiers of simultaneous events, with a complete horizontal order of the events and through-wires at each tier. It is a product of permutohedra, one per tier. Its faces split tiers with the induced orders. These cells are regular for marked diagrams. This also distinguishes two different simultaneous orders of scalar events at the same wire gap. The nerve of the tier poset is its barycentric subdivision. [Tier model, Followup 2, §§1–2](../answered/homotopy-polygraphs-2026-11-19.md#1-the-combinatorial-spaces-that-must-be-contracted)

We recall why the marked complexes calculate the required spaces without a bubble-freeness assumption. The labelled-mesh construction identifies the globular Segal localization of its grid presheaf with the free cellular category. It attaches the actual 2-globes on the specified words, allowing empty words. Replacing raw 1-term boundaries by these edge words uses the equivalence at the free 1-graph before attachment, where each path component is contractible. On the square grid, normalization with fixed marks gives the tier poset and its refinement maps. Forgetting artificial marks gives the action groupoid construction for the finite automorphism group of the underlying labelled diagram: a morphism is a relabelling followed by a marked refinement. Thus the space for an isomorphism type is the homotopy quotient of the marked space. At a grid with cuts, restriction to its cells has the same contractible interleaving categories as in the marked calculation. After forgetting marks this is still the Segal equivalence: ordered boundary wires determine the gluing along each cut, and independent automorphisms of the pieces fix these ordered boundary ports and glue uniquely to a cut-preserving automorphism. Vertices are discrete and the 1-arrow spaces are the discrete edge paths. These facts give the globular Segal conditions before localization. Completeness holds here as well. The number of generating vertices adds under pasting, so a vertically invertible diagram has none, and the vertex-free component at a fixed wire word is a point. Horizontal equivalences then have empty edge word, since edge lengths add. Equivalently, in rows with cuts an inverse must have zero total event count and all its wire words invertible. The identity rows so obtained have contractible fibres. The square-grid spaces over fixed paths therefore compute the 2-cell spaces of the free category. This use of the mesh calculation does not require the strict comparison to be an equivalence. [Labelled meshes, cellular attachment and grid restriction, Followup 2, §§4–6](../answered/homotopy-polygraphs-2026-11-19.md#4-a-mesh-model-with-the-globular-attachment-universal-property) · [Heidemann, mesh models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04870)

Fix a component of $K_G$ and let $N$ be the number of marked events. Label a directed right exchange at successive temporal positions $i,i+1$ by the positive braid generator $s_i$ of $B_N^+$. At a serial execution there is at most one *right* exchange at $i$. If the two events have input positions $a,b$ (numbers of wires to their left when executed) and the later event has $p$ inputs and $q$ outputs, the condition is $a\ge b+p$. After exchange their positions in the new temporal order are $b,a+q-p$. This formula also works at an empty gap. The alternative spatial order at a coincident gap gives the opposite incident orientation and must be retained as a separate edge when the tier data distinguish it.

Two properties give control of arbitrary sequences of exchanges.

1. **The positive braid relations lift in both directions.** For disjoint position pairs the moves commute and their simultaneous cell is the product square. For adjacent indices write $A,B,C$ for the temporal order, $a,b,c$ for the three successive positions, and $p_X,q_X$ for input and output lengths. Either three-swap braid word is executable precisely when
   $a\ge b+p_B$ and $b\ge c+p_C$. Indeed, on the $AB,AC,BC$ route the first and last tests give these inequalities. The middle test is $a+q_B-p_B\ge c+p_C$, which follows. On the $BC,AC,AB$ route the first and last tests give the same inequalities after cancelling the common change $q_C-p_C$. The middle test follows again. In that case the three events form a single simultaneous tier, read spatially as $C,B,A$, and the hexagon identifies the two routes. The inequalities ensure that the later inputs are in the original list to the left of the earlier operations, hence their labelled ports and through-wires also match. More generally any set of enabled indices at a vertex gives a single cell there: merge the consecutive runs into tiers, each in reversed temporal order. The cell's source is the given vertex, and its outgoing edges there have exactly those indices. Conversely every cell with this source is uniquely obtained in this way.
2. **Positive paths from one lifted vertex are determined by their braid.** Read inverse edges negatively. The square and hexagon relations give a braid label on the fundamental groupoid, since these are all two-dimensional faces of products of permutohedra. Two positive words for the same positive braid are related by the positive Artin relations. Property 1 lifts each such replacement, giving a homotopy relative to both endpoints. Conversely positive paths equal in the fundamental groupoid have the same braid, and the positive braid monoid embeds in the braid group. The braid label of a path between fixed vertices of the universal cover is well defined. In particular, if $v$ reaches $w$ by a positive path with label $\beta$, every left divisor of $\beta$ is realized as a prefix on a route from $v$ to $w$: lift a positive word representing the corresponding factorization.

Use the classical finite-type braid monoid facts that two positive braids have a common right multiple, left divisibility has joins, and the join of a subset of standard generators is the positive longest permutation braid on each consecutive block of indices. For two distinct indices this join is their commuting product or their length-three braid. The length is additive on positive words. [Brieskorn–Saito, *Artin-Gruppen und Coxeter-Gruppen*](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01406235)

Order the vertices of the universal cover by positive reachability. This is a partial order: a nonempty positive loop has nonzero braid exponent. Any finite set of lifted vertices has a common successor. Here are details of the latter assertion. For two positive paths with common origin and labels $\alpha,\beta$, traverse the first backwards and the second forwards. All vertices on this valley path have labels, relative to the origin, left dividing a common right multiple $D$ of $\alpha,\beta$. At a negative-positive turn with bottom label $g$, cancel a backtrack if the two generator indices agree. Otherwise use the square or hexagon at the bottom vertex to route over the join of the two outgoing edges. The replacement is homotopic in the cover. All new vertices still divide $D$, since $gs_i,gs_j$ divide $D$ and their join is $g(s_i\vee s_j)$. This procedure terminates: the selected valley disappears and any new valleys have strictly larger positive length, including ones newly exposed at the ends of the replaced segment. More explicitly, cancelling a backtrack may join its two upper endpoints into one new valley, also strictly above the old one. The multiset of nonnegative integers $|D|-|g|$ at valleys strictly decreases in the multiset order. The resulting positive-negative path supplies the common successor. Sharing a successor is now a transitive relation, by applying this construction to the two outgoing paths at any middle vertex. It holds along each edge, hence throughout the connected cover, and induction gives the finite-set assertion.

It remains to check that the actual **topological** universal cover is contractible. Put any finite subcomplex below a common successor $w$ of its vertices. Enlarge it to the complex consisting of cells whose vertices lie in the union of intervals $[a,w]$, taking $a$ over its original vertices. The intervals are finite, since outgoing degree is finite and every positive path between fixed lifts has the length of its braid. This union is upward closed below $w$. For any vertex $v$ in it, let $J$ be the indices of its outgoing edges staying below $w$. If $\beta$ labels $v\to w$, these generators divide $\beta$, so their join divides $\beta$. The cell of property 1 with index set $J$ has sink reached by exactly that join: reverse each tier by successive adjacent swaps, crossing each of its pairs once. Thus the entire cell lies between $v$ and $w$. Every other cell in the union with source $v$ is a face of this one. Indeed its index set is contained in $J$, and splitting tiers gives that face.

Delete the vertices other than $w$ in an order extending reachability. At deletion of $v$ its remaining closed star is precisely that maximal outgoing cell. Any incident cell with another source had a strictly earlier source. The intersection with the portion to retain is the union of faces not containing $v$, onto which a convex polytope strongly deformation retracts (radial projection from a vertex figure gives the retraction). This contracts the finite enlargement to $w$. Every sphere in the universal cover is contained in a finite subcomplex: the original marked complex is finite and its cover is locally finite. Consequently all homotopy groups vanish and the CW universal cover is contractible. The argument also covers zero or one event, when each component is a point.

Each marked component is therefore a $K(\pi,1)$. Passing to the unmarked homotopy quotient preserves 1-truncation, by the homotopy sequence over the classifying space of the discrete automorphism group (or the stabilizer of a component). This proves the asphericity assertion for arbitrary planar diagrams. For two floating scalar events the two ways of passing one another give a loop. The positive braid grading lives on the universal cover, so the proof allows exactly this phenomenon downstairs.

### Consequence at the original CaTT boundary

The ordinary realization theorem identifies the free raw algebra on $\Gamma$ with the free semantic cellular category up to weak equivalence; the unit into its semantic nerve is locally essentially surjective at prescribed parallel raw faces. A computad generated through dimension two can be realized in $(\infty,2)$-categories: their inclusion into $(\infty,\omega)$-categories has the right adjoint taking the core above dimension two, so its colimit of the generating globes gives the same cellular object. For fixed parallel raw 1-arrows, the iterated hom of their semantic images is the space of 2-cells just computed. In a 1-type the space of paths between specified points is 0-truncated. Between two such paths the space of homotopies is empty or contractible. As soon as two parallel homotopies are given, they therefore have a higher cell between them, and the same holds in every higher degree. Applied to the images of the boundary of an old $n$-type, $n\ge5$, this supplies a semantic filler at those very faces. Equivalently the restriction from the mapping space of the $n$-globe to the mapping space of its boundary has nonempty homotopy fibre at that boundary; a fibrant nerve realizes such a lift with the prescribed faces. The unit's weak-equivalence lifting test then gives a raw inhabitant. [Ordinary computadic unit theorem, Response 13](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-13)

Response 5 supplies the remaining dimensions for each word surface and insertion. In particular making units and interchange definitional is conservative on the scalar computad in *every* dimension for the definition in this question. For the final claim about larger contexts, through dimension four use Response 5 directly in the larger context. In dimension at least five, if the dependency closure of the original boundary has rank at most two, strengthen that old type to this subcontext, fill it there by the preceding theorem, and weaken back. No assumption on the support of a derivation using surface conversion is required. ∎

**Validation.** A finite check constructed 1,052 actual tier cells, including floating scalars, births, deaths, a scalar between two wires, and diagrams with boundary wires. The two sides of the local braid or commutation relation lifted together in all 765 tests; all 273 coinitial pairs had their specified common cell. Independently labelling lifts by the Artin action on a free group, the checker recovered the sink of the maximal outgoing cell in 8,378 interval-vertex tests through positive braid length six. The general result uses the universal-cover argument above. [Reproduce the tier and lifted-interval checks](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_planar_tiers.py)

## Researcher Response 11

**All eight binary surfaces are conservative for both kernels on every iterated bipointed suspension of a finite 2-computad.** This includes the Gray suspension of the scalar in Response 1. Thus the counterexample there to full Gray-image conversion is a context where *every* binary surface, even the one containing interchange, is conservative. The result extends to suspensions of arbitrary height by the following transfer principle.

Use $R$ for the ordinary surface generated by the two full binary clauses for $\{A,U,I\}$ together with unary disc removal. The extra rule equates the canonical operation on a disc, applied to a label, with the top entry of the label. If $R$ is conservative for ordinary CaTT in $\Lambda$, then $R$ is conservative for ordinary CaTT in $\Sigma\Lambda$ and every binary surface is conservative for the Gray kernel in $\Sigma\Lambda$. The disc rule accounts for what becomes of a spatial whisker under restriction to the single forward hom.

**Base of the induction.** The ordinary proof of Response 5 also works for $R$ through dimension four. Its history model $E(G)$ satisfies the additional disc equation. In rank one a one-edge composite is that edge. The rank-two generic disc operation has just one column and one event, so substitution of a history returns the whole history, with no added address. In rank three the top generator with its specified normal endpoints is itself the unique generic lift of its strict value. Substitution on it is evaluation on the input 3-cell: substitution into its two single-event endpoint faces gives the input normal representatives, with identity sorting transports. In higher ranks there is at most one cell at the specified boundary and the lower disc equations give that boundary. Thus the same surface interpretation, span of kernel comparisons and return at the raw 4-boundary apply. In lower ranks strict projection and ordinary prescribed-boundary lifting suffice. By Response 10 every old type of higher dimension over a 2-computad is inhabited outright. Hence $R$ is conservative on every such computad.

**Restriction of operations.** Colour the objects at the two poles by $0,1$. In a labelled globular arity $P=\bigvee_i\Sigma\Theta_i$ the root vertices have nondecreasing colours. For a term crossing from 0 to 1, exactly one column $j$ of its outer head crosses. Restriction keeps its crossing arguments, lowers their dimensions by one, and uses $\Theta_j$ as the new arity. A crossing variable restricts to the corresponding variable. For a path operation take its single crossing argument. For a higher head take the ordinary coherence over $\Theta_j$ on the restrictions of its generic faces, with the restricted labelling. Write $t|$ for this operation. A crossing 1-type restricts to $\star$ and higher types restrict at their base and both endpoints.

For ordinary kernel syntax this is the repository's restriction to a directed cut. For a Gray kernel head the same recipe gives an ordinary kernel term. Indeed the strict value after restriction is the projection of the ordinary strict image to its $j$th factor. On the Gray total cell this is the total of $\Theta_j$ padded to the local degree. If padded strictly above its dimension, the two projected faces are the same full-support strict cell. At the unpadded degree the faces are the two prescribed boundaries of the total. For a Gray coherence its full-support base projects to a full-support cell in every factor by Response 4. These give the ordinary operation or coherence side condition on the restricted faces: their variable supports agree with their strict supports by Response 9. The proof is simultaneous induction on generic boundaries and arguments; strict evaluation commutes with restriction on applications because both use the strict factor projection followed by the restricted labelling. Composing substitutions selects exactly the same root column by the object colours. Thus restriction commutes with substitution, including on the types of all retained arguments. For a suspended ordinary head it recovers the original head literally. [Raw cut construction and substitution proof, Response 4](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r7-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-4)

**Restriction of the equations.** In the ordinary target $R$, the restriction of a crossing binary word is equal to the following word over the selected child arity. Send crossing variables to their restrictions and identities to identities (a crossing word of dimension one just selects its crossing edge). At cut $k>0$ use the binary operation at $k-1$. At cut zero select the crossing operand and pad it by identities to the local output dimension. Denote this word by $w^c$. The formula works for an ordinary horizontal composite and for all directed binary operations. Applied to the literal source and target of a word it gives the literal source and target of $w^c$ whenever these local faces exist: the lower cut of a spatial composite keeps the same crossing factor, and taking a face lowers an identity tower by one once its top is reached. For a temporal cut this is the ordinary binary boundary formula with all indices shifted. These facts also prove that parallel generic words give parallel ordinary words *before* imposing any equations.

Here is why the raw restriction has this word as its value in $R$. An identity on a crossing argument is a suspended identity. The arity of a positive-cut binary operation is a suspension and restriction gives the lower binary operation. For a cut-zero binary operation, the chosen child arity is a disc and its top variable is precisely the chosen operand. In local dimension zero the projection selects that entry. If the output has the top dimension of this disc, its restricted generic boundary is the standard disc boundary after the same computation in lower degree. Congruence in the head type and disc removal give the top variable. If the output is higher, its two faces reduce to the same iterated identity word on the top variable. After congruence the head is between identical words with full support, so clause (ii) reduces it to their identity. Induction on the degree proves this calculation for the generic binary heads, and substitution gives it on arbitrary word arguments. It also proves the assertion about types when a crossing edge carries a longer spatial writing.

If $w,w'$ are among the generating pairs of any of the eight surfaces, their ordinary strict values agree. Their factor projections are the strict values of $w^c,(w')^c$. These are parallel ordinary words on a pasting arity, equal in the free ordinary strict word algebra (the full three laws in the Setting). Clause (i) of $R$ identifies them. For an instance of (ii) the restricted higher head has these two words as its endpoints up to congruence. Their common strict value has full support: it is the base of the projected identity, and the generic head projects with full support. More explicitly an unpadded total cannot be an endomorphism of positive dimension on the child pasting tree, whereas a padded total or a coherence has full base. Hence the ordinary head between the two words exists and clause (ii) of $R$ identifies it with the restricted identity. A crossing degree-one head in (ii) cannot arise, since an identity on a global object has equal poles. Finally the ordinary global disc equation itself restricts to disc removal, or to the identical variable at local degree zero.

This verifies the recursive interpretation also on a surface derivation. At variable and head rules use restriction, at conversion use the projected equality, and at substitution use the same column calculation as on raw terms. For a generic type already using conversion, ordinary strict evaluation still gives the indicated total or full identity. The supports needed for the ordinary head test are preserved: the support of an application is the union for its retained labels, and all target equations, including disc removal, have equal ordinary strict supports. This is the same induction on formation, equalities and conversion as on raw expressions, with clause (i), clause (ii) and disc removal handled above. In particular the interpretation at an *old* boundary is the raw restriction of that particular writing.

**Reflection at the forward hom.** For either kernel the raw ordinary hom algebra $H$ between the poles of the free algebra on $\Sigma\Lambda$ admits a map $H\to\mathrm{Free}_{\mathrm{CaTT}}(\Lambda)$ by this restriction. It respects the ordinary hom operations by the suspension calculation. It is a weak equivalence: the map in the other direction suspending the generators is a section and is itself a weak equivalence. For the latter assertion the semantic bipointed suspension has forward hom the original realized category. The map is that identification on globular arities and on the specified raw attachments, hence on their cellular colimit by the suspension–fixed-hom adjunction. This is the suspension argument of Response 6 for the directed theory; the ordinary case uses the same argument with cartesian enrichment. The ordinary realization comparison detects these weak equivalences. Consequently restriction reflects existence at any parallel raw faces in $H$, including faces which themselves use multi-column heads.

If a surface inhabits an old type across the poles, its restriction is an $R$-inhabitant at the old restricted type in $\Lambda$. Conservativity there gives a raw ordinary inhabitant and the weak equivalence just proved gives a raw filler in the original hom. The equal-pole semantic homs are terminal, so existence at old parallel faces there follows by the same weak-equivalence lifting test. The reverse hom is empty even in ordinary strict projection, and global vertices and edge paths lift directly. This proves the transfer for the Gray binary surfaces and the ordinary augmented surface $R$. Induction on suspensions starting from the 2-computad result proves the stated theorem. ∎

**Validation.** For both choices of crossing column, and for equal and mixed input ranks through four, the raw cut calculation checked 46 binary projections, their literal inferred types, and each disc or identity padding used to obtain the word formula. Both projected Gray-square heads were accepted by the certified ordinary Agda checker. The four projected endpoints put the local unit on opposite sides of the selected arrow, as required for applying the word equations. [Word-cut checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_word_cut.py)

## Researcher Response 12

**For ordinary CaTT, insertion is conservative in a finite computad $B$ if and only if it is conservative in its bipointed suspension $\Sigma B$. If there is a first failure, suspension raises its dimension by exactly one.** More generally, take a finite directed graph $D$ with a finite ordinary computad $B_e$ on each edge. Form $W$ by adjoining the vertices of $D$ and, independently for each $e:p\to q$, the declarations of $\Sigma B_e$ with poles $p,q$. Parallel edges and loops are allowed. Insertion is conservative in $W$ exactly when it is conservative in every $B_e$. In particular this gives an all-dimensional affirmative answer for insertion on any finite iteration of this construction starting from finite 2-computads. For these same ordinary contexts all eight binary surfaces are conservative as well.

### Insertion at an object cut

Use the restriction $t\mapsto t|$ of Response 11. It is defined at a crossing between colours 0 and 1 when all generating arrows respect the order of the colours. Write a tree as $S=[S_1,\ldots,S_k]$. In an arity with a crossing, write $j$ for its crossing column. We first check the standard coherences used in insertion. With the notation of Definition 4 of Finster–Rice–Vicary, for $h\ge\dim S$ the restriction of $C_S^h$ is definitionally equal to $C_{S_j}^{h-1}$. At degree zero on the right read this as the selected vertex. The same formula holds for the standard term and for its type, taking the restricted type in degree zero to be $\star$. Indeed a crossing path coherence selects its crossing edge. At the next stage the standard type is formed from the two standard terms on the signed truncations. Truncation at rank $r\ge1$ retains the root list and truncates each child at $r-1$. Its signed inclusion on crossing slots is the signed inclusion of that child. Restriction commutes with these substitutions. Induction therefore identifies both faces with the prescribed lower standard faces, including when a truncation already equals its tree. The choice of top variable for the standard term of a disc agrees with its standard composite by disc removal. This last step matters when the selected child is linear and the whole tree branches. [Standard coherences and insertion, Definitions 4 and 18–23](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303)

We will also use disc trivialization **in the insertion syntax**. In a disc, any surface term equals the variable or iterated identity specified by its ordinary strict value. Here are the details concerning conversion in this assertion. Take an insertion normal form and induct on dimension, then on term size within that dimension. Dimensions of arguments do not exceed the output dimension. A canonical identity is dealt with at its base. For any other head, each maximal slot has a normal argument which by induction is a variable or an identity tower. A canonical identity in such a slot admits pruning, the insertion of the identity on the disc of one lower dimension. Its trunk is long enough for any branch ending at that slot. Hence in a normal nonidentity head all maximal labels must be variables. Their faces determine the other labels up to equality, and distinct disc variables have distinct ordinary strict images. The labelling is therefore, up to equality, a globular map of the arity's variables into the disc. Such a map is a subdisc inclusion: a branching arity would require two consecutive positive cells composable at the branching level, which the variables of a disc do not supply. It remains to compute the head on this subdisc. Its generic faces have lower dimension, so apply the induction to them in that disc. At the disc's own degree the faces of the total are its two boundary variables and disc removal applies. Above it the generic faces have the same full strict value, hence become the same identity tower on its top, and endo-coherence removal applies. This is the disc-trivialization argument with the normal forms of the larger theory. It does not assume that a term typed using insertion already has a strictly-unital typing. [Disc trivialization, Theorem 45 and Lemmas 46–48](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2007.08307) · [Insertion normalization and the unit rules](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303)

Consider an insertion at branch $P$ of $S$ of a tree $T$. Work first over the joined arity $S\ll_P T$, using the exterior substitution $\kappa$. The general equality follows by the common labelling. Colour this arity by any monotone object cut; the old arity is coloured by $\kappa$. If the insertion takes place in a stationary column, both restricted heads use the same selected child, faces and labels. This includes a root splice by an empty list: both ends of that splice have the same colour. There are two remaining cases.

1. For $P=j::P'$ of length at least two, $T=[T_1]$ by the trunk condition. The selected child of the joined arity is $S_j\ll_{P'}T_1$. The interior and exterior substitutions there are the lower interior and exterior, since their defining terms on the selected column are suspensions. The labellings splice there by the lower labelling formula. The branch and trunk bounds both shift by one. The selected maximal argument equals the lower standard coherence by the standard-term calculation. If the redex is required to display the standard head syntactically, replace that entry by the equal standard term before applying the rule. It is a maximal variable, so no later declaration depends on it, and conversion checks its own type. Congruence then gives the desired equality of the original restricted heads.
2. For $P=[j]$, the old selected child is a disc $D^d$, where $d+1$ is the leaf height. The splice replaces the whole root column by the list $T$. Let $V$ be its selected child. The exterior on the retained slots is the disc substitution $b$ classifying $m=C_V^d$, up to the equality for standard terms above. At local output degree zero the generic path on either side selects the same vertex of $V$. In positive local degree $n$, let $B$ be the restricted generic head type over $D^d$. The two heads to compare are
   $$
   \operatorname{coh}_{D^d,B}[b],\qquad
   \operatorname{coh}_{V,B[b]}[\mathrm{id}].
   $$
   Fullness gives $n\ge d$. If $n=d>0$, the strict faces are the two faces of the top variable of $D^d$. Disc trivialization identifies $B$ with the standard disc type. The first head returns $m$ by disc removal. Since $b$ classifies $m$, the second head is the standard head at its type and equals $m$ too. If $n>d$, both generic faces have the strict value of the $(n-1-d)$-fold identity on the disc top. Disc trivialization identifies them with that same term. After substitution the two heads consequently reduce by endo-coherence removal to the $(n-d)$-fold identity on $m$. This also handles $d=0$ in positive output degree.

Disc removal itself restricts to disc removal or vertex selection. A crossing endo-coherence restricts to an endo-coherence on equal faces, and its identity restricts to the lower identity. These checks give restriction on derivations by induction, using the head and substitution clauses of Response 11. The standard ordinary head condition survives restriction: an unpadded total has the boundary of the selected factor's total, while a padded total or full identity has equal full-support strict faces there. Support in a pasting tree agrees with strict support by the support calculation of Response 9. Thus the argument applies to head types and substitutions formed using conversion. For an old type its restriction is its actual raw restriction.

Across the poles of $\Sigma B$, a surface inhabitant now restricts to an insertion inhabitant in $B$. A raw inhabitant at the restricted old faces lifts in existence at the original faces by the forward-hom weak equivalence of Response 11. Equal-pole homs are equivalent to the terminal category, and there is no reverse crossing. Conversely suspend any failing old type and its surface derivation. Suspension respects the three equations, for insertion by the suspended interior, exterior and joined substitution. A raw filler upstairs would restrict to one at the original type downstairs. This proves the assertion for a single suspension, including the dimension shift.

### Independent edge computads and repeated positions

Every term of positive degree in $W$ has an underlying path in $D$: send all local objects of $B_e$ to the single arrow $e$ and all its higher declarations to identities in the free category on $D$. These assignments respect the attachments, which are suspended. This strict interpretation respects all the surface equations. Thus parallel faces of a term of degree at least two map to the very same edge word. The strict interpretation of a head concatenates the successive column words. Within a column all the parallel arrow labels have the same word, since successive such labels are related by a 2-cell. In higher dimensions the underlying word is determined by the 1-faces.

First use a linear chain of edges. Cutting at position $i$ gives the ordinary lower computad $B_{e_i}$ on the nose. In the raw forward hom, the combined map on all crossed positions is a weak equivalence onto the product of the free raw categories on those lower computads. To verify **which map** realizes this product, for each $i$ form the two-object raw algebra with terminal stationary homs, empty reverse hom, and forward hom $\mathrm{Free}(B_{e_i})$. At a crossing head use the raw restriction formula, and at a stationary head take the unique cell. This defines an algebra by the raw substitution calculation. The map from the free bipointed suspension to it is a weak equivalence on all four homs by Response 11. The cut map from the free chain to this algebra is the identity assignment on the $i$th generating hom and takes every other generating hom to the stationary identity. Semantically, the chain is the free category cartesian-enriched in ordinary weak $\omega$-categories on its sequence of generating homs. Indeed a map out selects the vertices and a map from each $\Phi\mathrm{Free}(B_{e_i})$ into the indicated hom, by the bipointed suspension universal property and the gluing at vertices. The free enriched category with those data has, on a nonempty interval, the product of the traversed generating homs. Its composition concatenates tuples; its equal-vertex hom is terminal. By this universal property the cut map just described realizes projection onto the $i$th factor, since every other factor composes with a stationary identity. This proves the product comparison at the actual raw boundary. In particular, an inhabitant in each factor at the restricted raw faces suffices by local essential surjectivity. The empty interval is covered by the terminal-hom assertion.

For a general graph, fix the underlying word $w=e_1\cdots e_m$ of a surface filler. Unfold it to the linear chain $0,\ldots,m$ with a fresh copy of $B_{e_i}$ at position $i$, mapping the chain back to $W$. The filler and its boundary have simultaneous lifts to this chain. Here is the syntax argument which distinguishes different occurrences of the same edge. Lift a positive-dimensional expression over an occurrence of its word as an interval of $w$, and an object over its specified endpoint. A variable over $e$ lifts to its indicated copy and its attaching type lifts there because that type is suspended. At a head, partition the interval by the lengths of its successive column words. Place the root vertices at the resulting division points, and lift each label on its own subinterval. This determines also each zero-length subinterval, even when several are consecutive. Recursing on arguments constructs the raw lift. For a substituted expression these subdivisions refine one another by concatenation; thus lifting its faces and substituting lifted labels agree. For derivations with conversion, each generic equation lifts with exactly the same lifted labelling. For insertion this follows by the splice formulas: a first-level splice subdivides the old column by the inserted column words; a deeper splice refines inside its one selected column. For disc removal it is the single disc label, and for an endo-coherence the same subdivision of the common face is used on both sides. More generally both proofs of a fixed expression on a specified interval yield the same lift by the expression recursion. This proves the lifting assertion also for congruence, transitivity and change of type. At global degree one one may use the surface term's word to specify the chain; the lifts of its endpoints require only the two designated vertices.

Consequently a surface filler gives a lower surface filler at each position of this linear chain. If each relevant $B_{e_i}$ is conservative at the local dimension, choose a raw filler there at its restricted old type. The product comparison supplies a raw filler on the chain, which maps back to the requested type over $W$. In the opposite direction suspend a failing type from a single $B_e$ and include it on that edge. At degrees at least two any prospective raw filler has underlying word $e$ since its faces do, and the single-position lifting argument followed by restriction would give a raw filler below. Failure in local degree zero is impossible because object terms are variables. Vertices and global edge existence also lift directly. This proves the equivalence and identifies the first failing dimension of $W$ as one plus the least first failure among its edge computads, with infinity when there is none.

Finally, for the auxiliary ordinary binary surface $R$ of Response 11 the same word unfolding works: a generic word equation and its coherence equation lift under the same interval labelling, since strict paths of both words coincide. Each single-position restriction is sound for $R$ by Response 11. The product comparison is raw, so edgewise $R$-conservativity implies $R$-conservativity in $W$. Response 10 and the base case of Response 11 start the two inductions. ∎

**Validation.** On all nonempty ordered trees with at most five edges, the published normalizer checked 393 restricted standard coherences, including two padding degrees. The checker also constructs the actual exterior substitution for a middle disc flanked by two edges, including empty replacements, forms the joined head with its transported type, checks raw typing, and sends both terms of each proposed equality to the normalizer. It verified 260 root insertions, 260 nested insertions, 524 cuts inside the splice and 520 outside it. On all nonempty words through length four in two self-loops, an independent raw check folded and reconstructed 120 expressions and typechecked all 392 positional projections. Its edge computads include an attachment at a composite, and its expressions include empty subintervals at each seam. These finite checks exercise the arity and substitution calculation; the unrestricted conservativity statement uses the induction and fixed-hom comparison above. [Insertion checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_insertion_cut.py) · [Positional lifting checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_path_lift.py) · [Normalizer revision](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/tree/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc)

**Scope of the result.** Q3 has the all-context answer in Responses 5–6. The new equivalence concerns contexts whose attachments come from the individual edge computads. In a general computad a generating 2-cell can already replace one path of base edges by a different path. Its two faces then have no common list of positions on which to apply the product proof. Thus the unbounded universal classification in Q1, arbitrary-context insertion conservativity, and the general per-context classification in Q2 remain open here.

## Researcher Response 13

**Gray interchange has no uniform first-failure bound on finite contexts.** For every $d\ge3$ there is a finite context with acyclic generating 1-graph in which each of the four surfaces containing $I$ is conservative through dimension $d-1$ and first fails in dimension $d$. For every even $d\ge4$ there is also a one-object example with just two generating 1-arrows and the same first failure. Moreover **no finite extension of the one-scalar context can make any of these four surfaces conservative**. In every such extension they still fail in every even dimension strictly above the largest declaration dimension, starting at dimension four.

More generally, in the graph of generating 1-arrows write $b_1,\ldots,b_k$ for generating 2-cells with $t_0 b_i\leadsto s_0 b_{i+1}$. A generator is repeatable if $t_0 b\leadsto s_0 b$. Empty intervening paths are allowed.

* For **odd** $k\ge3$, such a string gives a failing old $(k+1)$-type if the context has no $(k+1)$-generator. Repeated occurrences are allowed.
* For **every** $k\ge2$ the same conclusion holds if none of the chosen generators is repeatable.
* In particular, if the largest declaration dimension is $D\ge2$, conservativity requires that the supremum of the lengths of these strings be **strictly less than $D$**. This is an effective necessary test on an arbitrary finite context. An infinite supremum gives a repeatable generator by finiteness, and hence a string of any odd length beyond $D$. With a finite supremum at least $D$, take $k=D$ in the second assertion.

### One square suffices to reverse the boundary of any cube in the surface

Use local dimensions in the fixed hom, so the interval $J=D^1$ represents a global 2-generator. Take the pasting arity $P$ with successive children $D^2,J,\ldots,J$, with $k-2$ copies of $J$. Its forward strict hom has a total local $k$-cell $c$. Choose parallel raw global $k$-terms $p,q$ over $P$ representing the source and target of $c$, respectively. Such simultaneous choices exist by lifting the common lower globe first and then the two faces at that globe. The quoted lifting property applies at every prescribed parallel raw boundary on a finite pasting arity. [Directed arity lifting](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r9-2026-11-20.md#a-directed-interchange-theory)

Substitute the generic directed square on two consecutive intervals for the $D^2$ label, and leave the other interval labels free. This gives a substitution $s$ on the arity of $k$ intervals. Write $R,L$ for the two raw walks of that square, in the order in Response 7. The strict map for $s$ replaces the 2-globe by the square atom. In the directed chain complexes the map sends the vertices to $00,11$, its two 1-faces to the signed boundary walks and its 2-generator to the top square. Tensor with the remaining identities. In particular the top chain of the resulting $k$-cell is one copy of the full cube. This uses the usual tensor differential, with $\partial e=e^+-e^-$ for a single interval. [Steiner, tensor products and atoms](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0403237)

The surface inhabits $q[s]\to p[s]$. To see this, clause (i) equates $R,L$ and clause (ii) equates the square with $1_R$. Thus $s$ is entrywise equal to the substitution sending its first top to $1_R$ and both 1-faces to $R$. This latter substitution factors through a **raw** substitution $u$ over the smaller arity with children $J,J,\ldots,J$: send the first 2-globe top to the identity on the first interval variable and its two 1-faces to that variable. The substitution back out of the smaller arity labels its first interval by the raw walk $R$. In the smaller arity the forward hom has dimension $k-1$. Therefore $c[u]$ is a strict identity and $p[u],q[u]$ have equal strict images. Arity lifting gives a **raw** arrow $q[u]\to p[u]$ at these particular parallel faces. Substitution followed by conversion gives the claimed surface inhabitant. This argument uses only the two clauses for $I$ at the original square, independently of the dimension of the cube. Finally substitute the $b_i$, right-whiskering by writings of the intervening paths as necessary. All the resulting boundary terms are old terms over the two-skeleton.

### A signed obstruction at every odd rank

Over the two-skeleton send all objects to one point, all edges to its identity, and all generating 2-cells to the single scalar $a$. All the stated whiskers go to stationary identities. By the scalar word filtration of Response 7 the strict fixed hom $C$ is the free polygraph with a local generator $g_r$ for each $r\ge1$. Its characteristic cube has all its proper faces attached by omitting stationary coordinates. In particular $g_1$ is the loop $a$. Its folded 1-word has length one; the two 1-faces of $g_r$ have length $r$ for $r>1$.

Keep the starting position of a word. Form the cover of $C$ along the length functor to the one-object category $B\mathbb N$, using the translation category with vertices $i\in\mathbb Z$ and arrows $i\to i+n$ for $n\ge0$. A cell lifts uniquely once its starting vertex is fixed, by lifting its paths from that vertex. The lifted polygraph has generators $g_{r,i}$. Indeed a generating step with starting vertex $i$ has one lift, and in a composite the starting vertices of its pieces are determined by the concatenated 1-words. This gives inverse descriptions by lifted pastes and by lifts of pastes; associativity, identities and exchange preserve the positions.

In the lifted characteristic cube at $i$, a vertex with $t$ completed coordinates is at $i+t$. A facet with coordinate fixed at $0$ is a copy of $g_{r-1,i}$ and the matching upper facet is a copy of $g_{r-1,i+1}$ in top degree. Thus in the additive chains of this polygraph,
$$
 \partial[g_{r,i}]
   =\sum_{j=1}^r(-1)^{j-1}\bigl([g_{r-1,i+1}]-[g_{r-1,i}]\bigr).
$$
This computation uses chains of the translated cells; the two terms in a summand have distinct starting vertices. Fix odd $k\ge3$ and give a lifted $(k-1)$-generator at position $i$ weight $i$, extending linearly. Every lifted $k$-generator increases the weight by one. The difference of the weights at the ends of any directed $k$-paste is therefore its nonnegative count of generating $k$-steps. This follows equally from linearization, where strict composition in any lower degree adds top chains and identities contribute zero. In the forward substituted cube the count is exactly one. Its reversed boundary has difference $-1$, so it has no directed filler. A filler before passing to the cover would lift, giving the same contradiction there. At $k=3$ the weight counts the offset of the crossing and recovers the distinction between $121$ and $212$.

If the original context has no global $(k+1)$-generator, the ordinary strict projection of a proposed raw $(k+1)$-filler is necessarily an identity on its source. That source uses only the two-skeleton. By support reflection and strengthening in Response 9 the filler itself belongs to the two-skeleton, where the scalar calculation excludes it. This allows arbitrary generators in intermediate ranks, with arbitrary attachments, and arbitrary declarations above the degree being tested.

### Distinguishing all axes when no chosen generator is repeatable

Map the vertices of the two-skeleton monotonically to $0,\ldots,k$ by sending $x$ to the number of $i$ for which $t_0 b_i\leadsto x$. These indices form an initial segment since there is a path across each $b_i$. At $s_0 b_j$ the value is $j-1$, and at $t_0 b_j$ it is $j$: a further reachability in either count would return from a chosen generator's target to its source. Map every edge to the unique corresponding path in the line, which has one edge $f_i$ at each position. Put one 2-endomorphism $a_i:f_i\Rightarrow f_i$ there. Send $b_i$ to $a_i$ and all other 2-generators to identities. This is a strict enriched assignment because paths with the same endpoints in the line agree and the chosen generators are distinct.

Its long strict hom is the tensor product of the $k$ one-object path categories on the $a_i$. In the word filtration each position contributes either its vertex or its single generating interval, so there is a local generator $g_A$ for each nonempty set $A$ of active positions. Its degree is $|A|$. Lift along the vector of counts of the letters $a_i$, now with starting vertex $v\in\mathbb Z^k$. The top generator $g_{[k],v}$ has a lower facet omitting $j$ at $v$ and its upper mate at $v+e_j$. Weight only the $(k-1)$-generators omitting coordinate 1, giving them weight $v_1$ at start $v$. In the signed boundary the term for $j=1$ contributes $+1$ and all others contribute zero. Thus in **every** rank $k\ge2$ the forward cube increases weight by one and its reverse cannot be a directed paste. As before the support argument excludes new raw fillers in any extension without a $(k+1)$-generator. For $k=2$ this is the directed-square obstruction with the two letters kept distinct.

### Realizing prescribed first failing dimensions

Let $E$ be the ordinary weak coherent walking equivalence on two objects, using the ordinary computad with separate inverse choices. Its forward arrow is a generator. Its truncation in each finite dimension has finitely many declarations: each required equivalence contributes two same-degree inverse generators and two next-degree cancellation witnesses, and the recursion continues on the witnesses. The source inclusion of the point into $E$ is an ordinary weak equivalence. Here the weak-algebra assertion follows from the walking-equivalence attachment theorem for the point, or from its groupoidal case; contractibility just of the strict reflection would not give the required conclusion. [Walking presentation, FHM §5](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.09849) · [Ordinary computad expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30254) · [Weak walking attachment at the point, Response 1](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-1)

Fix $d\ge3$ and put $k=d-1$. Form a chain of $k$ independent copies of the suspended $E$, gluing successive poles. Denote it by $X$. Its realized directed fixed hom over a nonempty interval of $m$ edges is $(\Phi E)^{\boxtimes m}$, equivalent to the tensor unit. Indeed the free enriched category on the chain has exactly this tensor word for that interval. It has the mapping property of the displayed globular attachments by the suspension–fixed-hom adjunction. Completion preserves its homs at the specified vertices. The directed realization theorem identifies the realized raw homs with these fixed homs. The map to the ordinary line sends each generating hom to the unit, so is a weak equivalence on each hom, including the stationary and empty ones. The same reasoning with the two poles of *one* $\Sigma E$ identified gives one object and a hom equivalent to the discrete category on $\mathbb N$: the hom is now the coproduct over all word lengths of the same tensor powers, including the unit for the empty word. The projection records that length. These colimit computations apply to $E$ by its cellular presentation; all terms and attaching expressions are finite. [Directed realization and fixed homs, Response 16](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-16)

In either infinite context the line or length projection interprets every surface in the question for the Gray kernel. At any old boundary where the surface supplies a cell, the projection therefore supplies one too. The weak equivalence on the fixed homs reflects existence at those prescribed raw faces. At global dimensions zero and one the vertices and the indicated paths have direct raw representatives. Now retain just the declarations of $X$ through global dimension $d-1$. This is a finite context. Any surface derivation there is still a derivation over $X$. A raw filler in $X$ of degree at most $d-1$ belongs to the retained context: at a Gray head the largest dimension of any argument slot is bounded by the degree of the output. For a total this follows from the sum of child degrees in the tensor, and for a full identity from support of its base. Induction on the raw expression and its variable types gives the restriction. Hence the finite context is conservative through $d-1$.

On the chain the forward arrow of $E$ gives a different, spatially consecutive 2-generator at each of the $k$ positions. The finite context has no global $d$-generators. The distinguished-axis obstruction gives failure at $d$. Its generating 1-graph is acyclic, and none of the higher generators has two ordinary strict identity attaching faces: a cancellation has a composite containing genuine generators on one side, and reversing a declared boundary retains that side. In the one-object construction its forward 2-generator is repeatable. For even $d$ the odd-rank obstruction gives failure at $d$ there as well. These establish the asserted *first* failures. Finally a scalar is repeatable in every extension of its context, and a finite extension has bounded declaration dimension. Applying the odd-rank result above that bound proves the claim about all finite extensions. ∎

**Validation.** For local ranks two through nine the checker applies both substitutions of the 2-globe to every row of the Steiner total, obtaining respectively the full cube and the identity on the smaller cube. It verifies 32,800 chain-map equations and 59,040 signed boundary equations at translated faces. The measured height changes are one at odd rank and zero at even rank with a single scalar label, and one at every tested rank with distinguished axes. It also constructs and typechecks the finite chain and one-object presentations for $3\le d\le6$, checking the rank cap and recovering each ordinary edge computad by its cut in the chain. The all-rank and first-failure claims follow from the proofs above. [Cube substitution and cover checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_odd_cubes.py)

## Researcher Response 14

**Both kernels are conservative for all eight binary surfaces on every finite 3-computad in which no two generators of dimension at least two are spatially composable, even with a path between them. Ordinary insertion is also conservative there.** The two occurrences in the exclusion may coincide. Attaching paths may change length, and the two faces of a 3-generator may be arbitrary parallel 2-terms. For instance a 2-rule may change $lf$ into $gr$, where $l,r$ are stationary loops on opposite sides of a cut. This case requires keeping the outside paths when passing to an ordinary hom.

Here is the reduction in arbitrary rank. The exclusion is equivalent to the existence of a partition of the vertices into left and right such that all edges are nondecreasing and **each declaration of dimension at least two crosses from left to right**. Indeed take the right vertices to be those reachable from the target of some such declaration. No source of such a declaration lies there. Conversely after crossing the cut one cannot return to another crossing source. Write $D_L,D_R$ for the free path categories on the two stationary subgraphs.

For $p$ on the left and $q$ on the right define an ordinary computad $B(p,q)$. For every crossing declaration $y:s\to t$ (here $s,t$ denote its object poles), introduce a generator $[l,y,r]$ of degree $\dim y-1$ for each $l:p\leadsto s$ in $D_L$ and $r:t\leadsto q$ in $D_R$. In particular the objects of $B(p,q)$ are exactly the edge paths from $p$ to $q$, written at their unique crossing edge. The attaching type of $[l,y,r]$ is the following projection of the actual attaching type of $y$, with exterior paths $l,r$.

For a crossing variable use its indexed generator. For a crossing head on a labelled tree $[T_1,\ldots,T_h]$, let $j$ be the single crossing column. Read the stationary path of each preceding and succeeding column; write their concatenations as $u$ and $v$, respectively. These paths are determined by the labels: each stationary hom projects to the discrete set of its edge paths, and the 2-labels in a column require its successive edge labels to have the same stationary path. Restrict the generic head to $T_j$ by the ordinary cut of Response 11. Label it by projecting the crossing entries with exterior paths $lu,vr$. For a path head just select its unique crossing entry by the same prescription. Project a crossing 1-type to $\star$ and higher arrow types recursively at their base and two faces. This construction defines the attaching types in declaration order. Prepending or appending stationary paths gives raw maps among the resulting ordinary computads by extending the two indices.

The substitution calculation with indices is worth making explicit. For a stationary expression between root vertices $a_i,a_{i'}$ in a labelled arity, its stationary word is the concatenation of the column paths on that interval. This follows by strict path evaluation on variables and heads, and includes the empty word. For a crossing expression between those vertices, nested heads subdivide that interval; each has just one crossing subinterval. All paths left of the retained argument are appended to the left index in their path order and all paths on its right are prepended to the right index in theirs. By associativity of path concatenation these are precisely the indices obtained by composing the substitutions first. The retained generic heads and their faces commute with that composition by the raw cut theorem. This proves by induction the projected typing, including equality between the substituted type of a retained variable and the inferred type of its projected label. For a suspended ordinary operation the entire tree is a single column, so the projection acts by that ordinary operation. In particular at $l=r=1$ it defines a raw ordinary algebra map from the actual fixed hom to $\mathrm{Free}(B(p,q))$.

For surface derivations the stationary projection is strict ordinary path evaluation. Across the cut the word equations of either kernel project to the equations of the auxiliary ordinary surface $R$ of Response 11. The ordinary insertion equations project to ordinary insertion by Response 12. In both verifications the only extra data are the exterior indices. Congruence in a stationary label preserves its path. In a generic equation the exterior indices attached to its crossing labels are determined by the two sides of their column in the original labelling. For a splice the refined stationary column paths concatenate to the old path, and within a crossing splice the exterior paths concatenate in exactly the order in the substitution calculation. Thus the equalities proved there still hold with the same indexed labels. This gives a surface inhabitant over $B(p,q)$ at the projection of the *given* old boundary.

**The raw map on each crossing hom is a weak equivalence.** To identify it, use the raw algebra with stationary homs the discrete path sets, empty reverse homs, and crossing homs the free ordinary algebras on $B(p,q)$. The preceding head and substitution formulas give its kernel action globally for either kernel. The free raw algebra of the context evaluates into it. Before any crossing declarations are adjoined the crossing homs are empty. The stationary ones realize the indicated discrete path sets by the free enriched path construction. Attach the crossing declarations one by one. In the enriched attachment filtration a new generating hom globe from $s$ to $t$ occurs at most once in an enriched word, since the intervening hom from $t$ back to $s$ is empty. Its outside homs lie on the two stationary sides and are discrete coproducts of tensor units. Thus at $(p,q)$ the attachment adds precisely one ordinary hom globe for each pair $(l,r)$, along its boundary. This applies to local object attachments as well. It works for both the cartesian and the lax Gray tensor, since in either case tensoring with those outside homs gives copies indexed by pairs of paths. On each copy the attaching map is the projection of the old attaching type with indices $l,r$: evaluation at the original poles gives the already constructed raw projection, and the outside path actions extend its indices by concatenation. To check the outside action at the actual attaching map, put a copy of the central crossing computad between two single stationary edges in a fresh four-vertex chain. Map the two edges to the specified paths and the copy to its central hom in the indexed algebra. On every raw expression across this chain, evaluation is extension of indices after the plain cut to the central computad, by the head formula. Semantically the long hom of the fresh free chain is the tensor of the two units with the central category. Its plain cut realizes the unitor: the map to the two-object cut algebra is the identity on the central generators and stationary on each outside edge, so the free enriched universal property identifies it with unit composition. Thus the semantic outside action is indeed the indicated index extension for arbitrary old boundary terms. This identifies the map on the whole attached globe by its top label $[l,y,r]$. The free ordinary model on $B(p,q)$ computes the same cellular attachments by ordinary realization. Induction on attachments now gives the asserted equivalence for the actual evaluation map. Infinite sets of outside paths pose no problem: the attachment is a coproduct over those paths. [Free enriched hom attachment filtration, Response 6](#researcher-response-6) · [Directed realization and identification of fixed homs](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-16)

It follows that ordinary insertion is conservative in the original context **if and only if** it is conservative in every $B(p,q)$. The same equivalence holds for the ordinary augmented binary surface $R$. For sufficiency use the projected surface inhabitant and lift raw existence at the prescribed faces by the raw weak equivalence. On stationary homs the discrete path projection itself is a weak equivalence, and the ordinary strict projection detects the path and excludes reverse crossings. For either kernel and any smaller binary surface, conservativity of $R$ on the lower computads suffices by the same argument.

For necessity, the inverse of the raw fixed-hom equivalence in the ordinary homotopy category is represented by an ordinary kernel map $s:\mathrm{Free}(B(p,q))\to H(p,q)$. Its domain is computad-cofibrant and its target fibrant, and the representative is a weak equivalence. Given any failed lower old type, suspend its surface derivation and label the crossing generators by their raw $s$-images at the fixed poles. The suspended equations hold globally: insertion respects suspension, and for the word rules every lower cut is simply shifted up one inside a single hom. Disc removal also suspends to disc removal for $R$. A raw global filler at this old boundary would be an ordinary hom filler at the $s$-images of the raw faces, contrary to reflection by $s$. In particular for a binary surface $S$ over **either** kernel, global conservativity requires ordinary $S$-conservativity in every $B(p,q)$. The distinction between this necessary condition and the sufficient one using $R$ accounts for the unary heads produced by spatial whiskering.

In a 3-computad each $B(p,q)$ is generated through ordinary dimension two. A finite surface derivation and its old boundary use only a finite dependency-closed part of this computad, since each attaching expression is finite and uses earlier declaration stages. Apply Responses 10–11 there and include the resulting raw inhabitant back into $B(p,q)$. This proves the all-dimensional assertion. ∎

## Researcher Response 15

**Adding unary disc removal to any of the eight ordinary binary surfaces preserves inhabitation of old types, in each context and dimension.** In particular the auxiliary $R$ used in Responses 11–14 has exactly the same conservativity classification as $\approx_{A,U,I}$. There is also a law-by-law version of the cut theorem. For $S=\varnothing,\{A\},\{U\},\{A,U\},\{U,I\},\{A,U,I\}$, the binary surface on a context with the cut of Response 14, for either kernel, is conservative **if and only if** the ordinary surface with the *same* $S$ is conservative on each of its contextual hom computads $B(p,q)$. In particular this is an equivalence between the Gray question on $\Sigma B$ and the ordinary question on $B$, including for the maximal interchange-free choice $\{A,U\}$.

### Absorbing the disc equation

Write $S^+$ for the ordinary binary surface $S$ augmented by $C^n_{D^n}[\sigma]=z[\sigma]$, where $z$ is the top variable and $n>0$. We construct an interpretation of $S^+$ in $S$ whose restriction $e$ to the raw kernel is defined by actual raw heads and preserves their ordinary strict value. There are two constructions, since a unit equation can equate a compound word to a variable.

Suppose $U\in S$. For each $n>0$ let $K$ be two vertically composable $n$-discs, with top variables $a,b$. Let $\kappa$ label $D^n$ over $K$ by the binary composite $a*_{n-1}b$. Let $\eta$ label $K$ over $D^n$ by $z$ and $1_{t z}$. The composite substitution sends $z$ to $z*_{n-1}1_{t z}$ and fixes **literally every lower variable**. Translate terms recursively, fixing variables and substituting the translated arguments. Keep a head on its arity with its translated type $A$, except that for *every* degree-$n$ head on $D^n$ use
$$
                  \operatorname{coh}_{K,A[\kappa]}[\eta]
$$
as its translated generic term. Its type is literally $A$: a type of degree $n$ mentions only cells of degrees less than $n$, as the arguments of an ordinary head have degrees at most its output degree. Fullness on the two signed faces is preserved, since $\kappa$ identifies the outside face discs with the two face discs of the total binary composite. In ordinary strict evaluation this composite after $\eta$ is the top of the original disc. This proves the raw typing and strict-value assertions by induction. The same substitution formula applies to a surface-formed head: conversion in its type is respected by reindexing, and the choice of arity depends only on the original arity and degree. There is no need to decide whether its generic faces can be converted to variables.

For the standard disc boundary the new head over $K$ is the designated binary vertical composite. Its value on $\eta$ is a word equal to $z$ by (i) for $U$ on the single disc: the two words are raw-parallel. Generic binary heads themselves have two leaves; their faces are binary composites or variables, recursively down to the object faces. This follows by truncating the two glued discs, selecting a variable on a disc boundary and the binary composite on a two-leaf boundary. The identity head has degree one greater than its arity and generic faces equal to its top variable. Hence all words are fixed by the translation. A head in (ii) cannot be a same-degree disc head: its two generic words have the same ordinary strict image, whereas the two faces of a positive-dimensional disc total are distinct. Its generic type and the identity on the other side are consequently fixed. This checks (i), (ii), and disc removal. Application is by substitution into the translated generic term, which proves compatibility with substitution also for non-word arguments.

Suppose $U\notin S$. In this case a term equal to a variable under $S$ is literally that variable. For the word algebra, this can be seen by equipping every globular boundary fibre with a distinguished composite value and distinct freely available primitive values. Define the operations by induction on degree, giving every binary composite and identity the distinguished value at its prescribed boundary. Associativity and the whiskering interchange equations have a composite at both roots; their lower boundaries agree by their boundary formulas and the lower laws, so both evaluate to the same distinguished value. Variables of a pasting arity can be assigned distinct primitive values with their specified boundaries. Thus a generating (i) with a variable side is reflexive on that variable. In the term congruence every other (i) and every (ii) has a head on both sides. Having the same variable, or both having a head, is preserved under substitution and term congruence. At the boundaries of (i) the original terms are raw-parallel; the additional endpoint equality for (ii) is (i) itself. This proves the assertion in the surface syntax.

Now translate a disc head to its top entry exactly when its translated generic type is the literal standard disc type, and keep all other heads on their arity with translated faces as before. This rule is valid for raw typing and preserves strict value. It is also compatible with congruence on surface-formed head types: all entries of a standard disc type are variables down to $\star$, and variable rigidity, together with arrow-type inversion, makes the test invariant under target equality. As above, it fixes binary words and the generic heads in (ii). It satisfies disc removal literally. This proves the interpretation for the other four choices.

To return to the *same old boundary*, take $Y$ to be the free $S$-surface algebra on the given raw context $\Gamma$, and use its underlying kernel algebra. Changing its raw operations by $e$ gives $e^*Y$ an $S^+$ action by the interpretation just proved. Response 3 constructs a span of kernel maps $Y\leftarrow M(Y)\to e^*Y$ both lifting at prescribed globular boundaries. Its construction needs only the raw action of $e$ and its agreement on strict generic values; it applies to this $Y$ as to any other kernel algebra. Lift the tautological assignment of $\Gamma$ to $M(Y)$ along the left map. Project that assignment to $e^*Y$ and evaluate the proposed $S^+$ inhabitant there. An old boundary already has specified lifts in $M(Y)$ from its raw expressions. Lift the evaluated cell at that boundary along the right map and project to $Y$. A cell there at the tautological boundary is an $S$-inhabitant of the original type. The other implication follows by inclusion. This argument compares existence of inhabitants; it requires no identity-equal raw lift for an individually conservative context. [Representing-globe span, Response 3](#researcher-response-3)

### Which laws a single cut uses

In the word projection of Response 11 the raw crossing head agrees with its projected word already in the lower $S^+$, provided the appropriate projected word equations hold. The calculation of each individual binary constructor uses only disc removal and clause (ii) for an identical identity tower on the top variable. At a positive cut one takes the ordinary operation one level lower. At cut zero one takes the crossing operand and applies enough identities to reach the lower output degree. A crossing path selects its crossing edge.

To check (i) with the stated $S$, take the free ordinary lower word algebra for those laws on the retained variables. Make it the forward hom of a two-object *word algebra* with singleton stationary homs and empty reverse hom. Interpret the positive-cut operations by the lower operations in the forward hom and uniquely in each stationary hom. At a spatial cut use the operand selection and identity padding just described, or the unique stationary cell when neither operand crosses. Identities in the forward hom are lower identities. The boundaries have the required globular form: taking a face of a padded operand shortens the tower until it reaches that operand, and a positive-cut face is the lower binary boundary formula. This construction also supplies a model for the Gray word signature by restricting to its allowed composites.

Associativity in a stationary hom is forced and at a positive cut in the forward hom is lower associativity. For spatial associativity, either all inputs are stationary or there is precisely one crossing operand. Both bracketings raise it to the maximum input degree, accumulating the same total number of identities. The positive-cut unit laws are the lower unit laws. Spatial unit composition selects the original crossing operand at its original degree, and identity distributes over spatial composition because both sides add the same identity to the selected operand. Thus each of $A$ and $U$ holds whenever required, independently. When $I$ and $U$ are both required, an interchange inside the forward hom is lower interchange. At a spatial interchange the changing input on the stationary side has equal faces and is unique at its degree. The two projected orders are the same crossing input with stationary identity steps on the two sides of its temporal composition. Removing those steps gives the same value by $U$, including when one input requires padding. Equivalently use the Godement form available under $U$: both projected spatial products select the same column, and distributing the identity paddings over the temporal product gives the product in that column. For unequal degrees this calculation first pads to the common degree, using identity distribution for the lower product. This proves the claimed laws of the two-object algebra.

Consequently any generating raw-parallel pair of $S$-equal global words projects to lower $S$-equal words: evaluate in this algebra with the actual retained variables as its free generators. Their lower boundaries are raw-parallel by the boundary calculation of Response 11. Clause (i) applies, and the corresponding (ii) is handled by congruence in the projected generic faces and lower clause (ii), as there. With the exterior paths of Response 14 the same argument applies at the indexed labels. Hence a global surface filler restricts to one in ordinary $S^+$ at the *raw* projected boundary. Disc absorption just proved supplies an $S$-filler there. If the lower computations are conservative, raw fixed-hom reflection gives the requested raw global filler. Necessity follows by suspending a lower derivation and using the raw hom inverse as in Response 14. This proves the equivalence for the six choices. For $S=\{I\}$ or $\{A,I\}$ the same proof gives sufficiency of ordinary conservativity for $S\cup\{U\}$ on the lower homs, while ordinary conservativity for $S$ there remains necessary. ∎

It also verified both translations fix the designated binary heads and their identities in 110 equal- and mixed-rank cases. [Disc split checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_disc_split.py)

## Researcher Response 16

**The associativity-only surface $\approx_{\{A\}}$ is conservative over both ordinary CaTT and the Gray kernel, in every finite context and every dimension.** The raw-parallel restriction in (i) matters. It is enough to make the top composition of $n$-cells strictly associative for every $n$. The other composites must have the prescribed globular faces, but we can choose their higher comparison data. Top associativity also occurs in the globular operations of Chanavat and Hadzihasanovic. Here the construction must additionally interpret clause (ii) and return a filler at the original kernel boundary. [Globular composition in a merge category, §4.3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.00146#page=95)

### Which associativity equations occur between parallel words?

Write a binary word as its ordered expression tree, retaining cut indices, identity constructors and variable labels. Flatten consecutive nodes with the same cut index into one ordered list. Equality in the associative word algebra implies equality of the resulting trees. Associative replacement preserves this tree, also under substitution. Equivalently the free associative word operations concatenate the lists at the chosen cut, retaining all other constructors. Taking either face respects flattening: at the top cut it selects an outside face, and at a lower cut it takes the product of the appropriately truncated operands. This applies to mixed ranks; below the top operation one retains any operand already at the boundary degree or lower. The flattening implication holds also for the Gray word signature with its restricted spatial composites.

For a word of degree $n>0$ over a globular arity, its two immediate faces have the same expression tree after erasing variable names while retaining their degrees. For a variable the two faces are variables, and for an identity they are the same term. Below the top cut the assertion follows by applying the binary boundary formula to each operand. At the top cut the two operands have the identical middle face by raw typing, so their common face shapes agree throughout. Call this erased face tree the shadow at degree $n-1$. For an operand of degree less than $n$ its shadow at that level is its whole erased tree. All these statements concern the generic words *before* labelling them by arbitrary cells.

Suppose two degree-$n$ words have the same flattened labelled tree and the same shadow. They can differ only by reassociations of **top** compositions (at the degree of the subexpression being reassociated). Here are the details of reconstruction. Induct on the flattened tree. At a variable there is no choice. At an identity root the shadow displays the entire erased operand, so together with the labelled leaves it determines that operand. At a root list of cut $n-1$, every factor has degree $n$, and every factor's shadow equals the given shadow by the middle-face condition. Apply induction factor by factor, then use top associativity on the list.

At a root list of cut $j<n-1$, every binary node of its original bracketing survives as a $j$-node of the shadow. To distinguish these nodes from $j$-nodes contributed by the factors, flatten the root $j$-block in the shadow. Each factor occupies a consecutive **nonempty** sequence of its leaves. The length for that factor is determined by its flattened input tree: for a degree-$n$ top list use the first factor's shadow, for a degree-$n$ identity use the operand, for a degree-$n$ variable use an anonymous boundary variable, and at a lower cut concatenate the truncated factors at that cut. For inputs of smaller degree just erase the names. These formulas compute the flattened shadow independently of all parentheses. In particular the lengths delimit exactly the same intervals of leaves in the two given shadows. Retaining one leaf per interval and suppressing unary nodes recovers the root bracketing. The subtree on each interval recovers the shadow of the corresponding factor (a one-leaf interval keeps its entire expression at that leaf). Thus the root bracketings agree. At degree $n$ apply induction to a factor; below degree $n$ its whole erased expression was recovered, so its labels and parentheses agree already. This proves the assertion. For raw-parallel words their literal common source gives equal shadows. Consequently **any interpretation with the given binary boundary formulas and strictly associative top composition satisfies (i) for $A$**.
