This conclusion requires neither units nor a choice of paths implementing a rebracketing.

### Representing nonempty strings of cells

Let $Y$ be a raw kernel algebra. We describe a globular set $Z(Y)$, functorial under strict kernel maps. In degree zero use the objects of $Y$. An $n$-cell is a nonempty list of beads. Its successive seams are $(n-1)$-cells of $Z(Y)$ with the same lower boundary, and each bead is a $Y$-cell between the evaluations of its two seams. In degree one the seams are objects. Its source and target are the outside seams. Its evaluation in $Y$ is the top composite of the bead labels, with a fixed left bracketing, or the label itself for one bead. It has precisely the evaluations of the outside seams as its faces. Top composition in $Z(Y)$ concatenates lists at their common seam; it is strictly associative.

A shape $\tau$ remembers the list and the shapes of its seams, recursively. Objects have one shape. Each shape has a finite representing raw computad $H_\tau$ (we use the same letter for its free algebra). At level zero use one vertex. Make distinct copies of the seam diagrams with their common boundary identified. Between each consecutive pair attach one generator at their two evaluated terms. Even when the labelled seams coincide, their generic interiors are distinct. Labels of $\tau$ in $Y$ are exactly raw maps $H_\tau\to Y$. The term evaluating its left-bracketed composite represents a map from the free $n$-globe $D_n$ to $H_\tau$. Its restrictions are the evaluation maps at the two outside seams. The union $H_{\partial\tau}$ of those seams along their common boundary is a subcomputad, including for a one-bead shape. If a globular arity $P$ is labelled with compatible shapes $\boldsymbol\tau$, gluing their charts on the indicated variable faces represents the entire labelling. Write $W=P[\boldsymbol\tau]$ for this expanded computad. It has the raw substitution taking the variables of $P$ to their evaluations in $W$.

We need the following prescribed-boundary fact: **for either kernel the projection $H_W\to C_W$ onto its own free strict image is a globular trivial fibration**. Here and below $H_W$ is the raw free algebra and $C_W$ is the ordinary or directed strict image as appropriate. We give the proof since arbitrarily subdivided seams occur when a higher generator is attached at a composite.

For the ordinary kernel these are strong Steiner presentations. In a chart the top chain of an evaluated string is the sum of its beads. Its differential is the top chain of its last seam minus that of its first: internal seams cancel and the two outside interiors are disjoint. Iterating signed boundaries thus gives augmentation one at each vertex end. This applies to each generator's atom, since the generator is one bead on its two seam diagrams. It also identifies its two prescribed strict faces with the evaluations of the seams in the atom tableaux. There is a compatible strong linear order on each chart. Order the interiors of the successive negative globular faces from the vertex up, then the interior, then the successive positive interiors down to the vertex. Inside the interior of a string place its beads in sequence, with the interior of each intermediate seam between its two beads; use the same prescription inside each seam. In degree zero the interior is the vertex. The resulting order restricts to the stated order on every seam. For each generator it puts all negative top boundary entries before it and all positive ones after it. In a globular sum these orders can be amalgamated: in the pasting-context construction a new globe with its fresh target meets what is already present in its source face. The charts meet on exactly that face chart, with the same induced order. Interleave the two ordered sets in the gaps of the common order. This changes none of the signed boundaries of an individual generator. The basis is therefore unital and strongly loop-free, and the free strict category is generated by its atoms with the displayed attaching pastes. Apply the ordinary strong Steiner comparison at the actual raw attaching terms. [Strong Steiner comparison, especially items 12 and 55](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r10-2026-11-21.md) · [Steiner's atom and generation theorems](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0403237)

For the directed kernel examine one root column of $P$ at a time. Its outer vertices $s,t$ are successive spine vertices. Its expanded 1-graph is a family of nonempty paths from $s$ to $t$ with disjoint internal vertices. Within one chart, each fresh intermediate 1-string contributes its own path, and sharing a 1-face shares its whole path. The globular-sum construction identifies whole such paths or their outer vertices, never proper subpaths. Every generator in this column of dimension at least two has object poles $s,t$, by the seam construction. All other nonempty fixed homs within the column, except $(s,t)$, are discrete path sets up to weak equivalence. At $(s,t)$ Response 14 identifies the raw hom up to weak equivalence with the ordinary free computad whose objects are the complete paths and whose higher generators are the column generators one degree lower. For an explicit cut take the source vertex on the left and all remaining vertices on the right; the stationary suffix of each initial edge is then unique on its way to $t$. This ordinary computad is itself an expanded ordinary chart on the child arity. Each 1-string becomes one object, each 2-string becomes a string of ordinary arrows, and at higher degrees each positive top cut becomes the top cut one degree lower, with the same list of beads and the projected seams. This last statement holds at the actual attaching terms because evaluation uses only top composites. Its ordinary strictification is precisely the outer fixed hom of the free strict enriched column: the only nonstationary generators span the whole column and have no nonidentity outside whiskers there. Thus the raw-to-strict hom comparison for the column is the ordinary strong Steiner comparison after the weak equivalence of Response 14.

The entire graph is a line of these columns joined at vertices. Between different columns the realized fixed hom is the lax Gray tensor of the traversed homs. This follows by the free enriched word construction at a gluing vertex: a word crossing the vertex consists of a hom in the left block followed by one in the right, with no return; composition within each block is its given action. These tensors, with the given within-block homs, have the enriched pushout mapping property. For interior endpoints the partial outside paths contribute only discrete path factors. The same calculation uses the strict Gray tensor on the strict side. For the complete column factors the two tensors agree under realization: strongly loop-free Steiner complexes form a suitable site closed under Gray tensor, and the inclusion of this monoidal site into the higher-categorical model is strong monoidal. The resulting objects and their homs are gaunt, so coinductive completion preserves these identifications. Discrete factors are coproducts of units. Hence strict projection is a weak equivalence on **each actual fixed hom**. It is bijective on vertices, and the target has no nonidentity coinductive equivalences, globally or in a hom. As in Response 3 this makes it a globular trivial fibration. [Campion, Example 2.2 and §3.1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.00205) · [Directed realization and fixed-hom criterion](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-16)

### The surface action

Choose operations generically, for each $P$ and each compatible input shape list $\boldsymbol\tau$; a label in $Y$ then specializes the generic answer along $H_W\to Y$. Induct on head boundaries and arguments. Each previously interpreted face is a string with labels in $H_W$. Require that the **strict value of its evaluation** be the prescribed strict value with the input variables evaluated in $W$. This requirement composes under substitution. In particular the designated strict cell of a new head, after this input labelling, is at the two strict boundary values just obtained. For $S=\{A\}$ the respective strict image interprets the surface in both kernels.

If the arity and degree are those of binary top composition, the designated value is the binary total, and the interpreted boundary is the two outside seams of the given inputs, use their concatenation. Its strict evaluation has the required value by strict associativity. In particular this choice applies to every instance of the designated binary top operation. For a strict identity with identical interpreted faces use a one-bead string labelled by the raw identity on their evaluation. In all other cases use a one-bead string at the two prescribed interpreted faces. Their evaluations are literally parallel in $H_W$ by the globularity of string evaluation. The chart trivial fibration gives its bead at exactly those faces and with the required strict value. Fix the choice for the same chart, interpreted boundary and strict value (with the top-composition prescription as above). These prescriptions are consistent: a generic concatenation has nonempty positive top chain on the independently labelled input beads and is not an identity. In particular the identity operation on an arbitrary string has exactly the one-bead value just specified.

These choices define operations with the indicated faces also on surface-formed head types. More explicitly the choice is made on *generic* shape labels, so applying it to an actual tuple simply composes its label map with the answer on the chart. Recursive evaluation of a substituted term uses the same generic operations on the same substituted tuples. Congruent generic head types have the same two interpreted boundary strings with their labels on the generic chart, and the same strict value. They thus receive the same choice. The word lemma and literal string concatenation give (i). For (ii) the two word faces already agree on the generic chart by (i), and the designated value is an identity. The identity prescription yields exactly the interpreted identity on the common word. This verifies the generating equations in the simultaneous induction on formation, equality and conversion. We have produced a surface action on $Z(Y)$ with the stated strict-value property for all generic kernel expressions.

### Returning to the original raw type

String evaluation need not preserve a chosen raw bracketing. We compare by a span of **kernel maps** with lifting at prescribed boundaries. Write $B_n=\partial D_n$. For each shape construct $J_\tau$ with two ends $H_\tau,D_n$ and a fold to $H_\tau$ equal to the identity on the first end and evaluation on the second. Inductively glue the lower $J$'s at the globular boundary to obtain $J_{\partial\tau}$ (initial for a vertex). Form $L_\tau$ from $J_{\partial\tau}$ by adjoining $H_\tau$ along $H_{\partial\tau}$ at the string end and $D_n$ along $B_n$ at the other. Factor its fold to $H_\tau$ as a cofibration $L_\tau\hookrightarrow J_\tau$ followed by a trivial fibration. All chart and boundary inclusions used to form the end attachments are cellular. Consequently gluing the folds at a boundary, or at all variables of a globular pasting arity, gives a weak equivalence onto the corresponding glued string charts: the gluing diagrams are cellular with cofibrant latching maps. In particular
$$
       J_{P,\boldsymbol\tau}\longrightarrow H_W\longrightarrow C_W
$$
is a weak equivalence. It is a trivial fibration by the same gaunt-target test as above, with surjectivity on vertices supplied by the string end.

Let $M(Y)_n$ consist of a shape $\tau$ and a map $J_\tau\to Y$. Restriction to its two ends gives maps of globular sets to $Z(Y)$ and $Y$. For a kernel head with input shapes $\boldsymbol\tau$ let $\rho$ be the output shape already selected for $Z$. On $L_\rho$ specify the previous boundary maps, the chosen string answer at the string end, and the original kernel head at the raw end, all with target $J_{P,\boldsymbol\tau}$. The boundary matches by induction, since these are the two evaluations of the same boundary expressions. Against the displayed trivial fibration prescribe on $J_\rho$ its fold followed by the string answer into $H_W$ and strict projection. On the raw end the square commutes by the strict-value property of the surface action. On the boundary it commutes by the lower-dimensional construction. Lift along $L_\rho\hookrightarrow J_\rho$. Application by composing with a label map constructs the raw action on $M(Y)$ and makes both projections kernel maps. To spell out the induction under substitution, a tuple of lower answers supplies maps of its individual $J$'s into the new labelled chart. Their string ends give the substitution on the expanded raw computads, hence a substitution on strict images. The lifting squares commute after this strict substitution on each whole $J$, so they also commute on its globular sum. This is the representing-globe argument of Response 3 with the two ends represented by different computads.

Both projections lift cells at any prescribed parallel faces. At the string end use the trivial cofibration from $J_{\partial\tau}\amalg_{H_{\partial\tau}}H_\tau$ into $J_\tau$. It is a cofibration by the other end attachment and the chosen factorization, and a weak equivalence by the boundary fold and the fold for $\tau$. For a lift at the raw end, choose the **one-bead** shape $\tau$ at the two already lifted seam shapes. Here $H_\tau$ is exactly $H_{\partial\tau}\amalg_{B_n}D_n$, using seam evaluation on the boundary. The map from $J_{\partial\tau}\amalg_{B_n}D_n$ to $H_\tau$ is a weak equivalence by gluing the boundary fold along the globe cofibration (all sources are cofibrant). Its inclusion into $J_\tau$ is therefore a trivial cofibration. At degree zero this uses the one vertex shape. Fibrancy of $Y$ gives the extensions.

Finally take $Y$ free on the given raw context and lift its assignment to $M(Y)$ along the raw projection, one declaration at a time. Project to $Z(Y)$ and evaluate a surface inhabitant there. Its old boundary has already lifted along the string projection by interpreting the old expressions in $M(Y)$. Lift the inhabitant at this boundary and project to $Y$. This is a raw inhabitant of the requested type. ∎

**Validation.** A finite enumeration grouped 28,062 typed binary words by associative flattening and literal inferred type, including mixed ranks and identities. All 1,397 additional representatives in non-singleton groups had the same tree modulo top reassociation. Omitting the boundary test gave 5,890 groups containing distinct trees modulo top reassociation. A separate construction expanded all pasting trees through five edges, with two subdivisions per tree. It typechecked all 128 charts (4,124 generators), tested strong loop-freeness and unitality from the actual attaching chains, checked that distinct column paths have disjoint interiors, and typechecked and tested all 262 desuspended column homs. The unbounded assertions use the reconstruction and lifting arguments above. [Word and expanded-chart checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_top_strings.py)

## Researcher Response 17

**A finite set of return problems for Q2.** Fix a Gray surface with $I\in S$. Consider finite positive 3-computads in which no generating 3-cell is spatially composable with a generating 2-cell in either order. Here positivity means that both attaching lists of every 3-generator contain at least one rewrite. On this class all-dimensional conservativity is **complete among recursively enumerable problems under computable many-one reductions**. The universal quantification over stationary intervening paths in Response 9 reduces effectively to a finite conjunction of individual reachability instances, even on cyclic object graphs. If no declared rule increases the length of its temporal list, the all-dimensional decision problem on the same class is **NL-complete** for explicit path-and-list inputs.

There is also a dimension-three decision procedure allowing a 3-generator alongside a 2-generator. It applies when there is no spatially composable triple of 2-generators and the rules admit a common positive weighting nonincreasing on the temporal lists. The proof gives a lower bound on the temporal space needed for a return even without such a weighting.

### The first move sees the gap

Use the oriented list presentation of Response 2. Write $s(a),t(a)$ for the two edge paths of a generating 2-cell and $s_0a,t_0a$ for their object endpoints. For $t_0a\xrightarrow{m}s_0b$, form the elementary square with initial edge path $s(a)\,m\,s(b)$ and no exterior whiskers. Call the list executing $a$ first $LR$ and the list executing $b$ first $RL$. The kernel square goes from $RL$ to $LR$. It suffices to find returns for these bare squares. Every contextual exchange has exterior spatial whiskers and temporal prefix and suffix, all of which can be reapplied to a return. Conversely the bare square is itself an instance.

First reject a spatially composable triple by Response 9. In the remaining graph a generating 2-cell cannot have equal or mutually reachable object poles, since it would compose with itself three times. Its source and target paths are nonempty, and $a,b$ of a square are distinct. Thus $LR\ne RL$. The first move from $LR$ cannot be a forward exchange. It must apply a declared 3-rule to one or both steps. Write $w$ for the spatial starting edge path of that rule (the initial edge path of its source list). In the state where the rule acts, a contiguous occurrence of $w$ contains the entire source segment of its first step. All steps of the rule execute inside that window, leaving the outside path fixed.

If the rule uses both steps, its window contains $m$. Indeed it contains the first active segment, and its fixed exterior on the right must still leave room for the second active segment after the first rewrite. If the rule uses just $a$, the initial path is $s(a)\,m\,s(b)$. A window ending at or before the end of $m$, after containing $s(a)$, would give an edge path from the target pole of the 3-generator to $s_0b$. This is the excluded spatial composition. Hence its window contains all of $m$. If it uses just $b$, its initial path is $t(a)\,m\,s(b)$. A window starting at or after the start of $m$, before containing $s(b)$, would exhibit spatial composition of $a$ with that 3-generator. Again it contains $m$. The argument uses the actual occurrence in a path, so repeated edges cause no ambiguity.

Let $D$ consist of the empty word and every contiguous subword of the spatial starting path of every declared 3-rule. For each ordered pair $a,b$ test whether **every** edge path from $t_0a$ to $s_0b$ belongs to $D$. This is a finite graph calculation. Follow the graph and the prefix trie of $D$ together, with one extra trie state for a word that has left it permanently. A reachable endpoint with an unaccepted trie state supplies an explicit gap whose square has no first move. If all the inclusions pass, the required squares are precisely the finitely many triples $(a,m,b)$ with $m\in D$ of the specified poles. Empty paths are tested at each matching vertex. By Response 9 conservativity in every dimension is equivalent to directed reachability from $LR$ to $RL$ for all these triples. In particular any directed cycle on a path between the two inner poles of a square gives a negative answer on this class, independently of the lengths of the temporal rule sides.

The graph of finite histories and rule applications is effectively enumerable. Search the finitely many returns in parallel and accept when all have appeared. For hardness use the effective construction in Responses 2 and 8, which has no triple and all its declared 3-rules in the long hom. It realizes positive semi-Thue reachability as all-dimensional conservativity. To see the full many-one hardness, a deterministic tape machine has positive string rules encoding one configuration as a nonempty bracketed tape segment with a state immediately before the scanned symbol. A right move rewrites the state, scanned symbol and right neighbour, appending a blank if the neighbour is the end marker. A left move uses the left neighbour, with the symmetric extension rule. Only the unique state can initiate a move. At a halting state change to a fresh cleanup symbol which successively absorbs tape symbols and brackets to its left and right; reaching the singleton cleanup symbol is equivalent to halting. All rule sides are nonempty. The marker construction in Response 2 demands reachability of that exact singleton. This proves recursively enumerable completeness.

### When temporal length cannot increase

Measure input size in the explicit finite graph, the two written edge paths of each mark, and the explicit ordered sides, positions and initial edge paths of the declared rules. A search starting at $LR$ now needs at most two events. A history at the fixed starting path is specified by at most two generator names and their starting positions in the successive edge paths. Each intermediate edge path has length at most the initial length plus twice the largest target length of a mark. The number of candidate histories is polynomial. Adjacency can be checked in logarithmic space. Store the marks and offsets, read an edge of the intermediate path by indexing the original word and the at most two replacement segments, and compare the source and target of a candidate move at the indicated offsets. Only rules with at most two source events can act, and their target length is bounded by their source length. A successful search has a simple path of at most the polynomial state count.

For the full logspace bound, a substring of an input word is represented by its input location and length. The prefix trie of the substring dictionary can use the first occurrence of each such string as its state; a transition is computed by scanning input substrings for the extended string. The graph/trie product therefore has polynomially many states with logspace transitions. A path witnessing a failed inclusion, or paths witnessing a spatial triple, can be guessed in nondeterministic logarithmic space. Absence of such witnesses is also in NL. Then loop over the polynomially many candidate triples, guessing each required return in turn and reusing the same workspace. This accepts exactly when all the required paths exist. [Immerman, nondeterministic space is closed under complement](https://people.cs.umass.edu/~immerman/pub/space.pdf)

For NL-hardness encode finite directed graph reachability from $u$ to $v$ on the two-edge chain of Response 2. Give the long hom one fresh letter $q_i$ for each graph vertex and a delimiter $d$, all 2-endomorphisms of the same bracketed two-edge path. Use the rules
$$
 LR\longrightarrow q_u d,\qquad q_i d\longrightarrow q_j d\quad(i\to j),\qquad q_v d\longrightarrow RL .
$$
All rule sides have length two. The sole bare exchange is the square of the two short-hom letters. Among the marked words only the displayed graph rules and the finish at $v$ can apply. The square has a return exactly when $v$ is reachable from $u$. The construction is logspace, also when $u=v$. This establishes the claimed bound in both directions.

### How much temporal space a long gap needs

Drop the exclusion on 3-generators beside 2-generators. Retain positivity and the absence of a spatial triple. Let $K$ be the maximum source **edge-path length** of a 2-generator and $M$ the maximum spatial starting length of a 3-rule (zero for an empty family). If a bare square with gap $m$ has a directed return using at most $H$ events in any intermediate temporal list, then
$$
                         |m|\ \le\ 2(HK+M).
$$

For suppose $|m|\ge 2(HK+M)+1$. Distinguish an edge occurrence in $m$ with at least $HK+M$ edges of $m$ on either side. No contiguous subpath of $m$ can be the source of a 2-generator $c$, because the prefix and suffix paths would make $a,c,b$ a spatial triple. In *any* history of length at most $H$ at the fixed initial path, after $r$ steps the interior of $m$ trimmed by $rK$ edges at both ends still occurs unchanged, with its original edge occurrences. Indeed the next step's nonempty source cannot lie entirely within that surviving interval. A rewrite meeting the interval from outside can consume at most $K$ of its edges from one end. The interval has enough remaining edges throughout this induction that it cannot be crossed. Changes in target lengths merely shift the position where its untouched part occurs.

In particular every event lies on one side of the distinguished edge. For each intermediate history label its events left or right by following that occurrence from its fixed initial position. A declared rule uses a window of length at most $M$ in the path where it starts. The window contains the first source event, which reaches outside the surviving interval, so it cannot also meet the distinguished edge with its surviving margins. All source and target events of that rule stay on the same side. Thus a rule preserves the property that all left events precede all right events. A forward exchange preserves it too: one acting across the two sides moves a left event earlier, and one acting within a side retains the side labels. Following the untouched occurrence gives the same positions in the common suffix after either move. The initial list $LR$ has the property and the proposed final $RL$ fails it. This contradiction proves the bound.

For an effective consequence, assign integer weights $w(c)\ge1$ to the 2-generators so every declared temporal rule has target weight at most source weight, counting all occurrences. Existence of such weights is decidable by rational linear feasibility followed by clearing denominators. Exchanges preserve weight exactly, so every return for $(a,m,b)$ stays within $H=w(a)+w(b)$ events. For each ordered pair first check whether a gap longer than $2(HK+M)$ exists by finite graph reachability with a length counter saturated at the threshold plus one. Such a gap supplies a failing old 3-type. Otherwise enumerate the finitely many permissible gaps and search their finite history graphs up to $H$. Finiteness follows because at each of at most $H$ steps there are finitely many names and occurrences on a finite path. Response 2 makes this an exact decision through dimension three under the stated hypotheses. It also works with all weight inequalities reversed: on a successful path every intermediate weight is then bounded by the weight of its final $RL$. For the spatially excluded class of the first part, Response 9 again makes the decision all-dimensional. ∎

**Validation.** The checker enumerated 272 dictionary-inclusion problems on branching or cyclic graphs and searched 1,248 bare squares with changing edge-path lengths. It checked the actual span of all 792 available first rule moves, including moves acting only on the second event after the first event changed spatial length. On all 576 source/target tests for directed graphs on three vertices without self-loops, return reachability agreed with an independent transitive closure. On a presentation allowing a polygon beside a mark, 60 bounded square searches traced the distinguished occurrence through 213 histories, including temporal expansions and spatial contractions. Agda 2.8.0 also accepted the composite for a one-edge graph instance at the literal raw Gray type from the sorted layering back to the unsorted layering. [Contextual rewrite and gap checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_return_gaps.py)

## Researcher Response 18

**Deciding the higher kernel side of Q2.** In a finite positive Gray 3-computad without a spatially composable triple of 2-generators, inhabitation of **each given old type of dimension at least four is decidable**. This includes contexts with a polygon beside an interval and contexts with two polygons side by side. The two cases give different directed obstructions. Every nonidentity local 3-cell moves a polygon strictly later in a measured temporal list. Every local 4-cell has a number of generating steps determined by its two faces. In particular the reverse of either kind of mixed generating prism, or of a two-polygon generator, has no kernel filler, even after adding other positive 3-rules subject to the same no-triple condition.

For the individual-context conservativity question, all failures *above* dimension three on this class are consequently recursively enumerable, with a decidable test of kernel noninhabitation at each reported boundary. When the temporal weights of Response 17 exist, failure in **any** dimension is recursively enumerable under the no-triple condition, including when a polygon sits beside another active factor. On an acyclic object graph, without a weight assumption, the all-dimensional conservativity problem for positive 3-computads lies in the differences of recursively enumerable sets. These statements hold for each of the four Gray surfaces containing $I$.

### What a polygon can pass

Use local dimensions in a fixed hom. By Responses 8 and 9 its free strict polygraph has vertices the edge paths, arrows the contextual single rewrites, and 2-generators the contextual polygons and directed squares. Its 3-generators are precisely a polygon tensored with an interval in either order; its 4-generators are pairs of polygons. Every such tensor may have stationary paths outside and between its factors. There are no higher generators. Indeed, choosing an arrow in either nonempty attaching list of a polygon gives a 2-generator between its poles with exterior whiskers. Three active tensor factors of any of these kinds would therefore give a spatial triple. We use the strict polygraph for existence at a *prescribed raw boundary* by the positive polygon comparison of Response 8.

A local 1-cell is a temporal list. Assign its individual event occurrences arbitrary positive rational masses. In a reading of a local 2-diagram as a sequence of generating 2-steps, transport these masses as follows. A directed square swaps its two masses along with their events. A polygon takes the sum on its source block and distributes it equally over its target block. At every polygon record the sum of all masses **strictly before** its source block in the current temporal list. Write $h$ for the sum of these records. The empty list is allowed and carries total mass zero. Positivity of both polygon sides ensures that all actual events retain positive mass and that each move conserves the total.

The output masses and $h$ depend only on the strict 2-diagram with its assigned input masses. Interchanging disjoint steps changes neither local input vector; a step on the left also preserves the *total* seen as a prefix by a step on the right. Equivalently, for temporal juxtaposition of two diagrams the sum of their scores gains the input mass of the left diagram times the number of polygons in the right diagram. For sequential execution add the two scores using the first output vector as the second input. These formulas respect strict exchange. Counts distinguish a declared polygon from a directed square even if their attaching lists coincide.

In a characteristic polygon-interval tensor write $V^-,V^+$ for the source and target lists of the polygon, and $b$ for the lone event. In **both spatial orders** the negative 2-face acts by the polygon before $b$ in the temporal list at the polygon step, and the positive face acts by it after $b$. For a polygon on the left the negative face swaps $b$ across $V^-$ and then applies the polygon; the positive applies it first and swaps $b$ across $V^+$. For a polygon on the right the negative applies it and swaps $V^+$ across $b$; the positive swaps $V^-$ across $b$ and then applies it. These signs follow from the tensor differential, with the polygon in degree two. In each face the swaps preserve the relative order within the polygon block. Both sides therefore have the same output masses and polygon count, and
$$
                      h(\partial^+) - h(\partial^-) = \operatorname{mass}(b)>0 .
$$
The calculation persists in any local context. Prefix diagrams supply positive masses, suffix diagrams receive identical masses, and external temporal factors give the same prefix correction on either face. Thus $h$ strictly increases along each generating local 3-step in context. This argument permits length-changing rules, coincident labels and revisited spatial vertices.

### Counting two-polygon steps from their faces

Give each input event a second, nonnegative rational number. At a square, carry the numbers with their events. At a polygon, distribute the input sum **plus one** equally over the outputs. This defines a function $F_D$ from input vectors to output vectors for every local 2-diagram $D$: disjoint replacements act independently. The two faces of a local 3-generator induce the same function, by the block-and-single-event description above. This equality respects all whiskers and composites.

To a local 3-generator assign a cost function of its temporal inputs, the number on the lone event it crosses. For a sequence of local 3-steps add the costs. Inside a local 2-diagram prefix evaluate the cost on the transported vector; a temporal juxtaposition adds costs on the two respective subvectors. More formally, represent a local 3-cell by its common input-output function $F$ for its 2-faces and a scalar function $g$ on the same inputs. Composition at cut two adds scalar functions at fixed $F$. At cut one, following $(F,g)$ by $(F',g')$ gives $(F'\circ F, g+g'\circ F)$. At cut zero take the product of the two functions on vectors and sum the scalar values on their separate inputs. Identities have cost zero. Associativity, units and interchange follow by function composition and commutative addition, so $g$ is independent of all presentations of the strict 3-cell.

For a tensor $P\otimes Q$ of polygons the negative facets cross $P$ past the individual **inputs** of $Q$ and $Q$ past the individual inputs of $P$. The positive facets use the **outputs** in both places. Indeed
$$
       \partial(P\otimes Q)=(\partial P)\otimes Q+P\otimes(\partial Q).
$$
To evaluate the contextual facet costs, one must know the numbers on those occurrences, not just the signs in this chain. The source temporal word consists of the two input blocks in right-before-left order. In a face diagram, the only boxes changing numbers are the individual polygons. Swaps between the blocks simply transport their separate wires. A wire on an input side of a characteristic polygon therefore carries its original number; a wire on its output side carries the share produced at that polygon. This can be calculated before labelling, on the two polygons with distinct boundary interiors, and then specialized. Each edge of the relevant side contributes one facet. The negative score is the sum of the two block inputs and the positive score is that sum plus two. Equivalently, begin with the 2-diagram executing $Q$, swapping its output block across the input of $P$, then executing $P$. Slide $Q$ past the individual inputs of $P$ and $P$ past the inputs of $Q$, or slide $P$ past the outputs of $Q$ and $Q$ past the outputs of $P$. Both reach the diagram executing $P$, swapping its output across the input of $Q$, then executing $Q$. Independent polygon steps on consecutive temporal blocks commute in these readings.

Consequently $g(\partial^+)-g(\partial^-)$ for a local 4-generator is the **constant function two**. In a whisker, precomposing a constant difference with a vector function preserves it, and the other contributions cancel by equality of the vector functions at both ends. For any directed local 4-paste the difference is twice its number of generating 4-steps. In particular this count is computable by evaluating both faces at the all-zero vector. The strict increases in the two preceding arguments exclude the claimed kernel reverses. Whether conversion supplies a reverse at a particular old boundary is a separate surface question.

### Finite search at the given boundary

All calculations below are on the free strict fixed hom. Elementary steps out of a specified cell are effectively finite in the required degrees. Here are details for matching in contexts of higher cells. A single top generator in context can be written with one pair of whiskers at each lower cut in increasing order, followed by the outside whiskers at the highest cut. At its source, split off these last two factors by top composition. On the middle factor take its immediate source and split off the next pair by top composition there. Continue down to the starting spatial edge path of the central generator. These are finite effective choices: free strict polygraphs have effective top factorization and decidable equality. In applying this to an individual cell, restriction to its finite generator support is enough; factors use only that support by the union formula for support. At the bottom, the indexed tensor generators with a given starting edge path are also effectively finite. Enumerate its divisions into stationary paths and the written starting paths of at most two active factors, and the finitely many labels for those factors. Reassemble each candidate tower and test equality to the *whole* given source, which verifies all the other faces as well. This enumerates the steps with their targets. Every higher paste is a sequence of such steps by strict exchange. [Thin context formula (16.9)](https://webusers.imj-prg.fr/~yves.guiraud/articles/polybook.pdf) · [Forest, effective factorizability in §2.3.2 and general polygraph equality in §2.4.3](https://members.loria.fr/SForest/data/thesis/thesis-updated.pdf)

For a global 4-type take its parallel local 2-faces $D,E$. A local 3-step preserves the number of polygons. Fix that count $c$ in $D$ and fix its local 1-source list $u$. There are only finitely many readings of 2-diagrams starting at $u$ with at most $c$ polygons. To see this, let $L$ be the maximum length of a target list of a polygon, or zero without polygons. Every temporal list encountered has length at most $|u|+cL$. With fixed initial spatial edge path there are finitely many such lists: at each event one chooses a named 2-generator and a position in the current finite edge path. In a stretch of consecutive directed squares the vector of starting positions decreases strictly in lexicographic order by Response 9. In particular, the spatially left event has nonempty source; an empty source would already allow a spatial triple by repetition. Each stretch thus visits each of the bounded lists at most once. There are at most $c+1$ stretches. These bounds enumerate all possible 2-diagrams in the search. Compute reachability from $D$ to $E$ by the elementary local 3-steps above and strict equality on visited states. In fact there are even finitely many directed readings of local 3-fillers here: evaluate $h$ with all input masses one to exclude cycles in this finite graph.

For a global 5-type the local 3-faces have computable cost difference at zero. Reject unless it is twice a nonnegative integer $k$. Search exactly $k$ generating local 4-steps from the first face and compare to the second, allowing the empty path for $k=0$. Finite branching gives a terminating decision and enumerates the strict fillers. For a global 6-type test equality of the local 4-faces, since only local identities remain at degree five. At every greater global dimension parallel strict faces are already equal. In each case the strict decision is exact for **raw inhabitation at the original parallel faces** by Response 8. If no two polygons are spatially composable, local 4-cells are already identities, so the last possible first failure is in global dimension five.

Finally enumerate finite surface derivations ending at old types of dimensions four through six. The equational proofs for the free word laws, substitution and conversion are finite derivations and can themselves be enumerated. Run the kernel decision at the displayed type of each such proof. This recognizes precisely the contexts having a failure in those dimensions; by the dimension bound these are all higher failures. Under the weight hypothesis decide the dimension-three condition by Response 17 and combine it with this recognition. For the acyclic-graph assertion, first exclude spatial triples by the decidable graph test (they force failure by Response 9). Otherwise only finitely many middle paths can occur. The dimension-three condition is recursively enumerable by searching for all the requisite returns. The all-dimensional positive instances are this recursively enumerable set minus the recursively enumerable set of higher failures, as asserted. ∎

**Validation.** The checker enumerated all 81 pairs of polygons with each of their four sides of length one, two or three. It computed 3,870 typed 2-diagram readings and checked 1,008 applications of mixed 3-steps in context on nonuniform rational input data. It reconstructed both directed 3-boundaries of each two-polygon atom modulo strict interchange of disjoint steps and measured the cost increase of two. Agda 2.8.0 also accepted the two raw global 4-prism heads, one for each spatial order, at the signed boundaries used in the proof. [Polygon height and raw orientation checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_polygon_heights.py)

## Researcher Response 19

**The reverse mixed prisms are absent even in the full Gray binary surface.** More generally, in the positive no-triple 3-computads of Response 18, suppose the two strict faces of an old global 4- or 5-type are comparable by a directed cell, in *either* direction. At that boundary each of the eight Gray binary surfaces is conservative. In particular, an actual higher failure in these degrees would need **incomparable** strict faces. Comparability is decidable by Response 18. The distinction matters already over the two-column arity of a polygon and an interval: conversion reverses its ordinary two-event square, but it cannot reverse its mixed prism.

We construct a surface model that retains the two inequalities of Response 18. The construction works for positive 3-computads with nonempty spatial source and target paths for the 2-generators, without a restriction on spatial triples. The no-triple hypothesis in the comparability result ensures that the mixed steps account for *every* nonidentity step in the relevant degrees.

### Which equations need to be checked in a strict enriched model?

Write a globular arity as $P=\bigvee_i\Sigma T_i$. In the strict Gray hom on a traversed interval of columns, call a paste pure if it uses generators in single columns with the other factors stationary, combined using the ordinary strict operations **in the hom**. The strict image of any binary word of positive global degree is pure. A spatial composition in a Gray word can only whisker a positive hom cell by a stationary path, and a positive cut uses a composition in a fixed hom.

For global degree at least three, the projection from the Gray tensor to the ordinary product is faithful on **parallel pure cells over this arity**. To see this, fix their common local 1-source, a temporal list of single-column generating arrows. Any pure cell above that list is a temporal composite of one bead at each event position. Each bead is a cell of the corresponding column with the other columns stationary. A generating higher cell has just one generating arrow as its 1-source and one as its 1-target, in the same column with the same object poles. Identities extend the decomposition, cut zero in the hom concatenates lists of beads, and at every higher local cut strict interchange composes corresponding beads. The stationary coordinates of each bead are fixed by the initial tuple and the preceding events, since varying a bead above degree one preserves its object poles. The projection to each column gives its subsequence of beads. That subsequence is recoverable: in a free strict category on a globular pasting set the hom across a string of object edges is the product of the child categories, so the temporal decomposition at a prescribed path into individual edge positions is unique. Equivalently this is the free enriched path formula applied inside that column. Interleaving the recovered subsequences in the prescribed 1-source order gives a common Gray paste for the two cells. The empty path gives only identities among pure cells. At local degree one, the product remembers only the ordered subsequence in each column; every two interleavings differ by swaps of adjacent events from distinct columns.

Consequently a strict Gray-enriched interpretation in which all spatial interval squares are identities satisfies (i) on raw-parallel words, even for $S=\{A,U,I\}$. Indeed any such equation holds after ordinary strict projection. In global degrees zero and one the projections already agree, in degree two use the swaps, and above that use the parallel pure-cell calculation **before any labelling**. For clause (ii), a generic identity-valued head already gives an identity. A total between the indicated words in global degree three can have either one active factor of local degree two or two interval factors. The one-factor total has distinct projected faces over the arity. The two-factor total is a rectangle of interval squares and maps to an identity. The same distinct-face argument excludes a nonidentity total in degrees one and two. Above global degree three the word faces already agree in the original strict Gray arity, where a nonidentity total has different faces. This checks both clauses on all generic words.

We will use a target which in addition has only identity **endomorphism cells** in local degrees three and higher. We spell out head congruence. A head accepted at the unchanged arity side condition is interpreted by applying the strict enriched labelling to its designated total, or by the identity on the interpreted base in the coherence case. The arity condition identifies its faces with the specified boundary calculation; composing with a labelling preserves that calculation. In the simultaneous interpretation of formation and conversion, a labelled substitution needs the interpreted variable faces to match. Thus a conversion inside a label only uses equality in the target. For congruent head types over the same arity, two totals give the same choice, and two identity choices agree on their interpreted base. If the two cases meet, the interpreted total has equal faces. In local degrees at least three the endomorphism condition makes it an identity. In lower degrees a total with nonidentity ordinary projection has distinct *generic ordinary* faces. Congruence preserves the ordinary strict interpretation, so such a type cannot be congruent to an identity-valued head type. The remaining mixed rectangle is sent to an identity as above. This verifies head congruence as well as substitution, including when the actual labels are given by converted terms.

### A fixed hom with decorated event occurrences

Use the given graph vertices as objects and edge paths as global arrows. The local 1-cells between two such paths are temporal histories modulo swaps of spatially disjoint rewrites in both directions. Denote the finite set of event occurrences of a history class $u$ by $O(u)$. The occurrence identifications here are canonical. Give the initial spatial edges distinct position labels. Name each rewrite occurrence by its generator and its ordered input edge labels; label each of its output edges by this occurrence and its output port. Every rewrite consumes a nonempty list, and an edge occurrence is consumed at most once. These names therefore distinguish all events, including repeated uses of the same generator. A disjoint swap leaves all names and the final labelled edge path unchanged. Temporal concatenation relabels the initial edges of its second argument by the final edges of its first. Spatial juxtaposition starts on disjoint blocks of the initial edges. Both operations give the indicated disjoint union of event sets, coherently for nested compositions by substitution of edge labels. In particular these claims apply to paths that revisit a vertex. Temporal concatenation and spatial juxtaposition on history classes obey strict exchange, since the intervening interleavings differ by disjoint swaps.

A decoration of $u$ assigns each occurrence a positive rational mass and a nonnegative rational number. Write $m(x),a(x)$ for their respective totals on a decoration $x$. A local 2-cell $u\to v$ consists of a nonnegative integer $c$, a function $F$ from decorations of $u$ to decorations of $v$, and a nonnegative rational scalar function $h$ on the input decorations, subject to
$$
                  m(Fx)=m(x),\qquad a(Fx)=a(x)+c .
$$
A local 3-cell requires the same $F,c$ at both faces and $h\le h'$ pointwise. Its datum is a nonnegative scalar function $g$ on the same inputs, zero wherever $h=h'$. There is a unique local 4-cell between parallel 3-cells if $g\le g'$, and above this use identities. This gives the required identity endomorphisms in local degrees three and higher.

For sequential 2-diagrams compose $F$'s, add the counts, and add $h$ after evaluating it at the respective inputs. For temporal juxtaposition of a left and a right diagram use the product of $F$'s on the disjoint event sets, add the counts, and use
$$
                 h_L(x_L)+h_R(x_R)+c_R m(x_L).
$$
These formulas are associative and unital. Interchanging sequential and juxtaposed compositions preserves the last summand because every $F_L$ preserves total mass and counts add on the right. For 3-cells add $g$ at cut two, add it at the successive inputs at cut one, and sum the two values on the separate inputs at cut zero. These are precisely the three compositions of cost functions verified in Response 18. The zero condition holds after composition: a zero increase of $h$ requires zero increase in both summands at the decorating inputs, since all increases are nonnegative and the prefix correction is the same on both faces. The order on $g$ is preserved by the compositions. This defines an ordinary strict higher category in each hom.

### Spatial composition and its mixed cells

An exterior stationary path acts by extending the whiskers and transporting all the data by the indicated occurrence bijection. To define the strict functor from the lax Gray tensor of two composable homs, it suffices to give its compatible values on tensor products of labelled globular pasting charts. The strict category of such charts is dense, and the lax Gray tensor preserves colimits in each argument. The tensors of the charts are freely generated by the tensor basis. [Berger, cellular density](https://math.univ-cotedazur.fr/u/cberger/nerve.pdf) · [Steiner, tensor presentations](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0403237)

On pure generators use the stationary whisker. A pair of local arrows gives the identity square: their two temporal orders have the same trace and identify each occurrence with itself. For a local 2-cell $P$ and a local arrow $b$ in either spatial order, the two 2-faces have the same count and the same transfer of decorations; the occurrence identifications carry the entire $b$ decoration unchanged. The positive face puts $P$ temporally after $b$, so the increase of $h$ is $c_P m(x_b)$. Give the mixed 3-cell cost
$$
                                c_P a(x_b).
$$
This is allowed. If the increase vanishes, either $c_P=0$ or the trace of $b$ is empty, and in both cases the cost vanishes.

At a pair $P,Q$ of local 2-cells the positive minus negative 3-cost is $2c_P c_Q$. Indeed the negative crosses each against the other's inputs and the positive against its outputs, as in Response 18. The two summands of the difference are $c_P(a(F_Q x)-a(x))$ and $c_Q(a(F_P y)-a(y))$, with $x,y$ the two respective block inputs. For a local 3-cell paired with an arrow, in either order, the two 3-costs agree. Both contain the cost of the 3-cell on its own block; the mixed facets on its two 2-faces contribute the same count times the same arrow number, since those two faces have equal counts. The stationary arrow lies outside that block on both sides, so the temporal juxtaposition rule for $g$ adds no offset. These are all mixed cases at total degree four. Their required 4-cells exist by the order on $g$. At higher degrees parallel faces have the unique required comparison since parallel 4-cells agree.

For compatibility under chart substitutions only total degree at most three needs a value check once the boundaries agree. A subdivided arrow square is a rectangle of identity squares. For a mixed 3-cell, write a substituted 2-cell as a sequence of whiskered generating 2-steps and the substituted arrow as a list. Tensor subdivision crosses every such 2-step once with each arrow entry: subdivision of sequential factors pastes their strips, while subdivision at temporal cut zero puts their strips in their respective temporal contexts. These are also the characteristic faces obtained by tensoring the two maps out of the globes with their cellular presentations. In all these crossings the arrow entries retain their decorations, because operations in the other spatial factor act on a disjoint block. The total cost is the sum of the step counts times the sum of the arrow numbers, exactly the displayed formula. Identity steps have count zero. This proves compatibility on mixed generators; pure ones use ordinary functoriality of whiskering. At degree four and above the choices are forced at fixed boundaries.

Associativity of spatial composition can likewise be checked on triples of labelled charts. Pure cells compose their exterior path whiskers. In degree two every mixed square is an identity. In degree three a mixed pair with a stationary factor retains the same two blocks and cost; with three arrows either grouping pairs an identity square of count zero with the third arrow. Both groupings then have cost zero. Higher comparisons are unique. Empty path whiskers act identically. This gives a strict Gray-enriched model with identity interval squares and the required endomorphism property, so the preceding word and head arguments make it a model of the full binary surface.

### Reflection at comparable faces

Interpret a 2-generator of the computad by its one event. For a 3-generator take $c=1,h=0$, sending its inputs to equal shares of their total mass and of their total number plus one on the output occurrences. Positivity of its two temporal sides makes this a valid 2-cell even when their lengths differ. These values specify a model assignment at the actual raw attaching faces, since the underlying traces are the classes of those very histories. Under this assignment the evaluations of strict diagrams are exactly the two height computations of Response 18: temporal contexts give the prefix mass, squares transport occurrences unchanged, and a mixed step has cost the number on the wire it passes.

If a nonidentity strict local 3-cell goes from $E$ to $D$, the evaluation of $D$ has strictly larger $h$ on positive input masses. Thus the full surface cannot supply a global 4-cell in the opposite direction. Similarly a nonidentity strict local 4-paste from $E$ to $D$ increases $g$ by twice its number of generating steps. The full surface cannot supply a global 5-cell back. Here absence of a spatial triple ensures all top generators in the respective degrees are of the mixed kinds considered. In the forward direction a strict filler already supplies a raw inhabitant at the given boundary by Response 8, including for equal strict faces. This proves the comparability assertion. In particular it is the strict direction of the mixed prism that survives all the binary equations, for either spatial order. ∎

**Validation.** The trace checker tracked all event inputs and output ports through 1,080 reorderings of length-changing spatial rewrites followed by an overlapping rewrite. In 81 choices of input and output lengths it evaluated two independent, sequentially composite decorations in both orders; in another 81 it compared subdivided mixed costs and the two-polygon cost difference against the unsplit formula. The signed raw prism heads and both tensor boundary orientations are checked in Response 18. [Trace transport and composite cost checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_trace_decorations.py)

## Researcher Response 20

**One-object tests for Q1, without a dimension shift.** For either kernel and each binary surface, the universal assertion in Q1 is equivalent to its restriction to finite one-object contexts. This also holds for ordinary insertion and strict units. More precisely, identify all objects of any nonempty finite context $B$, retaining every positive-dimensional generator with its own name and substituting the object identification into its attaching type. At every particular old type of dimension at least two from $B$, folding preserves **and reflects** inhabitation, separately for the raw kernel and for its surface. This statement permits arbitrary attaching terms, including those at iterated identities. Thus the least dimension of a universal failure can be tested on one-object contexts.

### Lifting through a graph of object identifications

We prove a more general reflection statement. Take a finite family of contexts $B_i$ for the same kernel. Form $V$ by identifying any of their objects, within or between the inputs. Its other declarations are the disjoint named input declarations with these substitutions in their types. The map from each $B_i$ to $V$ preserves and reflects inhabitation at its old boundaries of dimension at least two for all the theories just listed. No relation between the object graphs or the generating dimensions of the inputs is needed.

Make a bipartite undirected graph $J$. One kind of vertex is an input block $i$, the other an object class $s$ of $V$. Each original object $x$ of $B_i$ gives a distinct incidence edge $d_x:i\to [x]$, including separate edges for objects that have been identified. Use the free groupoid on this graph. Grade a generating arrow of $B_i$ from $x$ to $y$ by $d_x^{-1}d_y$, in traversal order. A path written entirely in that block has the same grade solely from its endpoints, by cancellation. Thus each higher declaration is sent to an identity at the indicated grade. This gives a strict groupoid interpretation of the raw theory and all the surfaces: the groupoid is regarded as an ordinary strict higher category with only identities above its arrows, and the Gray theory projects to it. In particular, parallel 1-faces in *any* surface derivation have equal grades whenever they bound a 2-cell.

Fix a block to be recovered and take the universal covering tree of its component of $J$ based at its block vertex. Vertices can be realized as reduced walks from that base. Build a lifted computad by placing one copy of $B_i$ at each lift of $i$; its objects are the incident lifts of the corresponding object classes. Distinct objects in a single copy remain distinct because a tree has no parallel edges. Each attachment is the literal renamed attachment within its copy. There is a fold of the lifted computad to $V$.

Given a positive-dimensional expression in a derivation over $V$, specify a lift of its source object. It then lifts with target the end of its lifted grade. For a variable of $B_i$ with poles $x,y$, follow the unique lift of $d_x^{-1}$ from the specified object to find its block copy, and take the variable there. Its target is reached by $d_y$. All variables in its attaching expression lift in this same copy when started at their corresponding objects: the internal grades telescope there, including for an empty path. This proves the variable boundary assertion in declaration order.

For a labelled head, lift its spine vertices successively using the grades of its columns. Within a column the arrow slots have the same grade, since the child arity connects them by 2-cells. Lift each positive slot from its column's initial vertex and each object slot at its indicated vertex. A head on the point uses just its chosen vertex. If an anchor inside a spine is prescribed instead, propagation backwards is unique as well. A generic subexpression on an interval of the spine has grade the product on that interval, by the groupoid interpretation. Consequently evaluating a substituted face by this rule gives the literal face of the lifted expression: path lifting for a concatenation is successive path lifting. This also proves compatibility with arbitrary nested substitution by induction on expressions.

Conversion lifts with the same anchors. For a word equation and its coherence equation the generic arity is unchanged and both sides acquire the same lifted tuple. For insertion, each refined spine subdivides a column product or lies inside one column, so the lifted exterior and interior labels are the joined substitution of the lifted labels. Disc removal keeps the lift of the disc label; endo-coherence removal uses the same lift of the common face. Pruning is the identity-label case, whose grade subdivides with an identity wherever the spine is shortened. Congruence, symmetry and transitivity preserve the anchored lift. These checks use the unchanged arity condition and apply to heads and substitutions typed using conversion. They do not require a simultaneous substitution lifting *all* the variables of $V$ to one sheet. Only the labels in the particular derivation are lifted. Each expression is finite, so it can be read in the union of the finitely many block copies it uses.

There is a **raw substitution back to the distinguished block**. It is the identity on its central copy. Each component on leaving that block vertex in the tree attaches at just one of its original objects $x$. Send all the objects in that branch to $x$. To interpret any other entire block with constant object labels, first interpret it over the free raw point. In declaration order its next variable has a well-formed type over that point. The strict point has the required identity and raw pasting-arity lifting at the point supplies a term at precisely that type, for either kernel. Specialize this point assignment to $x$. The choices agree at shared vertices, which are the only shared declarations. This constructs a raw substitution on any needed finite subcontext. In particular no equality of different raw spellings of a constant path is assumed.

Now take an old type $A$ of dimension at least two in the distinguished block. Anchor a filler of its folded type at the original source object in the central copy. Its two given faces and their common lower boundary lift to their original expressions in that copy by the variable and substitution calculations. For a 2-type it matters that the 1-faces themselves are specified: their grade fixes the lifted target object even if several original vertices project to the same one. Apply the raw substitution back to the block. The result inhabits $A$ in the very theory in which the folded filler was given. The converse is substitution into $V$. At dimension one the analogous assertion holds with the grade from the chosen lift of the source to the chosen lift of the target imposed on the candidate filler. Without that condition folding two vertices can supply a new identity arrow.

### Consequences for the universal problem

Neither a binary surface nor insertion can first fail in degrees zero or one: an object term is a variable, and existence of a surface arrow gives an edge path by ordinary strict projection, with a raw composite or identity as witness. Apply the reflection result to one block with all objects identified. Any failure at an old type now gives a failure of exactly the same dimension in the one-object context. Conversely these are particular finite contexts. The empty context causes no exception.

For ordinary Q1 there is also a test with an arbitrarily long **prescribed lower skeleton**. Fix $k\ge1$. It suffices to test contexts with a single object and precisely one generating cell in each degree $1,\ldots,k$, having the previous generator as both immediate faces (the object in degree one). First fold a failing ordinary context to one object. Suspend its failing type and derivation, then fold the two suspension poles. Suspension shifts the derivation for the same surface up one cut, including the coherence equation; ordinary insertion suspends by the splice formulas of Response 12. A raw filler of the suspended boundary would give a lower raw filler by the fixed-hom comparison of Response 11. Folding reflects it at that specific boundary by the theorem above. Iterating gives exactly the indicated skeleton, with the failure shifted up $k$ degrees. This implication uses suspension in the ordinary theory; the dimension-preserving one-object test applies to both kernels. ∎

**Validation.** On a three-object context with a 2-generator replacing a composite path, a scalar at an identity, a generator from an identity to a loop, and two different 3-attachments, the checker lifted 279 folded terms and their immediate faces, then checked their types under the raw retraction. The tests used 21 distinct translates and a 232-declaration lifted context. All original variables returned literally. Agda 2.8.0 accepted the retracted 3-term for an attachment reached in a different translate. [Object-folding checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_object_fold.py)

## Researcher Response 21

**When can object identifications introduce a failure?** Let $V$ be obtained from finite blocks $B_i$ by identifying objects as in Response 20. Suppose that in each block both iterated 1-faces of every generator of dimension at least two have nonempty underlying edge paths. In ordinary CaTT the first failing dimension of $V$ is the minimum of those of the blocks, with $\infty$ for conservativity in all dimensions. This holds separately for each binary surface, for strict units, and for insertion. The attaching paths within a block may change length, overlap and revisit objects. The theorem concerns *all* old boundaries in $V$, including ones that exist only after the identifications.

There is a different conclusion for Gray interchange. Suppose also that both ordered 2-faces of every 3-generator have nonempty step lists. Consider two 2-generators $a,b$, possibly the same, with the target object of $a$ identified with the source object of $b$. If they belong to different blocks, or those two objects differed in their common block, then every Gray surface containing $I$ first fails in $V$ at dimension three. In particular this applies when the two distinct object poles of a single 2-generator are identified.

### Recording the positions that survive composition

At each object of $V$ allow an empty word. A letter $[x,y]_i$ goes from the class of $x$ to that of $y$, where $x,y$ are original objects of $B_i$. In a composable word merge two adjacent letters precisely when they are $[x,y]_i,[y,z]_i$, replacing them by $[x,z]_i$. A loop $[x,x]_i$ still occupies one position. The maximal runs with matching original ends in the same block give the unique reduced word and the map from input positions onto its positions. Reducing subwords first and then reducing the resulting word gives the same map on positions. This proves associativity and defines a category with the empty words as identities.

Send a generating edge to the letter of its block and original ends. By the nonemptiness hypothesis the two 1-faces of each higher generator go to the same single letter. This gives a strict interpretation, with only identities above degree one, for either raw kernel and its surfaces (by ordinary strict projection). Call the value of a positive-dimensional expression its grade. A nonempty grade $w$ specifies a finite context $L_w$. For each letter $[x_j,y_j]_{i_j}$ take a fresh copy of $B_{i_j}$, joining $y_j$ to $x_{j+1}$ in the next copy. There is a raw fold $L_w\to V$. This construction also allows $x_j=y_j$ and repeated copies of one block.

A derivation with spatial grade $w$ can be read in these copies. More precisely, for a subexpression of grade $h$ supply its exterior words $u,v$ such that the reduced product $uhv$ is $w$. A positive variable has a single letter, whose image position selects its copy. An object label at a cut between $u$ and $v$ lifts to the common original vertex if the adjacent letters merge, and to the joined vertex between copies if they do not. At an exterior end take the first entrance or last exit. In a labelled head use for each column the grade of any of its 1-labels. They coincide because the intervening 2-labels have equal boundary grades. For a label in that column append the preceding column grades to $u$ and prepend the following ones to $v$. For a spine vertex use the cut there. In the case $w$ is empty there are no positive variables in the expression: a nonempty column grade would survive in the product, as would any nonempty grade in its labels by induction. Such a derivation lifts over the free point at its original object.

These rules lift the *given faces*. On a variable its attaching expressions lie inside its original copy, since the product of each of their traversed original edge paths is evaluated there. For heads the grade on a subinterval is its column product. The map on positions for a subdivided interval is the composite of the two maps on positions. At an empty interval this says that the two adjoining ends specify the same vertex in $L_w$, by the joining rule. Induction therefore commutes with evaluating a face and with nested substitution. A word equation and its coherence equation use the same lifted tuple. Disc and endo-coherence removal preserve the indicated label or face. For an insertion the substituted column product is the product of the splice, with the same cuts and positions by subdivision. A pruned identity contributes the empty grade at an object cut and has the grade of its base at a higher cut. The surviving labels have the same positions. Thus the conversion rules lift as well. In dimension at least two a filler and both its prescribed faces have the same grade.

### The ordinary hom at a reduced word

Write $X_i^+(x,y)$ for the part of the realized raw ordinary hom of $B_i$ consisting of nonempty grade. If $x=y$ the empty-grade summand is equivalent to the tensor unit. In the realized raw hom of $V$ the summand over $w$ is
$$
                  \prod_{j=1}^{|w|} X_{i_j}^+(x_j,y_j).
$$
The comparison from this product is enriched composition of the included block homs. The empty product is the unit at the appropriate object.

Here are details for derived attachments. Keep the object set fixed and start with the 1-graph. Its homs are discrete sets of paths. Cutting each path at the changes of block or the mismatches of original objects proves the formula at this stage. Attach the remaining generators in declaration order, using the realized hom globe $K=\partial D^m\to D^m=L$ for a global generator of dimension $m+1$. In the free enriched extension filter by the number $r$ of occurrences of $L$. A word at stage $r$ has $r$ marked letters, with old hom factors in the intervening and exterior gaps. Its attaching object is the punctured cube obtained by replacing one or more marked $L$'s by $K$. Evaluation there uses the actual specified boundary in the old hom. This is the enriched attachment filtration of Response 6, now with cartesian tensor. Equivalently its derived version follows by resolving under the free category on $K$ by free enriched categories, computing by the tensor-word formula levelwise and realizing. Finite products preserve colimits separately and geometric realization commutes with the finite products in the word objects. Passing to complete enriched categories preserves the fixed homs. [Enriched attachment and realization comparison](#researcher-response-6) · [Haugseng, free-algebra filtration](https://arxiv.org/abs/1312.3881)

Each mark contributes a nonempty letter with specified block and original ends. Its whole attaching boundary has that grade, including when the poles are the same object. Split the old gap factors into their word summands using the inductive formula. Reading the old letters and marks in order now assigns each to a unique position of $w$. Within a position they are exactly a word in the *block's* extension filtration. To check the inverse, start with a marked block word at each position, with a total of $r$ marks. Between successive marks concatenate the old pieces in that interval, including intervening whole unmarked positions. Do the same at the two exterior ends. This recovers the grade of each global gap and the product coordinates of its old hom. Inside a position an empty gap requires the same original object on its two sides. Between successive positions without an intervening old letter it requires their common *identified* object. Both gaps contribute just the unit. These rules also recover the empty local pieces at the ends of marked positions. Consequently the stage of total filtration degree $r$ has the same attaching objects and words as the total-degree filtration of the displayed product of block extensions. Replacing marks by their boundaries is compatible with this identification: their attaching maps are in the indicated block, and combining the adjacent old gap factors is composition, within a position or between positions as prescribed by the comparison. In particular no mark or attaching face can enter the empty-word summand. Induction on the filtration and on declarations proves the formula. The argument uses the ordinary cartesian product of the *weak* homs at each stage.

Apply this calculation also to $L_w$, grading by its distinct named copies. Its distinguished word has one letter per copy. There is a raw projection to each selected block, fixing that copy, sending every preceding copy through the raw point to its entrance, and every following copy through the raw point to its exit. Use the point assignments of Response 20 so this is a substitution at the literal attaching types. On the product comparison for the distinguished word these projections induce the product projections up to unitors: the selected factor is fixed, and every other factor goes through the hom of a point. Thus their joint realization gives the inverse equivalence on this summand. For a lifted expression its grade in the copy-labelled category is the specified distinguished word, by the same position calculation.

Now let every block be conservative through degree $n$, and take a surface filler at an old boundary of degree $2\le d\le n$ in $V$. Lift it and its boundary to $L_w$, then project to each block. Its projections give surface inhabitants at the projected *raw* types. Choose raw inhabitants there. Their semantic cells lie over the required grades since their 1-boundaries are prescribed. In the product they fill the joint image of the given lifted boundary. The equivalence on the word summand reflects existence at those faces by the prescribed-boundary lifting test of Response 6. Ordinary realization therefore gives a raw inhabitant over $L_w$ at the lifted old type. Folding gives one over $V$. For an empty word use the raw point lifting property. In degrees zero and one raw object and edge-path existence suffice. Conversely Response 20 reflects raw existence from $V$ to each block at its old boundaries in every possible failing degree. This proves the assertion about the least dimension.

### The separated Gray square

In the situation of the Gray assertion the grade of the composite of the two source paths has two distinct positions, even when $a=b$. In an ordered history on this spatial path, a generating step rewrites within one position: its original source and target paths are nonempty and lie entirely within one block, and substitution preserves the positions of the exterior edges. Give each step the position it affects. The exchange target has position list $12$ and its source has list $21$, in the orientation of the Setting. Every directed move from a list of the form $1^*2^*$ preserves that form. Indeed a 3-generator replaces a nonempty list within one position by a nonempty list in the same position. Spatial and temporal whiskering keep the outside events in place. A directed exchange within a position preserves the list, and one between positions can only move a 1 earlier than a 2. Thus $12$ cannot return to $21$. By the ordered-history criterion in Response 2 the reverse square has no raw filler at its original boundary. Interchange in the surface supplies one, and dimensions below three are conservative. ∎

**Validation.** In a block with a length-changing rule $fg\Rightarrow k$, its converse, a loop, and a 3-attachment, the checker compared 9,489 products of rewrite histories with 17,831 interleaved readings by their actual multiplicities. It then folded three objects, checked 193 positional lifts against their original expressions and immediate faces, and typechecked 494 terms at their projected block boundaries.
