### Two counits determine the free chart of a unit

Work in raw algebras, with maps sending generators to arbitrary well-typed terms. Suppose the cells of each dimension of a proposed surface model depend naturally on a raw algebra $Y$ and are a disjoint union of sets $\operatorname{Hom}(Q,Y)$ indexed by shapes, with each $Q$ free on a computad. Faces, identities and top composition are natural in $Y$. Response 16 uses precisely this description. Fix a seam shape represented by $Q$, and let $E$ represent the shape of its identity. The output shape of a natural operation on a representable summand is fixed: evaluate at the identity map of the representing object and specialize. The identity and its two faces therefore give raw maps
$$
  i_-,i_+:Q\longrightarrow E,\qquad e:E\longrightarrow Q,\qquad
  e i_-=e i_+=1_Q .
$$
Pairs of composable cells of shape $E,E$ are represented by $P=E\amalg_Q E$, with inclusions $j_1,j_2$ satisfying $j_1 i_+=j_2 i_-$. Their composite has shape $E$, by specializing one factor to the identity on the adjacent face of the other. This uses only the unit equations for the two parallel word pairs on the single $n$-disc, valid for every $S$ containing $U$. Thus top composition gives $d:E\to P$ such that
$$
            [1_E,i_+e]d=1_E=[i_-e,1_E]d.                         \tag{1}
$$
The two maps out of $P$ specialize respectively the right and the left factor to the identity. These are the counit equations for cocomposition. We can classify their solutions directly in raw syntax. For comparison, joint epimorphy of the faces of a cocategory is known in coherent categories. The argument here uses free computad maps and requires only (1). [Lumsdaine, *A small observation on co-categories*](https://arxiv.org/abs/0902.4743)

A raw substitution into a term headed by a coherence retains that head. In particular a preimage of a generator under any raw substitution is a generator. Since $e i_\pm=1$, the two face maps embed $Q$ as subcomputads of $E$. The pushout $P$ is consequently free on two copies of the declarations of $E$ with the specified copies of the seam identified. For a generator $z$ of $E$, (1) makes $d(z)$ a generator of $P$. If it lies in the first copy, the first projection forces $d(z)=j_1(z)$ and the second gives $z=i_-e(z)$. If it lies in the second copy the same equations give $d(z)=j_2(z)$ and $z=i_+e(z)$. In either case $e(z)$ is a generator. Every declaration of $E$ is thus a face declaration. An identification $i_-(a)=i_+(b)$ forces $a=b$ by applying $e$. The set $K$ of such shared generators is closed under their attaching types: the two renamed expressions for the attaching type agree literally, so the two inclusions agree on every generator occurring in those expressions, and recursively on its boundary.

It follows that $E=Q\amalg_K Q$ over its two faces, up to renaming. Conversely each such double has the fold $e$ and a unique solution to (1). On the first copy of $Q$ take $j_1i_-$, and on the second take $j_2i_+$. They agree on $K$ by the pushout relation. The preceding generator calculation proves uniqueness, including on the intersection. This cocomposition is automatically coassociative: in a threefold amalgam both iterations select the first entrance copy of $Q$ and the last exit copy.

Now assume $Q$ is generated through dimension $n-1$, has an evaluation $(n-1)$-term whose ordinary strict support contains all its generators, and the $n$-shape $E$ evaluates to a raw cell between the evaluations of its two faces. This includes the fully supported seam strings of Response 16. Since $E$ is the double of $Q$, its ordinary strict image has only identity $n$-cells. Thus the two copies of the evaluation of $Q$ have equal strict values there. Their generator supports are the respective entire copies, by naturality under the two inclusions and support invariance from Response 9. Their equality forces $K=Q$. This argument also works for the directed kernel by projecting to the ordinary strict image. In particular the actual identity summand on the vertex is represented by the point. Positive length and a distinguished bead for that unit cannot satisfy the two equations and evaluation hypotheses. Even a generic substitution-preserving choice of a raw binary arrow composite on the unchanged globes cannot have a literal right unit: every arrow across a two-edge arity has a coherence head, and specializing its last edge to an identity retains that head, whereas the surviving edge is a variable.

### Which planar charts with empty seams still lift?

Allow lengths zero in the strings of Response 16. An empty string has one seam and evaluates to its raw identity. Consider any expanded globular arity generated through dimension two. Within each spatial column first identify the two occurrences of the same seam at a zero-length 2-string. The resulting column consists of a list of spatial paths $p_0,\ldots,p_m$ and one generating 2-bead $p_i\Rightarrow p_{i+1}$ at each step, using the chosen raw writings of the paths. Each nonempty seam path has fresh edge names and private interior vertices. This follows directly by adjoining the intermediate seams of each nonempty 2-string and sharing the entire end seam between successive strings. Across columns only the spine objects can be shared. An empty spatial path identifies the two poles of its column. Distinct empty positions separated by a bead are retained in the list. A column with no bead has one seam.

If $p_r,p_s$ are empty with $r<s$ in one column, the beads between them compose to a raw scalar $b:1_x\Rightarrow1_x$. Each bead in this composite occurs once. At the diagonal of the two-variable braid of Response 1 substitute $b$ twice. Its strict image and that of the identity on $b*_1 b$ coincide, since the strict category has only identity 3-cells. Their raw 4-type is uninhabited. Indeed, in the list model assign a singleton to one constituent bead and the empty list to every other generating 2-cell. All lower generating boundaries have their unique interpretations, so this defines a labelling of the *whole* chart. The value of $b$ is a singleton. The two-colour calculation for the generic braid, specialized to this singleton, transposes the two positions, while the identity fixes both. This gives the asserted failure of prescribed-boundary lifting even when no individual bead is a scalar.

In the other direction suppose that each column has at most one empty seam. In each column that has one, mark all edge names of all paths strictly *after* that seam. Start a spatial rewriting history at an empty path. Every possible first rewrite has empty source and produces a nonempty path of marked edges. As long as all current edges are marked, a rule with nonempty source can apply only using the edge names of its own column and seam. Its source is strictly after the empty seam, so its target is again nonempty and entirely marked. An empty-source rule anywhere has the same effect on the invariant as the first rewrite. No nontrivial history from an empty path can return to an empty path. Strict 2-cells are represented by such spatial rewriting histories, and their generating-event counts are invariant under exchange. This proves bubble-freeness for the actual glued chart, including when several consecutive columns have the same object poles.

For a finite bubble-free 2-polygraph the semantic cellular realization is its ordinary strict category, by the planar homotopy-polygraph theorem. It applies to the present attaching words: the raw path writings map to the indicated words under the free-graph equivalence before the 2-cells are attached. The comparison on each actual fixed hom is strict evaluation, by the computadic realization theorem. The target has only identity equivalences: counts of generating events add, so an invertible 2-cell has no events, and an invertible edge path must have length zero. The comparison is bijective on vertices. The weak-equivalence and equifibration tests of Response 3 now give a globular trivial fibration onto the ordinary strict image. This proves both directions of the chart criterion. Its nonstationary raw hom realizes the ordinary free category on the planar chart and its strict hom is the ordinary strict category on that chart, by the fixed-hom comparison of Response 11. The stationary homs realize the unit and the reverse hom is empty. The affirmative direction follows by the same lifting test. In the negative direction the suspended braid and identity have equal strict images and no raw 5-cell, by the suspended two-colour argument of Response 1. In particular the obstruction to extending Response 16 by admitting empty strings occurs already on the ordinary 2-disc with two empty 1-faces and one free 2-bead, or on its suspended 3-disc for the directed comparison. The unit-chart theorem determines when the strict-image comparison required by that construction works in these dimensions. Q1 still requires a return from a derivation in the specified surface at an arbitrary old boundary. ∎

## Researcher Response 23

**The set of gaps admitting a Gray return can be any computably enumerable subset of the natural numbers.** More precisely, fix a binary surface with $I\in S$. From a recognizer of a computably enumerable set $A$ one can effectively build a finite 3-computad on the graph
$$
      x\xrightarrow{f}y,\qquad g:y\to y,\qquad y
         \xrightarrow{h}z
$$
where $g$ is a loop. It has a distinguished pair of 2-generators $L:f\Rightarrow f$, $B:h\Rightarrow h$. The elementary square of $L,B$ across $g^n$ has a raw return iff $n\in A$. Every other pair has returns at every gap. All 2-generators have nonempty spatial faces, and no spatial path supports three separated 2-generators.

Consequently **conservativity through dimension three in an individual finite context is $\Pi^0_2$-complete** for each of the four Gray surfaces containing $I$, even on the displayed class. In particular there is a *single* finite context where the varying old reverse-square boundary represents a computably enumerable complete set. Response 17 gave a recursively enumerable complete test under an additional restriction excluding a 3-generator beside a 2-generator. Allowing such generators can change even the quantifier complexity of the context test.

### Converting the stationary gap into a machine input

Use a finite directed string system with input word $\ell P C^n r$ and accepting word $\ell Q C^n r$. Here $\ell,r$ are distinct brackets, $P,Q$ are the initial and final states, and $C$ is the unary input symbol. Require that a run from this input can reach *any* word $\ell Q C^j r$ only upon acceptance, and then $j=n$. Such systems uniformly recognize every computably enumerable set. To construct one, use a deterministic tape machine which keeps a copy of its unary input, runs the recognizer on a work track and on acceptance restores the input and positions the head at the left end. Encode the finite visited tape segment between two brackets with the state immediately before the scanned symbol. A right move rewrites the state, symbol and right neighbour, extending the tape by a blank when the neighbour is the bracket. A left move includes the left neighbour; the machine may use a tape with a fixed left end. The empty input is handled at the right bracket by the initial state. Finite initialization and cleanup states convert between bare unary symbols and tape-track symbols, trim the work extension, and enter $Q$ at the left end only on completion. With state-directed rules every reachable nonfinal configuration has at most one successor, all rule sides are nonempty, and the two outer brackets are preserved. Use fresh symbols for the machine alphabet and controls except for the displayed interface.

Read juxtaposition of events in temporal order. All machine symbols, including brackets, are now generating 2-endomorphisms of $f$, distinct from $L$. Call them helpers. Add two 2-generators $d:fg\Rightarrow f$ and $e:f\Rightarrow fg$. The only generator on the right is $B$. At the level of ordered histories impose the following 3-generators. Each double arrow denotes two separate declarations.

1. On $fg$ and $f$ put $de\leftrightarrow 1_{fg}$ and $ed\leftrightarrow 1_f$, respectively. The right sides have empty event lists.
2. For each helper $X$ put $X\triangleright g\leftrightarrow d\,b(X)\,e$ on $fg$, where $b(P)=PC$, $b(Q)=QC$, and $b(X)=X$ for the other symbols. Also put the return $XB\to BX$ on $fh$ for every helper, with the spatial whiskers understood.
3. On $f$ put $L\to\ell P r$ and $\ell Q r\to L$. On $fh$ install each directed machine rule, whiskering its letters on the right by $h$. There is no generating return for $L,B$.

These are actual raw computad attachments. Write $fg$ and $fh$ as binary composites, an empty temporal reading as the identity on its indicated path, and nonempty readings with a fixed vertical bracketing. In (2) the variable $d$ ends at $f$, every letter in $b(X)$ is on $f$, and $e$ ends back at the specified raw $fg$. Both layers of the square on $fh$ are endomorphisms of the same raw binary path. The machine rules have that same path at both ends. Thus the two readings in each declaration have identical raw types, independently of conversion.

At any gap the moves $d,e$ themselves can pass $B$ backwards. For $u$ equal to either move let $v$ be the other. Write $B_p,B_q$ for the event at the right end before and after $u:p\to q$. Insert $vu$ after $B_q$, use the forward Gray square for $v$, then contract $uv$:
$$
       u B_q\ \longrightarrow\ u B_q v u
         \ \longrightarrow\ u v B_p u
         \ \longrightarrow\ B_p u .
$$
The local insertions and contractions are (1) with the stationary suffix. All other bare squares consist of a left helper or $L$, and $B$, with a gap $g^n$. This follows from the object poles: two left events cannot be spatial neighbours and nothing starts beyond $z$.

For a word $w$ of helpers read on the left with gap $n>0$, apply (2) to each letter and contract each intervening $ed$. This gives a reversible path to the reading $d\,b(w)\,e$ whose middle events now have gap $n-1$, where $b$ extends by concatenation. Iterating, the whole reading is equivalent in both directions to $d^n b^n(w)e^n$ with the middle word at gap zero. For each helper this gives a return across $B$: expand, move $B$ left through $e^n$, then through the zero-gap letters by their declared returns, then through $d^n$, and contract the expanded word on the other side of $B$.

For the special square start at $LB$ with gap $n$. Open $L$ by (3) and expand the helper word. At path $fh$ inside $d^n(-)e^n$ the resulting word is exactly $\ell P C^n r$. If the recognizer accepts, run the machine there to obtain $\ell Q C^n r$. Move $B$ to the front as above and reverse the expansion for $\ell Qr$ after $B$. The second rule of (3) gives $BL$ at the original gap. Response 2 lifts this ordered return at the two prescribed raw square boundaries, including the bracketing and whisker comparisons.

### Why no other return can accept an input

Throughout any history at this boundary the spatial path has the form $fg^i h$. The steps $d,e$ change $i$ by minus or plus one. Project a temporal history to a word as follows, reading the value of $i$ at the instant of each event. Omit $d,e$. Retain $B$, send $L$ to a distinct symbol $L_i$, send $P,Q$ to $PC^i,QC^i$, and retain each other helper. A local replacement with the same final spatial path leaves every following event's index unchanged.

Under this projection the inverse-pair moves and every helper expansion or contraction preserve the word. For instance $PC^i$ expands at $i>0$ as $(PC^{i-1})C$. A helper return at gap zero or a generic forward square can reorder $B$ with helpers. The only change to the relative order of $B$ and a protected symbol through such an exchange is $B L_i\to L_i B$. After erasing $B$, the remaining possible nonidentity projected moves are exactly instances of the following allowed rewrites (only containment in this list is needed):
$$
    L_i\to\ell P C^i r,\qquad \ell Q C^i r\to L_i,
       \qquad \text{a directed machine rule}.
$$
In particular a machine rule acts with index zero: its spatial window is $fh$ and there are no nonidentity paths before $x$ or after $z$. This check includes arbitrary temporal contexts, forward squares for the length-changing $d,e$, and their spatial whiskers.

Until $L_n$ is opened, a history projecting to $L_n B$ cannot put $B$ first. Once opened, its $B$-erasure is one bracketed input. Before the first closing move the sequence of changes of this erased word is a machine computation. There is no protected symbol left to open a second input. There is a unique pair of brackets, and the closing rule needs both, so it cannot accept a subword of a larger input. Reaching any protected symbol again requires machine acceptance on $n$. Since the requested final reading projects to $B L_n$, this proves necessity. It also rules out attempts to change the input by inserting navigation pairs or by temporarily sliding $B$ into the tape.

### Complexity of the context test

For any finite context, enumerate its bare elementary squares. For each one the statement that a specified finite sequence is a return is decidable by checking its ordered histories and labelled moves. The universally quantified existence of such finite certificates is $\Pi^0_2$, and Response 2 identifies it with conservativity through dimension three. A higher-dimensional declaration cannot affect this test. Conversely let a $\Pi^0_2$ predicate be given as $\forall n\,\exists t\,R(k,n,t)$ for computable $R$. Build the recognizer which searches for $t$ on input $n$, with $k$ fixed, and apply the construction. Every helper and navigation square returns. Thus the finite context passes precisely for those $k$ satisfying the predicate, a computable many-one reduction. Taking an individual recognizer instead proves the claimed classification of possible unary gap sets in both directions, since directed returns can always be enumerated. [Arithmetic hierarchy and totality](https://www.cs.cmu.edu/~cdm/resources/main-fund.pdf)

The same proof gives a $\Pi^0_2$-complete test for whether the *first* failure in a context occurs strictly after degree three or never occurs. For a non-total recognizer the first failure is exactly three. On a total recognizer the argument supplies all returns in degree three; mixed higher cells in this family can involve the machine's 3-generators. The all-dimensional equivalence on the family in Response 8 uses an additional higher-dimensional argument and cannot be inferred here from the complexity of the three-dimensional test. ∎

### A finite list under temporal positivity, without a bound on intermediate histories

There is a sharper upper bound when *both* sides of each declared 3-rule have nonempty temporal lists. Suppose also that no spatially composable triple of generating 2-cells exists. Form the finite dictionary $D$ of all contiguous subwords of the spatial source paths of the generating 2-cells and of the starting spatial paths of the generating 3-rules, including the empty word. **Every bare square which has a return has its stationary gap in $D$.** A polygon may occur beside a 2-generator or beside another polygon. Temporal list lengths may increase and decrease without bound.

To prove it, take a gap $m\notin D$ from $a$ to $b$ and fix the initial spatial path $s(a)\,m\,s(b)$. It is the same initial path for every temporal history in a proposed return. Every generating step has nonempty spatial source: an empty source would make its two poles coincide, allowing a spatial triple of that same generator. No possible *first* step on the fixed path can act entirely inside $m$, since its poles would give a spatial triple with $a,b$. No first step can touch both exterior segments either, for its source word would then contain $m$. Thus every possible first event touches exactly one of the two exterior segments. Call this its side. The histories stay nonempty under every move by temporal positivity.

The initial $LR$ has first event on the left. A move with a nonempty temporal prefix preserves the first event. A directed square at the start puts the spatially earlier of its two steps first and therefore cannot change the first side from left to right. A declared rule acting at the start has a single window on the *fixed initial path*. Both its first source event and its first target event have nonempty supports inside that very window, since each is executed before any other event of its respective list. To change their side from left to right, the window would have to contain the entire gap $m$, contrary to $m\notin D$. Thus the first side stays left, while $RL$ starts on the right. This proves the bound. A mixed rule can act deep inside a history after intermediate spatial contractions have brought the two sides together; the invariant concerns only the time-zero spatial path.

For each ordered pair of generating 2-cells, check by the finite graph/trie inclusion test of Response 17 whether every gap belongs to $D$. A failed inclusion supplies a specific old reverse-square type without a raw inhabitant. If all inclusions pass, conservativity through dimension three is exactly the conjunction of the finitely many directed returns for the enumerated gaps. This is recursively enumerable complete on positive no-triple 3-computads, with hardness already on the subclass in Response 17. In particular a directed cycle on a path between the inner poles forces a failure at degree three, regardless of the other positive 3-rules. The four navigation declarations with an empty temporal side in the machine construction are outside the hypothesis of the invariant.

This also bounds the **all-dimensional** context test on *every* finite positive Gray 3-computad by a difference of two computably enumerable sets, for each $S$ containing $I$. The first set rejects triples and failed dictionary inclusions and accepts upon finding the finite family of returns. The second set enumerates, among the no-triple contexts, those having a higher failure: enumerate surface derivations at old boundaries of dimension at least four and apply the raw decision procedure of Response 18 at each such boundary. A context with a triple always fails by dimension four by Response 9. For all other contexts the difference of the two sets consequently tests the desired predicate. This extends the difference bound to cyclic object graphs and arbitrary positive temporal length changes. ∎

The four odd inputs through seven halt in a nonaccepting projected configuration. In a separate check all 11,692 contextual moves generated on sampled histories obeyed the projection, including moves at empty temporal windows. Agda 2.8.0 checked both raw boundary readings of all 28 declared 3-rules of this example at the specified common types. ## Researcher Response 24

**For either kernel, restricting to disjoint generator faces and forbidding strict cycles of any fixed bounded positive length preserves the least failing dimension in Q1.** More precisely, fix an integer $m\ge2$. For either raw kernel there is an effective subdivision $f:\Gamma\to\Gamma_m$ of finite computads, in the direction of maps of free models. It preserves the maximum dimension of the generators. For **each** raw type $A$ over $\Gamma$ and each of the theories $T'$ in the question extending that kernel, including the kernel itself,
$$
             \Gamma\vdash_{T'} A\text{ inhabited}
    \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
             \Gamma_m\vdash_{T'} fA\text{ inhabited}.                 \tag{1}
$$
In $\Gamma_m$ the two immediate faces of every positive-dimensional generator have disjoint top-dimensional generator supports, at least one nonempty. For an edge the two vertices are distinct. In every positive dimension $r$, an ordinary strict $r$-cell with equal immediate faces uses a multiple of $m$ generating $r$-cells, counted with multiplicities. For the directed kernel the count is taken after ordinary strict projection. Consequently the proposed restriction in Q2 forbidding generators from identities to identities, even combined with any fixed lower bound on the length of nontrivial ordinary strict cycles in *all* dimensions, gives exactly the same **universal** conservativity question as Q1. There is also an exact answer for Gray interchange on subdivisions of finite 3-computads with positive path lengths preserved by rewriting. A composable pair of higher generators in the original graph gives failure in degree three. In the absence of such a pair the subdivided context is conservative in every dimension.

### The subdivision and its incidence

To fill a prescribed raw type $A$ with a chart, adjoin a fresh vertex if $A=\star$. Otherwise write $A=(a\to_B b)$ of dimension $r$. Construct $m-1$ independent charts at $B$, with evaluations $z_1,\ldots,z_{m-1}$. Set $z_0=a$, $z_m=b$, adjoin a generator $c_i:z_{i-1}\to_B z_i$ for each $1\le i\le m$, and evaluate the chart by their left-associated top composite. This operation exists in both kernels and has the specified raw type. Process the declarations of $\Gamma$ in order, applying the construction at each translated type and assigning its evaluation to the original variable. In particular any original composite attaching term is translated by ordinary raw substitution.

For each chain $c_1,\ldots,c_m$ and each interior $z_i$, choose a marker $p_i$. This is its fresh vertex in rank one, and otherwise one of the fresh top generators of the chart evaluating to $z_i$. Its multiplicity in that evaluation is one. Among all the declared $r$-generators, its only immediate face occurrences at top degree are in the target of $c_i$ and the source of $c_{i+1}$. To see this, every chain produced by the algorithm has exterior faces translated from the original context. This holds recursively since the lower charts are attached at $B$, whose own faces are the lower faces of the prescribed type. A translated old cell of dimension $r-1$ uses at top degree only generators in the chains assigned to old $(r-1)$-variables, or old vertex images in degree zero. This follows by applying the induced ordinary strict substitution to its old strict value. All private top generators of the various interior charts are fresh and disjoint from these. These facts also give the claimed disjointness and nonemptiness at each generating face pair, including when $a=b$ or both are identities.

Write $|u|_c$ for the multiplicity of the top-dimensional generator $c$ in an ordinary strict cell $u$, using the indicator of a vertex in degree zero. For every strict $r$-cell $u$,
$$
             |u|_{c_i}-|u|_{c_{i+1}}
         = |\mathrm{t}u|_{p_i}-|\mathrm{s}u|_{p_i}.                  \tag{2}
$$
On generators this is precisely the marker calculation. Identities give zero, top composition telescopes at the common face, and composition at a lower cut adds the two differences. The strict laws preserve the counts. If the faces coincide, or if both are translated old cells, the right side vanishes. All $m$ multiplicities in each chain are then equal. Every declared $r$-generator belongs to exactly one such chain, proving divisibility. The lower bound is attained on the translation of the scalar $\alpha:1_x\Rightarrow1_x$, which uses exactly $m$ 2-generators. For $m=2$ this is the composite $1_x\Rightarrow pq\Rightarrow1_x$ with $p:x\to y$ and $q:y\to x$.

### A return map at arbitrary attaching terms

Write $D_r$ for the free raw disc, $B_r$ for its boundary, and $K_r$ for the chart attached at the generic boundary $B_r$. Its evaluation classifies a map $i:D_r\to K_r$ fixing $B_r$. We first construct a raw map $\rho:K_r\to D_r$ fixing $B_r$ such that $\rho i$ is homotopic to $1_{D_r}$ relative to $B_r$.

Use the trivial fibration $p:D_r\to C_{D_r}$ of Response 3 onto the strict disc. The boundary labelling in the strict disc extends across $K_r$ with evaluation the specified top generator. Indeed, for any strict value $w:a\to b$, label every interior chart recursively with evaluation $b$, the first bead with $w$, and the others with the identity on $b$. At rank zero simply use the specified vertex. Strict top units give the required evaluation. Since $B_r\hookrightarrow K_r$ is cellular, this labelling lifts against $p$ with the original raw boundary fixed. Its lift $\rho$ satisfies $p\rho i=p$. Factor the relative fold $D_r\amalg_{B_r}D_r\to D_r$ as a cofibration followed by a trivial fibration. The two maps $\rho i,1$ on its ends extend across this relative cylinder by lifting against $p$ over their common strict label. This proves the desired relative homotopy. At rank zero take the identity.

Here are the attachment details ensuring that the return survives a generator whose type mentions arbitrary old composites. For a fibrant target $Y$, take a simplicial frame $Y_\bullet$ with $Y_0=Y$ and write $M(X,Y)_k=\operatorname{Hom}(X,Y_k)$. For cofibrant $X$ this is a Kan complex computing the derived mapping space. Restriction along a cofibration of cofibrant objects is a Kan fibration. Paths relative to a cofibrant boundary compute the same relative homotopy classes as cylinders in the category under that boundary. Raw free computads are cofibrant and all raw models are fibrant by Response 3. [Hovey, *Model Categories*, Chapters 1 and 5](https://www.sas.rochester.edu/mth/sites/doug-ravenel/otherpapers/hovey-model-cats.pdf)

Inductively let $f:X\to W$ be the translation so far, with a raw return $v:W\to X$ and $vf\simeq1_X$. For the next attaching map $a:B_r\to X$ form the actual pushouts
$$
 X'=X\amalg_{B_r}D_r,\qquad W'=W\amalg_{B_r}K_r,
$$
where the second attachment uses $fa$. Let $h:X\to X'$ and $z:D_r\to X'$ be the inclusions. Choose a path from $hvf$ to $h$ in $M(X,X')$. Restrict it along $a$. At its terminal endpoint the restriction extends across $K_r$ by $z\rho$. Lift the path **with that endpoint specified** along $M(K_r,X')\to M(B_r,X')$, a Kan fibration. Its initial label now agrees literally with $hvfa$ on the boundary, so it glues to $hv$ to give a raw return $v':W'\to X'$. Restricting the lifted path along $i$ and gluing to the path on $X$ gives a path from $v'f'$ to the map given by $h,z\rho i$ on the two pieces of $X'$. This gluing is literal in each simplicial degree by the pushout property. The relative homotopy above connects the latter map to $1_{X'}$ with the $X$ piece fixed. Starting at the empty context proves $vf\simeq1$ for the whole translation.

The strict categories admit an additional conclusion. Repeat the strict labelling in the original strict category at each declaration, taking $w$ to be its original generating cell. By induction every translated exterior face returns to its original strict face on the nose. This constructs a strict left inverse of the translation on the entire computad, both in the ordinary and the enriched strict images. Thus equality of strict images of old terms is also reflected.

### Reflection at the exact old type

A raw type of dimension $d$ is a boundary map $b:B_d\to H_\Gamma$ into the free raw model. Apply the canonical interpretation $q:H_\Gamma\to Y$ in the underlying raw algebra of the free $T'$-model on $\Gamma$. Inhabitation is precisely extendability of $qb$ over $D_d$, including when representatives in $T'$ use conversion. Forward transport in (1) is substitution by $f$. In the reverse direction, substitute the raw return $v$ into the given $T'$-term at $fb$. This extends $qvfb$. Since $vf\simeq1$, its boundary lies in the same component of $M(B_d,Y)$ as $qb$. The fibration $M(D_d,Y)\to M(B_d,Y)$ lifts a path to $qb$ starting at the extension just obtained. The terminal vertex is an extension at precisely $qb$. This proves (1) for every surface using only fibrancy of its raw reduct. ∎

Applying (1) to both theories in a proposed failure transfers its witness with its original degree. Taking the least degree over all contexts therefore gives the same answer on the indicated restricted class. For instance the sharper Gray strict-image conjecture of Q1(i) still fails there: translate the suspended scalar witnesses of Response 1. Functoriality preserves equality of their Gray strict images, while (1) reflects raw inhabitation of their comparison type.

### An exact Gray context test for subdivisions with positive path lengths

Suppose the original graph admits positive integer edge lengths for which the two spatial paths of each generating 2-cell have equal length. Require also that the iterated 1-source of each generator of dimension at least two has positive length. The first condition gives a length to every strict 1-path and makes it invariant under every 2-cell, so the iterated 1-target has the same positive length. For instance, unit lengths work on a computad with no 2-generators provided each higher attachment lies over a nonempty path. The hypothesis also allows length-changing rewrites in the ordinary edge count.

Call two occurrences of original generators of dimension at least two *spatially composable* if there is an old directed path from the target object of the first to the source object of the second. Allow an empty intervening path and repeated occurrences. For every $m\ge2$ and every Gray surface containing $I$:

- If there is a spatially composable pair, the **first** failing dimension of $\Gamma_m$ is three.
- If there is no pair, $\Gamma_m$ is conservative through dimension three. When $\Gamma$ is generated through dimension three, it is conservative in **every dimension**, for all eight binary surfaces.

Thus for finite 3-computads satisfying the length hypothesis this is an all-dimensional classification on their subdivisions for each surface containing $I$. The test on the original object graph is ordinary directed reachability.

To prove it, compress each subdivided 1-chain to a single edge when reading paths between original objects. The compression is unambiguous since all its interior vertices are private. Such an edge represents either an original edge or an interior spatial path of a 2-chart. Give the former its original length and the latter the common length of the exterior paths of that chart. The latter are translations of old 1-terms, both when subdividing an original 2-variable and when the 2-chart occurs recursively in a higher attachment. A generating 2-step replaces an exterior word by a private edge, one private edge by the next, or a private edge by an exterior word. All steps preserve length. Every declared 3-rule acts in a spatial window whose *initial and final paths consist entirely of original edges* after compression. Indeed they are the translated 1-boundaries of the type at which the 3-chain is constructed, shared by all its beads by globularity. Its temporal sides can be arbitrary histories there, including empty ones. Higher declarations introduce no further moves in dimension three. Replacing any private spatial edge by its chart's original source word also shows that reachability between original objects is unchanged.

For a composable pair choose a 2-chart in each of its two attachments, recursively if necessary. Take the last 2-step $q$ of the left chart and the first 2-step $p$ of the right chart. Insert the translated intervening path. In the initial spatial path of their square the left factor is a private edge just before $q$. In the final path the right factor is a private edge just after $p$. Mark a half-integer length coordinate inside each of these two factors. Positive integral lengths and length preservation place the marks in disjoint ordered intervals at the two ends of the square and allow the same coordinates to be read at each time in every proposed return. Each history starts with the left mark in the designated private edge and ends with it in the old exterior word. Until a step touches that mark the whole edge persists, and $q$ is its only outgoing step. The right mark starts in an old exterior word and ends in its designated private edge, so dually $p$ must be the last step touching it. This singles out one occurrence of each event even with identical variable labels elsewhere.

A declared rule whose window misses a mark cannot change any event touching it. A rule whose window contains the left mark cannot cover the first event touching that mark, or introduce one before it. At the start of such a replacement the window would still contain the private edge, but the rule starts on a word of original edges. At the right mark use the final word to exclude covering the last event or inserting one after it. This argument includes rules spanning both marks and rules applied at empty temporal windows. A forward square interchanges steps with disjoint windows, putting the spatially earlier one first. It preserves the identities of the distinguished events since two independent steps cannot both touch the same mark. If it interchanges the distinguished events themselves it puts $q$ first: the two windows contain the respective marks in that spatial order. Thus the reading with $q$ before $p$ cannot return to the reading with $p$ before $q$. Response 2 gives failure at this precise old type of the subdivided context, and conservativity below degree three.

Conversely every generator of dimension at least two in the subdivision has the object poles of the original generator whose attachment produced it. A spatially composable pair of new generators would therefore give an original pair, by the reachability calculation. In its absence there are no elementary squares of generating steps; Response 2 gives the degree-three assertion. For a 3-computad the subdivision is again a 3-computad with no two spatially composable higher generators, so the all-dimensional cut theorem of Response 14 applies.

For a concrete change of first failing dimension, take one object $x$, a loop $a:x\to x$, and one 3-generator $1_a\to1_a$ on the identity 2-cell. The original has no generating 2-step, so it is conservative through dimension three for every Gray surface. Give $a$ length one. The 3-generator is spatially composable with itself, and each subdivision first fails in dimension three for every surface containing $I$. Thus new boundaries can strictly lower the first failing degree of an individual presentation even though (1) gives exact reflection on every old boundary. ∎

The unbounded binary unit and interchange cases left by the previous responses and the arbitrary-context insertion conjecture remain open.

**Validation.** The checker subdivided a computad containing a loop, a scalar, a path contraction, a 3-generator attached along a composite 2-cell, and a 4-endomorphism. At widths two through four it verified (2) at 27,014 generator/marker entries and tested it on composites and a whiskering. On generic discs of ranks one through three it constructed all nine return substitutions at their prescribed faces and typed both comparison directions. In the single-loop example it recovered the two width-two 3-rules from the raw declarations and checked preservation of the event order on 1,893 moves, including circuit insertion at empty windows. In a graph with overlapping attaching words a second check verified 3,855 moves at protected events, including rules whose windows span both sides of the square. Agda 2.8.0 independently checked the six width-two comparisons, the translated scalar and 3-attachment, and the forward square at the new boundary in both kernels. The argument with mapping spaces proves reflection in arbitrary dimension. [Subdivision checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_subdivision.py)

## Researcher Response 25

**There is a sharp dimension bound on the data over which one can impose *all* strict-image equations while retaining conservativity in arbitrary contexts.** The bound is **one for ordinary CaTT** and **two for Gray CaTT**, using the respective strict images. It bounds the dimension of the generators on which an equation is imposed. Its two terms, the type to be inhabited, and the additional generators of the ambient context can have arbitrarily high dimension. The conversion is closed under unrestricted substitution and congruence, including congruence in the type of a coherence head.

Precisely, fix $d\ge0$ and allow strict-image equality on data in any finite computad generated through dimension $d$. This identifies terms with the same strict cell, with equality of their types obtained recursively on the faces, and permits conversion in the resulting theory. One may also impose **unrestricted disc removal and generic endo-coherence removal**. In the latter rule the two generic faces of the head agree in the theory. This includes clause (ii) for every pair already identified by the word equations under consideration. The bound above is unchanged with these rules. For Q1 this proves conservativity whenever the nontrivial word equations used are generated on such data and hold in the respective strict image. In particular, an ordinary proof using units, associativity, interchange or insertion can use the entire strict calculation on graph data inside higher attachments. A Gray proof using units or associativity can do this on arbitrary 2-computad data, including a nonidentity directed square. Equations reversing that square do not hold in the Gray strict image.

### Replacing the lower skeleton while retaining the actual higher generators

Write $H_X$ for the free raw algebra on a computad, $C_X$ for its strict image regarded as a raw algebra, and $B=X_{\le d}$ for its generating $d$-skeleton. We prove the following more general assertion for either kernel and any $d$. **If $H_B\to C_B$ is a raw weak equivalence, the indicated conversion theory is conservative at every old type of $X$.** No hypothesis on the strict comparison for the whole of $X$ is needed.

In raw algebras form
$$
                 V_X=H_X\amalg_{H_B} C_B .
$$
The map $H_B\hookrightarrow H_X$ attaches the higher generators in order, using their specified raw boundaries. By left properness its pushout $H_X\to V_X$ is a weak equivalence under the hypothesis. Left properness here includes a possibly noncofibrant $C_B$. [Raw left properness for the two kernels, Response 1](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r6-2026-11-20.md#properness-and-comparison-of-model-structures)

The canonical $C_B\to V_X$ embeds a raw subalgebra and is bijective on cells through dimension $d$. Indeed adjoining a raw generator extends an algebra by relative terms. Keep the old elements as constants, retain the new variable, and retain a well-typed head expression with at least one new argument. Evaluate a head on an entirely old tuple by the old algebra operation. Evaluation and recursive extension of any assignment of the new generator give the free-attachment property, with the indicated constants distinct. For a head of output dimension $j$ the argument arity has dimension at most $j$ in either kernel. Thus adding generators above $d$ introduces no cells through $d$. The same description applies to cellular unions.

Use pairs consisting of a raw algebra $Y$ and such a distinguished strict subalgebra $C$, containing all cells through dimension $d$. A map of pairs acts strictly on the distinguished strict structures. A raw labelling of a globular arity $P$ in $Y$ extends canonically to a map of pairs $V_P\to Y$. Its lower labels determine the strict map $C_{P_{\le d}}\to C$ by freeness, and the other labels are the given raw ones. These extensions compose because their restrictions to the strict base and to the free generators determine them. The same argument works for a raw labelling of a computad with composite attachments: if it maps into $Y$, the attaching terms of low variables evaluate in the strict subalgebra.

For every globular pasting arity $P$, $H_{P_{\le d}}\to C_{P_{\le d}}$ is a trivial fibration. The truncation is a globular set of generators with variable faces. The positional lifting argument of Response 4 lifts any prescribed strict cell with prescribed raw boundary on a globular set: lift the boundary to the positional arity, lift the chosen strict cell there, then substitute back. In the Gray case the pullback in that argument fixes the chosen Gray value as well as its ordinary projection. Consequently $H_P\to V_P$ is a weak equivalence. Its composite to $C_P$ is arity evaluation, a trivial fibration by Response 3. Hence $V_P\to C_P$ is a weak equivalence surjective on objects and therefore a trivial fibration by the gaunt arity target test of that response.

### Interpreting conversion in a head type

We need a surface action on $V_X$, since a higher head may be formed at a converted type, even when its arguments include a new high-dimensional generator. On each $P$ use the preceding trivial fibration to choose a cell of $V_P$ at the already interpreted generic faces and the designated strict value. Use the same choice for the same arity, interpreted type and strict value. For equal faces and an identity value use the actual raw identity. On a disc at the interpreted type of its top variable, with that variable's strict value, take the variable. The two prescriptions are compatible since a positive-dimensional disc generator has distinct strict faces. Interpret an application by the canonical map of pairs out of $V_P$ determined by its evaluated labels. Use the same construction on every pair $Y,C$ as above. It is natural under maps of pairs and respects substitution; it can differ from the original raw action of $Y$.

Here is the simultaneous check on formation and conversion. At the generic arity the two interpreted faces have the original strict values after projection, by induction on boundary expressions. The strict model interprets conversion, so the designated cell exists there also for a surface-formed type. The chosen lift has the required raw faces in $V_P$. A labelling of a pasting arity needs only the displayed globular incidences, so its interpreted entries give a raw labelling in $Y$. On the distinguished base every new operation agrees with strict evaluation: on a tuple in $C$ the map from $V_P$ factors through $C_P$ by strict evaluation at that tuple. Therefore any labelling of a computad generated through $d$ lands entirely in the base, and *all* expressions on that labelling, of any dimension, have their strict values there. This verifies the generating strict-image equations including after substitution by converted terms. The identity and disc choices verify the other two rules at every arity. Congruent generic head types yield the very same choice, proving the required higher congruence. In particular the proof is stronger than merely checking equations on the original low-dimensional terms of $X$.

### Comparing the two raw actions while fixing the low dimensions

Write $Y'$ for $Y$ with the new action restricted to the raw syntax. We construct a span of raw maps $Y\leftarrow M(Y)\to Y'$ lifting at every prescribed globular boundary. This step matters because the tautological assignment of $X$ in the original $V_X$ need not typecheck for the changed operations.

Use the globe cylinders of Response 3 *relative to dimensions through $d$*. For $n\le d$ take $J_n=D_n$ with both endpoint maps the identity. Above $d$, let $J_{\partial n}$ be the union for the globular boundary and form $L_n$ by adjoining the two end discs along its two boundary maps. Factor the fold $L_n\to D_n$ as a cellular cofibration $L_n\hookrightarrow J_n$ followed by a trivial fibration. One can use only generating cofibrations in dimensions $>d$ in this factorization. The map to be factored is already an isomorphism through $d$, since the ends and boundary cylinders coincide there. The small object construction using the higher globe inclusions preserves that isomorphism by the relative-term description above, and hence gives the lifting property also for the omitted lower globes.

For a pasting arity or globular boundary, write $J_P$ for the union of these cylinders. Its generating $d$-skeleton is exactly $P_{\le d}$. The endpoint inclusions $H_P\to J_P$ and the one-ended inclusions $J_{\partial n}\amalg_{\partial D_n}D_n\to J_n$ are trivial cofibrations. In low dimension the latter are identities. Above $d$ the inclusion is a cofibration by adjoining the other end and using $L_n\hookrightarrow J_n$. Inductively the folds are weak equivalences on boundaries by cellular gluing, and on individual globes by factorization; the one-ended source folds by a weak equivalence as it is obtained by pushing out the boundary endpoint trivial cofibration. Gluing the one-ended inclusions gives the endpoint assertion for $P$.

Apply the strict base construction to $J_P$. With either endpoint, $V_{J_P}=J_P\amalg_{H_P}V_P$ because their generating low skeleta agree. Hence $V_P\to V_{J_P}$ is a pushout of a trivial cofibration. In particular the cylinder fold followed by strict evaluation $V_{J_P}\to C_P$ is a weak equivalence surjective on objects, again a trivial fibration.

Take $M(Y)_n=\operatorname{Hom}_{\rm raw}(J_n,Y)$. A tuple for a head over $P$ is a map $J_P\to Y$, which extends canonically to $V_{J_P}\to Y$ respecting the strict base. To give $M(Y)$ its raw operations, construct a map $J_n\to V_{J_P}$ for each raw head over $P$. In degrees through $d$ the old and new operations agree in the strict base. Above $d$, the previously constructed boundary operations give the map on $J_{\partial n}$; at the two ends prescribe the original and new generic operations at their respective endpoint tuples in $V_{J_P}$. They define a map on $L_n$. In $C_P$ prescribe the fold $J_n\to D_n$ followed by the strict head. The prescribed maps agree there: on the ends this is preservation of the strict value, and on each boundary cylinder it is the inductive lifting square. Lift along $L_n\hookrightarrow J_n$ against $V_{J_P}\to C_P$. Specialization by the extended tuple defines the operation. Iterated specializations agree by uniqueness of extension on the strict base and the raw generators. Thus both endpoint projections preserve all raw operations. Their prescribed-boundary lifts are exactly extensions along the one-ended trivial cofibrations into the fibrant raw algebra $Y$.

Now take $Y=V_X$ with its original operations. Lift the tautological raw assignment of $X$ to $M(Y)$ declaration by declaration and project to $Y'$. A surface inhabitant at an old boundary evaluates in the surface action there. Lift it at the already specified raw boundary in $M(Y)$ and project to $V_X$. Finally $H_X\to V_X$ is a weak equivalence between fibrant objects. For $\partial D_n\hookrightarrow D_n$ the derived restriction square into these two objects has equivalent homotopy fibres at the given boundary. Restriction in right simplicial frames is a Kan fibration, so nonempty homotopy fibre supplies an actual map on $D_n$ at that exact boundary in $H_X$. This gives the required raw term. ∎

### The two cutoffs and their sharpness

For ordinary $d=1$ every generating skeleton in question is a graph, so the positional lifting result used above applies to it, including graphs with cycles. For Gray $d=2$ the cubical fixed-hom comparison of Response 8 is a weak equivalence on each actual hom of every free 2-computad with its strict Gray hom. Strict evaluation fixes the objects, so it is a raw weak equivalence by the directed fixed-hom criterion. These are the hypotheses of the theorem for *every* $X$ in the respective dimensions. More locally, ordinary strict calculation on 2-dimensional data remains conservative in arbitrary higher extensions of a bubble-free 2-skeleton by its strict comparison from Response 22.

For ordinary $d=2$, take the raw diagonal Eckmann–Hilton braid and the identity on the double scalar from Response 1. Their ordinary strict values agree on the scalar 2-computad. The new equation converts an identity into an inhabitant of the comparison 4-type, which has no raw inhabitant. For Gray $d=3$ suspend the same example. Its equal strict Gray values now live in one hom, and Response 1 excludes a raw filler of the comparison 5-type. Both counterexamples use parallel raw endpoints. They apply for all larger $d$ by inclusion of equation sets. These sharpness examples concern the theory imposing **every** strict equality at the indicated input rank. Response 4 distinguishes the diagonal braid from the identity on the actual ordinary binary surfaces.

### Changing the raw writings of attaching types

There is a consequence for the **original**, untruncated surfaces of Q1 and Q2. Suppose two raw $(d+1)$-computads have isomorphic strict generating presentations, with the prescribed generator correspondence, and the comparisons of both $d$-skeleta with their strict images are weak equivalences. Then their first failing dimensions agree for each original surface, taking infinity for conservativity. This holds for arbitrary ordinary 2-computads and Gray 3-computads with the indicated correspondence. It allows simultaneous changes to the raw writings of the faces, even when the new and old faces have different raw types.

Indeed both raw algebras map by weak equivalences to the *same* relative algebra $V$: after the identification of strict bases the remaining generators are freely attached to identical types there. The two maps agree on the corresponding strict generator labels. Since both free algebras are cofibrant and all models fibrant, the zigzag is represented by a raw homotopy equivalence $f$ between them. Its inverse is also represented by a raw map. For each surface, substitution by $f$ preserves inhabitation of a type, and substitution by the inverse followed by the boundary path-lifting argument of Response 24 reflects it. Applying this in both directions to raw and surface inhabitation proves the claim for all types in either presentation. One can also transport arbitrary subsequent higher declarations along $f$, using their actual raw attaching maps. At each step the two new free models are related by pushing out the weak equivalence along the old globe attachment, hence again by a weak equivalence. This transports subsequent boundaries; prescribing independent changes to those boundaries requires a separate comparison.

In particular the complete classification of Response 4 extends to any finite computad whose strict generating presentation is globular, even if the actual faces use compound CaTT terms. Here globular means that in the respective strict image each immediate face has the value of the indicated single generating variable. Compare with the presentation on those literal variable faces, by induction on the skeleton. Its comparison to its strict image is a trivial fibration in every skeleton by the positional argument. If the two raw comparisons are weak equivalences through rank $j-1$, the common relative algebra just constructed in rank $j$ maps to the common strict image. Its composite from the literal globular presentation is that presentation's strict evaluation, a weak equivalence. Two-out-of-three gives the same conclusion for the other presentation. Induction and the homotopy equivalence argument transfer the classification. Thus ordinary CaTT is conservative there for all eight surfaces and for insertion. Gray CaTT is conservative exactly when $I$ is absent or there is no spatially separated pair of generating 2-cells.

There is a limit to replacing attaching types solely by their strict readings. On the scalar 2-computad let $E,J$ be the diagonal braid and the identity of Response 1. Attach a 4-generator at $E\to J$ in one presentation and at $J\to J$ in another. The strict generating presentations coincide. The first has the displayed raw comparison by declaration; the second retracts onto the scalar 2-computad by sending its generator to $1_J$, so that same comparison has no raw inhabitant there. Suspension gives the corresponding example with a 5-generator for the directed kernel. This explicitly distinguishes a chosen change of higher attaching data from transport along a raw equivalence.

**Validation.** On a graph folded to one object, two declared 2-cells meet at different bracketings of the same three-edge path. The checker constructed a raw paste using both generators once. It then transported a 3-generator between structural 2-cells to a type with a different raw 1-boundary, verifying the two required 2-face corrections and one occurrence of the 3-generator. On the directed square it transported a 4-generator to a boundary padded by a top unit. It also constructed both raw substitutions between a globular 3-computad and one in which the edges and a 2-face of its attaching types are unit-padded. Agda 2.8.0 accepted all six witnesses at their exact types in both kernels, and the Gray checker reduced inference for the reverse generic square head to failure. The rank cutoffs follow from the weak-equivalence and obstruction arguments, independently of these finite tests. [Skeletal base checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_skeletal_base.py)

## Researcher Response 26
