### A semistrict base equivalent to the raw 2-computad

Write $B$ for an arbitrary finite 2-computad. We need a raw weak equivalence out of $H_B$, the free ordinary raw model, into a model of the selected surface. Reflection of inhabitation alone, as in the statement of Response 10, would not suffice for adjoining the higher generators.

Apply the ordered tricategory construction of Response 5 to $H_B$. Its map $e^*H_B\to K(T(H_B))$ is a **globular trivial fibration** here. It is the identity through dimension two, surjects onto the classes of 3-cells at their specified faces, and lifts 4-cells at prescribed faces by the definition of the order. In every higher degree all parallel faces in $H_B$ have a filler by Response 10. The change of operations $e$ leaves that underlying globular set unchanged. Moreover the order on classes of 3-cells is discrete. The free realization of $B$ lies in $(\infty,2)$-categories, so a 4-cell, if it exists, has a reverse there. The raw realization comparison reflects existence of that reverse at the prescribed boundary. The antisymmetric quotient of reachability therefore identifies just the mutually reachable cells. The cylinder span of Response 3 connects $e^*H_B$ and $H_B$ by trivial fibrations.

Now use the virtually strict triequivalences $T(H_B)\leftarrow\widehat T\to G$ of Response 5. With the discrete order the associated $K$-models have only identities above dimension three. The maps of $K$-models are raw weak equivalences. Indeed the triequivalence test is essential surjectivity on objects, local biequivalence, and hence equivalence on the categories at fixed parallel 1-arrows. At fixed parallel 2-cells it is bijectivity on 3-cells. These are precisely the iterated essential-surjectivity tests for these raw models: the equivalences in the first two tests have their tricategorical inverse data, and in dimension three reversibility is invertibility. Top faithfulness ensures that pulling the discrete order back along the triequivalences gives the same order on parallel 3-cells.

For insertion take $Q=K(G)$ with the column order of Response 5. For the binary surface take its history model $Q=E(G)$. In the latter case the model of triples $(u,z,j)$ of that response projects by trivial fibrations to both $K(G)$ and $E(G)$: the two projections lift at the prescribed boundary by the conjugation equation in dimension three and equality in dimension four. There are only identities higher up. Thus in either case the raw reduct of $Q$ is connected to $H_B$ by weak equivalences, and $Q$ carries the indicated full surface action. Since $H_B$ is cofibrant and $Q$ fibrant, the isomorphism in the raw homotopy category is represented by an actual raw weak equivalence $w:H_B\to Q$. No functorial choice of strictification for all nonglobular $B$ is needed.

For a finite ambient computad $X$ take $B=X_{\le2}$ and form the raw pushout $Y=H_X\amalg_{H_B}Q$ along $w$. Left properness gives a weak equivalence $H_X\to Y$. The relative-term construction of Response 25 shows that $Q\hookrightarrow Y$ is a raw subalgebra and contains every cell through dimension two. The higher generators are attached at the images of their actual raw boundaries, which can include braid terms.

### Specialization on a globular arity

We spell out how to use this base when a head over a higher arity has a converted type. The base is a Gray-category model or a history model, so its comparison with the ordinary strict image of a general $B$ need not be faithful.

For a globular set $Z$ of generators through dimension two or three, let $G_Z$ be the free usual Gray-category on $Z$, with invertible interchangers. Use $Q(Z)=K(G_Z)$ for insertion. For the binary case use the free version of the history model in Response 5: take only the generating 2-cells for the atoms of the histories and the 3-cells of $G_Z$ between the normal representatives. In either construction every globular labelling of $Z$ in a base of the same kind specializes $Q(Z)$ to that base. For $K$ this is the free Gray functor. For histories substitute the labelled histories into the atoms, send each 3-generator to its specified 3-cell between normal representatives, and conjugate its evaluated pastes by the sorting comparisons at both ends. An exchange of atoms expands to the rectangle of exchanges of their histories; the two sorts of a twice-expanded history have the same comparison by the exchange coherence of Response 5. These specializations compose and preserve the raw and surface operations. This statement allows arbitrary history labels, including labels with several events.

The projection $Q(Z)\to C_Z$ to the ordinary free strict category is a globular trivial fibration. To verify the part needed at the top, decompose the hom of $G_Z$ into words in the object graph. For each word its factors are the free strict 2-categories on the shifted globular generators at the indicated pairs of objects. The word hom is their usual Gray tensor, by free enriched composition. In $C_Z$ it is their cartesian product. The tensor projection is locally fully faithful: at two specified parallel 1-arrows of the tensor it is bijective on 2-cells. It covers the 1-arrows at any given endpoints by composing the moves in the individual factors in order. Both properties iterate over a finite word (equivalently, use the cubical section and local full faithfulness at each binary tensor). This proves existence and uniqueness of a 3-cell at given 2-faces and given strict value, also in the history version by applying it to the two normal representatives. Every strict 2-cell lifts to a history at its 1-boundaries by spelling its successive generating steps with their outside whiskers. Objects and edge paths lift directly. The 3-cell uniqueness gives the prescribed-boundary lifting above dimension three, since both targets there have only identities. The free globular data retain the positions even when vertex or generator names repeat. [Bourke–Gurski, *The Gray tensor product via factorisation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1508.07789)

For each globular pasting arity $P$, put $Z=P_{\le2}$ and
$$
                          V_P=H_P\amalg_{H_Z}Q(Z).
$$
The map $H_Z\to Q(Z)$ uses the original generator tuple and the raw reduct of $Q(Z)$. Its composite to $C_Z$ is strict evaluation, a trivial fibration by the positional lifting theorem of Response 4. Two-out-of-three and the preceding paragraph make $H_Z\to Q(Z)$ a weak equivalence. Consequently $H_P\to V_P$ is a weak equivalence by left properness. The resulting $V_P\to C_P$ is a trivial fibration by the gaunt arity target test of Response 3. Any raw labelling $P\to Y$ specializes $V_P\to Y$ by using its low labels in $Q$ and the specialization just constructed on $Z$. Composition holds for these maps with their specified base specializations. This applies likewise to arity targets $V_{P'}$.

Use $V_P$ to choose an operation at its interpreted generic faces and its designated strict value as in Response 25. In dimensions at most two use the base operation itself: $P$ then has dimension at most two and $V_P=Q(P)$. In higher dimensions choose a lift, using the same choice on equal generic data. Prescribe the raw identity for a generic endomorphism with identity strict value, and the top variable for a disc at its standard interpreted type and value. Both prescriptions agree with the base action: in low degrees the identity has an empty history and the disc operation simply returns its label; in degree three use top faithfulness at the generic boundary. Higher identities in a base are unique. Applications use the specialization on inputs, so the choices respect substitution.

The new operation on $Y$ agrees with the **given base operation on every tuple in $Q$**, including at heads of degree greater than two. For degree three consider the universal labelling in $Q(P)$, since such a head has arity of dimension at most three. It gives a raw map $V_P\to Q(P)$ by the base specialization on $Z$ and the raw interpretation of the generic variables. The chosen lift and the desired base operation now have identical interpreted 2-faces by the low-degree prescription. Their ordinary strict 3-values agree by construction. The top faithfulness just proved makes them equal in $Q(P)$. Specialize along the given tuple; composition of the base specializations proves the assertion there. In higher degrees the restriction of any chosen lift to a base tuple lies in the base by the pushout specialization into $Q$, and equality follows inductively from its boundary and thinness. These arguments apply to surface-formed generic types, by simultaneous induction on formation and conversion. In particular all allowed low-data equations hold: all entries of their interpreted context lie in $Q$, and evaluation there agrees with its full surface action. Equality of interpreted generic data makes congruence in a higher head type sound as well.

For completeness, the relative cylinder of Response 25 compares this new action with the original raw one. Take $J_n=D_n$ through degree two with identical ends. Above two use the relative cellular factorization of that response, so $J_P$ still has low generating skeleton $Z$. Here $V_{J_P}=J_P\amalg_{H_Z}Q(Z)=J_P\amalg_{H_P}V_P$ for either end. Hence $V_P\to V_{J_P}$ is a trivial cofibration and $V_{J_P}\to C_P$ a trivial fibration. A label $J_P\to Y$ extends by exactly the same free specialization on $Z$. The two ends of a raw head and its already constructed boundary on the cylinders now lift against $V_{J_P}\to C_P$. In degrees at most two the two operations agree in the base. This gives the two raw projections from the algebra of cylinder labels, each lifting at prescribed globular boundaries by the one-ended trivial cofibrations. Lift the assignment of $X$ into that span, evaluate the proposed surface filler, and return at its lifted old faces. This supplies a filler in $Y$ with its original raw action. The weak equivalence $H_X\to Y$ reflects existence at the specified boundary by the mapping-space argument of Response 25. ∎

The same proof applies with a 3-skeleton $B$ in a particular ambient context if $H_B$ is raw weakly equivalent to a base $Q$ of the indicated kind with only identities above three. Use $Z=P_{\le3}$ throughout and fix the relative cylinders through degree three. The tensor calculation gives the required comparison of $Q(Z)$ with $C_Z$, and agreement of operations on the base now follows directly through degree three and by thinness above. One sufficient test for the hypothesis is that every raw 4-cell in $H_B$ admits a reverse and every raw type of dimension at least five there is inhabited: the ordered truncation and the same span then supply $Q$. This is a conditional statement at rank three; the unconditional bound proved here for the actual surfaces is rank two.

### Why the two conclusions cannot be combined

In fact **published strict units plus just the single word equality between the two readings of the ordinary binary interchange square fail in dimension four on one scalar**. Write $R(a,b),L(a,b)$ for the two readings in Rice's order and $s(a,b)$ for the raw head $R\to L$ over the generic two-bigon arity. The strict-unit normalizer gives the following three equalities in the two-scalar context:
$$
 E(a,b)\equiv_{su}s(a,b),\qquad
 R(a,b)\equiv_{su}a*_1 b,\qquad
 L(a,b)\equiv_{su}b*_1 a .
$$
Here $E$ is Rice's raw two-variable Eckmann–Hilton term from Response 1. The equalities are conversions, as witnessed by the well-typed identities in the checker linked below. Pruning, disc removal and endo-coherence removal are the published strict-unit rules used in this calculation. [Rice’s raw term](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/blob/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc/examples/eh.catt) · [The two square readings](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/blob/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc/examples/eh-common.catt)

Once $R=L$ is imposed at the generic square, congruence changes the head type of $s$ to $R\to R$ and published endo-coherence removal gives $s=1_R$. Equivalently, the binary clause (ii) for this same square gives $s=1_R$ directly. Specialize $b=a$ and use the displayed strict-unit equalities. The result is $E(a,a)=1_{a*_1a}$. Conversion of the identity supplies a 4-cell at this exact old type. The two-colour argument of Response 1 excludes either raw orientation: the diagonal braid exchanges the two positions and the identity fixes them. The two sets of low-data judgements alone already suffice, since the generic square has generating rank two and all three strict-unit judgements have generating rank two.

Both sets of equations are sound in the ordinary strict image, so Known 2 gives conservativity through dimension three even for the combined theory. Contexts with at most one declaration are empty or a point; full strict-image conversion is conservative there. Thus four is the least failing output degree and two is the least possible number of declarations for the combined theory. The least input rank at which allowing the two families together can fail is exactly two, since Response 25 covers their joint strict equations on graph data. For the individual binary surface the positional model of Response 4 still separates the diagonal braid from the identity. For the individual published theory its normal forms separate them. This is why the two different choices of base in the affirmative theorem matter.

Consequently there is a law-by-law classification for the joint calculation on planar data. Choose either published equality and any binary subset $S$. Close their judgements on 2-dimensional data under conversion, congruence and substitution in arbitrary contexts. This is universally conservative **if and only if $I\notin S$**. In the affirmative cases both families are contained in published insertion: pruning is its identity-insertion case, and it contains the associative and unital binary surface by the Setting. The insertion base above therefore interprets even the planar consequences derived using both families together. In the negative cases the single square and the strict-unit judgements already give the scalar counterexample. This also gives the same negative answer for the joint theories with no input restriction whenever $I\in S$.

**Validation.** In a scalar context with an additional 5-generator, the checker changed its attaching boundary using a square followed by its reverse. It transported the generator, corrected both 4-faces over the generic square, folded the two labels to the same scalar, and checked that the resulting 5-cell has the prescribed raw type and uses that generator once. Agda 2.8.0 checked this cell and the folded raw swap. Rice's checker at the linked revision accepted the three two-variable conversion witnesses under each of `--su` and `--sua`; in separate runs both modes rejected the proposed diagonal identity conversion. The extra single-square equality in the negative result is the mathematical use of the additional law. [Planar base checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_planar_base.py)

## Researcher Response 27

**For either published equality, every surface cell over a globular set of generators has a raw representative at any prescribed raw boundary representing its faces. The representative has the specified surface normal form.** This holds in every dimension, with repeated labels, endomorphisms and cycles in the generating globular set. In particular it applies simultaneously to a whole surface substitution. For the published theories, identity-class comparisons between pairs of raw terms suffice for Q1 in an arbitrary finite context. For **every binary surface in both kernels** as well as the published theories, any failure in degree $n$ already has a witness supported on one strict paste, with each $n$-generator used exactly once.

### Fitting a specified surface cell to an old boundary

Fix ordinary CaTT, a raw context $X$, and $R=su$ or $sua$. Write $N_R$ for the unique normal form. Let $p_X:H_X\to U F_R X$ send each raw cell to its surface class, where the right side has the underlying raw operations of the free surface model on the same declarations. A globular lifting problem for $p_X$ specifies a surface cell and a parallel pair of raw cells over its faces. Fix $n\ge1$. Then these two conditions are equivalent.

1. Every such problem through degree $n$ has a raw lift, with object lifts included.
2. For every $k<n$ and every ordered pair of literal parallel raw $k$-terms $a,b$ in $X$ with $N_R(a)=N_R(b)$, there is a raw cell $h:a\to b$ with $N_R(h)=N_R(1_a)$.

In degree zero objects are variables. For necessity, lift the surface identity at the two specified faces. For sufficiency, first observe how to fit a raw term $r$ whose surface class is the desired one to a prescribed old type. Work up its boundary starting at dimension zero. At stage $k$, the current and prescribed $k$-source have the same surface class, and are raw-parallel since all lower faces already agree literally. The same holds at the target. Use condition 2 in the two required orientations to obtain a cell from the prescribed source to the current source and a cell from the current target to the prescribed target. Paste these on the two sides of $r$ at cut $k$, using the standard three-factor padding composite. Its $k$-faces are now the prescribed ones, and every lower face stays fixed. In the surface the two comparisons are identities. Pruning the two identity arguments and evaluating the resulting disc gives exactly the previous surface cell. This is the calculation in Rice's proof for $P_{k+1}$ and works for any labels with these types and identity classes. For $k=0$ the endpoints already agree as variables, so the stage can also be skipped. After the last cut the term has the required literal type. [Rice, §4.5.1, Definitions 4.5.4–6 and proof of (4)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.17068#page=194)

It remains to produce the initial $r$, since a surface head can itself have a converted generic type. Induct on the expression for the surface term, proving lifting at *any* specified compatible raw boundary. A variable starts with its declaration. For a head on a pasting arity $P$, apply Rice's Theorem 4.5.3 to the surface head at the identity labelling of $P$. It supplies a raw term over $P$ in that surface class, possibly at a different raw writing of its type. Lift the actual input substitution one entry at a time in declaration order. For an entry its required old type is the type in $P$ evaluated on the raw entries already chosen. This represents its surface type because those entries have the specified classes. The induction applies to the entry, a proper argument expression, also when its dimension equals that of the enclosing term. The arity dimension bounds used here follow from fullness. At an operation the output degree is at least the tree height. At a coherence its boundary already visits the whole tree. Substitution into the generic raw representative produces $r$. Fitting it as above completes the induction. Conversion of its assigned type does not change the expression or its surface class. Thus the reduction uses Rice's theorem only on the generic arity, with no assumption about the support of a term in $X$. [Rice, Theorem 4.5.3 and §4.5.2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.17068#page=194)

In particular, if published insertion first supplies a new inhabitant in degree $n$ in some context $X$, condition 2 fails there at some $k<n$. Condition 2 prescribes the normal form of the comparison between that pair. Conversely a failure of condition 2 alone detects failure to represent a *specified* class. Inhabitation in Q1 permits every class at the boundary.

### Separating occurrences before taking a normal form

Let $Z$ now be a globular set of generators, so its attaching faces are actual generating variables. Denote its free ordinary strict category by $FZ$. We use two properties of a globular relabelling $f:Y\to Z$, even when it identifies generators.

First, normalization of a well-typed surface term commutes with $f$. The relabelling acts on argument variables and leaves every bound arity and its generic head type fixed. In the reduction system a disc is recognized on the generic arity and an endo-coherence on its generic type. Pruning recognizes an identity head in an argument. Standard insertion recognizes an identity or standard composite there and tests the heights of the branches of the two trees. None tests equality of two ambient labels. Inspection of the head and induction on its arguments, including substitutions, show that a normal expression stays normal. Relabelling its reduction sequence gives the same reduct at each corresponding position because the exterior substitution in insertion is defined on the trees. Uniqueness of normal forms proves the assertion. In particular two *different* ambient labels becoming equal does not create a generic endo-coherence redex. [Finster–Rice–Vicary, §4.1 and Theorems 40–43](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303#page=21) · [Strict-unit reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.08307)

Second, two well-typed surface expressions over $Y$ that relabel to the **same syntax** and have the same lifted ordinary strict value must be the same syntax (using bound positions for arity variables). For a variable this is the cartesian unit of the free strict category monad. At a head, both expressions have the identical generic head. Its ordinary strict positional shape is the whole arity, including for an identity on a full-support cell. This holds with converted head boundaries as well because ordinary strict evaluation respects conversion and full support determines the visited subtree. The strict argument labelling downstairs is read from the common expression. This labelling and the given lifted cell determine the strict values of *each* argument upstairs by the cartesian multiplication of the free strict category monad, just as in Response 4. Induction on the proper argument expressions proves equality, without an existence assumption about lifting a surface derivation. Together the two properties show that two surface terms of equal strict value upstairs are $R$-equal whenever their relabellings are $R$-equal, by syntax uniqueness applied to their normal forms. [Positional lifting and support](#researcher-response-4) · [Leinster, Appendix F](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0305049)

Take literal parallel raw $u,v$ over $Z$ with the same $R$-normal form. Their common ordinary strict value consists of a finite positional pasting shape $P$, its generic cell $c$ (identity-padded to the given degree), and a globular labelling $f:P\to Z$. By raw positional lifting in Response 4, lift $u$ and $v$ along $f$ with strict value $c$. The lifts $\bar u,\bar v$ are literally parallel because their faces lift the same old face with the same strict value, hence agree by uniqueness. Both terms have full support in $P$ by Response 9. The preceding reflection argument gives $\bar u\equiv_R\bar v$ **in $P$**. Therefore the raw coherence on $P$ from $\bar u$ to $\bar v$ is accepted, and congruence in its generic type followed by endo-coherence removal makes it $R$-equal to $1_{\bar u}$. Relabel it back to $Z$. This proves condition 2 in every dimension and also gives the other orientation by exchanging the terms. It is essential to perform endo-coherence removal before specializing the generic head. The same construction on a point handles dimension zero.

It follows that $p_Z$ is a globular trivial fibration. For an assignment of any raw computad’s generators to surface cells over $Z$, lift each entry at the boundary determined by its predecessors. The argument also works relative to a previously lifted subcomputad. It preserves each entry's surface normal form. Furthermore, the square for $UF_R(-)\to F(-)$ at any globular relabelling is a pullback on cells. For existence, first lift the given downstairs surface cell to a raw representative, lifting its globe of faces in order. Lift that representative at the given upstairs strict value by Response 4 and take its surface class. Uniqueness is the reflection argument above. In particular identifying labels loses no additional information once their positions in the strict cell are specified.

### Localization to the support of one strict paste, for both kernels

For either kernel and any of its binary surfaces, or for either published ordinary theory, let $Z$ be a dependency-closed subcomputad of $X$. Then the square
$$
\begin{array}{ccc}
 U F_S Z &\longrightarrow& U F_S X\\
 \downarrow &&\downarrow\\
 FZ &\longrightarrow& FX
\end{array}
$$
is a pullback on cells, where both lower entries are **ordinary** free strict categories, including in the Gray case. The bottom map is an inclusion because every strict equality preserves closed generator support by the union calculation of Response 9. A strict expression supported in $Z$ is an expression there, and the strict laws relating such expressions restrict there, since their composite factors and their faces all have support in $Z$. We call such a strict cell supported in $Z$.

Here is a proof of the upper inclusion that does not require a normal-form theorem for the binary laws. First, the support $Q$ of an ordinary strict cell of a globular pasting arity $P$ is itself a pasting arity and has a retraction from $P$ by raw words. Indeed its vertices are a closed subinterval of the spine of $P$. In each traversed column its support is the suspension of the support of the component strict cell in that child. This is the positional description in Response 4, including a point for the support of an identity on a vertex. Clamp the spine to the subinterval. In a column outside it send each arrow to the identity on its clamped vertex and each higher generator to the iterated identity there. In a traversed column suspend the recursively constructed child retraction. The assignments fit at the common spine vertices. They use only variables and their iterated identities and fix every variable in $Q$. This proves the word retraction in both kernels, with no equational laws imposed.

Construct a surface model above $FX$ by induction on cell dimension. Over a strict cell supported in $Z$ take the cells of $UF_S Z$ with that strict value, with their existing faces. Over any other strict cell take exactly one cell for each compatible choice of lifted boundary (one choice for a vertex). All the surface laws hold in the ordinary strict target. For a head with a specified labelling, if its output strict value is supported in $Z$, **all** its input labels have strict values supported there. Indeed its generic ordinary strict cell visits every slot, and the support of the labelled paste is the union of the supports of those labels. This is the full-arity property in Response 4, also for the ordinary projection of a directed total. At a converted generic boundary strict interpretation still has the designated total or full identity. Use the given surface operation in $UF_S Z$ on such a tuple. It has the already interpreted faces, by induction on their expressions. Otherwise use the unique lift at the interpreted boundary. These definitions compose with substitution by evaluating on the resulting tuple. At congruent head types the designated strict values agree and so do the already interpreted faces. In the supported case one uses head congruence of the given surface algebra.

An equation between two binary words can involve *unused* variables of its ambient arity. If their common strict value on a tuple is supported in $Z$, restrict the tuple to the support $Q$ of the common generic ordinary strict cell. All entries needed there are supported in $Z$ by the strict substitution formula. The two words involve only $Q$, by raw support reflection. Substitution of the word retraction in the generic equality in the free word algebra proves their word law over $Q$. Thus evaluation on the restricted tuple gives equal cells of $UF_S Z$. For clause (ii) the coherence uses the entire arity and the same argument uses the whole tuple. For the published rules use the disc, the generic endo-coherence, or the single spliced arity of an insertion (the pruned arity for an identity insertion). In the last case the right-hand head uses all labels of that arity and the left-hand side uses their exterior and interior substitutions. Both therefore agree by the rule in $UF_S Z$. In all cases, at an output strict value outside $FZ$, equal strict values and equal lifted faces suffice by uniqueness. The faces in clause (ii) use the word equality just verified. These checks give the simultaneous interpretation of formation, equality and conversion.

Interpret the generators of $X$ in this model, taking those in $Z$ to their own classes, and each remaining generator to the lift of its strict generator at its interpreted boundary. On $UF_S Z$ the composite of weakening and this interpretation is the inclusion of the supported cells, so weakening is injective. Conversely any surface term over $X$ with a supported strict value is in its image. For a variable this is the support test. At a head all argument cells are supported, so each has an antecedent by induction on the expression. Their antecedents agree on the required boundaries, since weakening is injective and the generic arity has globular incidences. Apply the same surface head to those antecedents. Its weakening is the original term class. This proves the pullback assertion.

Consequently, for a specified surface class at an old boundary, the existence of a raw representative of that class is exactly the same in $X$ and in the support of its strict paste. Its raw faces belong to this subcomputad by Response 9. Any raw representative has the same strict value and hence strengthens there. The upper inclusion reflects equality of classes, and weakening proves the converse. A failure to represent a *specified class* persists under every extension by fresh declarations, even one that introduces another inhabitant of the same old type. For published units or insertion, a class with globular attaching faces throughout its support always lifts by the preceding theorem, regardless of the rest of $X$. For ordinary binary surfaces, a putative failure of *inhabitation* also restricts to this support, since any raw filler of any class there would weaken to $X$. By Response 4, that support cannot be a globular computad.

### Reducing possible counterexamples to terms linear in their top generators

For every surface just considered there is a further reduction in an arbitrary lower-dimensional context. Fix $n>0$ and any raw $(n-1)$-skeleton. Form extensions by $n$-generators with specified old attaching types. An occurrence below refers to a free $n$-variable in the argument expression, counting repetitions. There are none in the inferred boundary or in an argument of smaller dimension. Distinct occurrences can therefore be replaced independently by new $n$-generators with the same literal declarations, preserving raw typing for a raw term and surface typing for a surface term. To see this after localization to the support, first send every used generator to a designated first copy by raw substitution. This supplies the original typing derivation on the larger list of copies. Change the chosen occurrence in each argument recursively. Its replacement variable has the identical raw declared type. Every required entry type and every inferred result type uses ambient terms only below degree $n$, so these types and the conversions between them stay literally the same. The generic head has its own bound context and stays fixed.

For the two published theories, normalization transports these occurrences bijectively. At an $n$-head of arity dimension less than $n$ there are no $n$-occurrences. Otherwise they are exactly the disjoint lists from its $n$-slots. Disc removal carries the top argument once. An endo-coherence head of degree $n$ uses a full $(n-1)$-face and so has no $n$-slot. At an insertion the enlarged tree retains each outside top slot once and the replacement branch contributes each top slot of its inserted tree once. This follows directly by splicing the child lists at the branching path. Shared faces of the branch have dimension below its leaf and cannot duplicate a top slot. The inserted tree has no $n$-slots for a lower-dimensional entry or for an identity in degree $n$. This includes pruning. Reducing a generic head type affects none of the argument occurrences. Structural reductions compose the bijections inside the affected argument. When all occurrences receive fresh generator names before normalization, every reduction uses the same tree tests and carries each fresh name to exactly one place. Uniqueness of the resulting coloured normal form makes the matching independent of a choice of reduction sequence. The argument requires no restrictions on the attaching types in the fixed skeleton. [Insertion labelling and exterior substitution, Definitions 21–23](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303#page=17)

This gives two tests relevant to the unrestricted question.

- Universal **inhabitation** conservativity in degree $n$, for each binary surface in each kernel and each published theory, is equivalent to its restriction to proposed surface witnesses in which each declared $n$-generator occurs exactly once, and whose strict support is the whole context. Indeed localize a failing witness and its old boundary as above. Copy each top generator once per occurrence, with the same lower-dimensional type. Label the witness by these distinct copies and take its support. The old boundary is unchanged since it lies in the lower skeleton. A raw filler of any class in the new context would give one in the original context by the raw substitution folding the copies. For a published theory the witness may also be required to be in normal form, since giving each occurrence its own name leaves a normal expression normal. For a binary surface no normalization is used. This deduction also covers witnesses with no $n$-generators.
- To test condition 2 **universally** in rank $k>0$, it suffices to test raw parallel pairs in which each $k$-generator occurs once on each side and occupies the same position in their common coloured normal form. Give each occurrence in the common uncoloured normal form its own fresh declaration and pull the names back along the occurrence bijections for the two raw terms. The resulting raw pair has unchanged lower faces and equal coloured normal forms. An identity-class comparison for the coloured pair specializes to one for the original pair. One can also pass to the support throughout. Thus if Q1 fails in degree $n$ for published insertion there is an identity-class obstruction of this form in some rank $k<n$ (degree zero has no obstruction). An arbitrary-context proof of this linear identity-class test in all ranks would prove the stronger rehydration statement and hence Q1 for insertion.

### Which equality ranks can account for a first failure?

Fix $k\ge1$. In either kernel allow **every** equation between raw-parallel $k$-terms over a pasting arity having the same strict value in that kernel. Take their substitution and congruence closure, with ordinary conversion, leaving all term equalities below $k$ literal. In every finite context the resulting theory is conservative through dimension $k+1$. Thus, in a failure in dimension $n\ge2$ of any ordinary binary surface, of published insertion, or of an interchange-free Gray surface, one cannot give a derivation using only strict-sound generic conversions at the last boundary rank $n-1$ while keeping all lower ranks raw. Here a generic conversion has parallel raw sides *before* substitution. A lower equality used inside a generic head counts at its own rank. The condition for the directed result uses the directed strict image.

In this fragment all terms through degree $k$ are raw and their types have their literal raw boundaries. We first prove that a derived equality between $k$-terms supplies raw $(k+1)$-cells in both directions. At a generating equation use strict arity lifting at the two given faces, with the strict identity as the desired value. If the arity has unused variables, first restrict to the support of the common ordinary strict value, using the word retraction above. This is an arity of dimension at most $k$, and applying the retraction preserves the equality of the respective strict values. Its labelling is raw. Substituting the two generic lifts supplies the comparisons in the ambient context.

Two-way raw reachability is closed under replacing $k$-dimensional arguments of a raw $k$-head. To see why, vary one $k$-slot of its arity by a single directed $(k+1)$-cell. The slot is maximal. Raising it replaces its generator by two parallel copies joined by a new generator and leaves the other maximal labels fixed. This is a pasting arity with two copies of the original labelling, and the two copies of the head have identical literal boundaries. In the ordinary strict image its $k$-paste can be lifted to a $(k+1)$-paste from the first copy to the second. Carry the new generator through the paste, whiskering by identities on stationary factors, or evaluate the top-dimensional strict composite one slot at a time. For the directed kernel, when $k\ge2$ a $k$-head with a $k$-slot must be a total. The column containing that slot already contributes $k-1$ to the tensor degree, leaving every other column stationary. After the raise its column is still the only active factor. The same ordinary strict pasting calculation works inside this fixed hom, with exterior arrow whiskers. For $k=1$ it is simply a path with one arrow raised and the others stationary. Arity lifting gives a raw cell at the required two head expressions, and substitution gives the claimed move. A reverse input supplies the other orientation by the same construction. All remaining degree-$k$ congruences follow by expression induction, since a generic head type of that degree has lower faces. Compose moves for transitivity and reverse their order for symmetry. Raw substitution preserves both witnesses. [Raised pasting arities and their two typed inclusions](../../../src/CaTTExp/PsRaise.agda)

Finally consider a surface $(k+1)$-term at an old type. Its generic head, if any, is already raw because its boundary consists of raw $k$-terms whose typing tests use only lower-dimensional faces. A conversion between two writings of that boundary can change its $k$-faces, but each individual admissible head uses the original side condition. In its input tuple keep all entries through degree $k$ at their original literal expressions. For each $(k+1)$-entry induct on the proper argument expression to obtain a raw representative **at its assigned raw boundary**. There are no higher entries. The earlier $(k+1)$-choices cannot change the type of any later entry since they do not occur in those types. Apply the raw head to this tuple, or start with the declared variable in the variable case. Its $k$-faces are equal in the fragment to the specified ones and their lower faces are literally the same. Precompose and postcompose with the raw comparisons just proved to fit the old type. At this last rank only existence of the comparisons is needed. This argument does not require the comparison cells to reduce to identities. Equations in degrees strictly above $k$ that presuppose equality of their types add no typing tests for terms through degree $k+1$, since converting such a type uses term equality only up to its $k$-faces.

### What happens at a single higher attachment

The published comparison $p_Z$ on globular data can lose prescribed-boundary lifting when a single generator is freely adjoined **in raw algebras**. Take $Z=(x,f:x\to x)$ and put $v=1_x *_0 f$. In both published theories $p_Z(v)=p_Z(f)$. Form the pushout of $p_Z$ along the raw extension adding $a:f\to f$. The target is the raw algebra $U F_R Z[a]$ with one freely adjoined 2-cell. It has the cell $a$ at the images of the raw faces $v,f$. This particular cell has no raw lift at those faces. Indeed in the relative-term description an expression on entirely old arguments evaluates in the base, and a coherence containing the new generator remains headed by its coherence. Only the variable $a$ itself can map to the adjoined variable. Its literal source in the free raw algebra is $f$, distinct from $v$. The pushout is nevertheless a weak equivalence by left properness. For the actual surface extension $U F_R(Z,a)$, the left unitor $v\to f$ followed by $a$ does have the required raw type and has surface class $a$. Thus the distinction already occurs in degree two, even when both the initial and final generating contexts are globular. A proof of Q1 by induction over arbitrary attachments would have to account for the surface equations on cells using the new generator. ∎

**Validation.** An independent search found variable-and-identity retractions for all 258 distinct cell supports across the pasting trees with up to four edges. Agda 2.8.0 checked all 258 substitutions in each kernel. Over $(x,f:x\to x,a:f\to f)$ Rice’s weak checker accepted the raw 2- and 3-cell representatives at their prescribed unit-padded faces and the transported 2-generator of the attachment example. In each of `--su` and `--sua` the checker verified seven class equalities, including both identity-valued boundary corrections and the ordered occurrences of two generators at the composite attaching path $ff$. It distinguished the two folded temporal readings, the two orders of the fresh labels, and the fresh 3-generator from the existing swap at its boundary. Both Agda kernels also checked a raised input slot of a unit-padded binary operation at each of ranks one through four. The reductions and lifting properties in unbounded dimension are proved above. ## Researcher Response 28

**Exact rehydration through dimension three.** Let $X$ be any finite ordinary CaTT computad. For either published equality $R\in\{su,sua\}$, every surface cell of dimension at most three has a raw representative at *any prescribed literal raw boundary* representing its faces. The representative has the specified $R$-class. In particular this holds for a 3-generator attached to composite 2-cells, even when fitting the generator changes the writings of paths shared by adjacent 2-cells. The new assertion needed for this result is $EL_R(2)$ in arbitrary $X$.

### Moving the ends of a raw 2-cell

Write $G=X_{\le1}$ for the graph of objects and generating arrows. A *correction* from $p$ to $q$ is a raw 2-cell in $G$ with surface class $1_{[p]}$. It exists whenever $[p]=[q]$. Indeed the support theorem in Response 27 reflects this equality into $G$, and exact rehydration there supplies the cell. For raw 2-cells $A,B$ at the same literal endpoints write $A\sim B$ if there are raw 3-cells in both directions of surface identity class. This is an equivalence relation by identities and vertical composition. Any two corrections with the same ends are related, by exact rehydration on the graph in degree three. In particular an out-and-back composite of corrections is related to the literal identity. [Globular lifting and support reflection](#researcher-response-27)

For $A:p\to q$, corrections $l:p'\to p$ and $r:q\to q'$ give the unbiased vertical composite $[l,A,r]:p'\to q'$. Its surface class is $[A]$ by pruning and disc removal. We need three facts about this change of endpoints.

1. A raw 2-head preserves $\sim$ in any one of its 2-dimensional inputs if the other inputs are fixed. Raise that maximal slot of its tree to two parallel slots joined by a 3-generator. The two copies of the original head give its source and target operations. They have literally the same exterior paths, and their supports are the two boundary copies of the tree. The resulting raw 3-head can be labelled by the input comparison. In the surface, replace that label by its equal identity, prune the raised branch, and observe that the two copies then coincide on the remaining tree. Endo-coherence removal gives the required identity class. Use the reverse input for the reverse comparison. This also proves preservation under composites of top-slot moves. [Raised arities and inclusions](../../../src/CaTTExp/PsRaise.agda)
2. Changing either correction to any other correction with the same ends preserves the $\sim$-class of the result, by the graph assertion and fact 1. Changing $A$ within its class for $\sim$ does as well.
3. These changes of endpoints compose on $\sim$-classes. Over the generic string of five 2-slots compare the two raw readings $[l',[l,A,r],r']$ and $[l' * l,A,r * r']$, where $*$ is binary vertical composition in temporal order. Their equal literal exterior paths and full supports give raw 3-coherences in both directions. Specialize the four corrections. Pruning their identity classes collapses both readings to the central slot on the remaining disc, so both comparisons have identity class by endo-coherence removal. Changing endpoints by literal identities is the identity on classes: over the disc the parallel terms $[1,A,1]$ and $A$ have equal surface classes, so Response 27 there gives both comparisons. Facts 1 and 2 now show that changing endpoints and changing them back are inverse on classes.

### Simultaneous changes at shared paths

Let $H$ be any raw 2-head on a pasting arity $P$. Suppose two raw labellings $\sigma,\tau$ agree on object labels, give surface-equal labels at each arrow, and give related 2-labels after changing their endpoints as above. Then the two outputs of $H$ are also related after changing their endpoints. All endpoint changes in this statement may use arbitrary corrections.

If $P$ has height at most one, both outputs and their fitted versions live on $G$. Head congruence and the identity classes of the corrections give equal surface classes there, so Response 27 supplies the comparisons. Otherwise $P$ is a row of columns. Each column is a vertical string of 2-slots or a stationary arrow. Write $S,T$ for the raw source and target expressions of $H$ on the initial and final paths. They can include arbitrary raw operations on those paths.

First expand each stationary column to one 2-slot. Use its initial arrow in $S$ and its final arrow in $T$. The new paths have full boundary support, hence define a raw head. At identity labels in the added slots its literal faces are the old ones and pruning gives $H$ in the surface. Over the old generic arity Response 27 therefore compares the two readings in both directions with identity class. Substitute each of the actual labellings into these comparisons. The two new identity labels across the labellings satisfy the hypothesis since they and all their corrections live in $G$. Thus it suffices to prove the assertion for nonempty columns.

In one such column let $A_i:p_{i-1}\to p_i$ be the labels of $\sigma$, $1\le i\le m$. At the corresponding seams $q_i$ of $\tau$ choose corrections $l_i:q_i\to p_i$ and $r_i:p_i\to q_i$. By facts 1 and 2 we may use $[l_{i-1},A_i,r_i]$ for each label of $\tau$. Make a fresh ordinary pasting tree $E$ by putting the following string in each column, with each symbol in the display initially an independent generic slot:
$$
 (l_0,A_1,r_1),\ (l_1,A_2,r_2),\ \ldots,\ (l_{m-1},A_m,r_m).
$$
The two exterior arrows of an interior pair $r_i,l_i$ are distinct in $E$. They specialize to the same $p_i$. This distinction ensures that the following comparison is a legal coherence over a pasting arity.

One reading of $E$ applies $H$ to the consecutive triples. Another is $[L,H(\rho),D]$. In each column the slots of $\rho$ read $A_i*(r_i*l_i)$ for $i<m$ and $A_m$ for the last. For $L$ take the tree with one 2-slot per column, with $S$ evaluated at the initial and final paths as its two faces, and label its slots by the first connectors $l_0$ of all the columns. They have precisely the paths required to reach the source of $H(\rho)$. Construct $D$ from the last connectors $r_m$ in the same way with $T$. Both heads satisfy fullness since $S,T$ visit their respective exterior paths. After specializing the connectors, $L,D$ are graph corrections: pruning all their slots leaves equal generic faces and hence gives identities.

The two readings of $E$ have the same *literal* exterior paths, use every slot, and therefore admit raw 3-coherences in both directions over $E$. To determine their specialized classes, first represent the connector labels by their equal surface identities. Pruning the corresponding slots reduces $E$ to the original tree of the $A_i$. Every triple then reduces to its centre, every interior right unit reduces to $A_i$, and the exterior $L,D$ reduce to identities. Thus the two generic faces agree in $R$ on the remaining tree, and endo-coherence removal gives identity class for the specialized 3-cells. This argument works for strict units alone. Finally, each specialized detour $r_i*l_i$ is related to $1_{p_i}$ in $G$. Fact 1 and the raw comparison $A_i*1\sim A_i$ from the generic disc allow its removal inside $H(\rho)$ and inside the exterior triple. We obtain $[L,H(\sigma),D]$. Independence of the exterior corrections follows from fact 2. This proves the assertion for every finite row, including repeated actual labels.

### Interpreting the surface equalities

Form a structure $M(X)$ through degree two. Its objects are those of $X$ and its arrows are the surface arrow classes. Choose one raw writing of each arrow. A 2-cell between two such arrows is a raw 2-cell at their chosen writings modulo $\sim$. Facts 1–3 identify these sets bijectively with the corresponding sets at any other writings. Projection $\pi_X$ to the surface takes the class of the raw cell. It is well defined and invariant under changes of endpoints.

Evaluate a 2-head in $M(X)$ by choosing one raw writing at each arrow slot, fitting a representative of each input 2-cell to its incident writings, applying the raw head, and fitting the output to the chosen exterior writings. The simultaneous-change lemma proves independence of all the choices, including choices at internal seams. The result projects to the surface operation. Every labelling of a pasting arity through height two is realized in this procedure by one literal raw tuple: choose its arrow labels first, then choose each 2-label at exactly those endpoints. Any raw substitution between computads acts on these structures by substitution followed by fitting. It carries graph corrections to graph corrections and $\sim$-witnesses to witnesses. Fact 3 proves functoriality. It commutes with head evaluation because one may use the substituted literal tuple to evaluate the head in the target.

Here is why this interpretation respects surface conversion. At an arity $P$, $\pi_P$ is bijective through degree two. Response 27 gives surjectivity at the chosen writings and supplies both identity-class 3-cells for injectivity. Consequently generic equations valid in the surface are valid in $M(P)$, since the operations just constructed commute with projection. In degree two even a surface head uses raw 1-expressions at its two faces: the typing of those paths tests only object equalities, and the arity side condition is unchanged. For head-type congruence use the equation on that very arity. For disc and endo removal use the arity of the rule. For pruning or insertion use the pruned or spliced arity: its exterior and interior substitutions give the two sides from the same tuple. Every labelling of this arity is realized by a literal raw substitution, along which the generic equation specializes by the preceding naturality. If a premise first identifies an argument with an identity or a composite, apply congruence to the interpreted premise first. These checks give the simultaneous induction for formation, substitution and equality. No equality of higher degree enters a typing or equality premise at these ranks. [Pruning and insertion rules and their substitutions](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303#page=17)

Interpret each generator through rank two by its own class, fitted at the chosen writings. By induction every raw 2-expression evaluates to its own $\sim$-class fitted at those writings, since its literal input tuple is among the permitted choices. Now let $u,v$ be literal parallel raw 2-cells with the same surface class. Their interpreted values coincide by soundness. Fit both values back to their original common endpoints. Fact 3 gives $u\sim v$. This proves $EL_R(2)$. The lower tests hold on the graph, so the exact-boundary criterion of Response 27 with $n=3$ gives the theorem. By choosing representatives in declaration order the same argument lifts a whole surface assignment through degree three, relative to any already lifted subcomputad. In particular, changing a family of 3-generator attachments to surface-equal writings over a fixed 2-base gives raw substitutions in both directions inducing the specified surface identification and fixing the base. ∎

A first failure of exact rehydration in arbitrary ordinary contexts for either published theory must now have degree at least four. A failure of Q1 for published insertion would require the coloured identity-class obstruction in Response 27 at some rank $k\ge3$. The comparison above treats paths shared by 2-slots. Shared 2-faces in a 3-dimensional arity require a higher comparison and are outside this proof.

**Validation.** Rice's raw checker accepts both orientations of the expanded-tree comparison with two columns, including a stationary column and nonstandard exterior paths. After independently changing the writing of the shared arrow between two generating 2-cells, it accepts a representative of a specified 3-generator at the prescribed raw faces. It rejects direct use of that generator there. Under each published equality it verifies the two rectangle classes, interior cancellation, stationary-column expansion, a raised-slot comparison, and the class of the fitted generator. It rejects the identity in place of that last class. The general statement follows from the tree and interpretation arguments above. ## Researcher Response 29

**Gray interchange at one scalar fails in every global dimension $d\ge4$.** More generally, a spatially composable string of $k\ge3$ generating 2-cells gives a failing old $(k+1)$-type for every binary surface containing $I$, provided there is no $(k+1)$-generator. All the entries of the string may be the same. Thus every finite extension of the scalar still fails in **every** dimension at least four above its declaration bound. There is also a finite one-object example with first failure exactly $d$ for every $d\ge3$.

The point requiring proof is the case of even $k$ with repeated labels. In the scalar strict hom the two facet sums of an even cube agree after recording only the starting position of each facet. We distinguish them by lifting the actual higher pastes with their individual occurrences coloured. The lift also gives a boundary reflection result for arbitrary pastes on a cube.

### Lifting a folded pasting expression

Use local dimensions in the strict hom of the Gray scalar context. Write $C$ for this hom, $a$ for its generating 1-loop, $Q_m$ for the directed tensor $m$-cube, and $F:Q_m\to C$ for its characteristic map. The free polygraph of $C$ has one object and one generator $g_r$ for each $r\ge1$. Its attaching diagram is the boundary of the $r$-cube, sending each face atom to the generator of that face's rank, with the order of the free coordinates inherited. This is the cubical word presentation of Responses 7–8 applied to the empty edge path and the one scalar. In particular relations between pasted expressions in this presentation are the strict category axioms. [Cubical presentation](#researcher-response-8) · [Characteristic cube in the scalar hom](#researcher-response-13)

Fix $m$ colours, totally ordered. Make a second free cubical polygraph $E_m$. A vertex is a subset $B$ of the colours. A generating $r$-cube is a pair $(B;(c_1,\ldots,c_r))$ with distinct colours outside $B$, in any order. A lower facet deletes its coordinate from the list. An upper facet also adjoins the deleted colour to $B$. These face maps satisfy the cubical identities by deletion and union, and gluing the ordinary directed cubes by these faces gives the indicated polygraph. Forgetting the colours gives $E_m\to C$. The cubes with increasing lists alone give the usual presentation of $Q_m$: $B$ records the fixed upper bits, the listed axes are free, and the remaining bits are zero.

A coloured 1-path over $a^h$ consists of its starting subset and an ordered sequence of $h$ distinct fresh colours. **Every cell of $C$ of dimension at least two has a unique lift to $E_m$ upon giving a coloured lift of its iterated 1-source.** Here the coloured 1-target is an output of the construction. For a generating $g_r$ the iterated 1-source of its characteristic cube executes the coordinates in reverse list order, and the target executes them in list order. Indeed the tensor boundary formula gives the source edge in coordinate $i$ with exactly the coordinates to its right completed, and the target edge with exactly the coordinates to its left completed. Thus the coloured 1-source determines the whole generating cube, including $B$. For a square the effect is to interchange the two adjacent colours.

For detail on passing from generators to cells, lift a pasted expression recursively. At cut zero split the given input path at the two factors' lengths. The base subset at the split includes the colours of the first factor. At cut one use the first factor's output order as the second factor's input. At any higher cut both factors start at the same coloured 1-path. Their common gluing face lifts identically by the lower-dimensional assertion. Identities lift to identities, allowing identity padding of lower-dimensional factors before a cut. These operations preserve the strict defining equations. For associativity at cut zero both sides split at the same two positions, and at cut one both pass the successive output orders along the three factors. At higher cuts all factors receive the same input. For exchange involving cut zero the two subwords keep their sets of colours under every higher operation, and passing the reordered subwords to the next factors is the same as passing their concatenation. For exchange involving cut one and a higher cut the two factors meeting at that higher cut have the same 1-target, since they share a face of degree at least one with that target (for cut two the common 2-face has that target). For two cuts above one the input paths stay fixed throughout. Thus the same factors with the same coloured inputs occur on either side and strict exchange applies upstairs. Units split off an empty word or pass the given order unchanged at the applicable cut. This proves independence of expression by induction on dimension and the strict equations; when proving it at a given dimension the lifted boundaries have already been computed in lower dimensions. Conversely, lifting the expression obtained by forgetting any coloured expression recovers it, by the rule for generators and recursion on its cuts. This gives uniqueness, including for a paste with whiskers at several cuts.

### Why an arbitrary return must use increasing cubes

To a coloured 2-cell assign the number $b$ of squares whose axis list is in decreasing order. Equivalently sum their coefficients in its positive top chain. This count is defined on strict cells, adds under the two cuts of 2-cells, and is zero on identities of 1-paths. For a generating $r$-cell with $r\ge3$ the two iterated 2-faces have the **same** count, namely the inversion number of its axis list. In the characteristic cube each of these faces uses one square for each pair of distinct axes, with their induced order. To verify this, read either 2-face as a path of adjacent swaps from the reverse coordinate order to the forward order. In the generic cube each square exchanges a descending adjacent pair into ascending order. Each pair must exchange once to reach the final order and cannot exchange again. Passing from coordinate indices to their colour names gives the formula for any list. A generating square itself has $b=0$ exactly when its list is increasing.

It follows that in any cell of degree at least three the two iterated 2-faces have equal $b$. For a composite below cut two the counts add. At cut two the target of the first and the source of the second are the shared 2-cell, and above cut two both factors have the same exterior 2-faces. Identities preserve the assertion. This induction gives more: **if the 2-source has $b=0$, every generating cube in any expression for the cell has increasing list.** At the low cuts use nonnegativity to deduce zero in each factor. At cut two use equality of the two counts on the first factor to pass zero across the join. The higher cuts preserve it directly. The generator calculation and the zero count of a 2-paste finish the induction, including whiskers of lower-dimensional cells.

Consequently $F$ reflects the existence of strict fillers at every specified parallel pair of $Q_m$-cells of dimension $j\ge2$. Given an alleged $(j+1)$-cell between their folded images, colour its input using the common 1-source of the specified faces in $Q_m$. Their own expressions already lift with the given colours, so uniqueness identifies the two lifted $j$-faces with those expressions in $E_m$. The source has $b=0$ at dimension two (at $j=2$ use the source face itself). The lifted expression therefore uses only the cube generators of $Q_m$. It also has the prescribed faces *in* $Q_m$. To see this last point directly, an equality of coloured expressions at such faces is a sequence of strict equations and their substitutions. Each intermediate expression denotes the same coloured cell and has zero count at degree two, so uses only increasing cubes by the preceding induction. At a 1-face the given ordered path already determines its edges. Thus each equality of faces is a valid equality in the increasing-cube presentation. The converse follows by folding. In particular, for raw boundaries of local degree $j\ge2$ across the poles of the arity consisting of $m$ consecutive intervals, specializing all intervals to one scalar reflects **raw** inhabitation of their comparison. A raw scalar filler gives a strict folded filler, and the reflected strict cube filler lifts at the original raw faces by directed arity lifting. [Directed lifting on a pasting arity](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r9-2026-11-20.md#a-directed-interchange-theory)

### The reverse boundary and its consequences for Q2

Take $m=k\ge3$. In $Q_k$ the positive top cell has no directed return from its target facet paste to its source facet paste. Indeed there is only one generating $k$-cell. The coefficient of its upper facet in coordinate one changes by $+1$ from source to target by the signed tensor differential. Any directed $k$-paste has nonnegative top chain, and its boundary therefore changes this coefficient by a nonnegative integer. The reverse requires $-1$. The two faces have degree $k-1\ge2$, so the reflection result excludes a return after folding *all* axes to the scalar. The dimension condition matters: for $k=2$ the two 1-paths fold to the same word $a^2$ and its identity supplies a strict return. An initial colouring for a 2-cell determines its output order, which the folded word alone does not prescribe.

Now take the two raw $k$-faces of Response 13. In the generic $k$-cube their strict images are precisely the two facet pastes: substituting the directed square for the first 2-disc carries its top chain to the single cube generator and its lower rows to the corresponding faces. The binary surface with $I$ supplies a reverse at these raw faces by specializing that square to the surface identity. Folding to the one-scalar context preserves the surface derivation, and the preceding strict obstruction excludes a raw reverse. The same argument works on the two-skeleton of any spatially composable string of $k$ generating 2-cells. Absorb intervening nonempty paths by right whiskering, then map the two-skeleton strictly to the scalar, sending every generating edge to the identity and every generating 2-cell to the scalar. The images of the two specified faces are the folded cube faces, also when generators or intervening paths repeat. [Raw faces, square substitution and degeneration](#researcher-response-13)

In an extension without a $(k+1)$-generator a proposed raw return has ordinary strict value an identity on its source, which is supported on the two-skeleton. Support reflection strengthens it to that two-skeleton, giving the contradiction. The same conclusion holds if **every** $(k+1)$-generator has ordinary strict source containing a generator outside the two-skeleton, or if every such generator has that property at its target. Indeed the first generating step of a nonidentity top paste would have its source support inside the support of the original source. At the other end use the last step. These are the support and top factorization arguments of Response 9, now applied in rank $k+1$. [Ordinary strict support and strengthening](#researcher-response-9)

In particular a repeatable generating 2-cell in a finite context forces failure for each $d\ge4$ for which there is no $d$-generator, including all $d$ above the declaration bound. On the scalar 2-computad itself the exact set of failing degrees for each $I\in S$ is therefore $\{4,5,6,\ldots\}$, using the degree-three return calculation of Response 7. For prescribed first failure $d\ge4$ take the one-object truncation of the suspended coherent walking equivalence from Response 13. Its discrete length projection and the degree bound on raw arguments give conservativity through $d-1$ there. Its forward 2-generator is repeatable and there is no $d$-generator, so failure occurs at $d$ for both parities. For $d=3$ a single vertex, one loop $f$, and one 2-endomorphism of $f$ give the nonscalar separated pair of Response 7. ∎

**Validation.** The checker read the actual signed 2-face tableaux through cube rank eight as sequences of adjacent swaps. Across all permutations of the colours it verified 184,910 hemisphere readings, including the output order, the ordered pair crossed at every step, and the zero-count criterion. At rank four an independent enumeration of all 16 reduced adjacent-swap readings recovered the two four-step routes of braid moves and matched their individual facets to the two signed tableaux. The checker also verified the forward and reverse facet signs in ranks three through eight, 2,880 whiskered braid relations and 4,320 exchanges of disjoint swaps. The all-rank obstruction uses the lift of arbitrary strict pastes proved above.
