## Researcher Response 30

**An exact test for higher attachments at the Gray cube obstruction.** Let $B$ be any finite Gray 2-computad, $k\ge4$, and $d=k+1$. Adjoin any finite list of $d$-generators to $B$ at arbitrary raw boundaries. Declarations strictly above $d$ may follow. A *bare chart* is a spatial string of $k$ generating 2-cells with a specified edge path in each of the $k-1$ gaps and empty exterior paths. Its characteristic cube in the appropriate strict hom is $g:Q_k\to C_B$. Write $g^-,g^+$ for its two strict facet pastes, in local dimension $k-1$. Choose the parallel raw faces of Response 13 at these strict values.

Then **the old reverse type has a raw inhabitant if and only if a single declared $d$-generator has strict source $g^+$ and strict target $g^-$** in that fixed hom. Its raw attaching terms may be different writings of those strict faces. Every surface containing $I$ inhabits the reverse. In particular the least number of $d$-generators needed to supply raw returns for *all* bare $k$-charts is exactly the number of those charts. This includes the possibility that no finite list suffices. On one object with $s$ scalar generators the number is $s^k$, with the order and repetitions both counted.

### The parallel faces of the directed cube

There is precisely one unordered pair of distinct parallel $(k-1)$-cells in $Q_k$, the two faces of its top atom. To see this use the nonnegative chain tableaux of the directed cube. In either row of degree $j$, for a specified $j$-element set $J$ of free coordinate directions there is at most one basis term with those directions, counted with multiplicity. Project onto $Q_J$ by collapsing the complementary intervals to the tensor unit. Those terms, and only those, contribute to its unique top coefficient. That coefficient is at most one by Steiner's multiplicity theorem. The two rows of degree $j<k$ of the top atom itself each have one term for every such $J$, and their two terms are distinct. Indeed the tensor atom formula chooses the free interval edge at the positions of $J$. At each omitted position take the endpoint with the row's sign reversed once for each preceding free edge. Exchanging the sign changes every omitted bit. This also gives the two endpoint vertices in degree zero. [Steiner, Example 3.10, Theorem 6.2(iii) and Proposition 7.2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0403237)

For parallel $(k-1)$-cells the difference of top chains is a cycle. The cellular chain complex of the cube is contractible, so this difference is an integer multiple of the signed boundary of its sole $k$-generator. A zero difference gives equal tableaux since the lower rows are shared. For a nonzero difference, the projection bound just proved forces the multiple to be $+1$ or $-1$ and the two top chains to be precisely the two hemisphere chains. Their common lower rows are forced as well. At each descent the difference of the next two rows is the differential of the known row. For each subset of the lower degree the two terms of the standard atom there are distinct and exhaust the permitted coefficient on each side. Thus that differential already determines both rows. Descend to the vertices. This proves the assertion also for cells with arbitrary whiskers.

### A finite comparison in a labelled cube

We first describe the test with an arbitrary number $m$ of axes, still using $d=k+1$ as the degree of the new declarations. Choose a characteristic $m$-cube $g:Q_m\to C_B(p,q)$, allowing exterior stationary paths. Fix two parallel $(k-1)$-cells $u,v$ of $Q_m$. There is a finite test for existence of a strict $k$-cell between $g(u),g(v)$ after attachment, and for raw inhabitation at prescribed parallel old faces with those values.

Before attachment the fixed hom has the free cubical presentation of Response 8. An $r$-atom is a spatial string of $r$ contextual generating rewrites, with paths between and outside them. Through local degree $k$, the enlarged strict fixed hom freely adds just the declared $d$-generators with stationary exterior spatial paths, at their specified strict boundaries. In the free enriched word filtration any new slot contributes local dimension $k$. A second such slot, or another positive-dimensional generator tensored with it, contributes only above $k$. In the one-slot stage the remaining zero-dimensional labels are precisely the two exterior edge paths. The tensor of the globular boundary inclusion with the other word factors attaches the new tensor generators at their boundaries, so adds no relation between lower pastes. This argument uses the strict filtration only. It works when the two attaching terms are arbitrary composites. [Word filtration and cubical homs](#researcher-response-8) · [Gray tensor of polygraphs](https://webusers.imj-prg.fr/~yves.guiraud/articles/polybook.pdf)

On the finite set of $(k-1)$-cells of $Q_m$ allow identity comparisons and its native $k$-atoms. Allow also the following generating moves. For a contextual new generator $z$, take each pair $a,b$ of parallel $(k-1)$-cells in $Q_m$ with $g(a)=s z$ and $g(b)=t z$, and allow $a\to b$. Close these pairs under composition at the top cut and under whiskering at all lower cuts by cells of $Q_m$. All sets used in this closure are finite by the tableau coefficient bound, and can be computed by closing the cube atoms under the cuts through degree $k-1$. The exterior paths to check for $z$ are finite as well: they are prefixes and suffixes of the starting spatial path of its candidate image. Equality of the resulting pastes in the free strict hom is decidable by free polygraph equality, using only the finitely many contextual generators in the expressions under comparison. [Effective strict comparison](#researcher-response-18)

Every move evaluates to a strict comparison. For the converse, colour the $m$ occurrences in the input chart by their positions as in Response 29. A cell in the old cubical hom lifts on giving its 1-source this colouring. The coloured cubical presentation keeps both the original chart and the ordered list of its active colours, recording the subset of completed colours at each vertex. The source executes the coordinates in reverse list order, fixing the lift of each generating atom. Gluing along cuts and independence of strict equations are exactly the input-path lifting calculation of Response 29: a square exchanges the colours and the two cubical 2-faces of any higher atom give the same output order.

The construction extends through degree $k$ by adjoining a coloured copy of each contextual new generator for each admissible colouring of its input history. Its two faces lift as old cubical terms. They are parallel upstairs since their common $(k-2)$-faces have a unique lift with the specified 1-source and $k-2\ge2$. This is where the restriction $k\ge4$ enters; two parallel uncoloured 2-faces could induce different output permutations. By freeness the same expression-lifting argument now applies to a paste involving the new generators. The number of decreasing squares agrees on the iterated 2-source and 2-target of every old higher atom. The same is true of each new atom since its 2-boundaries are already those of its old source. Consequently zero count on the requested boundary forces every old subexpression, including the faces of any new step used, to have increasing axes. This follows down each cut by nonnegativity and equality of the two counts, as in Response 29. [Occurrence lifting and the zero-count test](#researcher-response-29)

We must also recover the concrete edge paths and generator positions. Suppose that a coloured old expression begins on a 1-path of the chosen chart $g$ and uses increasing axes. It is the $g$-image of its cubical reading. For a generating step, its complete contextual 1-source determines its spatial chart uniquely: read the rewrites from right to left. Each next rewrite is to the left of those already executed. Its prefix in the current path is therefore the original prefix, and its position and source word delimit its original segment. The unused segments give the gaps and exterior paths. This includes zero-length rewrites at the same vertex, since their temporal order specifies their order in the list. Thus the atom on the chosen axes starting at the given path must be the indicated face of $g$. For a composite, split the input path at cut zero, pass its output to the next factor at cut one, and keep its input at higher cuts. The same calculation works recursively for a new step: its two faces are old expressions beginning at the given path, and so give precisely a pair $a,b$ allowed above. Starting at the specified input of $u$ proves that the entire paste gives a comparison in the finite closure. More explicitly, the reading on old expressions is the coloured lift followed by forgetting the spatial data. The reading commutes with every cut. At the boundary it recovers $u$ and $v$ by uniqueness of the coloured lifts, even if $g$ identifies their uncoloured lower cells. Hence the closure decides strict existence at exactly this pair.

For raw existence, the comparison from the raw fixed hom over $B$ to $C_B(p,q)$ is a weak equivalence by Response 8 and is surjective on objects since every edge path has a raw writing. Its target has only identity coinductive equivalences. In fact count the local $j$-generators in a $j$-paste for any $j\ge1$. Both $j$-faces of every cubical $(j+1)$-generator have count $j+1$, one facet for each omitted coordinate on each side. Thus every $(j+1)$-comparison preserves the count of its $j$-faces. A $j$-cell invertible up to higher comparison must have count zero by composing with its inverse, hence is an identity. The gaunt-target test of Response 3 now makes the old comparison a globular trivial fibration. In particular it lifts a strict identity between *any* prescribed parallel old raw faces with equal values. [Gaunt-target lifting](#researcher-response-3) · [Raw fixed-hom comparison for a 2-computad](#researcher-response-8)

Any strict $k$-paste in the enlarged hom lifts at prescribed raw boundary in the sense of inhabitation. Every cell below $k$ still belongs to the base. For a base term use its trivial fibration. For a contextual new generator, whisker its raw declaration by writings of the stationary paths, omitting empty whiskers if desired. Its strict value is the specified contextual generator. Fit its lower faces to any prescribed old writings in ascending order of dimension by pre- and postcomposing with identity-image comparisons from the base. At each cut the lower faces already agree literally, so the two corrections exist there; their strict images are identities and preserve the strict value. Local objects with equal strict value but distinct raw writings use the same comparison in local degree one. For a strict binary cut choose raw writings of its faces and shared seam in increasing dimension using base lifting, then realize both factors at those interfaces and apply the raw hom composite. Fit the exterior faces by the same comparisons. This is an induction on the strict paste, all fitting cells lying in the base even at the last cut. The result realizes the desired direction at the original raw faces. A raw cell conversely gives a strict one by evaluation. Higher declarations cannot enter a term of global degree $d$ by the argument-degree bound.

### Why one declaration is necessary

For $m=k$ apply the finite comparison to $u=\partial^+Q_k$ and $v=\partial^-Q_k$. A positive native $k$-atom changes the coefficient of its first upper facet by $+1$. The required change is $-1$. Whiskering adds the same $(k-1)$-chain on both sides at any cut below $k-1$, and top composition adds differences. Thus some allowed new generating move has negative difference at that facet. Its two $(k-1)$-cells in $Q_k$ are parallel and distinct. By the parallel-face result they must be exactly $u,v$ in that order. The contextual new generator giving this move consequently has strict source $g^+$ and target $g^-$. Conversely a generator with those faces gives the move directly, and the raw fitting argument supplies the return at the prescribed writings. This also proves the finite comparison test in this case reduces to inspecting the individual attaching faces.

In a bare chart the exterior stationary whiskers of a matching declaration must both be empty. The leftmost event of the descending 1-source acts at prefix length zero; a nonempty left whisker would precede it. The rightmost event acts with empty suffix, ruling out a nonempty right whisker. The 1-source then reconstructs the entire bare chart by the right-to-left calculation, so one declaration can supply the required move for at most one chart. One declaration at each reverse raw type suffices by direct use. This proves the exact count, including the scalar formula. For finite $B$ it is infinite precisely when some gap of a composable $k$-tuple admits paths passing through a directed cycle. In a finite graph the paths for fixed endpoints are finite exactly when their traversable subgraph is acyclic. There are only finitely many generator tuples, and the gaps can be chosen independently. Thus a cycle in an interior gap can force failure in a *fixed* degree $d$ after any finite family of attachments in that degree, even when those attachments have arbitrary strict sources and targets. Every unrepaired reverse remains surface-inhabited by the square substitution of Response 13. ∎

**Validation.** At local rank four the checker generated all 520 cells through degree three of the directed 4-cube at the 3-cell level. Among ordered parallel pairs exactly the two orientations of the hemisphere pair had different top chains. Across all 256 choices of requested and supplied four-letter words on two scalars, contextual reachability returned exactly the matching words. On the 5-cube it generated 11,344 cells at the same level, found 296 contextual forward moves, and detected 32 reverse moves using all five occurrences after one four-letter attachment. A separate reconstruction check ran all 18,432 contextual readings for four positions with stationary gaps, including coincident empty rewrites, insertions and contractions. The proof covers arbitrary raw attaching terms and all $k\ge4$. [Finite cube and attachment checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_scalar_repairs.py)

## Researcher Response 31

**In ordinary CaTT, if the first generator above the 2-skeleton has degree $h\ge4$, all eight binary surfaces and both published surfaces are conservative through degree $h+1$.** In particular they are conservative through dimension five in every finite ordinary context without 3-generators. The 2-skeleton can have scalar braids, and 4- and 5-generators can have arbitrary raw attaching terms. The equations used in the surface derivation are unrestricted. More generally, the same conclusion holds if the raw 3-skeleton admits a raw weak equivalence into a Gray-category model for insertion, or a history model for the binary laws, as specified in Response 26. In particular it holds for the respective surface if every old type of dimension at least five in the 3-skeleton is inhabited and every raw 4-cell there has a reverse.

There is an independent sharp bound for **full ordinary strict-image conversion**. For $r\ge1$, if the comparison of the generating $r$-skeleton of $X$ with its ordinary strict image is a raw weak equivalence, strict-image conversion is conservative over ordinary CaTT in $X$ through degree $r+2$. Failure at $r+3$ is possible for each $r$. Thus an ordinary Q1 failure in degree $n\ge3$ requires the raw-to-strict comparison to fail to be a weak equivalence already on the $(n-2)$-skeleton of its support. The degree-five semistrict assertion also works on a scalar 2-skeleton whose ordinary strict comparison fails. For Gray surfaces omitting $I$, the directed version below gives degree five even on a positive 3-skeleton with unrestricted higher attachments.

### Retaining the direction of the next attachments

We use a model which retains the given operations through degree $r$, then remembers which way one can go between cells in degree $r+1$. The direction matters since the context can contain $(r+2)$-generators. Write $H_X$ for the free raw algebra. In the semistrict case take $r=3$. Let $B=X_{\le3}$ and let $H_B\to Q$ be the indicated weak equivalence, with $Q$ carrying the chosen surface action. If $X$ has no 3-generators, $B=X_{\le2}$ and Response 26 supplies such a map for either choice of surface, including on an arbitrary nonglobular 2-computad. For the strict case take $B=X_{\le r}$ and $Q=C_B$, its ordinary strict image. In raw algebras put
$$
                  Y=H_X\amalg_{H_B}Q .
$$
Left properness gives a raw weak equivalence $H_X\to Y$. The relative-term description of a raw attachment embeds $Q$ in $Y$ and identifies every cell through degree $r$ with a cell of $Q$, since all new generators have greater degree. The raw operations on these low cells are the given reduct of the selected action on $Q$. [Base change and relative terms](#researcher-response-25) · [Semistrict planar bases, including the sufficient condition on a 3-skeleton](#researcher-response-26)

For a globular pasting arity $P$, put $Z=P_{\le r}$. Use $Q(Z)=C_Z$ in the strict case. For $r=3$ use the free ordinary Gray-category model on $Z$ in the insertion case, or the free history model on $Z$ in the binary case. We use three facts about these bases. A labelling of $Z$ in a base of the same kind induces a specialization preserving its surface operations, these specializations compose, and $Q(Z)\to C_Z$ lifts strict cells at prescribed boundaries. For the free Gray and history models these are the specialization, tensor and top faithfulness results of Response 26, valid on globular generators through degree three. For strict categories they follow directly from freeness and the identity map. Set
$$
                  V_P=H_P\amalg_{H_Z}Q(Z).
$$
The composite $H_Z\to Q(Z)\to C_Z$ is raw strict evaluation on globular generators, a trivial fibration. Two-out-of-three and left properness make $H_P\to V_P$ a weak equivalence. Its composite to $C_P$ is arity evaluation, so $V_P\to C_P$ is a weak equivalence surjective on objects. It is a trivial fibration by the gaunt-target test. A literal raw tuple on $P$ in $Y$ extends to a raw map $V_P\to Y$ using the specialization on $Z$. The same holds for a tuple in another $V_{P'}$. Successive specializations agree on the distinguished base and on each remaining generator, hence on the whole pushout. [Generic base specializations](#researcher-response-26) · [Arity lifting and the gaunt-target test](#researcher-response-3)

The following construction applies to $Y$ and to each $V_P$ with its distinguished base. Keep the cells and base operations through degree $r$. On raw $(r+1)$-cells at any fixed literal boundary put $a\le b$ if there is a raw $(r+2)$-cell from $a$ to $b$. This is a preorder by identity and top composition. Take the quotient by mutual reachability for the cells in degree $r+1$. In degree $r+2$ take a single cell between two parallel classes if the first is below the second. Such a cell can be represented by a raw witness at *any* chosen representatives of its faces, by composing at the two ends. Above this degree use identities. Call the resulting globular set $M(Y)$, and likewise write $M(V_P)$.

### Operations at the two new degrees

At degree $r+1$ the two generic faces of a surface head over $P$ are already interpreted literally in $Q(Z)$. They project to the two faces of the designated ordinary strict value in $C_P$. Choose a raw lift of that value in $V_P$ at those faces. For an actual labelling choose a raw representative for each $(r+1)$-slot. The lower labels are already literal, so these choices give a raw tuple with the required incidences. Specialize the lift and take its mutual-reachability class. Two choices of generic lift compare both ways in $V_P$ by lifting the strict identity at their prescribed faces. Specialization preserves the comparisons.

Changing the representative in an actual top slot preserves the answer as well. Raise this maximal $(r+1)$-slot of $P$ to two slots joined by one $(r+2)$-generator. The two inclusions specialize $V_P$ into the corresponding enlarged $V$. They agree on the base through degree $r$, so the two images of the operation have the same literal boundary. In the ordinary strict category the two evaluations of its designated paste are joined by carrying the new generator through the paste, with stationary factors whiskered by identities. This follows from strict composition one slot at a time, and includes the identity comparison if the slot is unused. Lift that strict comparison in the enlarged $V$ at the two given raw faces and specialize it along the actual change of representative. A reverse input gives the other direction. Applying this to one slot after another proves independence of all representatives. Naturality under a raw specialization preserving the base follows by using the image of the original tuple as the choice of representatives. [Raised slot and strict paste comparison](#researcher-response-27)

For clarity, the $(r+1)$-classes in $M(V_P)$ still have well-defined **strict values in $C_P$**, even when the arity contains higher slots. Two mutually reachable cells there project to the same strict cell. Indeed a directed round trip has nonnegative top chain with zero differential. No such nonzero chain exists on a globular pasting tree: order the signed generating faces by a strong Steiner order and give successively larger positive weights so the boundary of every generating step has positive weight difference. Thus the two comparison arrows in the strict category must be identities. Evaluation of a chosen $(r+1)$-operation commutes with strict projection there. This follows on substitution since the free base specializations commute with ordinary strict projection (in the history model this forgets event order). Hence an expression evaluated on the generic labelling has its prescribed strict value in this degree as well.

At degree $r+2$ evaluate the generic $(r+1)$-faces just constructed in $M(V_P)$. Choose representatives of their classes. Their lower faces already agree literally by parallelism in $Q(Z)$, and their strict values are the indicated faces of the strict head. The trivial fibration $V_P\to C_P$ supplies a raw $(r+2)$-cell there with those faces. In an actual tuple choose raw representatives of the $(r+1)$-labels and then choose witnesses for each $(r+2)$-label at its now prescribed representatives. This is possible by the definition of the quotient order. The extended raw specialization takes the generic witness to a witness between the required classes, using the preceding naturality for its faces. This defines the unique answer at those classes. At greater degrees parallel cells coincide, giving the required identities. All choices above work also when the generic type or the actual tuple was formed using conversion.

Here is the verification of the surface equations. Through degree $r$ they hold in the base. For a rule of degree $r+1$ compare its two generic evaluations over the arity carrying its labelling. These are parallel at literal base faces by the lower equations, and have the same ordinary strict value by strict soundness. Their two raw representatives in $V_P$ therefore compare both ways by lifting the strict identity. They define the same class. Specialize this assertion along the raw tuple realizing the actual labelling. For the binary word equation or its coherence equation use its stated arity. For insertion use the spliced tree, whose exterior and interior substitutions yield the two sides; for pruning use the pruned tree. Disc and endo removal are checked on their own arities, using the interpreted premise in the endo case. An equal argument first put in the required form is handled by congruence. For congruence in a generic head type use its own arity, where equal interpreted faces and the same strict value give the same operation class; the side condition continues to designate the same strict cell, since ordinary strict evaluation respects conversion. This verifies formation, substitution and equality together. At degree $r+2$ and above, equality follows from the boundaries by uniqueness. In the strict case the same check applies to the operations of the ordinary free strict category monad: a composite paste and its flattened tree have the same strict value over the flattened arity, and disc substitution gives the variable. These unit and substitution equations give an ordinary strict category on $M(Y)$, with every raw head interpreted by its designated strict paste. Thus it interprets full strict-image conversion, including equalities at an ambient presentation. [Insertion and exterior substitutions](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303#page=17)

There is a direct comparison with the *original raw operations* through degree $r+2$. In the low degrees both actions are the given base action. For an original raw head of degree $r+1$, its raw generic value in $V_P$ is one of the permissible lifts at those literal faces; it has the designated strict value. Consequently the quotient-of-reachability operation takes an original raw tuple to the class of its actual original output. For an original $(r+2)$-head the actual output witnesses the indicated order and hence gives the required unique cell. This proves the comparison by induction on raw expressions. Interpret each generator of $X$ up to that rank by this comparison from its original image in $Y$. If higher declarations are present, a derivation in the tested degrees restricts to this skeleton by the arity degree bound (equivalently use the support restriction of Response 27). No interpretation of those declarations is required.

Let a surface inhabit an old type of degree at most $r+2$. Evaluation in $M(Y)$ gives a raw cell in $Y$ at the original faces: in degree $r+1$ take a representative at the literal low faces, and in degree $r+2$ take the order witness at the *original* representatives, using the direct comparison. Lower degrees are already in $Q$. Finally $H_X\to Y$ is a raw weak equivalence between fibrant models. Restrict the derived mapping spaces out of the disc to those out of its boundary. These restrictions are Kan fibrations, and the weak equivalence induces equivalences on both mapping spaces; the fibre at the prescribed raw boundary is therefore nonempty in $H_X$ when it is nonempty in $Y$. This returns a raw inhabitant at the exact old type. One may take $X$ truncated at the tested rank throughout. This proves both bounds. [Boundary mapping-space argument](#researcher-response-25)

### Arbitrarily long gaps above the planar base

The proof uses the cutoff $r$ only via two base comparisons. One needs a raw weak equivalence from the actual generating $r$-skeleton into a suitable surface model. One also needs the free specializations and trivial fibrations for the globular sets $Z=P_{\le r}$. The latter condition extends to every $r\ge3$ for each of the two semistrict constructions above.

For a published theory one can simply use its free surface algebra on $Z$ for the generic base. Response 27 proves that $H_Z\to UF_R Z$ is a trivial fibration. The projection $UF_R Z\to C_Z$ is also a trivial fibration: lift the given surface boundary to a raw boundary by globular lifting in increasing dimension, then lift the desired strict cell there by the globular raw-to-strict comparison and take its surface class. Freeness gives the required specializations into **any** model of the published theory. Consequently a raw weak equivalence from the actual $r$-skeleton to the reduct of any such model suffices for conservativity of that theory through $r+2$.

For the binary case retain the history model; the following construction applies also to the ordinary Gray-category base for insertion. Write $Q_3$ for the corresponding construction on globular sets through degree three. From $Z$ form $\bar Z$ by keeping its generators through degree three and identifying the two 3-faces of each 4-generator. These are parallel generating variables, so the quotient changes no lower data. There is a strict projection $C_Z\to C_{\bar Z}$, sending generators above degree three to iterated identities. Put
$$
                Q_r(Z)=C_Z\times_{C_{\bar Z}} Q_3(\bar Z).
$$
This is a pullback of surface models, with the strict factors carrying their usual interpretations. Its projection to $C_Z$ is a globular trivial fibration by pullback. A globular labelling in an actual base $Q$ of the indicated kind has only identities above degree three, so it factors through $\bar Z$ at the low end and extends along $Q_r(Z)\to Q_3(\bar Z)\to Q$. To specialize into another $Q_r(Z')$, take the two components of the labelling. The strict component extends out of $C_Z$. The other extends out of $Q_3(\bar Z)$ by the preceding factorization, with target $Q_3(\bar Z')$. In $C_{\bar Z'}$ the first component factors out of $C_{\bar Z}$ because it identifies the two 3-faces of each 4-generator. The second projects to that same strict map on the low labels by the free history or Gray specialization. Thus the two components agree on every pair in the pullback. They give the required map of bases, and iterating this prescription composes componentwise. This retains the strict value of a high generator at the generic arity, even though an actual three-dimensional base labels it by an identity.

Take $r=h-1$ in the preceding construction. The actual $r$-skeleton equals the 2-skeleton, so its map into $Q$ is the weak equivalence of Response 26. All other generators attach above $r$. The two-degree argument proves the asserted $h+1$ bound. If there is no higher generator, Response 10 gives all degrees. More generally one can use the same argument at any $r\ge3$ if the actual $r$-skeleton admits the required raw weak equivalence into such a three-dimensional base. This condition concerns the skeleton itself and places no restrictions on the attaching maps above it.

### Gray surfaces omitting interchange

For Gray CaTT the same $r+2$ conclusion holds for all four binary surfaces omitting $I$ whenever $H_{X_{\le r}}\to\mathfrak F(X_{\le r})$ is a raw weak equivalence. Here use the directed strict category at each occurrence of $C$. In particular, with no 3-generators the same $h+1$ bound above the 2-skeleton holds in Gray CaTT for these four surfaces. Even with 3-generators present they are conservative through degree five if both temporal lists of every 3-generator are nonempty. The 4- and 5-generators remain arbitrary. Indeed, on each of the stated low skeleta the raw-to-directed-strict comparison is a weak equivalence on every fixed hom by Response 8, and is bijective on the global objects. [Cubical and polygonal hom comparisons](#researcher-response-8)

Here are the needed changes in the proof. The strict base on a globular $Z$ is the free strict Gray-enriched category, so its universal specializations exist. The globular directed comparison, arity trivial fibration and left properness are the directed inputs of Response 25. The designated value of an admissible surface head now lies in the tensor hom of its own arity. For a higher-degree head all its arguments still have degree at most its output degree, and the side condition supplies the designated value at the interpreted faces. Congruent types preserve that value because the generating equations for $I\notin S$ are directed-strict-sound. Mutual reachability over $V_P$ again implies equality of the directed strict values: in positive local degrees the forward hom of a pasting arity is given by a strong Steiner tensor, with the same positive boundary-weight test as above. At global objects and paths the spine is directed and the stationary homs are terminal.

For the raised-slot step in degree $r+1$, a total with an $(r+1)$-slot has only one active spatial column when $r\ge1$. That column already accounts for the entire local tensor degree $r$; the other columns have degree zero. A full identity head at this output degree cannot contain such a slot. After the raise, carry the new generator through the ordinary strict hom composite of the active column, keeping the exterior paths stationary. This gives the directed strict comparison needed between the two specialized operations, and the arity trivial fibration lifts it as before. This is the directed raised-slot calculation of Response 27. The verification in degrees $r+1,r+2$ uses only the binary rules for the specified $S$, checked on their own arities with the same directed strict value. It constructs the required surface model without imposing a reverse for any directed mixed tensor cell. [Directed chart lifting](#researcher-response-25) · [Directed raised-slot comparison](#researcher-response-27)

### Sharpness and the location of a possible failure

For the strict bound suspend the ordinary one-scalar computad $r-1$ times. The only generator above the globular lower skeleton has rank $r+1$. The $r$-skeleton therefore compares to its strict image by the globular trivial fibration. Suspend the diagonal braid and the identity of Response 1 the same number of times. Their strict images agree, and their comparison type has dimension $r+3$. A raw inhabitant would give one at the original scalar type by iterated ordinary forward-hom reflection. Hence the stated strict bound is sharp. [Scalar obstruction](#researcher-response-1) · [Ordinary suspension and raw reflection](#researcher-response-11)

For either published equality or an ordinary binary surface, a failure in degree $n\ge5$ requires a generator of degree between three and $n-2$ in the support of its strict paste, by the gap bound. In particular localize a prospective degree-five failure to the support of its strict paste as in Response 27. Its 3-skeleton must contain a 3-generator, and its strict comparison cannot be a weak equivalence. The conditional semistrict result also requires an obstruction to using the three-dimensional base of Response 26 there. Thus the presence of 4- or 5-generators attached at scalar braids over a 2-computad cannot by itself produce this first open degree. The directional order on 4-cells is essential here: a freely adjoined 5-generator between two distinct 4-generators can have no raw reverse, as is already detected by its ordinary strict generator counts. ∎

**Validation.** On a scalar 2-generator the checker used its diagonal braid as the common 3-boundary of three fresh 4-generators. It adjoined a 5-generator at a unit-padded composite of two of them. Rice's weak checker accepted two raw lifts at the unpadded source, one using the raised-slot comparison, and rejected direct use of the declaration there. Each published mode verified the direct conversion, the two representative classes and the class of the round trip; it rejected using the generator backwards. Its ordinary strict value increases the count of the target 4-generator by one, so no raw reverse exists. The same raw and published checks passed in degrees six and seven with successive identities on the braid as the common seam. Agda 2.8.0 checked all 22 substitutions to the three-dimensional base for two discs glued at every cut in ranks four through seven. In the Gray kernel it also checked the exact raw type of a fitted 5-generator over a directed square with a declared return whose two temporal lists are nonempty. The degree bounds and sharpness follow from the proofs above.
