## Researcher Response 32

**All eight binary surfaces preserve positional lifts along arbitrary maps of globular generators, in both kernels.** In ordinary CaTT the free binary surface algebra on such generators lifts strict cells at every prescribed *surface* boundary. Consequently, if the free raw algebra on the generating $r$-skeleton is weakly equivalent to the reduct of **any** model of the chosen ordinary surface, the full surface is conservative through degree $r+2$ for arbitrary higher attachments. Its equations on $r$-dimensional data are conservative in that context in **every** output degree. These statements also hold for each published surface. In particular all ten ordinary surfaces are conservative through dimension five above the suspension of **any** finite 2-computad, with arbitrary 4- and 5-declarations. This includes a 3-skeleton on which ordinary strict-image conversion already fails in dimension five.

### Lifting a word law along a map which both folds and misses generators

Write $LZ$ for the free surface algebra on a globular set $Z$, $FZ$ for its ordinary free strict category, and $p_Z:LZ\to FZ$ for ordinary strict evaluation, also for the Gray kernel. For every globular map $f:Y\to Z$, the following map of globular sets is an isomorphism:
$$
                  LY\longrightarrow LZ\times_{FZ}FY,\qquad
                  a\longmapsto(Lf(a),p_Y(a)).
$$
It suffices to construct a family of lifts respecting conversion. We give the construction because two distinct labels can have the same image, and some variables in the arity of a word equation can have no lift.

For a strict value $d\in FZ$, put $I(d)=\{c\in FY:Ff(c)=d\}$. Make a globular set over $FZ$ as follows. Over $d$, choose first a compatible boundary in the globular set already constructed (empty in degree zero). Then choose, for each $c\in I(d)$, an element of $LY$ of strict value $c$. Its two faces must be the entries of the chosen boundary families at the two faces of $c$. The boundary is part of this data even when $I(d)$ is empty. Denote the resulting globular set by $D$. We equip $D$ with surface operations.

Consider a strict pasting expression $b$ on a globular tree $P$ and a tuple of strict labels $\sigma:P\to FZ$. The positional shape of $b$ embeds as its support $K\subseteq P$, with the identity padding prescribed by its degree. An element $c\in I(b[\sigma])$ splits uniquely into a compatible strict tuple on $K$ above $\sigma|_K$ whose substitution into $b$ gives $c$. Indeed $b$, labelled by $\sigma$, is an element of $FFZ$. Cartesianity of free strict multiplication gives the unique element of $FFY$ over it with product $c$. Its positions are precisely $K$. Splitting a second time on an inner paste gives the split of the flattened paste, by the uniqueness and associativity of multiplication. Taking a face restricts the split to the positions of that face, by the same uniqueness and globularity. This includes empty edge paths and identity arguments. [Leinster, §8.1 and Appendix F](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0305049) · [Positional shapes on trees](#researcher-response-4)

For a head on $P$, its designated ordinary strict cell has $K=P$ in both kernels. On a tuple in $D$, split $c$ as above, evaluate each input family at its assigned strict cell, and apply the **same surface head in $LY$** to the resulting tuple. This is a globular labelling of $P$ because the entries in $D$ satisfy the face condition and the split respects the incidences. The prescribed source and target families are the interpretations of the two generic boundary expressions. To check them, more generally evaluate an expression on a tree tuple in $D$ and inspect its entry at a lift of its strict result. The answer is its evaluation in $LY$ on the split tuple on the support of that expression. To type the restricted expression, substitute the word retraction onto that support from Response 27. It fixes the expression because each head visits every argument of its syntax and the bound generic type has no ambient labels. A variable selects its own entry. At a head apply the splitting calculation first to the head and then to each argument. Common face positions receive the same strict label, and the two successive splits give the flattened split. This proves the assertion by expression induction, also for the boundary expressions of a newly formed head. Thus the family has the prescribed faces. A generic type formed using conversion is interpreted by this same recursion on its formation derivation. Congruence in the generic type acts by the given surface head congruence in $LY$. No choice of a raw representative of that type enters the construction.

For (i), let $b$ be the common ordinary strict value of the two words on their generic arity $P$. Both words use only its support $K$, by support reflection. They give a word law over $K$ itself: substitute the word retraction $P\to K$ of Response 27 into their equality in the free word algebra. This fixes the two restricted writings and their declared boundaries. Under any tuple in $D$, the split at $c$ gives the **same** tuple of $LY$ entries on $K$ for both words. Apply the law there. For (ii) the coherence uses all of $P$ and the identity on the word has the same full strict value. Its split is therefore the same on both sides and the instance of (ii) in $LY$ gives the equality, with the endpoint equality already verified by (i). These are equalities of families with the same prescribed boundary. Substitution composes splits as above, so the verification includes converted substitutions and congruence. This gives the simultaneous interpretation of formation and equality in $D$. [Support reflection](#researcher-response-9) · [Word retraction onto the support](#researcher-response-27)

Assign each variable $z$ its family of generating preimages. Cartesianity of the strict unit identifies these generators with $I(\eta z)$, compatibly with both faces. Freeness now gives $LZ\to D$. Write $t[c]$ for its entry on a surface cell $t$ at $c$. Then $Lf(t[c])=t$, by induction on any representative: the split of a head covers every slot and relabelling its lifted arguments recovers the original arguments. Conversely $(Lf(a))[p_Y(a)]=a$. At a head, the strict argument tuple of $a$ is itself the unique split at $p_Y(a)$, so the induction on arguments returns each argument of $a$. The variable cases are the unit calculation. The two identities exhibit the inverse to the displayed map. For either published equality the same isomorphism follows from Response 27.

### The ordinary strict projection at a prescribed surface boundary

Fix ordinary CaTT. Let $c\in FZ$ have positive degree $n$, with prescribed parallel surface faces $u,v$ lying above its strict faces. Present $c$ by its positional arity $P$, its generic strict cell $b$, and a globular labelling $g:P\to Z$. The isomorphism just proved lifts $u,v$ over $g$ at the two faces of $b$. Their lower common faces agree in $LP$ by uniqueness in that same isomorphism. Thus the two lifts form a surface type over $P$. If $n=\dim P$, their strict supports are the two signed boundaries of the total. If $n>\dim P$, their strict images are the same full-support cell. In either case the ordinary head on those lifted endpoints is admissible. Indeed the closed variable support of each endpoint is its strict support, also for a surface expression on a tree: at a head the generic strict cell visits every argument, and strict substitution takes the union of their supports. Converting the type preserves that strict boundary. Applying $g$ to the head gives an $n$-cell at $u,v$ with value $c$. Objects lift as generators. Hence $LZ\to FZ$ is a globular trivial fibration. The composite $H_Z\to LZ\to FZ$ is another such fibration by Response 4. Two-out-of-three gives a **raw weak equivalence** $H_Z\to LZ$ on every globular set of generators.

In particular take an ordinary computad $X$, $B=X_{\le r}$, $r\ge1$, and a weak equivalence $H_B\to UQ$ into the raw reduct of a model of this surface. A zigzag of weak equivalences suffices since $H_B$ is cofibrant and $UQ$ fibrant. In the two-degree construction of Response 31 use $L(P_{\le r})$ on **each** generic lower skeleton. Its comparison with the ordinary strict category is the trivial fibration just proved. Every globular labelling into $Q$ extends by surface freeness, and successive extensions compose. These are exactly the two generic-base hypotheses there. In particular the strict values used to compare two generic operations in degree $r+1$ are calculated by the natural strict projection on this free base. The mutual-reachability quotient at degree $r+1$, and the directed witness at degree $r+2$ at the original representatives, therefore prove conservativity through $r+2$ without a dimension bound or a thinness hypothesis on $Q$. A first failure in degree $n\ge3$ forces the raw $(n-2)$-skeleton of its support to lie outside the weak equivalence class of **every** reduct of a model of the surface in question. [Two-degree construction](#researcher-response-31)

There is a sufficient condition one can check on successive skeleta using only raw inhabitation. Suppose $H_{X_{\le j-1}}$ is weakly equivalent to a surface reduct, with $j\ge2$. If **every raw type of degree at least $j+2$** in $X_{\le j}$ is inhabited, then $H_{X_{\le j}}$ too is weakly equivalent to a reduct of the same surface. To see this, apply the construction just used with $r=j-1$ and ambient context $X_{\le j}$. Its raw pushout $Y$ is weakly equivalent to $H_{X_{\le j}}$. Existence at all boundaries of a given degree transfers across this equivalence in either direction: mapping spaces out of the cofibrant boundary and globe compare by weak equivalences, and their restriction maps are fibrations. Surjectivity on components of the restriction is exactly existence at all literal boundaries. In particular the stated high types are inhabited also in $Y$. The projection $Y\to M(Y)$ of Response 31 is a raw map. This holds through $j+1$ by its generic operation comparison and above by uniqueness at the projected boundary. It lifts globes through degree $j+1$ by the choice of a representative and the order witness at arbitrary representatives. Above $j+1$ any requested cell is unique at its boundary, and the raw high-type hypothesis supplies its lift. Thus the projection is a trivial fibration in every degree, and $M(Y)$ has the full surface action. Starting at the planar base of Response 26, any ordinary first failure in degree $n\ge5$ therefore requires some generating $j$-skeleton of its support, $3\le j\le n-2$, to have an uninhabited raw type in degree at least $j+2$. This test uses the raw theory alone. [Planar starting models](#researcher-response-26) · [The projection to the ordered quotient](#researcher-response-31)

### Equations on the lower generators at unrestricted output degree

Under the same hypothesis on $B$, impose all equality judgements of the chosen surface on contexts generated through $r$, in the sense of Response 26, and close under substitution, congruence and conversion. We prove conservativity at **every** old type of $X$ for this theory. The issue above degree $r+2$ is that two generic lifts with the same strict value and boundary may have different images in $Q$. We use the given operation of $Q$ whenever every label belongs to $Q$.

Put $Y=H_X\amalg_{H_B}UQ$, so $H_X\to Y$ is a weak equivalence by left properness. Work with free raw extensions $Y$ of distinguished surface algebras $Q$ by declarations above $r$. In such an extension a relative term is a base constant, a fresh generator, or a head having at least one argument outside the base. A raw specialization preserving the surface action on the base and sending every fresh generator outside the new base preserves and reflects membership in the base. This follows by induction since a head with an outside argument remains a relative head. [Relative terms and left properness](#researcher-response-25)

For a tuple on a tree $P$, let $R\subseteq P$ consist of the slots labelled in the base. It is a globular subset and contains $P_{\le r}$. The tuple determines a raw map
$$
                  W(P,R)=H_P\amalg_{H_R}ULR \longrightarrow Y .
$$
On $LR$ use surface freeness, and on the remaining generators use their given labels. The map reflects base membership by the relative-term description. Such specializations compose. Since $R$ is globular, $H_R\to ULR$ is a weak equivalence. Hence $H_P\to W(P,R)$ is a weak equivalence by left properness, and $W(P,R)\to FP$ is a trivial fibration by the gaunt-target test at the arity.

Define operations first at the generic tuple of $W(P,R)$. For $R=P$ use the surface operation of $LP$. For $R\ne P$ choose a lift of the designated strict cell at the already interpreted generic boundary, making a single choice at each arity, subset, interpreted boundary and strict value. Its full strict support forces this lift to lie outside $LR$, whose image in $FP$ is supported in $R$. Specialize the result by the displayed map. Thus an output is in the base exactly when all the slots of its head are in the base. On the generic tuples the interpretation commutes with strict projection by induction, so the requested strict lift exists at the interpreted faces. Under a specialization reflecting membership, each subexpression retains the same subset of base slots, so evaluation commutes with specialization by induction on its arguments and generic faces. In particular this proves the substitution formula at the displayed maps. At an entirely base tuple *every* expression has its given surface interpretation, by the $R=P$ choice and surface freeness. Every assignment of a context generated through $r$ lands on such data. This verifies the allowed equations in arbitrary output degrees. Head congruence holds by the identical choice at equal interpreted boundaries and by surface congruence in the base case. These checks justify the simultaneous construction on surface-formed types and tuples. Write $Y'$ for the resulting action restricted to the raw theory.

Here are details of the comparison with the original raw action. Both actions agree on $Q$. Use the relative raw cylinders $J_n$ of Response 25, constant through $r$, with fold to the raw globe $D_n$. Define cells of a comparison algebra to be the following maps $J_n\to Y$, inductively requiring the specified kind of map on each boundary globe. Allow the constant cylinder on any base cell (factor through the fold), and every cylinder with **both** endpoints outside the base. Its projections will be the two endpoints in $Y$ and $Y'$.

For an allowed cylinder tuple on $P$, use its common subset $R$ of base slots at the two ends. The universal target for this tuple is
$$
                K=J_P\amalg_{J_R}ULR
$$
using the fold $J_R\to H_R$. This is a free raw extension of $ULR$ since $J_R\to J_P$ is cellular. Each end $W(P,R)\to K$ is a trivial cofibration. In fact $J_R\amalg_{H_R}H_P\to J_P$ at either end is a trivial cofibration, obtained by attaching the one-ended inclusions for the slots outside $R$ in globular order. Push it out along $J_R\to ULR$. Each endpoint inclusion sends outside generators to outside cells: under $K\to FP$ they have the strict values of their own slots. The fold gives $K\to FP$ a weak equivalence, surjective on objects, hence a trivial fibration.

For a raw head, prescribe its cylinder operation over $P,R$ on the boundary cylinders by induction, and at the two ends prescribe the old and new generic operations. These agree on the intersections by the boundary formula. If $R=P$ use the constant cylinder on the common base operation. Otherwise extend these prescriptions to $J_n\to K$ over the constant cylinder on the designated strict value in $FP$, by the trivial fibration and the cofibration attaching the two-ended boundary of $J_n$. The prescribed values on the boundary have the required strict projections by induction. Both endpoints are outside by full support. On any actual tuple specialize by $K\to Y$. Internal cylinder cells may specialize to base cells. What the construction needs is preservation of the two **endpoint** subsets: each restriction $W(P,R)\to Y$ is the membership-reflecting specialization above. Thus for any boundary expression evaluated on the universal cylinder tuple its endpoint subsets persist after specialization, by the output membership formula at the two ends. Induction on that expression gives the same specialized boundary cylinders as evaluation on the actual tuple. This proves substitution for the constructed operations and makes the two endpoint maps raw homomorphisms.

Both projections lift at prescribed globular boundaries. For a specified base cell the given boundary cylinders must be constant base cylinders, so take its constant cylinder. For an outside cell $a$ at one end, write $T=J_{\partial n}\amalg_{\partial D_n}D_n$ for the one-ended domain. The prescribed data give a map $T\to Y$. We need an extension whose opposite endpoint is still outside. Over $F(D^n)$, the free strict category on the generating $n$-globe, choose a retraction $J_n\to T$ of $T\hookrightarrow J_n$. Indeed $T\to F(D^n)$ given by folding the boundary cylinders is a trivial fibration: the boundary endpoint inclusion is a trivial cofibration, so $D_n\to T$ is one too, and the strict globe is gaunt. The required diagonal follows from cofibrancy of the one-ended inclusion, using the cylinder fold for its bottom map. In $T$ the opposite endpoint of this retraction must contain the specified $n$-generator. Its strict value is that generator, whereas all of $J_{\partial n}$ maps into its strict boundary. Under $T\to Y$ a raw term using the specified generator retains an outside argument or is $a$ itself. Thus the retracted cylinder has both ends outside and the given boundaries. In the constant degrees both projections are identities. This proves the two globular trivial-fibration assertions.

Lift the original assignment of $X$ into this span along the first projection and evaluate the proposed inhabitant in $Y'$ at the second projection. The second trivial fibration lifts it at the prescribed images of its old faces in the span. Project to $Y$ and use the weak equivalence $H_X\to Y$ to reflect existence at the original faces, by the boundary mapping-space argument of Response 25. This proves the unbounded output assertion. The same proof uses the published globular comparison of Response 27 when $L$ is either published surface. [Relative cylinders and reflection at an exact old type](#researcher-response-25)

### Suspended planar bases with arbitrary subsequent attachments

Let $B=\Sigma^m A$ for a finite ordinary 2-computad $A$, with $m\ge0$. Its free raw algebra is weakly equivalent to a model of the full binary surface, and separately to a model of insertion. Start with the models for $A$ in Response 26. The binary history model also satisfies disc removal, by Response 11. Given such a model $Q$, form a model on two poles with terminal stationary homs, empty reverse hom, and forward hom $Q$. At a crossing head use the restriction to its unique crossing column evaluated in $Q$. The checks in Response 11 give the full binary equations and the disc equation. For insertion the splice and disc calculations of Response 12 give its equations. At stationary tuples there is a unique cell of each required degree. A raw map out of $H_A$ into $UQ$ induces the map out of $H_{\Sigma A}$ at the suspended generator assignment. Its forward hom map is raw restriction followed by the original map, since restriction of each suspended head is its original operation. Raw restriction on that hom is a weak equivalence, the stationary homs are weakly terminal and the reverse hom is empty. Thus if the original map was a weak equivalence then so is the induced map, by the fixed-hom criterion. Iteration proves the assertion for $B$. [Binary restriction and the fixed-hom comparison](#researcher-response-11) · [Insertion at a crossing](#researcher-response-12)

If $X_{\le r}=B$ for $r\ge m+2$, the preceding results now apply to all eight ordinary binary surfaces and both published surfaces. The actual higher generators of $X$ may have arbitrary raw attaching types. For example take one suspension of the scalar $a:1_x\to1_x$. The suspended diagonal braid and the suspended identity have equal ordinary strict value, but no raw 5-cell between them in the skeleton, by restriction to the forward hom and Response 1. Nevertheless all ten surfaces are conservative through degree five in **every** extension starting in degree four, and every equation on data generated through degree three is safe in that extension if it holds in the selected surface. The same example shifted $m$ times gives the distinction in degree $m+4$. For a prospective unrestricted failure the two-degree criterion tests the skeleton for a weak equivalence to a surface model. The existence of such an equivalence on arbitrary nonglobular skeleta remains open for the unresolved ordinary surfaces. The Gray lifting isomorphism above uses the ordinary strict projection, so it gives no return across a directed interchanger. The unrestricted cases of Q1 and the general context classification in Q2 therefore remain open. ∎

**Validation.** With two generating colours folded to one and an additional downstairs generator outside the image, the checker reconstructed 1,372 lifted strict words in ranks one through four. For both bracketings it read the colours back from the typed lifted term, including when one or more input words were empty. It also checked the two coherence orientations and their identities under the same fold. For 49 labellings of a directed square it read the two spatial event lists independently from both temporal faces. Exchanging two differently coloured lists can change the lifted strict value while leaving the folded syntax identical. Agda 2.8.0 checked the selected terms and substitution equalities in **both** kernels, including fitted 5-, 6- and 7-cells above suspended scalar bases. Rice’s insertion checker accepted both lifted associativity comparisons and a composite requiring conversion in an input slot. It rejected the permuted boundary, and the weak checker rejected the direct associativity conversion. The finite checks exercise the positional and attachment calculations. The gluing, cylinder and skeletal arguments above supply the dimension-independent proofs. [Reproducible checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_globular_lifts.py) · [Rice checker revision](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/tree/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc)

## Researcher Response 33

**In ordinary CaTT all eight binary surfaces and both published surfaces are conservative through dimension five in every finite context without 1- or 2-generators.** The 3-generators may be scalars at iterated identities. Their attaching terms can have arbitrary raw writings, and the 4- and 5-generators can have arbitrary boundaries. Full ordinary strict-image conversion already fails in dimension five on a single such scalar.

More generally, suppose the first positive generator degree is $n\ge3$ and the next occupied degree is $h>n$, with $h=\infty$ allowed. The bound through which all ten surfaces are conservative is at least
$$
                            \min(2n-1,h+1).
$$
On the generating $n$-skeleton itself, an old $(n+2)$-type has a raw inhabitant exactly when its two $(n+1)$-faces induce the same permutation of the individual scalar occurrences, after identifying the source and target occurrences at the common boundary. The permutation test below computes this criterion with repeated as well as distinct generators. The bound $2n-1$ is sharp for the comparison *with permutations*. A higher homotopy group first escapes that comparison at a specified old $2n$-type.

### Occurrences of arbitrary rank in the surface laws

Fix a degree $n\ge2$ and a set of colours. We construct two ordinary surface models. Each has a single cell in each degree below $n$. In the binary model an $n$-cell is a finite table of lists of colours indexed by finite binary strings. In the published model it is one list of colours. In either model an $(n+1)$-cell is a colour-preserving bijection of the positions in its two incident $n$-cells. It may change both the address and the index in a list. Above bijections use only iterated identities.

For the binary model interpret a degree-$n$ head by its ordinary strict $n$-generator occurrences. A binary composite at cut $n-1$ concatenates the lists pointwise. At a binary cut $j<n-1$, prefix the addresses of each operand of degree $n$ by its side, 0 or 1. This includes a whiskering with just one such operand. For other arities concatenate pointwise in a fixed order of the strict occurrences. For the published model, at every head concatenate in the planar order of the $n$-generators visited by the strict paste. On a tree of height $n$ this is the left-to-right order of its depth-$n$ branches, and on a tree of smaller height the list is empty. These choices depend only on the arity and designated strict cell. Changing a generic head type by conversion preserves them.

For both models, higher heads are determined by their action on individually distinguished occurrences. On a globular arity form the equivalence classes of $n$-generators joined by $(n+1)$-generators. A strict $n$-cell uses at most one occurrence from each class. In each class the directed graph of transports, after identifying parallel generating transports joined by an $(n+2)$-generator, is a string. The corresponding bijections are equal for any admissible labelling since the only cells above bijections are identities. Start with distinct auxiliary colours for all positions in one table or list per class, disjoint across classes, and transport those colours along the string by the given bijections. Each evaluated $n$-expression contains precisely the occurrences contributed by its strict top chain, by induction on the head and the substitution. The two $n$-faces of an $(n+1)$-head use the same classes. Matching the auxiliary colours therefore gives a unique bijection between them. Its underlying map is independent of the auxiliary names. An $(n+2)$-head has parallel bijections which preserve these same distinct colours, so they agree. This defines all head operations, including those at a converted generic type. The operations act on an evaluated tuple, so substitution composes. Recolouring leaves the underlying positions and bijections fixed. [Slot and transport lemmas in arbitrary degree](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r3-2026-11-19.md#a-catt-model-of-lists-and-bijections) · [The degree-two construction](#researcher-response-4)

For the binary equations the essential verification is at degree $n$. Read the literal $(n-1)$-source of a generic $n$-word as its constructor tree. At each leaf that is an $(n-1)$-variable record the path of generating $n$-steps changing that leaf. A generating $n$-variable supplies one step from its source leaf to its target leaf. An identity has empty paths. At a lower cut the cut node remains in the source and target trees and the paths take the corresponding left and right addresses. An operand of degree at most $n-1$ stays stationary with all its paths empty. At the top cut the two words have the very same intervening face, so concatenate the paths leaf by leaf. These rules prove by induction that the target tree has the same shape, with exactly the indicated changes of leaf labels.

Two raw-parallel $n$-words on a globular arity with equal ordinary strict value have the same path at **each** leaf. Indeed the occurrences of $(n-1)$-variables in their common source are precisely its top chain, by the binary and identity formulas. Apply the slot lemma one degree lower. Distinct such leaves belong to distinct transport classes for the $n$-steps. Thus the strict $n$-chain gives the multiset of steps at each individual leaf. The order is forced because the sources and targets follow the directed string in that class. Parallel alternative edges have the same endpoints, but the chain records the name of the edge used, and the string traverses that position at most once. Leaves inside a stationary identity contribute nothing. On any labelling, the interpreted table is obtained by concatenating the input tables along these paths and prefixing by their leaf addresses. The addresses of different leaves are prefix-incomparable. This proves (i) even for the full set of laws and for auxiliary-colour labellings. At degree $n+1$ the two parallel words induce bijections that agree on the distinguished positions. In (ii), a degree-$n$ coherence over equal words has empty top chain, and a degree-$(n+1)$ coherence over equal $n$-words fixes every position by the equality on the refined labelling. In the other degrees the equations follow from the singleton lower cells or uniqueness at the boundary. This verifies the full surface theory under substitution, also inside head types.

For the published laws, disc removal returns the input list. An endo-coherence of degree $n$ has no $n$-occurrence. At a degree-$n$ insertion the order on the joined arity is the old planar order with the selected branch replaced in place by the planar order of the inner tree. Descending along the branch changes only the chosen child at each shared trunk level. At the splice the inner sequence of branches replaces one branch between the unchanged prefix and suffix. For a selected maximal slot of degree $n$ the exterior substitution gives its standard composite of the inner slots in exactly this order. If the inner height is smaller there are no $n$-slots in that block. A selected maximal slot of degree below $n$ changes no depth-$n$ entry. These observations include identity pruning and the disc case of the standard term. They prove the rank-$n$ insertion equality for any outer head and any labels. In degree $n+1$ compute both sides on the joined arity with the refined colours. Their evaluated endpoints agree by the transported type and the rank-$n$ calculation. Both maps preserve the distinguished colours, so they are the same bijection. A disc gives the given bijection by transport, and a generic endo-coherence on equal faces fixes all positions on the refined labelling. In higher degrees only the boundaries matter. Thus the list model carries the published insertion action, including strict units. Each model separately defines a raw map out of the scalar computad by assigning a singleton of its own colour to every generating scalar. [Insertion and the exterior substitution, §3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303)

### Distinguishing top-generator occurrences in every dimension

There is a consequence for *arbitrary* attaching terms. Let $Z$ be a finite ordinary computad generated through $n$, and $Y$ the same lower skeleton with a set of copies of its $n$-generators at their original raw types. The copy map $f:Y\to Z$ can omit generators. Write $L$ for the selected free surface model, $Q(C)$ for the appropriate occurrence model above, and $C_Y,C_Z$ for the sets of top generator names. Evaluate each such generator as its named singleton and every lower generator as the unique cell in its degree. Then there is an isomorphism of globular sets in **all** degrees
$$
 LY\ \cong\ LZ\times_{Q(C_Z)} Q(C_Y).
$$
Consequently any failure of conservativity on $Z$ in degree $k\ge n$ has a failure over a context of copies in which each top generator occurs exactly once in the witness's degree-$n$ source, counting positions in its table or list. At $k=n$ count the witness itself. At $k>n$ this separates occurrences in the boundary, even when the witness uses high-dimensional coherences and the top generators are attached at identities or composites.

To verify the isomorphism we give the splitting calculation for this square. At a head of degree $n$, every $n$-slot of its arity supplies its input positions exactly once, with the injections into the output specified by the table or list formula. A recolouring of the output restricts uniquely to all those inputs. No colour is needed in smaller degrees. At any head of degree $k>n$ its iterated $n$-source uses exactly one generating $n$-variable from every transport class of the arity. For this last fact collapse each class to one $n$-generator and send all higher generators to iterated identities on it, retaining the lower generators. This is a strict globular labelling, since higher variables have parallel variable faces. The designated ordinary value visits the entire arity. By the strict support union formula its image visits each collapsed $n$-generator. Above rank $n$ that image is an iterated identity, hence its $n$-source already visits every collapsed generator. The slot lemma gives the at-most-once assertion before collapse. [Full arity and support under substitution](#researcher-response-27)

A lift of the output in $Q(C_Y)$ prescribes colours on its $n$-source occurrences. Read them back through the injections for that source expression (at degree $n$, every occurrence of a used $n$-variable has its own block of positions). Transport these colours along the labelled bijections of each string. This uniquely recolours every input of dimension $n$, including the middle variables which the $n$-source does not itself use. The unique lift of each higher input keeps its underlying bijection at rank $n+1$ and takes the identity tower on it above that rank. Evaluating on this lifted tuple gives the requested output lift by the distinguished-colour definition of the head. The argument also covers a head whose arity has height below $n$, where there are no positions. For nested heads, splitting twice gives the input colours obtained by evaluating first, since both assign each distinguished position its prescribed colour, with every intermediate bijection acting by transport. For a boundary expression using only part of an arity this argument applies on the support of its generic ordinary strict cell, which has the word retraction of Response 27.

For clarity about conversion, construct a surface algebra over $Q(C_Z)$ by the family construction of Response 32, now indexing a family over $q$ by the lifts of $q$ along $Q(C_Y)\to Q(C_Z)$. Its values are cells of $LY$ over the indicated index, with the chosen compatible boundary families as there. For a head use the split just constructed and apply that head in $LY$. Splitting under substitution and restriction to the boundary give its typing. For a generic binary word equation on its common support, splitting the two outputs produces the same recoloured inputs. Indeed the word law holds when each position of each transport class receives its own auxiliary name. Thus the two words use the very same named occurrences at each output position. Propagating those colours back gives the same tuple on the support. Apply the restricted word law in $LY$ there. Clause (ii) uses the entire supported tuple. For a published insertion the right-hand head visits every slot of the joined tree, and splitting its exterior and interior substitutions on the left returns those labels by the planar-order and bijection calculations. For pruning use the pruned tree, for disc removal the single label, and for a generic endo-coherence the supported tuple on its arity. In the latter case generic head congruence and its premise hold in the given algebra $LY$. This verifies the family action with all the required equations. Below rank $n$ a generator receives its original cell over the unique index. A top generator receives the family of its named copies, well typed since its raw attaching expressions have smaller degree and are shared. Evaluating $LZ$ in this algebra gives the inverse map in the displayed formula. Relabelling recovers the original tuple at each head, and the input colours of an existing upstairs expression are the unique split of its evaluated colours. Induction on the expressions gives both inverse identities.

For the consequence, assign a fresh copy of its top generator to each position at the $n$-source of a surface witness. For $k>n$ transport these colours using its bijection, and use its forced identities higher up. For $k=n$ assign the colours directly on the value. Lift the surface witness by the isomorphism. To lift its old boundary in the **raw** theory, take the prescribed copy at a variable and retain each raw head with the same split on its tuple. At a shared face the two lifted raw writings agree literally, since the original writing and the assigned colours determine the lift uniquely by this recursion. In particular these lifts have precisely the surface faces of the lifted witness. A raw filler there would relabel to a raw filler at the original boundary. This proves the consequence with no normal-form assumption on the binary surface. It explains why an obstruction based solely on folding two top-generator names cannot distinguish surface inhabitation from raw inhabitation once the occurrence bijection has been fixed.

### The raw scalar mapping space and its first homotopy group

First take a single object and a finite set $C$ of degree-$n$ generators, with no others. Write $B$ for this computad. The realization of $H_B$ is the $n$-fold delooping of the free little-$n$-cubes algebra on the discrete space $C$. Here is the universal-property calculation. With the image $x$ of the object fixed, a map from the cellular realization specifies one $n$-endomorphism of the iterated identity of $x$ for each element of $C$, with the full space of such choices. The attaching maps for other raw writings of the boundaries give the same derived pushout because they factor through the raw algebra on the single point, whose realization is terminal. For a pointed target the underlying space of its $n$-fold endomorphisms represents exactly these choices (take the space of objects and equivalences of the final hom). The $n$-fold delooping–endomorphism adjunction, followed by the free-algebra adjunction, gives the same mapping space for the proposed delooping. This calculation can be performed in $(\infty,n)$-categories because they contain the point, the $n$-globe and its boundary. Their inclusion into higher categories preserves the colimit since it has the right adjoint taking the invertible core above $n$. This identifies the generator maps as well as the object. For several vertices take the disjoint union of these calculations. [Computadic realization](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-13) · [Gepner–Haugseng, Corollary 6.3.11 and Remark 6.3.16](https://people.math.rochester.edu/faculty/doug/otherpapers/gepner-haugseng2.pdf#page=114)

At multiplicities $(k_c)$, put $k=\sum k_c$. The space in this final hom is equivalent to
$$
            \operatorname{Conf}_k(\mathbb R^n)\big/\prod_{c\in C}\mathfrak S_{k_c},
$$
where the numerator has ordered points. Indeed the free operad algebra is the disjoint union of the little-cubes spaces with coloured inputs modulo simultaneous permutation. Taking centres of cubes gives the equivariant equivalence to ordered configurations. To compute the connectivity of the ordered space, forget the last point. This is a fibration with fibre $\mathbb R^n$ minus a finite set, homotopy equivalent to a wedge of $(n-1)$-spheres when the set is nonempty. The long exact sequence starting with a point gives $(n-2)$-connectivity. For $n\ge3$ the covering thus gives fundamental group $\prod_c\mathfrak S_{k_c}$ and vanishing $\pi_i$ for $2\le i\le n-2$. The empty component is contractible and is the only component invertible for the multiset product. [May, *The Geometry of Iterated Loop Spaces*, §§2 and 4](https://people.math.rochester.edu/faculty/doug/otherpapers/May-geo.pdf) · [Fadell–Neuwirth, *Configuration Spaces*](https://journals.msp.org/mscand/article/view/2674)

For either occurrence model $Q$ the $n$-fold fixed hom of its raw reduct is the ordinary groupoid of the indicated tables or lists and colour-preserving bijections. Indeed the suspended unary identity and binary arrow composition act by identity and composition of the displayed bijections by the refined-colour calculation. All higher cells there are identities, and every path of bijections acts by that composite by the same calculation. Its components are the same multisets, with the indicated permutation groups as automorphisms. The map $H_B\to UQ$ induces the multiset bijection on components. It also induces an **isomorphism on fundamental groups**, as can be checked without choosing a geometric motion for a particular coherence. At a top composite of $k$ generators, temporarily distinguish all $k$ inputs by fresh colours. In the free coloured configuration space there is a path to the top composite in any permuted order, since ordered configurations are connected. This gives a raw $(n+1)$-cell at the specified two raw faces by the realization comparison on the fibre of the globe over its boundary. In $Q$ that arrow must carry each distinguished position to its new place. Fold the colours back. Any permutation preserving the original colours is thereby realized as an automorphism of the original table or list. Surjectivity and the equality of the finite group orders just computed give the isomorphism. Paths to a top composite transport this conclusion to any basepoint. If a generator has a nonstandard raw attaching type, the equivalence of the cellular pushouts transports its singleton assignment to the same one in the final hom, since on the point all structural $n$-cells evaluate to the empty list or table and their transports are unique there. One may equivalently choose the colour-distinguishing paths after replacing the skeleton's boundary writings by iterated identities.

In particular two parallel raw $(n+1)$-cells admit a raw $(n+2)$-cell precisely when their two positional bijections agree. In the configuration-space hom this asks whether the two paths at fixed endpoints are homotopic relative to those endpoints. The groupoid map just proved is fully faithful on the fundamental groupoid up to equivalence. Lower structural boundaries of the raw terms may be transported to the iterated unit boundaries using the point subalgebra. On the semantic side the resulting homotopy-fibre identification preserves existence, and on the raw side a lift in that fibre is a term at the original faces by cofibrancy of the globe boundary and fibrancy of the raw model.

More generally the map $H_B\to UQ$ solves **derived globe lifting problems** through degree $d=2n-1$. This means that every compatible choice of raw boundary and target cell admits a source lift up to homotopy of the whole square. Below $n$ all cells up to equivalence are the single lower units at the chosen vertex, with contractible equivalence choices because in the last hom invertibility forces the empty multiset with its contractible component. At $n$ use surjectivity on multiset components. Above $n$ the remaining data are in spaces. For a globe of degree $n+i$ these are the relative $i$-disc problems in the map of final homs, since the interval with fixed ends in a space gives its path space, and iterating takes paths between paths at fixed boundary. For $i=1$ the isomorphism on fundamental groupoids suffices. For $2\le i\le n-1$ use injectivity on $\pi_{i-1}$ and surjectivity on $\pi_i$. The former follows from the fundamental-group calculation or the connectivity of the ordered cover. The latter holds because the target groupoid has zero higher homotopy groups. This also proves the assertion for a disjoint union, where any target cell fixes its vertex.

The limitation at the next degree is genuine even for a single scalar. The two-particle component is homotopy equivalent to $\mathbb{RP}^{n-1}$ by taking the unoriented difference line (before passing to the quotient the direction lies in $S^{n-1}$). Its $\pi_{n-1}$ is $\mathbb Z$ for $n\ge3$. At the double scalar choose a nonzero class and the zero class, represented by parallel raw $(2n-1)$-cells based at the iterated identities, using the same globe-fibre comparison. Their old $2n$-type is uninhabited. Their images in $Q$ coincide, since $2n-1\ge n+2$. This identifies the obstruction to extending the permutation comparison. Whether either surface itself inhabits this old type is a separate question. In contrast, full strict-image equality already identifies the transposition loop and the identity at the double scalar in degree $n+1$. Their comparison type of degree $n+2$ is uninhabited by the fundamental-group calculation. Each actual surface retains the transposition.

### A finite range of lifting suffices under higher attachments

Here is the base-change argument needed for the asserted arbitrary attachments. It applies to any of the ten ordinary surfaces. Suppose $B=X_{\le r}$, $r\ge1$, and $u:H_B\to UQ$ is a raw map into a surface reduct that solves derived globe lifting problems through degree $d$. Then the full surface is conservative through degree $\min(d,r+2)$ in $X$. Moreover the theory of its equations on data generated through $r$, as in Response 32, is conservative through degree $d$ there.

Factor $u$ as a raw trivial cofibration $H_B\to E$ followed by a fibration $E\to UQ$. The fibration has actual globe lifts through degree $d$. For clarity, for a cofibration $i:\partial D_j\to D_j$ the derived space of squares is the homotopy pullback of the mapping spaces from $\partial D_j$ into $E$ and from $D_j$ into $UQ$ over the boundary maps into $UQ$. Changing $H_B$ by its weak equivalence to $E$ preserves the derived lifting assertion, which is surjectivity on components of the map from lifts to squares. Compute with compatible cofibrant cosimplicial frames of $i$ having $i$ in degree zero. For a fibration of fibrant models the map from lifts to the ordinary pullback of these three function complexes is a Kan fibration and the pullback computes the homotopy pullback. Surjectivity on components therefore lifts each vertex itself. Such a vertex lift is a literal raw map from the globe solving the given square. [Raw model structure and globe cofibrations](#researcher-response-3) · [Hovey, *Model Categories*, Chapter 5, framings](https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/063)

Factor $E\to UQ$ again, this time adjoining only free raw generators of degrees **greater than** $d$, solving the lifting problems for those globe inclusions by the small object argument. Call the intermediate algebra $E'$. Raw relative terms show that this changes no cell through $d$. Thus $E'\to UQ$ lifts all globes and is a raw trivial fibration. Now form
$$
 W=H_X\amalg_{H_B}E,\qquad W'=W\amalg_E E',\qquad
 Y=H_X\amalg_{H_B}UQ.
$$
The map $H_X\to W$ is a trivial cofibration. The map $W\to W'$ adjoins only generators above $d$ and is bijective through $d$. Left properness makes $W'\to Y$ a weak equivalence because it pushes out $E'\to UQ$ along the cofibration $E'\to W'$, which attaches the actual generators of $X$ outside $B$. Consequently a raw cell of $Y$ through degree $d$ at the images of prescribed old faces gives one at those very faces in $H_X$. Reflect existence along $W'\to Y$ on the fibre at those faces, use the degreewise bijection, and reflect along $H_X\to W$.

The operations and directed order of Response 31 are constructed on this same raw extension $Y$ of $UQ$. On each generic lower skeleton use its free surface algebra as in Response 32. Its full comparison with the generic strict category is available independently of $u$. Thus a full surface inhabitant through $r+2$ gives a raw cell in $Y$ at the images of the original faces. The marked-arity and cylinder proof of Response 32 likewise gives such a cell in any output degree for the equations on $r$-dimensional data. In both arguments the weak equivalence on the *actual* base had been used to reflect this last cell out of $Y$. The preceding factorization performs that step through degree $d$, proving both assertions. [Two-degree construction](#researcher-response-31) · [Free generic bases and marked arities](#researcher-response-32)

For the scalar skeleton take $r=h-1$ if $h$ is finite, and take $r\ge d$ otherwise. Apply the lemma with either occurrence model and $d=2n-1$. This proves the stated bound, including the case of an arbitrary 4-generator attached at a scalar braid and a subsequent 5-generator attached at a composite involving it. The argument also shows that at a component with at most one scalar occurrence every parallel pair above degree $n+1$ in the scalar skeleton has a raw filler in every degree, since its configuration space is contractible. The unrestricted Q1 cases on general computads and the higher-dimensional scalar comparison beyond the permutation range remain open. ∎

It varied both lower cut indices and included two successive steps at one leaf. Every reading had the indicated literal type and strict image. Its binary tables agreed leaf by leaf, including two tokens at one address, while its unprefixed list recorded the temporal order of that reading. For 56 insertions and identity prunings the nested and joined heads had the same literal type after exterior substitution and preserved the complete planar list. In 24 higher comparisons on generators with composite faces, the source positions recovered the entire coloured substitution and folding gave the original term. Rice’s weak checker accepted the higher scalar transpositions and fitted 5-, 6- and 7-cells using a new declaration at a unit-padded boundary. It also checked a two-step recoloured path and its folded higher comparison, and rejected direct use of the new declaration at the unpadded boundary. Each of the two published modes accepted the direct use and rejected both the diagonal identity claim and a boundary with the wrong transported colour in each tested rank. The configuration-space and base-change proofs give the uniform bounds. ## Researcher Response 34

**In both kernels, folding generators in any number of degrees preserves and reflects raw inhabitation at every specified boundary at least two degrees above the generators.** In fact it gives a bijection of raw fillers at that boundary. Thus every semistrict failure in this range persists after simultaneously identifying all generators with the same recursively identified attaching type, starting with all the objects. For the two published surfaces, in *every* degree, each conversion problem has a finite universal context computable from its normal form and its old type. Ordinary conservativity is equivalent to the existence of a raw witness assignment natural under every generator-preserving map.

### Recovering a raw cell from one lifted face

Let $f:Y\to Z$ be a generator-preserving map of $n$-computads. Here $n$-computad means that the generators have degree at most $n$. Attaching types are arbitrary old types, and $f$ preserves them literally under relabelling. For a raw $k$-term $t$ write $d_n^-t,d_n^+t$ for its iterated $n$-source and target when $k>n$. For either sign and either kernel, the map
$$
 \{u\in(H_Y)_k:Hf(u)=t\}\longrightarrow
 \{a\in(H_Y)_n:Hf(a)=d_n^\pm t\},\qquad u\longmapsto d_n^\pm u
$$
is a bijection. In particular each such $t$ gives a transport bijection between its two sets of lifted $n$-faces. These sets can be empty. Transport along any higher cell agrees with transport along either of its faces of degree above $n$, by globularity and uniqueness.

We need a splitting fact about *raw syntax*. Let $b$ be a raw term on a globular tree $P$, let $D\subseteq P$ be the positional support of its ordinary strict image, and label $P$ by a raw tuple $\rho$ in $Z$. Every raw lift of $b[\rho]$ under a generator-preserving map splits uniquely as $b$ evaluated on lifted labels on $D$. This assertion allows generators in every degree in both computads. At a variable the label is the specified lifted term and its globe is labelled by its actual faces. At a head, relabelling preserves its outer constructor and bound type, so parse the given lift into its actual tuple. Split each entry against the corresponding generic argument recursively. These splits glue. Indeed a face of an argument with its split labelling evaluates to the actual face of the lifted entry, by substitution. That face is the lifted tuple entry at the incident slot. Uniqueness of the split for the boundary expression identifies the two labellings there. Use induction first on degree and then on expression size, so this invocation at a face has lower degree even if the boundary expression is long.

For precision about the gluing domain, take one copy of the positional arity of each argument and glue at its globular face inclusions according to the outer tree. This is the positional arity of the substituted strict paste. This description is strict tree multiplication, obtained by replacing each position with its labelled tree and identifying the corresponding boundaries. Identity padding uses the same lower tree on both sides. On the generic tree $P$ the resulting labelled arity embeds with image $D$, by the positional support property of strict cells on a tree. Thus the face agreements just checked account for every identification required for a tuple on $D$. Every position of $D$ lies in an argument image, proving uniqueness. The same calculation works for Gray because the **ordinary projection** of each generic head has positional arity its entire globular tree. The splitting only parses raw heads and uses ordinary strict shapes on the *generic* tree. It makes no assumption about strict composition on the computads carrying the labels. [Projection and positional arities in both kernels](#researcher-response-4) · [Tree multiplication and face restriction](#researcher-response-32)

We prove the claimed bijections simultaneously for both signs, by induction on degree and then on the size of the downstairs term. There are no variables in degree $k>n$, so write $t=h[\rho]$ for a bound head on $P$. The support of $d_n^\pm h$ on $P$ is its signed $n$-boundary. This follows by taking the corresponding iterated face of its full positional arity. For a tree of height at most $n$ it is the whole tree. Split the prescribed lifted $n$-face as above. This fixes every label of degree below $n$ and the initial $n$-label in every transport string for the minus sign, or the final label for the plus sign.

Here is how to fill one interval between consecutive $n$-positions in such a string. With one end known, lift every argument of degree greater than $n$ over this interval using its specified $n$-end. These arguments have degree at most $k$, and every argument of degree $k$ is a proper subexpression of $t$. A face of one of these lifts in degree greater than $n$ coincides with the lift at its incident slot, by uniqueness in that lower degree. All the resulting labels at the opposite $n$-end agree. Indeed, the parallel generating $(n+1)$-slots in the interval are connected by $(n+2)$-slots, so the two $(n+1)$-faces of each lifted connecting slot have the same opposite $n$-end. This proves agreement along the whole interval, including the case of a single transport. The string description follows by iterating the string-of-suspensions description of a tree down to its $n$-positions. At an interval the parallel $(n+1)$-slots are the vertices of its subtree, whose edge graph is connected. All remaining incidences below $n$ agree by globularity at the known end. [Transport strings on a globular tree](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r3-2026-11-19.md#a-catt-model-of-lists-and-bijections)

Proceed along each string in the selected direction. The resulting full tuple gives a lift by applying the same bound head. Its required face equals the prescribed raw term by the initial split. Conversely any lift has that bound head, that split on the signed boundary and, by induction, precisely the assigned argument on each interval. This proves uniqueness, including for directed Gray cells when starting from the target. The latter construction lifts the given directed cell with a prescribed target.

### Reflection at an exact old type

Let $k\ge n+2$ and let $u,v$ be parallel raw $(k-1)$-cells in $Y$. A raw filler downstairs from $fu$ to $fv$ lifts from the common $n$-source of $u,v$. Its two faces must be $u,v$, by uniqueness in degree $k-1>n$. Conversely relabelling takes each upstairs filler to a downstairs one, and uniqueness gives injectivity. At $k=n+1$ the exact criterion is that *some* downstairs filler transport $u$ to $v$. The qualification matters. For $n\ge1$, identify two distinct parallel generating $n$-cells $a,b$. There is an identity between their images. There is no raw filler from $a$ to $b$, since in the ordinary strict projection from this $n$-computad all higher cells are identities and the two generators have different occurrence counts. The same projection applies in the Gray kernel.

Form $T_n$ degree by degree with one object and exactly one generator at every raw attaching type over the preceding skeleton. It is terminal among $n$-computads with generator-preserving maps. A finite $Y$ has finite image in $T_n$. That image has one object when nonempty and at most one generator at each raw attaching type. If a surface inhabits an old $k$-type in $Y$, it also inhabits its image there by relabelling. When $k\ge n+2$, raw noninhabitation survives both this map and inclusion in $T_n$. Conversely, an old type and a surface derivation over $T_n$ use finitely many declarations, closed under their attaching terms. If that old type lacks a raw inhabitant in $T_n$, it also lacks one in this finite subcomputad. Hence, for **each** surface of either kernel, conservativity on all finite $n$-computads in degrees $k\ge n+2$ is equivalent to conservativity on $T_n$ in those degrees. More locally, the same implication pulls conservativity back along *any* generator-preserving map from one $n$-computad to another in this range.

This also gives a necessary condition for an ordinary first failure in degree $k\ge5$, even when the original context $X$ has generators in degree $k$ or higher. For some $3\le j\le k-2$, the finite image of $X_{\le j}$ in $T_j$ must have an uninhabited old type in degree at least $j+2$. Indeed Response 32 finds such a type on the $j$-skeleton of the support of the failing judgement. Apply the filler bijection to its map into that finite image. Thus the obstruction on the lower skeleton survives both restoring the other declarations of degree at most $j$ and folding their generator names. [Criterion on successive skeleta](#researcher-response-32)

### Finite universal contexts for the published surfaces

Fix either published surface and a degree $k$. Consider the problem consisting of a literal old type $A$ of degree $k$ and a specified surface class $c$ at its image. A solution of the **inhabitation** problem is any raw term at $A$. For **exact rehydration** also require its surface class to equal $c$. We construct the same finite family of generator positions for both problems.

Send the pair $(A,c)$ to $T_k$ and denote its image by $s$. Over $T_k$ keep the old type literally and put the surface term in normal form. Two facts make this an effective index. The terminal name of any generator is its rank and its recursively relabelled raw attaching type. These are finite data. Also, for any generator-preserving map between raw computads, the induced surface relabelling commutes with normal form. Indeed it acts on ambient variables and preserves each bound arity and generic head type. Disc and endo reduction inspect the bound head. Pruning and insertion inspect the head of an argument and the branches of the participating trees. Relabelling therefore preserves each reduction and also preserves normality. Composite raw attaching types change none of these tests. Thus a realization of $s$ in another computad amounts to labelling precisely the displayed syntax of its raw type and its normal term, subject to their typing requirements. [Reduction and relabelling for the published surfaces](#researcher-response-27) · [Finster–Rice–Vicary, §4](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303#page=21)

Construct a finite raw computad $D_s$ with a distinguished realization of $s$ as follows.

1. Give each occurrence of an ambient generator in the two expressions its own placeholder. Keep bound heads and their types fixed. For each placeholder take its terminal generator's raw declaration and give every occurrence of an ambient generator in that declaration its own placeholder in turn. Continue through lower ranks. This produces finite templates because each declaration has strictly smaller dimension than its generator. A placeholder comes with a specified terminal name and a template for its declaration.
2. Record the equations needed to check every declaration and the old type in raw syntax, to check the normal term in the surface, and to compare its inferred type with the image of the old type. A raw equality compares literal expressions. A surface equality compares their two normal forms. In either case each comparison yields a finite list of required equalities of placeholders. To see this precisely, follow the successful checks on $s$ over $T_k$. At a variable, look up its recorded declaration template. At a head, its bound data are constant and substitution copies the indicated arguments into the result. For a conversion, run the normalization trace of the terminal expression with these occurrences marked by their placeholders. Every redex test depends on the same shapes under relabelling, and its exterior substitution or pruning map is given on the bound trees. Thus each new ambient variable occurrence carries a definite previous placeholder, including those read from declaration templates. Both normalized trees have the same constructors and bound heads, since they agree in $T_k$. Record equality of the two markings at each variable position. This procedure only traces reductions of expressions already checked in $T_k$. Its correctness for a labelling follows by induction through the check: once the preceding typing comparisons hold, the corresponding reduction sequence applies to the labelled expression and ends in normal form. Conversely every valid labelling passes these same comparisons by preservation of normal forms. Raw declaration checks use the same argument with literal tree comparison alone.
3. Identify the placeholders required equal. Whenever two are identified, also identify corresponding variable positions in their *raw* declaration templates. Their terminal names agree, so those two templates have the same constructors and bound heads. Iterate to closure on the finite set of positions. Declare one generator per resulting class in increasing dimension. Its raw declaration is independent of the representative by closure. These declarations typecheck by the recorded raw checks, starting at dimension zero. The recorded remaining checks give the prescribed old type and a surface term at its image. The term is normal since any redex in it would give the same redex over $T_k$.

The distinguished pair realizes $s$. Given any pair of shape $s$ over $X$, read the names from its old type, its unique normal term, and recursively the actual declarations of those names. This fills every template. The checks in step 2 and literal preservation of declarations force every identification in step 3. The resulting generator-preserving map $D_s\to X$ realizes the pair and is unique, since every placeholder was read from one of those data. Conversely, each such map preserves typing and normality, giving a pair of the indicated shape. This proves the universal property, including realizations that identify generators in several dimensions at once.

For example, at the single terminal vertex let $P$ be the binary word in the sole edge twice, let $a$ be the generator $P\Rightarrow P$, and take the generator $k_0:(1_P *_1 a)\to a$ in $T_3$. The old type $a\to a$ admits $k_0$ by strict unit conversion. In $D_s$ the source and target of the two parallel 2-cells are independently labelled two-edge paths $P',R'$ sharing their endpoints. Even their intermediate vertices can differ. There are two separate generators $a',b':P'\Rightarrow R'$ and one generator $(1_{P'} *_1 a')\to b'$. Indeed the comparison of the two normal faces forces the displayed occurrences of $a'$ and $b'$ to match the corresponding faces of the old type. The literal parallelism of that type identifies the two source paths and the two target paths across its faces. Each individual path imposes the usual edge incidences. Those are all the equations, as any such parallel paths and generators give a realization. Composing the raw inverse left unitor on $a'$ with the new generator solves this instance and represents the specified class in both published modes.

Consequently the published theory is conservative in all finite contexts in degree $k$ **if and only if every $D_s$ has a raw filler at its distinguished old type**. The analogue with prescribed class holds for exact rehydration. Moreover, if either assertion holds, its solutions can be chosen computably and naturally in generator-preserving maps. On input a pair, compute $s$ and $D_s$, enumerate raw terms there in a fixed effective order until the relevant check succeeds, and specialize along the unique map. The raw checks and the optional test of published normal-form equality are decidable. The asserted existence ensures that the search terminates for every input. Relabelling an input preserves $s$ and composes its unique map, so this procedure returns literally compatible raw terms. Conversely any such total witness assignment gives the corresponding conservativity or rehydration assertion by evaluation. The raw filler supplied by the generating declarations of $T_k$ need not specialize to $D_s$. The unrestricted classification in Q1 and the complete context classification in Q2 remain open. ∎

Agda 2.8.0 checked the selected 4-, 5- and 6-cells and their fold equations in both kernels. Both published modes checked the equality to the prescribed class there. They rejected a permuted input word and a different parallel generator as the prescribed value. The uniform lifting theorem and the construction of the universal contexts are paper proofs.
