## Researcher Response 35

**Neither published surface fills the old $2n$-type exhibiting the two-particle sphere obstruction in Response 33.** More generally, let $n\ge3$ and let the only positive-degree generators have degree $n$. In every such finite ordinary computad both published surfaces are conservative through degree $2n$, including arbitrary multiplicities of repeated scalars. This also proves the bound for the binary surfaces contained in published unit and associativity conversion. In particular the bound is six on the pure 3-scalar skeleton.

At degree $2n$ a permutation cannot detect the obstruction. With two distinguished scalar occurrences we construct an integral invariant of their relative position. Its target has the homology of $S^{n-1}$, and exchange of the two labels acts antipodally. Equivariance forces the invariant to detect every nonzero element of the first higher homotopy group of the raw two-particle component.

### The normal forms with two distinguished inputs

Fix one vertex, use the standard iterated identity as the two faces of each generating scalar, and take two generators $a,b$. Write $L$ for either published free algebra. We use its iterated fixed hom at the lower units, so an $i$-cell of this hom is an $(n+i)$-cell of $L$. Restrict to the component using $a$ once and $b$ once. These multiplicities are defined on the $n$-source and agree on the $n$-target. At a head they sum over the $n$-slots, meaning the depth-$n$ branches of the truncated tree. All $n$-labels in a transport string for one slot have the same multiset. [Occurrence and transport calculation](#researcher-response-33)

All positive-dimensional cells below $n$ are iterated identities on the vertex, by disc trivialisation. The same holds for cells of multiplicity zero: every argument at or above $n$ again has multiplicity zero, so induction gives an expression on the point. In a component with a single occurrence the only normal forms at and above $n$ are that generator and its iterated identities. To prove this, normalize the arguments of a head. In a normal head other than a canonical identity, no locally maximal argument can be an identity, by pruning. Every leaf must therefore reach $n$, and every $n$-slot carries positive multiplicity since its subtree has a leaf. A leaf above $n$ with one occurrence would be labelled by an identity by induction on subexpressions. This leaves a single leaf at $n$. Its arity is a disc, on which a full head acts as the top input or its iterated identity. The same disc calculation applies to generic head types in the insertion theory: every term there on a globular disc has a raw representative by Response 27, and the unit theory already trivialises that representative. [Rice, Theorem 4.2.9](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.17068#page=163) · [Rehydration on globular generators](#researcher-response-27)

In the two-occurrence component a nonidentity normal head with **two occupied slots** has exactly two leaves at $n$. Each slot has just one occurrence, so a higher leaf would have an identity label. The tree is
$$
                    P_j=D^n\amalg_{D^j}D^n,\qquad 0\le j<n.
$$
Its two top inputs are $a,b$ in one of the two orders. Call each such normal expression a generator, labelled by $(j,+)$ if $a$ comes first and by $(j,-)$ otherwise. Distinguish its entire bound head, including its type. Generators can have degree above $n$. A nonidentity normal head with **one occupied slot** has tree $\Sigma^n K$, since every leaf reaches $n$ and the truncation there has just one leaf. All its arguments at or above $n$ belong to the two-occurrence component.

In fact this component of the fixed hom is the **free published algebra** on the displayed generators, shifting their degrees down by $n$. Their attaching terms are obtained by reading their two actual faces in the fixed hom. Here a free presentation may attach a generator at an already constructed surface type. We give the normal-form verification, since freeness is what allows an invariant on these generators to evaluate arbitrary composites.

First, a normal crossing expression on a bipointed suspension of a globular arity is itself a suspension. An expression at a single pole uses only that point, by ordinary strict positional support, hence is an iterated identity. In a nonidentity normal crossing head, every stationary column would have a locally maximal positive-dimensional input at a single pole, contradicting pruning. There is exactly one crossing column. Its tree is $\Sigma J$, and its crossing arguments and bound endpoints desuspend recursively. A crossing path is just the chosen edge since its unary composition reduces on the disc. A crossing identity desuspends to the identity on its desuspended operand. Conversely suspension preserves the elementary normal-form tests. Disc and endo tests shift in dimension, and at a branching tree both the depth of the relevant branch point and the required trunk height for insertion shift by one. This also follows directly by descending through the common unary root in the insertion formula. Linear arities are covered by disc trivialisation. Iterating gives the same assertion for $\Sigma^n K$. [Crossing restriction and standard operations](#researcher-response-12) · [Finster–Rice–Vicary, Definitions 18–23](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303#page=17)

Decode a normal head on $\Sigma^n K$ by desuspending its bound head and recursively decoding its arguments at and above $n$. Decode the two-slot expressions as single generating symbols. Encode in the reverse direction by applying the suspended head to the encoded tuple, using the unique lower units, and expanding each symbol to its defining two-slot expression. The following facts check the mutual recursion on types, terms and faces. In the grouped case both operations on the bound type are inverse by the preceding suspension calculation. Identities shift to identities. In the two-slot case the attaching expressions are obtained by decoding the normalized faces, in strictly lower degree. Thus their shared faces agree inductively. The suspended single-slot operations preserve published conversion, including insertion, by the common-trunk formula. Consequently the encoding of an attaching expression is the corresponding face class, and encoding extends by the presentation property. When decoding a tuple, the incidence types can be checked in the lower degrees already constructed: their encodings are equal there by typing of the original tuple, and injectivity in those degrees identifies their decodings.

There is no additional reduction at the interface with a generating symbol. A positive-degree symbol expands to a nonidentity head on a tree of height $n$ in degree strictly above $n$. It cannot be a standard composite eligible for insertion: a standard term strictly above its tree height is an identity in the published theory. It cannot be pruned either. A degree-zero symbol could expand to a standard $n$-composite, but an $n$-slot is locally maximal in a grouped arity $\Sigma^n K$ only if $K$ is the point. Operations on this disc already act by the top cell or its iterated identity. In every other case a reduction inspecting a grouped input is exactly the suspended reduction of its decoding. A change in an ambient label cannot create a redex in a bound type. Hence encoding a normal expression gives its indicated normal form, with canonical identities treated explicitly. Conversely the classification exhausts the normal expressions, and parsing recovers every encoded normal form. This proves the free-algebra assertion in each dimension. In particular a choice of images of the symbols with the specified faces in a strict groupoid extends to a map of raw fixed-hom algebras.

### Which generators can occur in a face?

Put an order on the $2n$ labels by declaring $(j,\epsilon)<(l,\delta)$ whenever $j<l$, with either sign allowed. The only relation at the same height is equality. **Every generator in the decoded faces of a generator with label $\alpha$ has label at least $\alpha$.** This includes all occurrences inside their composite subexpressions.

Here are the strict and syntactic checks behind the assertion. Mark two distinct $n$-slots in a tree. Their label is the depth of their last common vertex and their left-to-right order, using the two particle names to read the sign. Under a strict labelling into another globular tree, suppose the two slots of an expression carry the two different marked events of the target. Its split in the expression has label at least their split in the target. It suffices to use the $n$-source and truncate the target tree at $n$, identifying its transports. At a cut of depth zero the two contributing paths traverse disjoint consecutive intervals of the target spine. The marked events must be in different target columns and in the indicated order. Conversely, if the two marked events of the target lie in a single column, a cut separating them must have positive depth. Project the contributing paths to that column and repeat one level lower, using the description of strict homs on a globular tree as products along a spine interval. This proves the inequality, with order preservation at equal depth. The same argument allows stationary factors: each factor which carries a mark must traverse its marked column. Positional support ensures that a single occurrence in the target is used only once in the strict reading. [Strict positional arities](#researcher-response-4) · [Injectivity on truncated slots](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r3-2026-11-19.md#a-catt-model-of-lists-and-bijections)

For a written expression in the scalar context impose the following condition for a fixed $\alpha$. In its ambient argument subexpressions, including the expression itself, every head where the two particles belong to distinct $n$-slots must split them with label at least $\alpha$. Expressions with fewer occurrences impose no condition. In a generic tree labelled by such a tuple, require also the bound on the split of its two occupied slots if there are two. Then evaluating any generic expression on the tuple preserves the condition. Indeed, inspect its heads before substitution. Two marks arriving via distinct slots of the tuple satisfy the just-proved strict inequality at every head separating them. Marks arriving through one slot of the tuple travel together in the generic calculation, and any separation inside its actual labels was already checked. This applies also to the iterated faces, by the transport strings. Substitution copies actual expressions into variable positions and leaves their internal splits intact.

The condition persists during normalization. Disc removal selects the specified argument. Endo removal uses the instantiated generic boundary, to which the preceding paragraph applies, and takes its identity. In a pruning or insertion the new argument entries are inherited from the participating tuples. For the new head inspect the truncated spliced tree. At a splice the inner list of children replaces one child in place. Above the splice both trees descend the same unary trunk in the inner tree. Thus two marks inherited from different exterior branches have their former divergence depth and order, including when one comes from the replacement of the selected branch. Two marks in different interior branches have their divergence depth and order in the inner tree. If the splice is above degree $n$ in the globular dimension, its common ancestor at depth $n$ remains a single slot. An empty replacement carries no particle of its own; in an identity above $n$ the retained lower slot carries its existing particles. These are all cases by the two clauses of the tree insertion recursion. A reduction in a bound type leaves the ambient split unchanged. Starting with a generator on $P_j$ and its singleton labels therefore gives the asserted bound on its normalized faces. This argument does not assume that a specialized reduction can itself be performed on the original generic tree. [Tree splice and labelling formulas](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2302.05303#page=17)

### An integral invariant of the spherical class

Let $K$ be the order complex of these labels. It is the join of $n$ copies of the two-point set, hence the boundary sphere of the $n$-dimensional cross-polytope. Switching the particle names acts antipodally. The upper set of a label $\alpha$ has a least vertex $\alpha$, so its order complex $K_\alpha$ is a cone.

For a subcomplex $E$ use the strict abelian chain groupoid $G(E)$ of its integral simplicial chains with augmentation one at objects. Concretely an $i$-cell is a signed chain tableau with rows $(c_l^-,c_l^+)$ for $0\le l\le i$, equal top entries, augmentation one in row zero, and $\partial c_{l+1}^{\pm}=c_l^+-c_l^-$. Faces truncate and select the indicated entry. Composition adds tableaux and subtracts the identity extension of the common seam. These formulas are the chain-complex construction of a strict higher category with all chain signs allowed, so all arrows have inverses. In a cone any two parallel tableaux have a filler: the difference of the prescribed top chains is an augmented cycle and coning it supplies a chain with that boundary. These choices may be made within the cone in all dimensions. [Steiner, chain tableaux and the $\lambda,\nu$ adjunction](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0403237)

Send each degree-zero symbol with label $\alpha$ to its vertex. Inductively send a higher symbol to a filler in $G(K_\alpha)$. Its boundary is already there by the bound on faces and the inclusions $K_\beta\subseteq K_\alpha$ for $\beta\ge\alpha$. Strict composition and identities stay in this subgroupoid. Swapping $a,b$ acts freely on the symbols: the two distinct variable names exchange places in their normal expressions. Choose a filler on one representative and its antipodal image on the other. By the presentation just proved this gives an equivariant map from the published two-particle fixed hom to $G(K)$. Composing with the raw-to-surface map gives an equivariant raw map.

Write $m=n-1$. On realization the raw source component is $\operatorname{Conf}_2(\mathbb R^n)\simeq S^m$, equivariantly for particle exchange, by the free little-cubes calculation. Its difference direction is the antipodal sphere coordinate. The realization of $G(K)$ is $(m-1)$-connected with $\pi_m=\mathbb Z$. Moreover there is a canonical equivariant map from $|K|$ to it inducing an isomorphism on $\pi_m$. To verify these statements, its Street nerve has $r$-simplices the augmentation-preserving chain maps $C_*(\Delta^r)\to C_*(K)$. Indeed the oriental is the category of the simplex chains, its linearization recovers those chains, and all signs are allowed in the target. This nerve is the augmentation-one part of the Dold–Kan simplicial group of $C_*(K)$. A choice of vertex identifies that part with the simplicial group of the reduced chain complex. The canonical map sends a simplex to its chain map. On normalized chains it is the linearization map, taking a fundamental cycle to that very cycle. This gives the asserted generator by the Dold–Kan identification of homotopy and homology. Realization of the strict groupoid as a weak category gives this space by the strict nerve comparison. [Orientals and simplex chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0601383) · [Ara–Maltsiniotis, comparison of the nerves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.00266) · [Scalar realization with its generator maps](#researcher-response-33)

Every equivariant map from the antipodal $m$-sphere to this target has odd coefficient on $\pi_m$. Indeed compare it with the canonical map on the lifted cellular decomposition of $\mathbb{RP}^m$, with two cells in every degree through $m$. The two maps are equivariantly homotopic on the $(m-1)$-skeleton, by the connectivity of the target, extending on one cell per free orbit and then on its mate. The two differences on the top discs are related by the involution, which acts by a sign on $\mathbb Z$. Their total contribution on the fundamental cycle is even. The canonical coefficient is one. This proof applies to a map in the homotopy theory of spaces with involution as well: the sphere with its free cell structure is cofibrant for equivariant maps with underlying weak equivalences. The raw map above therefore induces an injection on $\pi_m$. In particular a surface filler cannot connect two old $m$-discs in the fixed hom whose relative difference is a nonzero element of that group. Such a filler would also give a homotopy after evaluation in $G(K)$.

### Several occurrences and prescribed old faces

For $r$ individually named occurrences, forgetting all but each ordered pair jointly detects $\pi_{n-1}$ of $\operatorname{Conf}_r(\mathbb R^n)$. Forget the last point. The configuration fibration has a section, for instance by placing the new point beyond all the others on the first coordinate axis. The fibre is a punctured $\mathbb R^n$ and its $\pi_{n-1}$ is free abelian on the small spheres about the $r-1$ old points, by connectivity and Hurewicz. Projection to each old point paired with the last point reads its own sphere with coefficient one up to orientation and all the other spheres with coefficient zero. Earlier pairs detect the image in the base by induction. This proves the claim, including the contractible cases $r=0,1$. [Fadell–Neuwirth configuration fibration](https://journals.msp.org/mscand/article/view/2674)

Suppose an old $2n$-type has a published filler. Apply the colour lifting of Response 33 to distinguish the occurrences at its $n$-source, lifting both the old raw boundary and that surface filler. In the ordered configuration component the parallel raw faces now give two $m$-discs with the same boundary. A raw filler exists at those literal faces exactly when their difference sphere represents zero, by the globe-fibre comparison of Response 33. The lower globular boundaries may have any raw writings on the point. The fixed hom at those writings has the same configuration-space identification, since their space of transports in the point subalgebra is contractible. Projection to the published algebra takes all these writings to the lower units. For each pair keep its two names and send the other scalars to units. On the raw realization these maps forget the corresponding little cubes, by the generator assignments in the free-algebra adjunction. The surface filler specializes too. The odd-coefficient test above forces the difference sphere of every such pair to vanish. Joint detection then gives the required raw filler, which folds back to the original boundary.

For a nonstandard attaching type on the point use a raw cell on the point to forget a scalar. To keep one, fit the standard generator to its prescribed faces by the raw padding at successively higher cuts of Response 27. The point has exact rehydration in every dimension, so all the padding comparisons have identity surface class. The retained generator has the same published class, and in the derived free-algebra calculation this assignment is the canonical generator transported along the attaching maps in the point. Thus these substitutions implement the same configuration projections. Different vertices give separate components. The lower degrees are supplied by Response 33. This proves the stated $2n$ bound and settles its specific nonzero two-particle comparison for strict units and insertion. The unrestricted arbitrary-context cases of Q1, the binary cases containing interchange at this new scalar bound, and the general context classification in Q2 remain open. ∎

**Validation.** The checker enumerated the strict cells of every two-disc arity at every cut in ranks two through six. All 90 composable two-event factorizations respected the claimed cut and orientation, and both orders occurred at every strictly greater cut. It checked 18,088 marked pairs across insertions and prunings on trees of up to five edges. On the integral chain complexes it checked 3,270 cone identities, the antipodal sign on the fundamental cycle, and top-cell changes of coefficients from $-3$ to $3$ giving the predicted odd degrees. Agda 2.8.0 checked the raw 4-, 5- and 6-cell projections retaining a chosen pair and forgetting a scalar with two literally different attaching faces. In ranks three through five Rice’s two published modes accepted the specialized syllepsis, its composite with the reverse generator, and the singleton obtained by setting one input to an identity. Both modes rejected the exchanged boundary and direct identification of the composite with an identity. The normal-form presentation, the odd-coefficient theorem and joint detection in arbitrary multiplicity are the dimension-independent parts of the proof. [Reproducible checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_scalar_pairs.py) · [Rice checker revision](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/tree/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc)

## Researcher Response 36

**All ten ordinary surfaces are conservative in every dimension on a 2-scalar context with higher generators obtained by labelling the scalars by a finite globular set.** The labels may have arbitrarily high rank. There is also an exact reduction for $n$-scalars with directed labels. In each degree and for each surface separately, conservativity on the labelled context below is equivalent to conservativity on the context with just one $n$-scalar. This applies, for example, when arbitrarily many parallel generators act on a single scalar, with distinct directed paths between the same colours.

Let $n\ge2$ and let $D$ be a nonempty finite ordinary computad. Write $X_n(D)$ for the following presentation. Start at a point $x$. For each object of $D$ put an $n$-generator at the iterated identity, with both faces $1^{n-1}_x$. For each positive-dimensional generator of $D$ attach its $n$-fold suspension, using its actual attaching terms. In this suspension identify the two outer objects with $x$, and send the two poles in each degree $0<j<n$ to $1^j_x$. Thus an arrow of labels is a generator of degree $n+1$ between two scalars. A relation between parallel paths of labels attaches one dimension higher, at the corresponding suspended paths. Write $B_n=X_n(*)$ for the single scalar. Assume there is a raw weak equivalence $H_D\to UC$ into the ordinary raw reduct of a strict $\omega$-category $C$. This includes every globular set of generators, by globular arity lifting, and the strong Steiner presentations of Known 2 by their strict comparison. [Globular comparison](#researcher-response-4) · [Strong Steiner comparison](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r10-2026-11-21.md)

### Tagging higher generators with their scalar

For a finite set $J$, make $|J|$ separate copies of $D$ above the same point, giving $X_n(\coprod_J D)$. Forget the copy names by $f$. Use the occurrence model $Q$ of Response 33 for the surface in question. In $Q(1)$ each $n$-generator gets one position, and every higher generator gets the unique bijection between its singleton ends or an identity above it. This assignment is typed. The iterated $n$-ends of a suspended positive-dimensional generator are the scalars for the two object ends of its label. In $Q(J)$ give every position in a single copy its copy name. If $L$ denotes the free surface algebra, then
$$
 L X_n(\coprod_J D)\ \longrightarrow\
 L X_n(D)\times_{Q(1)}Q(J)
$$
is an isomorphism of globular sets.

Indeed the head and equation calculations for the family algebra in Response 33 concern only the globular arity of the head or rule. They apply verbatim in the presence of ambient generators above $n$. The extra check is at a variable of degree $d>n$. Its value has just one position at its $n$-source, so a lift index is exactly one $j\in J$. Use the variable in copy $j$ at this index. Both of its attaching terms lift to their literal writings in that copy. To see this, evaluate the copy inclusion on the attaching expression. Its fold is the original expression, and each contributing position has tag $j$. The two inverse identities of the family construction in smaller declaration rank therefore identify this writing with its required lift. At a variable of degree $n$ use its named copy, and at the point use $x$. This supplies all variable families in declaration order. Reading a head at its split input indices and folding its value back gives the two inverse identities just as in Response 33. The same recursion on raw expressions keeps each raw head and gives the literal lift of an old boundary at the indicated tags. Thus a surface witness of degree $k>n$ lifts together with its old boundary after giving its $r$ positions at the $n$-source distinct tags, including when it uses higher generators. [Head splitting, transport and the surface equations](#researcher-response-33)

### The directed labels at fixed configurations

Let $\mathcal D$ be the ordinary higher-categorical realization of $H_D$. The $n$-fold endomorphism category at the point of the realization of $H_{X_n(D)}$ is the free $E_n$-monoidal higher category on $\mathcal D$. To check this assertion on the actual generators, map into a pointed target $(Y,y)$. A labelling of the suspended presentation is precisely a map of the cellular presentation of $\mathcal D$ into $\Omega_y^nY$. At a label globe the space of choices is the fibre of the globe restriction at the two already assigned suspended faces. This is the globe fibre in the iterated hom by the suspension–hom adjunction. The two descriptions agree on attaching maps by substitution, so successive cellular pushouts give the same mapping space. Delooping the free $E_n$-algebra has exactly that mapping property by the two adjunctions. One can work with $(\infty,\dim D)$-categories as coefficients, since the inclusion of the resulting $(\infty,n+\dim D)$-categories has the right adjoint taking the invertible core above that dimension. In particular an arrow of $D$ retains its direction in this calculation. [Computadic realization and the fixed hom](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r4-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-13) · [Gepner–Haugseng, Corollary 6.3.11 and Remark 6.3.16](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1312.3178#page=79)

Choose $r$ distinct tags and retain the sector containing each of them exactly once. With a fixed ordering of the tags, the endomorphism category in this sector is
$$
             \operatorname{Conf}_r(\mathbb R^n)\ \times\ \mathcal D^r. \tag{1}
$$
Indeed the arity-$r$ summand of the free algebra in cartesian higher categories is the homotopy orbit of $E_n(r)\times(\coprod_J\mathcal D)^r$ by simultaneous permutation. The summands using the $r$ prescribed tags once are freely and transitively permuted, so their orbit is one copy of $E_n(r)\times\mathcal D^r$. Taking centres identifies the first factor up to equivalence with the ordered configuration space. Each tag multiset specifies an entire summand even on noninvertible morphisms. The zero-particle summand is terminal. [Free operadic algebra formula, §3.1.3](https://www.math.ias.edu/~lurie/papers/HA.pdf#page=346) · [Unlabelled configuration calculation](#researcher-response-33)

We need interpretations available on the surface witness that detect the actual projections in (1).

* Erase the labels within each tag to its pure scalar. This is a raw substitution to the pure $r$-colour context. In increasing declaration rank, the required image of a positive-dimensional label has parallel old faces in the singleton component of its tag. That component is contractible, so the globe-fibre comparison gives a raw image at those faces. The whole assignment on that copy represents the constant label functor to the point in the singleton component, since that component is equivalent to the terminal category. By the free-algebra calculation this substitution realizes the configuration projection on (1).
* To read label $i$, form a strict commutative monoidal $\omega$-category $M(C)$. Its weight-zero summand is terminal, weight one is $C$, and each integer weight at least two has its own terminal summand. There are no cells connecting weights. Tensor adds weights, acts as the identity with a zero input and uses the unique value whenever the sum is at least two. These prescriptions are strictly functorial, associative, commutative and unital. Its strict $n$-fold delooping uses tensor at the cuts below $n$ and the operations of $M(C)$ at the higher cuts. This is a strict ordinary $\omega$-category, hence interprets each of the ten surfaces. Send the labels of copy $i$ to $C$ by the given raw map and all other copies to the weight-zero unit. The assignment respects the actual attaching terms since evaluation of a suspended raw paste within a copy is strict evaluation of its label paste at the shifted cuts. On sector (1) the resulting map to the weight-one hom is projection to factor $i$ followed by $\mathcal D\simeq UC$ on realization. Indeed the operation on one input of weight one and all other inputs of weight zero is the identity on that input, at every cell dimension. The induced $E_n$-operations in this strict delooping factor through the commutative operation, so this projection is constant on the configuration factor. This verifies its action on families of cells as well as on objects.

### Reflection and its consequences

Suppose the chosen surface is conservative in $B_n$ in degree $k$, and take a surface witness at an old $k$-type in $X_n(D)$. At $k\le n$ the old boundary lies in the point and admits the required raw filler there in positive degree, by point lifting; $x$ handles degree zero. At $k>n$ lift the witness and its old faces with distinct tags. After label erasure the configuration coordinate of that boundary has a filler. For $k=n+1$ this follows from path connectivity of ordered configurations, including the point for $r=0$. For $k\ge n+2$, fold all the pure colours to the single scalar. The surface witness specializes, so conservativity in $B_n$ gives a raw filler there. Response 34 lifts it at the specified old faces in the pure $r$-colour context. This use of the covering lemma is only on computads generated through $n$.

For each label coordinate, evaluate the lifted surface witness in the corresponding strict delooping. It supplies a cell at exactly the image of the old faces, hence a filler of the derived globe boundary in that factor since $\mathcal D\to UC$ is an equivalence. In (1) these choices are independent because restriction from a globe to its boundary in a product is the product of the restrictions. They therefore give a filler in the labelled realization. For old lower boundaries written on $x$, transport to the iterated units in the point subalgebra first. Its contractible mapping data give the same transport for the configuration projection and for every label projection. The raw globe-fibre comparison now returns a term at the literal old boundary, and folding its tags gives the required inhabitant in $X_n(D)$. Conversely, to exhibit $B_n$ as a raw retract, select any object label and erase all labels to the same scalar by the singleton construction above, fixing the selected generator literally. A failure in $B_n$ thus gives one in $X_n(D)$ in the same degree. This proves the equivalence. [Exact-boundary reflection](#researcher-response-25) · [Raw covering at pure coloured faces](#researcher-response-34)

Taking $n=2$ gives all degrees for all ten surfaces by Response 10. For $n\ge3$ this gives all ten through $2n-1$ and both published surfaces and the $I$-free binary surfaces through $2n$. In particular degree six is covered for strict units and insertion even with directed changes between 3-scalars and higher comparisons of those changes. [Planar conservativity](#researcher-response-10) · [Scalar bounds](#researcher-response-33) · [The first spherical class](#researcher-response-35)

There is a degreewise version for **every finite label computad $D$**. At $k=n+l$, $l>0$, it suffices that $B_n$ be conservative at $k$ and that ordinary strict evaluation on $D$ reflect existence of an $l$-cell at old boundaries. Indeed use $C=F(D)$ in the selector. For the label coordinate of the boundary in (1), choose a raw representative of its derived boundary diagram in $H_D$. Such a representative exists because the free globe boundary is cofibrant and $H_D$ fibrant, so every component of their derived mapping space contains an actual map. A filler after strict evaluation at the original diagram gives one at the image of the representative by transport in the globe restriction fibration. This is a literal cell at that strict boundary by the same cofibrant globe test. Apply the assumed reflection on $D$, then transport the resulting raw filler back along the boundary homotopy in $\mathcal D$. This supplies exactly the coordinate needed in the product proof.

In particular no hypothesis on $D$ is needed for the label test through $l=3$, by Known 2. To apply it, a strict cell at the images of old faces has a representative under full strict-image conversion: evaluate a generating strict paste by the corresponding term composites, converting at each common seam and at the prescribed exterior faces. Known 2 returns a raw inhabitant. Thus **all ten surfaces are conservative through degree five on $X_2(D)$ for every finite $D$**; for $n\ge4$ the same unconditional bound is $n+3$, and for $n=3$ the published and $I$-free cases reach six. The strict label test itself is sharp. Take $D$ to be the diagonal 2-scalar context and work at the single particle in $X_n(D)$. The suspended braid comparison of Response 1 has degree $n+4$. Its configuration coordinate has a filler, and its strict label coordinate admits an identity. Its raw label coordinate has no filler, because the one-particle category in (1) is equivalent to $\mathcal D$ and the comparison in $D$ is the original braid comparison. This example concerns the two projections with *strict* labels; it supplies no violating surface inhabitant. [Diagonal comparison](#researcher-response-1)

There is a syntactic extension for a graph of labels. Allow arbitrary writings of the scalar attaching types on the point. For each $(n+1)$-generator require only that each of its two raw $n$-faces has exactly one occurrence of a generating scalar, counted by the ordinary strict top chain. Form a graph with one edge from its source colour to its target colour. The resulting raw model is weakly equivalent to that for $X_n$ of this graph. First transport the scalar attachments in the point as in Response 33. At a fixed common lower boundary each of the two ends of an edge is in its corresponding one-particle component, which is contractible. The ends can thus be moved independently to their named scalars after the common boundary transport. This is a homotopy of the attaching map of the boundary globe, so its cellular homotopy pushout is equivalent relative to the scalar base. All the edge attachments use only that base. A weak equivalence of free raw models preserves the context-wise conservativity test in every degree. Represent the equivalence in each direction by an actual raw map using cofibrancy and fibrancy. Such a map is a substitution on the generators, so it transports a surface witness, while raw existence at the given old boundary reflects along its realization equivalence. This proves the same conclusions for the stated raw writings.

For $n=2$ the graph case also gives the hypothesis of Response 31 for higher attachments. Each mapping space in the final hom is a disjoint union of path spaces of ordered planar configurations indexed by a permutation and a tuple of label paths. Those configuration spaces are aspherical by the forgetful fibration, whose fibres are punctured planes. Hence the mapping spaces are homotopy discrete. Every raw 4-cell has a reverse, and every parallel pair in degree at least four has a raw filler, by the globe-fibre test. Thus if the next arbitrary generator after this 3-skeleton has degree $h\ge4$, all ten surfaces are conservative through $h+1$, with unrestricted raw attaching terms in those higher degrees. Label erasure *alone* first fails to reflect exact old boundaries at degree $n+2$ among comparisons above a given configuration path: two parallel graph edges $p,q:a\to b$ erase to the same identity if the scalar writings are standard. There is no raw cell $p\to q$, since in the one-particle summand the two edges are distinct arrows of the free ordinary category. At degree $n+1$ erasure can already forget direction between different colours. The label factors in (1) retain both distinctions. The unrestricted cases of Q1 and the classification of arbitrary finite contexts in Q2 remain open. ∎

## Researcher Response 37

**For either published theory, the exact test on $X_n(D)$ uses the same theory on $D$, for every nonempty finite ordinary computad $D$.** Take $n\ge2$ and $l>0$. The surface is conservative in degree $n+l$ on $X_n(D)$ if and only if it is conservative in degree $n+l$ on the single scalar $B_n$ and in degree $l$ on $D$. The same equivalence holds for the six binary choices $S=\varnothing,A,U,AU,UI,AUI$ (juxtaposition denotes a subset). For $S=I,AI$ both conditions are necessary, and sufficiency holds with $S\cup\{U\}$ on the label context. No strictification hypothesis on $D$ is involved.

In particular, start with any finite 2-computad and apply $D\mapsto X_2(D)$ any finite number of times. All ten ordinary surfaces are conservative in **all** dimensions on the resulting contexts. Scalar braids can occur independently at every stage. The proof also gives surface models weakly equivalent to these raw bases, which allows further attachments with arbitrary raw boundaries.

### Cutting an arity at its one positive input

Here is a construction on an arbitrary ordinary raw algebra $Y$. Make a new globular set $W_n(Y)$ with one cell in each degree below $n$. Its $n$-fold hom is a disjoint union indexed by a nonnegative integer weight. In weight one use $Y$ itself. In weight zero and in each weight greater than one use a terminal globular set. Thus below degree $n$ the single cells serve as the poles of all the summands.

Weights have their ordinary strict interpretation in the $n$-fold delooping of the additive monoid $\mathbb N$ with identity higher cells. In a tuple on a tree $P$ each subtree at depth $n$ has a weight: all its vertices, which are $n$-variables of $P$, have that weight because successive vertices are joined by higher labels. The weight of a full head in degree at least $n$ is the sum of these weights, one summand per subtree, by the full positional support and the transport-string calculation of Response 33. For an expression of partial support the same rule applies on its strict positional arity. Both assertions include identity padding in the strict reading.

For output of weight one there is therefore a unique selected subtree $T$. Follow its $n$ child indices from the root of $P$. At each child index take the ordinary directed cut of Response 11, colouring the spine vertices before this child by zero and the others by one. For a degree-$n$ head this returns the object label at the selected slot. In degree $n+l$ it returns an ordinary expression of degree $l$ on $T$; evaluate it in $Y$ at the retained labels. They all have weight one. Use the unique value at every other weight and below degree $n$. These are cuts on the **generic arity**. The ambient one-object computad need not admit any nonconstant cut on its objects.

To check typing and substitution, fix a weighted tuple on $P$ and a supported subexpression of weight one. Its ordinary strict positional arity contains exactly one of its own depth-$n$ slots contributing weight one. Under the labelling this is the slot through which the selected occurrence enters. At the root, substitution concatenates the intervals of columns in the ordinary strict paths. The selected column belongs to exactly one of these intervals; the selected column of that factor is its preimage cut. For a head of positive local degree, taking its cut restricts its generic type and keeps precisely the labels in that column, by the cut-substitution formula. For a crossing path it just selects the corresponding edge label. Apply this calculation on the retained child, successively down the $n$ levels. Thus cutting an expression on $P$ and interpreting its cut agrees with interpreting its constituent heads on their selected subtrees. The same calculation on a face restricts the selected labelling to that face; above $n$ both faces have the same weight. This proves the boundary formula, commutation with substitution and naturality in raw algebra maps $Y\to Y'$. In a suspended head on $\Sigma^n T$ the $n$ cuts recover the original head on $T$ literally. In particular the raw fixed hom in weight one is the original raw algebra, including its choices of coherences. [Ordinary cut and substitution](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r7-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-4) · [Slots and full positional support](#researcher-response-33)

For the published theories the construction on a surface algebra gives a surface algebra with exactly this raw action. Indeed check each rule in the common arity carrying its two labellings. At weight one each successive cut takes an insertion to the equality proved in Response 12, using the common joined arity and the cut pulled back along the exterior substitution. The verification for **strict units alone** needs only pruning, disc and endo removal. At one cut, pruning a stationary column or a peak within it leaves the selected child and its labelling fixed; below a crossing column the retained peak and its projection substitution are the lower pruning. A pruned generating edge at the root, labelled by an identity, cannot cross the cut pulled back from the pruned arity. When the selected child becomes a point the path rule selects its vertex, and the linear-disc cases use strict-unit disc trivialisation. Disc and endo rules restrict to their respective rules or vertex selection. These checks use the generic equality on the smaller tree, so they apply again at the next selected child. In every other weight equality follows from uniqueness and the lower boundary equalities. This gives the simultaneous interpretation for converted generic types and labellings as well. [Insertion at a cut](#researcher-response-12) · [Pruning and its projection substitution, Figure 10](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2007.08307#page=12)

For a binary $S$ put $T=S$ for the six choices in the equivalence and $T=S\cup\{U\}$ for the two exceptional choices. Use $Y$ with the lower $T^+$ action of Response 15, where the superscript adjoins unary disc removal. On the supported generic arity of (i) the two projected words agree at each cut under the $T^+$ word calculation there. More precisely, one cut translates a $T^+$ derivation to a $T^+$ derivation at the selected child, preserving the raw restriction on old expressions. A positive cut lowers its index, a spatial product selects and pads its crossing operand, and the disc rule handles the resulting unary heads. For spatial whiskering interchange the stationary steps on the two sides can be absorbed by $U$ when interchange is required. Clause (ii) restricts to the lower coherence equation after these word equalities. An unused slot in (i) is handled by first restricting the word law to its common strict support using the word retraction of Response 27. This deals even with extra positive weights outside that support. Now iterate the cut calculation $n$ times; it gives a $T^+$ action on $W_n(Y)$ and hence the required $S$ action. In the exceptional cases projection of a spatial interchange can introduce a unit step on opposite sides of a temporal composite. The proof uses the indicated stronger label theory precisely there. [Law-by-law cut and disc absorption](#researcher-response-15) · [Word support retractions](#researcher-response-27)

### Returning a label filler at its original raw faces

Take a surface witness in degree $n+l$. Lift its old boundary and its witness with different tags on the particles as in Response 36. Fix one tag $i$. Map its copy of the label presentation into the weight-one summand of $W_n(H_D)$ by the generating assignment of $D$; send the other copies to weight zero. The actual suspended attachments typecheck by the suspended-head formula. Write $A_i$ for the old degree-$l$ type read in $H_D$ at the two tagged faces (for $l=1$ the base is $\star$). The same assignment in $W_n(LD)$, with $L$ the lower published or augmented binary theory specified above, reads those faces as the images of the very same raw terms by naturality. Evaluating the surface witness supplies an $L$-inhabitant of $A_i$. In the binary case disc absorption gives an inhabitant for $T$ at that old type. Label conservativity now gives a raw term in $D$ at $A_i$.

This term fills the $i$th label coordinate in the product (1) of Response 36. To verify the identification, the induced map to the iterated endomorphism category of $W_n(H_D)$ sends copy $i$ by the identity in weight one, and the other copies to the monoidal unit in weight zero. The weight-zero category is terminal and contains the actual unit. In a unital little-cubes algebra, substituting units in all but one input restricts the operations via $E_n(r)\to E_n(1)$, by operadic substitution of nullary operations. The unary space is contractible and its identity acts by the identity functor. Consequently on this tagged sector the map is projection to the $i$th copy of $\mathcal D$, also on all higher cells. This argument only uses the unital structure on the **realization**. On raw terms $A_i$ retains the particular cut expressions; their realization supplies the comparison to the projected boundary. The common lower boundary written on the raw point is transported to the iterated units as in Response 36.

The configuration coordinate is filled by the single-scalar assumption, with the pure-colour covering argument when $l\ge2$ and path connectivity when $l=1$. The empty multiset has terminal component. Combining the raw label fillers with this coordinate in (1) and reflecting along the globe-fibre comparison gives a raw filler at the original faces. Conversely any failure in $B_n$ persists by its raw retract. For a failure of the same surface in $D$, suspend the old boundary and surface witness $n$ times, then substitute the iterated units for the poles to obtain them in $X_n(D)$. Suspension preserves the word and coherence laws, pruning and insertion by their suspended substitution formulas. Any raw filler would evaluate in $W_n(H_D)$ at weight one and give an inhabitant of the original type in $D$, since cutting the suspended faces returns them literally. This proves both necessities and the stated sufficiency. [Tag lifting and the configuration product](#researcher-response-36) · [Suspension of insertion](#researcher-response-12)

The degrees at most $n$ are covered by point lifting. Since $B_2$ and every ordinary 2-computad are conservative for all ten surfaces, the asserted iteration follows, using the full binary surface as the sufficient label theory when necessary. For the two published theories and the six exact binary cases the least failing degree on $X_2(D)$, if any, is exactly two more than the least failing degree on $D$. More generally, for **every** one of the ten surfaces a universal ordinary Q1 failure can be tested on the subclass with one object, no generating arrows, exactly one generating 2-scalar and only higher generators whose iterated 2-faces are that scalar. Indeed fold $D$ to one object by Response 20 and apply the necessity construction with $n=2$. The potentially arbitrary lower-dimensional label attachments survive in the higher attaching terms. [One-object reduction](#researcher-response-20) · [The planar theorem](#researcher-response-10)

### A surface model retaining the braids and all label cells

Here is the stronger base comparison in scalar degree two. Use $R$ equal to published insertion, or to the full binary surface with unary disc removal. Suppose $H_D\to UY$ is a raw weak equivalence into an $R$-model. Then $H_{X_2(D)}$ is raw weakly equivalent to the reduct of an $R$-model. We give the construction because separate tests on each label and on the unlabelled configuration would lose the correlation between the braid and the output order of the labels.

For any colour set $J$ use the braided strict monoidal groupoid of words in $J$ and braids respecting endpoint colours. Regard it as a Gray-category $G_J$ with one object and one arrow. Its interchanger is the block crossing, with its two multiplicativity axioms given by the braid relations. Use the discrete order on parallel 3-cells. Put $P(J)=K(G_J)$ for insertion, or $P(J)=E(G_J)$ for the binary laws, with the operations of Responses 5 and 11. These are ordinary surface models. In the first model a 2-cell is a word. In the second it is an event history modulo the specified exchanges; an atom is labelled by a word. Its strand positions are the letters in the word obtained by evaluating the normal representative. In either model a 3-cell is an actual braid between the words at its two ends, and higher cells are identities. The history normalisation depends on the event addresses and stationary arrows, so renaming the letters preserves the normal representative. Consequently an uncoloured 2-cell can be coloured uniquely from its strand positions, even for an empty atom, and a braid can be coloured uniquely from its input. Recolouring commutes with all the operations: for histories a sort replaces an exchange by its block braid, with the same endpoint permutation after renaming. [History, triples and insertion models](#researcher-response-5) · [Disc action](#researcher-response-11) · [Baez–Neuchl, one-object Gray monoids and braided strict monoidal categories](https://math.ucr.edu/home/baez/neuchl.pdf)

The pure scalar map to $UP(1)$ sending the scalar to the singleton (a one-event history in the binary case) is a raw weak equivalence. To verify this first for $K(G_1)$, the raw double hom has one component per cardinality, with the $r$th component the unordered planar configuration space. Its fundamental group is the braid group $\mathrm{Br}_r$ and it is aspherical, by the configuration fibration. The target double hom is the braid groupoid. The induced map surjects on each fundamental group. At two scalars Rice's raw $E(a,a)$ evaluates to the elementary crossing or its inverse: the published unit equality identifies it with the generic square, and the unique Gray lift of that square on the generic two-column arity is the structural interchanger. Its evaluation is the single crossing in $G_1$. At $r$ scalars place this raw path at a chosen pair of consecutive positions by top composition of 2-cells with the other scalars stationary. Rebracket to and from one fixed raw composite of the $r$ scalars. The rebracketing comparisons for this vertical composite evaluate to identities, since their arities are in one strict Gray hom. Thus the image at this basepoint contains every adjacent braid generator up to inversion. [Unit calculation on the raw braid](#researcher-response-26) · [Planar configuration calculation](#researcher-response-36)

For precision, a surjective endomorphism of $\mathrm{Br}_r$ is injective. The group is finitely generated and residually finite: its faithful Laurent polynomial matrix representation separates a nonidentity matrix after reducing modulo a suitable prime and evaluating the invertible indeterminates in a sufficiently large finite field. For any finite group $H$, precomposition by a surjection permutes the finite set $\operatorname{Hom}(\mathrm{Br}_r,H)$, so every finite quotient kills the kernel. This proves the assertion, including the trivial groups for $r=0,1$. It follows that the map is an equivalence on each aspherical component. The lower cells are the point and its unit up to equivalence. For $E(G_1)$ use the raw span of trivial fibrations on triples from Response 5 with the comparison on the singleton atom chosen to be the identity. The generating assignment lifts to that triple, and two-out-of-three proves the same result. With a set of colours $J$ the comparisons are also equivalences. Over an uncoloured component both colourings are covers with fibre the assignments of the prescribed colours to its $r$ positions. In the raw configuration description this is the ordered cover modulo the colour stabilizer; in the groupoid descriptions it is unique path lifting of a coloured braid. At the fixed $r$-fold composite the map identifies those fibres position by position. Naturality in renaming now gives equivalence on the covers. [Krammer, faithful representation](https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2002/155-1/p03)

Decorate $P(1)$ as follows. At degree two attach an object of $Y$ to each position. At degree $2+j$, $j>0$, attach a $j$-cell of $Y$ to each input strand. For $j=1$ its ends are the input label and the output label at the braid's transported position. For $j>1$ the two faces have the same braid, and the faces of the labels are taken on the same strands. The lower cells are those of $P(1)$. To apply a surface head, first evaluate its $P(1)$ component. Trace each output position back to the input arity. Along an interval of 2-slots transport by the input braid, and on different slots use distinct names for all their positions. At rank two the head uses each contributing slot once: in the insertion model it concatenates event lists in column order; in the history model it substitutes the events and sorts, transporting their letters by the sorting braid. Thus the output position selects one name. Evaluate the tuple for this name in $W_2(Y)$, putting its label wherever that strand occurs and using weight zero where it is absent. Use the resulting weight-one entry for this position.

To check this operation, colour the tuple in $P(J)$ with the fresh names just used. In every evaluated subexpression over the arity each name occurs at most once in its degree-two source: the positional arity takes at most one 2-variable from each transport string. Consequently the auxiliary tuples for a nested evaluation are exactly the tuples obtained by first evaluating for each name in $W_2(Y)$ and keeping the indicated entry. Faces and substitution commute by this observation. Both sides of a rule have the same $P(J)$-value on the named tuple, so at each output position they use the same name. The $W_2(Y)$ equation for this name gives the same label there. Use the common supported arity for a binary word law and the common joined or pruned arity for a published rule. Head congruence follows by the same check on the head's arity at the interpreted faces. This defines an $R$-model $V$, including its operations on mixed labellings and surface-formed types. The construction with tagged positions in $P(J)$ gives the same model with tags remembered, and forgetting tags is a surface map.

Assign the generators of $X_2(D)$ by taking singleton strands with the actual images of their labels in $Y$. This gives a raw map $H_{X_2(D)}\to UV$. To prove it is a weak equivalence, first mark the $r$ positions in a component with distinct tags. In the raw fixed hom of the target the tagged sector is literally the product of its tagged $P(J)$ sector and $r$ copies of $Y$. In a suspended hom operation every name on the input passes through the sole depth-two subtree, and evaluating its label cuts the suspended head back to the original operation. Thus these product coordinates preserve all the ordinary raw hom operations. The comparison on realization in these coordinates is the pure configuration comparison and $r$ copies of $\mathcal D\to UY$. The first follows by forgetting the $Y$ entries and the free-algebra calculation; the others follow by applying the one-tag construction and inserting the nullary operations at the other particles. All are equivalences.

Here is descent to the untagged hom at exact ends. Choose an ordering at each of two objects with $r$ strands. In the source realization the mapping category is the disjoint union over permutations of the mapping categories between the corresponding tagged objects, by the simultaneous permutation orbit formula in Response 36. In the target every arrow has one braid and hence one of those permutations. All higher comparisons preserve it, and its source tags uniquely extend along the braid. After fixing the permutation, the mapping category is the one at those tagged ends: already in the raw model its operations use the same braids and strand labels, with uniquely determined tags. Distinct cardinalities have empty hom on both sides. The equivalence on the tagged sectors proves full faithfulness. Each untagged object lifts to a tagged one, giving essential surjectivity there as well. At the point and its unit the corresponding fixed-hom comparison now proves the required equivalence of the whole raw models.

By Response 26 the required label base is available for every finite 2-computad, separately for the full binary model (which satisfies disc removal) and insertion. The construction thus supplies both bases after every finite iteration of $X_2$. If such a base is the generating $(h-1)$-skeleton and all additional generators have degree at least $h$, with $h\ge4$, all ten full surfaces are conservative through $h+1$, with arbitrary higher attachments. The equations on data generated through $h-1$ are conservative in **every** output degree of that extension. These follow by applying Responses 31 and 32 to the raw weak equivalences into the two decorated models. For example the result applies above the 4-skeleton consisting of the point, a 2-scalar $a$, and a 4-generator $u:1_a\to1_a$. It allows arbitrary 5-generators at composites involving $u$ and arbitrary subsequent declarations. Keeping the braid of the labels is essential in this example. [Replacement under arbitrary higher attachments](#researcher-response-32)

The product criterion and the decorated models apply to unary label attachments in the stated sense. An arbitrary presentation can also introduce interactions whose source or target involves several particles. The unrestricted ordinary and Gray classifications in Q1 and the remaining contextual cases of Q2 are open. ∎

**Validation.** In scalar ranks two through four, all 72 selections of a weak label from a mixed term had the prescribed raw faces. Eighteen nested cuts through actual interchange comparisons commuted literally with substitution. Agda 2.8.0 checked the selected label types and substitution equations. The checker also compared 90 strand transports through braid relations against the abelianized Artin action, distinguishing the pure square from an identity and retaining the order of two label steps. Rice's weak, strict-unit and insertion modes accepted a label braid with a second particle present and a fitted higher comparison, and rejected its proposed identity conversion at each tested rank. The general equivalences and the closure of the planar bases under decoration are paper proofs. [Reproducible checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_weight_cut.py) · [Rice checker revision](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/tree/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc)

## Researcher Response 38

**In an ordinary scalar 3-computad, all ten surfaces are conservative in every dimension if each generating 3-cell has exactly one scalar occurrence in its target.** The source may have any number, including zero. Repeated inputs, parallel rules and arbitrarily long chains of merges are allowed. The dual result holds if each source has one occurrence and the targets are arbitrary. Here a scalar 3-computad has one object, no generating arrows, generating 2-scalars and generating 3-cells. Counts refer to the ordinary strict top chain of the actual attaching term.

More generally, let $n\ge2$, with scalars in degree $n$ and rules in degree $n+1$, and suppose at least one scalar is present. Under the single-output hypothesis, conservativity of **each** of the ten surfaces in a given degree is equivalent to its conservativity in that degree on $B_n$. This equivalence also holds under the dual single-input hypothesis. The single-output case includes $g:a*_{n-1}a\to a$: no positive integer weight on $a$ is preserved by that rule. The proof assigns trees to individual occurrences of a derivation.

### The mapping spaces of a presentation by merges

First take standard scalar boundaries at the point. For every rule $g$ choose an enumeration of the $p_g$ scalar occurrences in its $n$-source. To interpret a source with repeated colours, first distinguish its occurrences in the pure scalar raw syntax. In the ordered configuration description this specifies a point $\alpha_g$ of $E_n(p_g)$ up to transport; use any representative. The configuration of its singleton target can likewise be transported to the unary unit. These choices describe the actual attaching map: before the rules are adjoined the $n$-fold hom is the free little-cubes algebra on the scalar colours. Moving a boundary along these paths gives an equivalent cellular pushout relative to this base. This also allows arbitrary scalar attaching writings on the point, by the boundary transport of Response 36.

Let $P$ be the ordinary free coloured symmetric operad on one operation for each $g$, with those ordered input ports and its one output colour. Give it a map $p:P\to E_n$ by $g\mapsto\alpha_g$ (for example in simplicial operads via singular complexes). In the realization of the raw computad its $n$-fold hom is the underlying category of the **relative monoidal envelope** $\operatorname{Env}_{E_n}(P)$. To verify the derived universal property, the free discrete operad used here is already built by cofibrations of simplicial operads: adjoin the colours, then attach at each profile the free corolla on $\varnothing\hookrightarrow\Delta^0$. Identifying two input colours does not impose invariance of the operation under their transposition; the generator still has ordered ports. These pushouts compute homotopy pushouts, also for nullary corollas, since the attaching cofibrations have cofibrant domains and the stages are cofibrant. Thus mapping this presentation into an operad over $E_n$ specifies colour images and operations over the indicated points, with their full spaces of choices. For an $E_n$-monoidal category a lift over $\alpha_g$ is precisely a morphism from its $\alpha_g$-product of the input objects to the output object. By the envelope adjunction this is the mapping property of the indicated $E_n$-monoidal category. Delooping $n$ times gives exactly the pointed cellular mapping property of the raw presentation, including its directed $(n+1)$-generators. As in Response 36 one can work in $(\infty,n+1)$-categories here. [Cisinski–Moerdijk, simplicial operads](https://arxiv.org/abs/1109.1004) · [Hinich–Moerdijk, comparison including constants](https://londmathsoc.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1112/topo.70003) · [Lurie, *Higher Algebra*, Construction 2.2.4.1 and Proposition 2.2.4.9](https://www.math.ias.edu/~lurie/papers/HA.pdf#page=211) · [Delooping at the scalar base](#researcher-response-36)

An object is represented by a tuple $x$ of $r$ named input positions and a configuration $\alpha\in E_n(r)$. For a second such object $(y,\beta)$ the mapping space is
$$
 \coprod_{f:x\to y\ {\rm active\ in}\ P^\otimes}
       \operatorname{Path}_{E_n(r)}\big(\alpha,\ \beta\circ p(f)\big).
 \tag{2}
$$
Here $f$ is a rooted forest with one tree per position of $y$. Its leaves use each numbered position of $x$ exactly once; a nullary node uses no input. A tree can also be a single input wire. The map underlying an active arrow sends every input to its root, and the operations at the roots specify exactly these trees with their port orders. To derive (2), take the fibre of the relative envelope at the unique colour of $E_n$. A morphism is an arrow on the source tuples together with a commuting triangle of active operations ending at that colour. For fixed $f$ the space of triangles is the path space displayed in (2). The category of operators of the discrete free operad has discrete hom sets, yielding the coproduct. This is the homotopy-pullback computation of the fibre and thus applies after operadic fibration replacement. Fixing the actual two objects fixes their tuples in this hom calculation, including when colours repeat.

For $n=2$ every summand of (2) is homotopy discrete by asphericity of ordered planar configurations. Thus the double hom is equivalent to an ordinary category. Every raw 4-cell has a reverse and every old type in degree at least five has a raw inhabitant: after fixing its lower boundary the question above degree four is a higher globe problem in a homotopy discrete mapping space with an already specified common boundary. Transport on the raw point and the exact globe-fibre test return the terms at the original faces. Response 5 gives ordinary conservativity through degree four for all ten surfaces, proving the first assertion. Response 31 now also applies when arbitrary higher generators are attached above this 3-skeleton: if the next generating degree is $h\ge4$, all ten are conservative through $h+1$. Neither conclusion requires a bound on the number of times a rule is used. [Planar asphericity](#researcher-response-36) · [Four-dimensional theorem](#researcher-response-5) · [Higher attachments](#researcher-response-31)

### Transporting a state through a change in the number of positions

Here is the surface calculation used at general $n$. Give each scalar colour $c$ a set $V_c$ of possible states. Use the degree-$n$ tables or lists of Response 33 with those scalar colours. For a value $u$ let $V(u)$ be the set of assignments of a state in $V_c$ to each position coloured $c$. In degree $n+1$ admit **all functions** $V(u)\to V(v)$ between parallel ends, even when their cardinalities of positions differ. Above this take identity towers on those functions. Lower cells are the singletons of Response 33. There is also a model over this one in which every $n$-cell has a specified state assignment, and an arrow must take its specified input to its specified output.

We verify the operations in both models. At degree $n$ the version with assignments uses the same table or list construction on colours $(c,s)$ with $s\in V_c$. On a tuple for an $(n+1)$-head, the starting state on its evaluated source splits into assignments on exactly the initial $n$-variables of the depth-$n$ subtrees. This uses the position injections at degree $n$ and the one-occurrence-per-transport-string calculation on the generic source. Pass these states along each string by applying the functions on its $(n+1)$-variables in order. Evaluate the generic target with the resulting states on its $n$-variables. This gives the required output function. If states are already specified compatibly on all entries, the output is that specified target state, by induction on the source and target expressions. More generally in any tree, on a tuple with compatible states, any $(n+1)$-expression takes its evaluated source state to its evaluated target state: at a variable this is compatibility, and at a head the states induced on its argument tuple are compatible by induction, so the just-described evaluation applies. This proves compatibility with substitution.

For an $(n+2)$-head prescribe an arbitrary state at its iterated $n$-source. Extend along all its transport strings as above. If there are parallel $(n+1)$-slots in one interval, the $(n+2)$-slots connect them and their assigned functions are equal. Thus the extension is simultaneous on the arity. Both evaluated faces take this input to the state of the same $n$-target, so they agree as functions. This defines the required identity at the boundary. The same argument works for generic types formed with conversion. In detail, at degree $n$ the equations hold for arbitrary refined colours on the supported $n$-slots by Response 33. At $n+1$ use the common arity for a rule and propagate an arbitrary input assignment. The two evaluated target assignments agree by the rank-$n$ equations on the refined entries. For a word equation take the support of its common ordinary strict paste and restrict the law there as in Response 27; the initial state need cover only this support. For (ii) the supported word equality identifies the two states and gives the identity function. For insertion and pruning use the joined and pruned trees; the two evaluations with their common compatible tuple agree on its target state, including for the exterior and interior substitutions by the substitution check. Disc and endo removal use the same argument on their own arities. In applying a rank-$n$ boundary equality only the supported $n$-variables are needed: their strict support has height at most $n$, and the two expressions and their equality restrict to it by Response 27. No requirement that a higher transition be a colour-preserving bijection enters this check. Congruence of head types at a fixed arity follows by equality of the initial and final refined boundary evaluations. In greater degrees parallel functions already determine all higher cells. This proves the asserted actions for each of the ten surfaces. [Positions under the laws](#researcher-response-33) · [Restriction of equations to their support](#researcher-response-27)

Use as $V_c$ the set of finite operation trees of output colour $c$, allowing in addition individually named leaves in their assigned colours. At a generator $g$ the function grafts the input trees onto its ordered ports and assigns the resulting tree to its one output position. The order of ports is identified with the positions of the actual raw source by distinguishing its occurrences before evaluating it. This prescription works for tables as well as lists: one can use as port names the actual occurrences of the raw source. A nullary generator builds its nullary tree.

There is a corresponding computad of copies. Each tree of colour $c$ supplies an $n$-generator at the original scalar type. For each tuple of input trees at $g$, include a generator at the original raw attaching terms recoloured by that input and its grafted output. Folding these declarations forgets their tree names. A surface witness above degree $n$ lifts, with its old faces, once its initial positions have been given leaf names. For clarity, the family-algebra proof of Response 33 applies to the map from the specified-state model to the function model just constructed. At an $n$-head split the given state using the position injections. At higher heads its input determines all argument states by forward propagation on the arity as above. A lifted output over a function is determined by its input, so these splits respect faces and compose on nested heads. For an equation use the same compatible state tuple on the common supported arity as in the preceding verification. The evaluations in the free surface algebra of copies then agree by that equation. This gives the family action of Response 32. Its generator entry at a given index is precisely the recoloured declaration just described, including its prescribed output tree. Evaluating in this algebra gives the lift. Keeping each old raw head in the same splitting recursion gives literal lifts of its old faces. The argument works with infinite tree sets; the lifted derivation uses only finitely many declarations and their dependencies. [Family action and its inverse identities](#researcher-response-32) · [Lifting the old and surface expressions together](#researcher-response-33)

### Reading the forest and its path separately

Use distinct fresh leaves at the $r$ positions of the initial $n$-cell of the lifted boundary. In the envelope of the computad of copies, at most one forest in (2) can have these inputs and its specified ordered output tree names. Read each output backwards. An initial leaf identifies its own input position. A node gives its original generator, its ordered tuple of children, and hence the specific copied generator. A nullary node is distinguished from all initial leaves. This recursively recovers the entire forest. Repeated closed subtrees cause no ambiguity: their places are specified by root positions and ordered ports, and they use no input positions. Conversely the actual tree labelling propagated by a witness gives a forest using precisely the initial inputs once. At a generator grafting has this property; at a head propagation composes disjoint collections of graftings and rearranges their positions. This latter assertion can equivalently be checked by induction on an $(n+1)$-expression with distinct leaves on its source. Thus at degree $n+1$ a forest exists and (2) has a path in its summand by connectivity of $E_n(r)$. At degree $n+2$ both lifted raw arrows at the boundary lie in the same forest summand by uniqueness. In larger degrees their common arrow boundary already fixes the summand.

There is a raw substitution from the copies to the pure scalar context on the fresh leaves. Send a tree to a chosen composite of its leaves and use the unit for an empty list of leaves. Send an initial leaf to its singleton generator. At each copied rule, the two substituted old faces use the same multiset of leaves, so choose a connecting raw $(n+1)$-cell by the pure configuration calculation. For nonstandard lower faces use point transport at the prescribed raw boundary. All choices concern the given direction of the rule. This substitution takes a whole path space in the selected forest summand of (2) **equivalently** to the mapping space at the two images in the pure context. Indeed its input tuple consists of $r$ different singleton leaves. On the space of structural configurations of this tuple the induced map is the ordered configuration equivalence, by the generator assignment in the free-algebra formula. A morphism in the $f$-summand consists of the path from $\alpha$ to $\beta\circ p(f)$ followed by the fixed application of $f$ under $\beta$. Applying the substitution therefore sends its variable path by that configuration equivalence and then composes with the fixed image of this application. The latter is a path in the pure configuration space, and composition with a fixed path is an equivalence of path spaces, by composing with its reverse. This proves the assertion also for $r=0$, where the path space is contractible. It does not require a positive weighting on the original colours.

If the single-scalar theory is conservative, the specialized pure old boundary in degree $k\ge n+2$ has a raw filler. Indeed the specialized surface witness can be folded to $B_n$, and the high covering of Response 34 returns the raw filler to the distinct leaf colours. By the equivalence on the identified forest summand it gives a filler of the original mapping-space boundary. The exact globe-fibre test and folding the copies give the required raw term. Degrees at most $n$ use the point; degree $n+1$ was covered by forest existence. Conversely include the pure context on a chosen scalar. In (2) its mapping spaces comprise precisely the summands on its tuples whose forest consists of identity wires with their possible permutations. Each such summand maps by the same path-space formula, and introducing operations creates additional disjoint summands. Hence existence at any pure old boundary of degree at least $n+2$ reflects along the inclusion. The pure context has no failures in smaller degrees. This gives necessity without requiring a substitution sending a merger on the same colour to a pure scalar path. [Pure scalar covering](#researcher-response-34) · [Reflection at fixed old faces](#researcher-response-36)

For the single-input version take the opposite of the final $(\infty,1)$-category. Reversal there is an involutive, product-preserving equivalence on the theory of $(\infty,1)$-categories and hence on their $E_n$-algebras. Its cellular presentation has the reversed directed rules; (2) now reads paths on the target configuration. On surface terms use functions from target states to source states, propagating backwards along each transport string. The rule and head checks above compare functions at an arbitrary state at that end, and the tree is grafted onto the original input. This gives the same proof at the actual face writings, without a duality assertion about a particular surface normalizer.

For example consider $m:a*_{n-1}a\to a$. The two merge orders on three inputs, fitted to the same source by the ordinary associator, have distinct forests and no raw comparison. At two inputs precompose $m$ with the diagonal exchange $E(a,a)$ twice. This has the same forest as $m$. For $n=2$ the extra path is a nonzero winding in the ordered configuration of two points, by the single-crossing computation of Response 37, so no raw 4-cell compares the two paths. For $n\ge3$ the ordered space is simply connected and there is a raw $(n+2)$-cell at these faces. In particular the forest alone would give the wrong raw answer in the planar case. For $n\ge3$ the general reduction gives all ten surfaces through $2n-1$ and the published and $I$-free surfaces through $2n$, now with unrestricted numbers of inputs for each single-output rule. The unrestricted classifications in Q1 and Q2 remain open. ∎

**Validation.** The checker evaluated 42 typed forests in scalar degrees two through four. It tested both merge orders at every permutation of three named leaves, a merge with one nullary branch, and a merge with two nullary branches. Six lifted paths folded to their original raw terms and expanded at the prescribed leaf boundaries. Agda 2.8.0 checked the parallel merge types, the fold equations and the typed expansions. Rice's weak, strict-unit and insertion modes accepted both merge orders, a birth followed by a merge, and a double exchange followed by a merge. Each mode rejected the proposed identity comparison between the two merge orders and between the double exchange followed by a merge and the original merge. These controls check definitional equality. Formula (2) determines whether a raw comparison exists. The general envelope calculation and the state-propagation argument are paper proofs. [Reproducible checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_scalar_forests.py) · [Rice checker revision](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/tree/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc)

## Researcher Response 39

**All ten ordinary surfaces are conservative in every dimension on a finite 3-computad if each generating 3-cell has a generating variable as target.** The 2-skeleton is arbitrary. In particular its 2-generators can have composite or identity boundaries, and a 3-rule can merge an entire diagram of 2-cells. The same result holds with source and target interchanged throughout the top declarations.

More generally let $B$ be any finite ordinary $n$-computad, $n\ge2$. Form $D$ by adjoining $(n+1)$-generators $z:s_z\to g_z$ where $g_z$ is literally an $n$-variable and $s_z$ is any parallel raw term. Fix one of the eight binary or two published surfaces. In each degree $k$, conservativity on $B$ implies conservativity on $D$. For $k\ge n+2$ the implication is an equivalence. For $k\ge n+3$ even the assertion that **every** raw $k$-type is inhabited passes from $B$ to $D$. All three statements have the single-source dual. We first give the raw mapping-space argument that allows the lower skeleton to be arbitrary.

### A fresh target with a specified incoming arrow
