Start with $B$ and form a finite sequence $C$ of pairs of declarations. At each step take an already formed $n$-term $s$, introduce a **fresh** $n$-variable $t$ of its type, and introduce $z:s\to t$. Later pairs may use $t$ repeatedly. Write $i:B\to C$ for inclusion. There is a raw substitution $e:C\to B$ (in the direction of maps of free algebras) fixing $B$, sending $t$ to $e(s)$ and $z$ to $1_{e(s)}$. It is well typed since the added $n$-variable has exactly the type of $s$.

Realize these computads as in Response 36. Fix a raw boundary below degree $n$ and take its iterated hom. Denote the resulting $(\infty,1)$-categories by $H_B,H_C$, so $n$-cells at these poles give objects, and $(n+1)$-cells give morphisms. The lower poles are in the common skeleton. For $x\in H_B$ and $y\in H_C$ the induced map is an equivalence of spaces
$$
 e:\operatorname{Map}_{H_C}(i x,y)\ \longrightarrow\
       \operatorname{Map}_{H_B}(x,e y).                                      \tag{3}
$$
It suffices to construct, on each such hom, a natural transformation $\eta:ie\Rightarrow 1$ that restricts to the identity on $iH_B$. To do so take the arrow category $\operatorname{Fun}([1],-)$ in the *innermost* enrichment direction. This functor preserves cartesian products, with evaluation at either end and the constant-arrow functor product-preserving as well. Applying it to hom objects and their composition, and iterating the change of enrichment through the $n$ outer directions, defines an $(\infty,n+1)$-category with these arrow categories as its fixed iterated homs. One may take the same objects at each enriched step before completion. Completion is fully faithful on homs at those objects. Thus a map into this target gives ordinary natural transformations on the fixed homs, compatible with composition in the other directions. [Haugseng, comparison with iterated enrichment](https://arxiv.org/abs/1312.3881) · [Cellular realization and fixed homs](#researcher-response-36)

On $B$ take the constant arrows on $i$. Suppose the map has been constructed through the preceding pairs, with evaluations $ie$ and the inclusion there. Its value on $s$ is an arrow $\eta_s:ie(s)\to s$. Assign to the new $t$ the composite $z\circ\eta_s$. To the new $z$ assign the square from $\eta_s$ to this composite with sides $1_{ie(s)}$ and $z$. A compositional triangle of the pair $(\eta_s,z)$ provides precisely this square, including the identity side. These are cells in the same iterated hom, since $s,t$ have the same actual lower type. The cellular pushout for the pair extends the map with the specified evaluations, up to homotopy relative to the preceding stage. Explicitly the new object and its incoming arrow in the arrow category are the chosen composite and square. Their endpoint diagrams are $(ie(s),1_{ie(s)})$ and $(t,z)$, the two required attaching diagrams. Induction constructs $\eta$, with the endpoint identifications relative to $B$.

Postcomposing $i(p)$ with $\eta_y$ gives a map back in (3). Its composite after $e$ on the left-hand mapping space is homotopic to the identity by naturality and $\eta_{ix}=1$. Its composite on the right-hand space is postcomposition with $e(\eta_y)$. This is an equivalence because $B$ has generators only through degree $n$, so its innermost hom at these poles is a space. Together these two calculations prove (3). In particular the calculation respects all higher homotopies and applies at any prescribed parallel faces in the mapping space. It uses a directed arrow $z$ only in its declared direction.

### Applying the comparison to repeated rules

Give each $n$-generator of $B$ a leaf bearing its name. Use the tree states of Response 38, grafting a node $z$ onto the states of the occurrences in the actual raw $s_z$ to give the state on $g_z$. All lower generators map to the unique cells below $n$ in the state model. The original lower cells and their writings are retained in the computad of copies. Specifically start this computad with the whole of $B$. For each node with its tuple of input trees, recolour the occurrences of $s_z$ by those trees, obtaining $\bar s_z$. Add a fresh $n$-variable of the type of $g_z$ named for the resulting tree, and a rule from $\bar s_z$ to this variable. The lower boundary of $\bar s_z$ is the original one literally: recolouring an $n$-slot changes neither of its $(n-1)$-faces. A nullary node uses the actual term with no $n$-occurrences. Ordering the nodes by height gives exactly the pairs of the preceding construction. Write $q$ for folding the copies to $D$.

We spell out where the state-lifting argument applies. The rank-$n$ position injections and transport strings of Responses 33 and 38 are on the **generic arity** of a head. In the state model they depend on the input $n$-positions; in lower dimensions every value is the singleton. Thus arbitrary $B$ has the required assignment. In the family algebra of lifts, a generator below $n$ has its original entry, and an $n$-generator has the copy of the requested name over its original lower boundary. A generating $z$ with a compatible input assignment has the arrow of the new pair at precisely the recoloured raw faces. In a head, split the state at its $n$-source, propagate through each higher slot, and evaluate the same head on the lifted tuple. At lower slots take the original terms. The face and equation checks on this tuple are exactly the generic checks of Response 38; none used the scalar hypothesis on the ambient lower declarations. Applied to a raw expression this recursion keeps its syntax and gives a unique raw lift from the specified input states. For an old face of a surface judgement use this raw recursion on its actual writing. The assignment on that face determines its literal lift: at rank $n$ split among the occurrences in that writing, and above it the input determines each slot along a transport string. Thus equal prescribed raw faces with the same assignment lift identically. Any single derivation uses finitely many trees, closed under taking children, so (3) need only be used on a finite such $C$. [Family action on state assignments](#researcher-response-38) · [Occurrence lifts at arbitrary attaching types](#researcher-response-33)

Start a surface witness in $D$ with the original leaf at every initial $n$-position. Its initial $n$-cell in $C$ is then $ix$ for the original raw $x$. Write $y$ for its lifted final $n$-cell. For $k=n+1$ it supplies a decorated choice of $y$ above the prescribed raw target. For $k\ge n+2$ it lifts the old parallel faces at a common decorated boundary. In particular the two lifted $(n+1)$-faces at degree $n+2$ agree on their literal $n$-target: the common assignment there is split on the same raw target writing. Apply $e$ to the lifted surface judgement. This gives a witness at the expanded old boundary in $B$, since a raw substitution between presentations acts on all ten theories. If the surface is conservative there in degree $k$, take a raw filler in $B$. For $k=n+1$, (3) gives a point in the original mapping space. For $k>n+1$, it reflects existence of a filler at the specified higher boundary in that mapping space. Concretely an equivalence of spaces induces equivalences on spaces of paths with specified endpoints, and iterating does the same at the given globular boundary. The globe restriction fibration returns a raw filler at the literal faces of $C$. Folding by $q$ completes the proof. At $k\le n$ the judgements use the base, by the dimension bound on arguments and restriction to the support of the strict value. [Exact globe-fibre test](#researcher-response-36) · [Restriction to a subcomputad](#researcher-response-27)

Conversely for $k\ge n+2$, a raw $D$-filler at old $B$-faces has ordinary strict value an identity on an old label, since $D$ has dimension $n+1$. Its strict value lies in $F(B)$, so it is already a raw term on $B$ by support restriction. This gives the reverse conservativity implication. For the assertion about **all** old types, take parallel $(k-1)$-terms of $D$ with $k\ge n+3$. Lift them separately by raw recursion from the original leaves. They share a raw $(n+1)$-face, and their whole common boundary lifts identically by uniqueness from those leaves. They therefore give a parallel pair in $C$ from $ix$ to the same $y$, without assuming any surface witness. Apply $e$, use the raw-type hypothesis in $B$, then (3) and $q$ as above. For the dual theorem start at the terminal leaves and propagate backwards. At a fresh source $t$ with $z:t\to s$ construct $1\Rightarrow ie$ by assigning $\eta_s\circ z$ to $t$. The same square in the opposite innermost hom proves the mapping-space equivalence for maps **to** old objects. The surface proof uses the backwards functions of Response 38.

The degree $n+1$ argument also gives an exact test at an individual raw boundary $x\to v$. For either raw or surface inhabitation, consider every decoration $y$ of the occurrences of $v$ by finite trees of the indicated colours. Test $x\to e y$ in the **same theory** on $B$. The original type is inhabited iff at least one of these tests succeeds. Necessity is the lifting argument. For sufficiency, $\eta_y$ has a raw representative at $ie y\to y$ by the globe test. Compose the given base witness with this representative and fold. It is important to allow all decorations of the terminal face in this degree.

Starting at any finite ordinary 2-computad and iterating these extensions gives all ten conservativity assertions in all dimensions. The orientation can be chosen separately at each new degree. If the final dimension is $d$, every raw type of degree at least $d+2$ is inhabited for $d\ge3$: for the first step use Response 10 in degrees at least five, and for each subsequent step apply the high-type assertion. By Response 26 the initial 2-skeleton is weakly equivalent to a reduct of each surface. Response 32 now passes that property through each step, using the high-type bound. Hence if the next arbitrary generators first occur in degree $h>d$, the ten surfaces are conservative through $h+1$ even with unrestricted attachments there and subsequently. In particular a failing ordinary context of least generating dimension for a specified surface and output degree must have, in its highest occupied rank, both a declaration with a nonvariable source and a declaration with a nonvariable target. They may be the same declaration. The unrestricted case in Q1 still includes such presentations. [Planar base](#researcher-response-10) · [Models of the base](#researcher-response-26) · [Successive-skeleton and gap bounds](#researcher-response-32) ∎

### A gluing condition equivalent to the unrestricted ordinary question

Fix one surface and one output degree $k$. Consider the following restricted family of pushouts of finite presentations. Start with an $n$-computad $B$, $n\ge2$, and a finite list of parallel pairs $s_j,t_j$ of $n$-terms. Add to $B$ a fresh $n$-variable $w_j$ of their common type for every pair, obtaining $B'$. Over $B'$ one side $D_-$ freely adds just $a_j:s_j\to w_j$. The other side $D_+$ freely adds just $b_j:w_j\to t_j$. The two sides use the same indexed variables, and the original attaching terms use none of them.

The universal ordinary assertion in degree $k$ is **equivalent** to closure of conservativity in that degree under precisely these pushouts: if $B',D_-,D_+$ are conservative, so is $D_-\amalg_{B'}D_+$. In one direction this follows immediately from universality. For the other, use induction on generating dimension, starting with Response 10. Given an $(n+1)$-computad $X$, take $B=X_{\le n}$ and the actual top declarations $z_j:s_j\to t_j$. The induction hypothesis gives conservativity on $B'$, which still has dimension at most $n$. The two orientations of the theorem give it on $D_-$ and $D_+$. The closure hypothesis gives it on their pushout $P$.

Map $X$ into $P$ by the identity on $B$ and the top composite of $a_j,b_j$ at $z_j$. A raw return sends $w_j$ to $s_j$, $a_j$ to $1_{s_j}$, and $b_j$ to $z_j$. The round trip on $z_j$ is the left-unit padding of $z_j$. Its raw unitor gives an invertible comparison relative to the two original faces. These comparisons give a homotopy of the two maps $X\to X$ relative to $B$: the space of maps out of this cellular extension with the base map fixed is the product of the spaces of fillers at the individual attaching boundaries, and the unit comparison is a path in each such space. Thus raw inhabitation reflects at every translated old type by the globe restriction fibration. Every surface inhabitant translates along the forward substitution. A failure in $X$ would give one in $P$, proving the induction. In particular, if a universal case fails, there is a failing pushout of this form with both pieces and their intersection conservative in the failing degree. This isolates the remaining gluing question even for contexts with identities in both faces of an original declaration. [Reflection at a prescribed type under a homotopy retraction](#researcher-response-24) ∎

**Validation.** At ranks two, three and four the checker formed a horizontal composite at a nontrivial lower boundary, an identity at that boundary, and a rule merging their fresh targets. It compared simultaneous evaluation with both serial orders and repeated the checks for fresh sources. Agda checked the 12 higher comparisons at their literal boundaries and their 24 images under folding and expansion. It also checked the two-step presentation of a rule having composite faces on both sides and its return unitor at each rank. Rice's weak, strict-unit and insertion modes accepted both orientations of the path and its raw boundary correction in rank two. The two published modes also accepted direct identity conversion at the padded boundary. All three rejected reversing the order of the two distinct ports. Formula (3) and the unrestricted gluing equivalence are paper proofs. [Reproducible checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_fresh_targets.py) · [Rice checker revision](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/tree/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc)

## Researcher Response 40

**For both kernels, any failure in a source-positive computad is already a failure at the universal strict cell of a single finite polyplex, with the weak attaching terms lifted from the given computad.** In the lifted problem there is no raw filler at its old boundary, even if one allows any strict value. Above the generating dimension this also gives a criterion in the opposite direction for an individual context. The statements apply to all eight binary surfaces and to both published ordinary theories. One may independently specify source- or target-positivity in each rank. Here positivity on a chosen side means that the ordinary strict face on that side of each generator of degree at least two is a nonidentity cell in the immediately preceding dimension. No bound is imposed on the lengths of these faces.

### Lifting the strict labelling of a whole derivation

Write $F$ for the **ordinary** free strict image throughout, including for Gray input. Let $L_X$ mean the free algebra of the selected theory on the raw declarations of $X$; we may select either raw theory itself as well. Let $f:Y\to X$ preserve generators and their actual attaching terms. Suppose the strict functor $Ff$ has the following properties.

* A specified identity or binary composite in the image of a cell lifts uniquely as that identity or as a composable pair above the specified two factors, at every compositional cut.
* Every strict preimage of a generating cell of $X$ in its own degree is a generating cell of $Y$.

The first condition is the discrete Conduché lifting property. Then, in **every** degree, the canonical map is an isomorphism of globular sets
$$
                 L_Y\ \longrightarrow\ L_X\times_{F(X)}F(Y).                  \tag{4}
$$
For the raw theories these are literal terms. For the surfaces these are conversion classes. In particular the statement records the lift of the entire term at its specified strict value, not just the existence of a term at some boundary.

Here are the details for passing from globular generators to arbitrary attaching types. On globular input write $L(Z)$ and $F(Z)$ for the corresponding free algebras. The map $L(Z)\to F(Z)$ is cartesian in globular maps $Z\to Z'$ by Responses 4, 27 and 32. In particular use $Z\to 1$, with $1$ the terminal globular set. An operation is given by its generic operation on its positional tree and a labelling of precisely that tree: the strict cell in $F(Z)$ supplies the tree and its labels, and its image in $L(1)$ supplies the operation there by the same pullback. Composition of operations substitutes the trees, since the strict projection commutes with substitution. This describes operations **after** all equations of the selected theory; it applies also to generic head types formed using conversion. [Raw positional lifting](#researcher-response-4) · [Published comparison](#researcher-response-27) · [Binary comparison](#researcher-response-32)

Regard $P=F(X)$ and $Q=F(Y)$ as algebras of this theory by strict evaluation. Pullback of algebras along $Q\to P$ preserves colimits in the slice. To verify this, let $A\to Q$ be an algebra over $Q$. Define a right adjoint as follows. Over a $j$-cell $p$ of $P$, choose parallel boundary families already defined over its two faces, and for each $q$ over $p$ choose a cell in $A$ over $q$ with faces the entries at the two faces of $q$. In degree zero there is just the family of entries. Keeping the boundary families separately handles empty fibres. For a labelled operation over $P$ and a specified strict lift of its result, split that lift over the entire strict positional tree of the operation. Choose any composition expression for the tree, use unique binary lifts and lift the identity paddings. The resulting labels are compatible on incident faces. Equivalently, restriction of strict labellings of the tree to its two pasted pieces is bijective onto pairs agreeing on their common boundary, and the unique lifts give just such a pair. Splitting is independent of the expression: an associativity, unit or exchange step composes the already lifted factors on its other side, with uniqueness recovering the same factors on decomposing back. This includes whiskerings by identities and steps inside a larger expression. Thus restriction to a face gives its split, and splitting a substitution in two stages gives the split on the substituted tree. Evaluate the operation in $A$ on the family entries at these lifted labels. The face, identity and composition checks follow from these split identities and the algebra laws in $A$. This gives the family algebra over $P$. A map over $Q$ from the pullback of an algebra $M\to P$ to $A$ is exactly a map over $P$ from $M$ to these families: evaluate or collect at $(m,q)$. The same split formula verifies that either passage preserves operations. This proves the slice adjunction and colimit preservation.

Compute the pullback of the cellular presentation of $L_X$ in that slice. For one generator $b$ of degree $j$, let $G$ be its formal globular $j$-disc, and consider $L(G)\to P$ given by $b$. The strict pullback $F(G)\times_P Q$ is free on the globular set $Z$ of pairs consisting of a generating disc position and a strict lift of its assigned label; that label can be a composite on a proper face. Indeed in the strict disc every cell is a position or an iterated identity. Unique identity lifting gives the corresponding description of its pullback. At any cut, composability in a disc requires a unit at that cut on at least one side, and that side lifts as a unit. Thus the displayed cells have exactly the free globular-disc compositions on these positions. The same calculation works for the boundary $\partial G$ and for $j=0$. By the globular comparison, the algebra pullback of $L(G)$ is $L(Z)$. Relative to the pulled-back boundary it freely attaches one $j$-cell for every strict lift of $b$. These lifts are exactly its generating preimages by the second hypothesis. Induct on the cellular presentation. At the preceding stage the actual raw attaching faces of each such generator have the required two projections, its lifted strict faces and the old attaching expressions of $b$. By the preceding pullback isomorphism they are exactly the attaching faces in this pushout. Thus every stage attaches precisely the generators of $Y$ over that stage with their given weak boundaries. This computes the whole pullback and proves (4).

### A positive strict cell and its weak attaching terms

For a source-positive strict polygraph, Henry associates to each ordinary strict cell $c$ a finite polyplex $P_c$ with universal cell $\gamma$ and a generator-preserving labelling $P_c\to X$ representing $c$. The representation is unique up to unique isomorphism of the polyplex **with its cell**. In a specified composite the representing object is the pushout of the factor polyplexes along the polyplex of their common face. Consequently generator-preserving maps between source-positive polygraphs have both hypotheses of (4). To lift a specified factorization restrict the representing labelling upstairs to each piece of that pushout. Conversely compatible restrictions glue uniquely. An identity has the same representing diagram as the cell it pads, and the polyplex representing a generator is a plex with its universal generator; all its labellings send that position to a generator. Global strict opposites can be taken independently in each positive dimension: reversing the arrows in degree $j$ switches the two $(j-1)$-faces of every $j$-generator and preserves whether either is an identity. Applying the chosen involution to the strict diagram, doing the calculation, and undoing it gives the same representation and factor lifting for every choice of side per rank. This invokes opposites only in ordinary **strict** categories, so no invariance of directed Gray syntax under these operations is needed. [Henry, §§2.2.6, 2.2.9, Corollary 2.3.12 and Theorem 2.4.8](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1711.00744)

There is a canonical choice of **raw** presentation of $P_c$ relative to the labelling and the given raw $X$. In rank zero keep its vertices. For a generating position labelled $b$, lift the two actual terms in the attaching type of $b$ at the strict values of the two faces of that position. Apply the raw version of (4) on the already constructed lower skeleta. The two lifted faces are literally parallel because their own faces lift identical terms at identical strict values. This defines the next weak declaration. Induction gives a finite presentation $\widetilde P_c$ and a map $f:\widetilde P_c\to X$ preserving the actual declarations. It also proves uniqueness over this particular labelling. Identity padding of $c$ to a higher output degree pads $\gamma$ at the same generating diagram.

Now let a surface judgement at a prescribed old boundary in $X$ have strict value $c$. Lift its cell along $f$ at $\gamma$ by (4), and its old faces by the raw version at the faces of $\gamma$. Parallelism is literal, and these are the faces of the lifted surface cell after passage to its surface class, by uniqueness in (4). Any raw filler of this lifted boundary folds to a raw filler in $X$. Hence a failure in $X$ is a failure on this one $\widetilde P_c$, allowing all candidate raw fillers there. In particular the universal question within each indicated class of finite computads is equivalent to testing its polyplex presentations at such universal values. The proof retains the weak writings of the declarations even where full strict-image conversion fails on the underlying positive polyplex.

For an individual $X$, raw and surface fillers of a **specified** strict value $c$ correspond bijectively to fillers of value $\gamma$ at that lifted boundary, separately for each theory. If $k>\dim X$, the only possible strict $k$-value at two given $(k-1)$-faces is the identity on their common strict value; different strict faces already preclude a surface witness. The lifted presentation has dimension less than $k$ too, so its lifted old faces likewise admit only that identity value for a filler. It follows that conservativity in this degree on $X$ holds exactly when it holds on all these lifted boundary problems over its labelled polyplexes. In lower degrees a different strict $k$-cell at the same boundary can supply a raw filler in $X$, which the fixed-value bijection handles by testing the other labels separately.

For ordinary CaTT one can combine this with Response 39. Within a fixed rankwise positivity class, take a failure of minimal generating dimension in a specified output degree. Its lifted polyplex still belongs to the class and has no greater generating dimension, hence has this same minimal dimension. The bound for 2-computads and the one-sided attachment theorem show that the highest rank of this single polyplex must contain a nonvariable source and a nonvariable target among its lifted declarations. This gives a necessary form for a counterexample even when distinct positions throughout the original lower boundary had the same generator name. The conclusions leave open whether any such presentation actually gives an ordinary failure in the remaining cases of Q1. [Conservativity on arbitrary 2-computads](#researcher-response-10) · [Single-sided attachments](#researcher-response-39) ∎

**Validation.** On the source-positive rules $ff\to ff$ and $f\to 1$, the checker compared two ways to lift a strict diagram. One enumerated the coloured rules step by step along each reading. The other assigned rules to all vertices independently and imposed the incidence equations on the wires. The sets of lifts agreed in 624 comparisons across 52 diagrams, including 16 with multiple readings. For 129 choices of a diagram and initial colours there was no lift; 243 had more than one. Both certified Agda kernels checked four different raw lifts of a directed exchange after a rule at a composite boundary, at their explicitly constructed source and target terms, and checked that all four fold to the same raw term. Rice's two published modes accepted the unit-padded identity conversion at this boundary and distinguished exchanged port labels, while the weak mode accepted the explicit unitor. The isomorphism (4) in all degrees and the universal-cell reduction are paper proofs. [Reproducible checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_polyplexes.py) · [Rice checker revision](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/tree/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc)

## Researcher Response 41

**The positive 3-computad with a shared edge and a circle of 3-cells is conservative for all ten ordinary surfaces in every dimension.** Its two known raw 4-paths around the circle admit no surface 5-cell between them, although full strict-image conversion identifies them. There is a raw substitution taking the circle generator to a full two-strand twist. We compute exactly when this substitution still detects all loops if the two generating changes are replaced by paths of changes.

Use four objects in a line with $f_0,f_1:0\to1$, $g:1\to2$ and $h_0,h_1:2\to3$. For finite directed graphs $L,R$ use a 2-generator $\alpha_i:f_0\to f_1$ per vertex of $L$ and $\beta_j:h_0\to h_1$ per vertex of $R$. For each edge $i\to i'$ of $L$ add the 3-change $\alpha_i g\to\alpha_{i'}g$, and for each $j\to j'$ of $R$ add $g\beta_j\to g\beta_{j'}$. Use the indicated binary whiskerings of 2-cells. The circle example takes $L=R=[1]$ as directed graphs. We allow arbitrary labels, loops and parallel edges in both graphs. [The original computad, its two tiles and the raw circle obstruction](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#a-positive-bubble-free-3-computad-with-a-circle-as-a-full-support-fibre)

### The mapping space of a pair of paths

Write $A,B$ for the free categories on $L,R$, and $I,J$ for their discrete object categories. In the fixed outer hom from 0 to 3, take the further hom between the opposite 1-paths $f_0gh_0,f_1gh_1$. Its objects are the corners $(i,j)$. As an $(\infty,1)$-category this hom is
$$
 V=(A\times B)\amalg_K(A\times B),\qquad
 K=(A\times J)\amalg_{I\times J}(I\times B).                       \tag{5}
$$
All pushouts here are derived. Indeed, in the enriched outer line, the two intervals supporting the new rules are $[0,2]$ and $[1,3]$. They cannot both occur as separate factors of an outer composition. On the first interval the inner walking family of arrows indexed by $I$ becomes the two-object category enriched with forward hom $A$; likewise for $B$ on the second interval. At $[0,3]$ take the pushout of the two extensions, each multiplied by the unchanged family at its exterior edge. The inner objects form a square with four side homs $A,B,A,B$. The colimit of the input diagonals is $K$. There are precisely two compositions via a middle object. For each one the old product of sides contributes $K$ and the product of the resulting sides is $A\times B$. The attaching map from the two old copies of $K$ is the fold. This gives (5) by the same enriched universal property as for the circle. Even if the graphs have loops, nonempty words in a free category are never invertible, so these side categories add no equivalences of lower objects. All other nonidentity fixed homs at this depth are free path categories or discrete categories, as one reads from the shorter intervals or sides of this square. The strict comparison on (5) records just the pair of paths in $A,B$.

Fix such a pair $(p,q)$ of lengths $m,n$, counting occurrences even when labels repeat. The space of 3-cells over this pair, with its lower poles fixed, has the following cubical model. Its vertices shuffle the two lists in their given internal orders. Two adjacent unlike entries can switch in **two** ways, one for each sheet of (5). Any collection of disjoint adjacent switches spans a cube for every choice of their sheets.

To derive this model, present each free category by its vertices and a copy of $\Delta^1$ for each generating edge. Cartesian product in $\mathrm{Cat}_\infty$ preserves colimits separately. Thus (5) attaches two copies of the simplicial square for each pair of edges to the grid graph, identifying their perimeters, also when endpoints of generating edges agree. Equivalently use simplicial sets: attaching a generating interval or a square along its boundary is a homotopy pushout since the boundary inclusion is a cofibration and the objects are cofibrant. In simplicial rigidification the two triangles in each square adjoin a diagonal arrow and intervals from the two serial routes to that diagonal. More explicitly the image of $\Delta^2$ has an interval from the direct edge to its factorization at the middle vertex. Rigidification preserves these attaching colimits. In a fixed mapping space the words can consequently contain single steps, one of the two diagonal arrows for a pair, or one of the comparisons between a diagonal and its two-step route. Simultaneous intervals in a word occur exactly on disjoint blocks. Composition concatenates words. Hence these products of intervals subdivide exactly the stated cubes, with one subdivision vertex on each switch edge. Fixing the two projected words indexes every occurrence even in a loop; no quotient by permutations of equal names is taken. [Dugger–Spivak, rigidification and the mapping simplicial sets of a simplex](https://arxiv.org/abs/0910.0814)

This complex is connected by sorting. Its vertex links are flag: choices of directions span a simplex precisely when their adjacent pairs are pairwise disjoint, with at most one choice at each pair. The complex is nonpositively curved by the cubical link criterion, so each such space is aspherical. Its fundamental group has a presentation with a generator $t_{ij}$ for each crossing of the $i$th letter of $p$ and the $j$th of $q$, and only the relations
$$
                  [t_{ij},t_{kl}]=1 \qquad(i<k,\ j<l).              \tag{6}
$$
To see this, orient a switch by moving the right-list letter to the left. Starting with all of $p$ first, the resulting inversion sets are ideals of the rectangular poset (decreasing left index and increasing right index). The subcomplex using only the first-sheet edges is simply connected: every monotone path to an ideal is a linear extension of that ideal, and interchanging incomparable consecutive moves is a square. Choosing these paths from the initial word trivializes every first-sheet edge in the fundamental groupoid. An oriented edge on the other sheet now gives one generator for its pair, independent of the enabling ideal. Indeed the enabling ideals contain all strict predecessors of the pair, omit it and its successors, and are connected by moves incomparable to it. Slide the edge along those moves by mixed-sheet squares. Two other-sheet edges span a square exactly for incomparable pairs, giving (6). Conversely label the first edges by 1 and the second by the indicated letters in (6); all squares commute there. This verifies that there are no further relations. In particular all well-formed old types of degree at least six in these contexts already have raw fillers, by asphericity and the exact globe-fibre test. [Cubical link condition and Cartan–Hadamard theorem, Bridson–Haefliger, II.5.20 and II.4.1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-662-12494-9) · [Fibre test at an old boundary](#researcher-response-10)

### The braid at the middle edge

Take the bipointed suspension of the ordinary 2-computad on two 2-scalars. It has one crossing edge $d:s\to t$ and 3-generators $a,b:1_d\to1_d$. Send $g$ to $d$, all $f$'s to $1_s$, all $h$'s to $1_t$, and the $\alpha,\beta$ to the corresponding identities. A left generating change goes to $1_s *_0 a$, fitted to its two declared 2-faces, and a right change to $b *_0 1_t$, similarly fitted. The fit for the first is the comparison between $1_{1_s} *_0 d$ and $1_s *_0 1_d$ and its reverse, in the globular arity of $d$; the second uses its right-hand version. Both are raw operations at these actual parallel faces. This is a raw substitution, hence transports any surface derivation.

In the fixed hom of the target, the two colours are particles in the free planar configuration algebra. Equivalently use the two-colour braid Gray-category of Response 37. The fit cells are structural unit comparisons in the arity of $d$. The lower part of the substitution is the constant unit on either side of $d$ and the middle edge itself, up to precisely these comparisons. A generating change in either side category evaluates to its one coloured strand. For a unit square of (5), composition via its middle 1-path composes those two changes in the two local lanes. In the braid Gray-category its structural interchanger is the elementary crossing; in the opposite order of lanes the comparison in the **same direction between serial routes** is the inverse crossing. More explicitly the cubical compositor evaluates the diagonal in the order of its two factors. Its triangle for that serial order has identity comparison and its other triangle gives the block braiding. The second sheet has its factors in reverse order. Thus the two full switch edges map to $\sigma_i$ and $\sigma_i^{-1}$ at the adjacent positions, up to a simultaneous choice of sign. This calculation is relative to the perimeter: at a corner the configuration is empty and on an individual generating side it is a singleton. Both spaces are contractible, so fitting the actual raw corner and side writings gives the same identification. A longer edge word is a concatenation of these cells with the stationary strands; it uses the block inclusion at the prescribed adjacent positions. [Suspended fixed homs](#researcher-response-11) · [Comparison with the braid Gray-category](#researcher-response-37)

For $n=1$ the cubical complex is an interval with both edges at each step, of fundamental group free of rank $m$. Place the $m$ left-list strands at fixed points on a real line in the plane. The single right-list point lies in one of the $m+1$ gaps. Its two moves between adjacent gaps detour above or below the intervening puncture. After identifying base configurations in the same linear-order chamber, these are the two elementary braid edges just computed. The resulting graph is a homotopy model for the plane punctured at those $m$ points: its connected system of upper and lower arcs gives the usual free basis of meridians. The punctured plane includes as the fibre of forgetting the last point in ordered configuration space. This inclusion is injective on $\pi_1$, since the base has $\pi_2=0$ by the successive point-forgetting fibrations. Passing to the cover allowing repeated colours preserves injectivity. The same holds with the two paths exchanged, and a zero-length path gives a point. For $m=n=1$, traversing one switch and returning by the other consequently gives one full twist, of exponent $\pm2$. [Fadell–Neuwirth, configuration fibrations](https://journals.msp.org/mscand/article/view/2674)

At a raw 5-boundary the two old 4-paths have a common pair of 3-ends. Their difference is a loop in the corresponding component just calculated. If its braid is nontrivial, a surface filler would give one in the suspended 2-scalar context where all ten surfaces are conservative, contradicting the raw braid obstruction there. If the loop is trivial, a raw filler already exists at those faces. Thus every boundary with $\min(m,n)\le1$ is settled by the braid test. This proves the opening assertion and all-dimensional conservativity whenever at least one of $L,R$ admits no path of length two, even if the other has arbitrarily long recurrent paths. Degrees through four use Response 5. [Conservativity in suspensions](#researcher-response-11) · [Published suspension comparison](#researcher-response-12)

### The first loop missed by the braid test

For $m=n=2$, write $c=t_{21}, x=t_{11}, y=t_{22}, d'=t_{12}$. Only $x$ and $y$ commute in (6). The loop $[c,x^{-1}d'x]$ is nontrivial, as seen by sending $x,y$ to 1 and keeping $c,d'$ freely independent. Its braid is trivial. For the calculation use the sign convention in which the transport paths from the initial shuffle use positive switches, and take the positive full lasso as generator (inverting all other-sheet generators gives the same presentation). After a common change of basepoint past the first switch the braid values are
$$
 c=v^2,\qquad x=u^2,\qquad y=w^2,\qquad
 d'=(uw)v^2(uw)^{-1},\qquad (u,v,w)=(\sigma_1,\sigma_2,\sigma_3).
$$
Indeed a lasso first follows the chosen path to the adjacent letters, makes their full twist, then retraces that path. Put $b'=u^{-1}wvw^{-1}u=u^{-1}v^{-1}wvu$. The braid relations give
$vb'=u^{-1}v^{-1}u wvu=u^{-1}v^{-1}w uvu=b'v$.
Thus $x^{-1}d'x=(b')^2$ commutes with $c$ in the braid group. This loop persists for $m,n\ge2$ by whiskering the four-letter calculation with the remaining steps. Forgetting all other indexed letters retracts the shuffle complexes, while adding stationary braid strands preserves the braid identity. This proves that the braid projection is faithful on these fundamental groups exactly for $\min(m,n)\le1$. In particular the four-letter commutator gives a raw 5-boundary missed by this projection. Its nonzero class can be represented at a literal old boundary by taking a raw 3-term for the initial serial route and using the computadic unit comparison on its based loop space. ∎

**Validation.** The checker computed the two-sheet meridians, their changes of basepoint, and the commuting squares for every grid up to four by four. The Artin action distinguished 704 freely reduced one-blue words across the four tested ranks through length three and verified the four-strand commutator equation and its unconjugated negative control. Agda checked the raw substitution on all 15 declarations of the original circle example, both specialized 4-tiles, and all four corner comparisons at their translated types. The asphericity and the injectivity criterion for arbitrary path lengths are paper proofs. [Reproducible checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_shared_edge.py)

## Researcher Response 42

**All ten ordinary surfaces are conservative in every shared-edge computad of Response 41, in every dimension, for arbitrary finite directed graphs on the two sides.** In particular the four-letter commutator at the end of that response has no surface filler. At degree five the full obstruction is equality of paths in the groupoid of the doubled shuffle complex, equivalently triviality of the difference word under (6). Both choices of switch have to be retained to obtain this test.

### A surface interpretation with strict composition across objects

Let $M$ be a category enriched, using **cartesian product**, in ordinary Gray-categories and strict Gray-functors. The interchangers *within* a Gray-category here are invertible. Number the objects of $M$ in degree zero, the cells of a hom Gray-category in degrees one through four, and extend by identities. There are two useful interpretations on this data.

For the published surfaces use the column-order model $K$ of Response 5 on each hom, with equality as its order on top cells. Its construction preserves strict Gray-functors and products: evaluating each column paste, whiskering, and top comparison in a product evaluates them componentwise. Thus the exterior composition maps are strict maps of these local algebras. For a global operation on a tree $[P_1,\ldots,P_k]$, a path of degree one is the exterior composite of its selected edges. In positive local degree restrict the generic operation and its faces to each traversed column by the object cut of Responses 11–12. Evaluate the resulting operations in the corresponding homs and apply their exterior composition. The empty interval uses the unit from the terminal local algebra.

To check substitution, consider any subterm on a labelled tree. Its object poles select an interval of the root list. Inductively its value is the exterior composite over this interval of its evaluated restrictions. At a head, each crossing argument has such an interval; applying a local operation to its tuple of column composites commutes with exterior composition since the latter is a strict local algebra map. This gives precisely the operation on each restricted tuple followed by composition over the concatenated interval. A stationary interval inserts an exterior unit. This proves the formula for the application and its faces, hence for general substitutions. In an insertion, restriction to each traversed column gives the local insertion equality, including the root splice and the splice at a disc, by Response 12. Disc and endo removal give the local disc and identity equations. Composing the resulting equalities proves the global published equations, also on converted faces. Write $K_o(M)$ for this interpretation. [Local column model](#researcher-response-5) · [Object cuts](#researcher-response-11) · [Insertion at a cut](#researcher-response-12)

We need the following free calculation. On a globular set of generators $P$ through degree four the free enriched category of this kind has, for each pair of vertices, a disjoint union over paths in the graph of ordered vertex pairs. The factor for a step $(a,b)$ is the free Gray-category on the local globular set of positive generators with object poles $(a,b)$, desuspended once. A path summand is the cartesian product of its factors, with the empty product for the empty path. This is the enriched path construction: a globular assignment gives strict functors from its factors and their exterior composites give its unique extension. The ordinary strict evaluation has the identical description with each local Gray-category replaced by the free strict 3-category. In particular **a specified strict 4-value lifts uniquely at prescribed parallel 3-faces mapping to its faces**. First retain the common path and then use contraction and top uniqueness in each free Gray factor. The latter applies over a globular set by using the operations at the given strict arity with its given positional labels. In particular it covers identities and paths of length zero. [Local Gray operations and top uniqueness](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0305049#page=277)

For the binary surface one needs to retain the syntax of **both** lower cuts in the global 3-words. Keep $M$ through degree two, where its operations are strict. A 3-event is an atom labelled by a 3-cell or an event in a specified argument of a binary node at cut 0 or 1. Record the cut, the argument side and the actual stationary operand, including its degree. The ends of this event use the corresponding strict lower paste. A history is a list of events composable at cut 2, with the empty list allowed. At a common binary node permit the exchange
$$
 R(se,f)\,L(e,tf)\ \longrightarrow\ L(e,sf)\,R(te,f)
$$
and its inverse, also under identical exterior event contexts. Here $e,f$ are the events in the two arguments; the stationary labels displayed are their actual degree-two ends. A whiskering by a lower-degree operand can be part of the exterior context. Take exchange classes for the 3-cells. Execute binary pairs from left to right with the indicated stationary operands, whisker by adding a node, and concatenate at cut 2.

Evaluate histories by composition in $M$. Each exchange has a comparison between the evaluations: the identity at a cut-0 divergence, the local Gray interchanger at a cut-1 divergence, and the whiskered comparison under an exterior context. All exchange paths between fixed histories give the same comparison. Indeed each directed switch reduces the inversion number of the sequence of left/right addresses. Disjoint switches commute. In a critical triple remove the common address prefix. Its node tags and stationary labels match by the two overlap tests. At the first split there are two events in one argument and one in the other. The singleton can pass across either of the two; once it has passed both, their common stationary operand has changed from its one end to the other. Their own exchange preserves their two total ends. These are the six edges of the joining hexagon of Response 5, with the same cut tag at each use of the split. If the split is at cut 1, the hexagon commutes by multiplicativity of the Gray interchanger and its naturality for the comparison inside the double argument. If it is at cut 0, the cross comparisons are identities; whiskering the internal comparison on the two routes agrees by functoriality out of a cartesian product. This includes an internal cut different from the split in either case. Whiskering restores the common prefix. Terminating induction proves uniqueness of normal representatives and of the maps to their evaluations. Write $r(u)$ for the normal evaluation of a class. Put the actual $M$-cells $r(u)\to r(v)$ in degree four, with only identities higher up.

Here are the generic operations and their equations. In the free calculation use only generating 3-cells for the history atoms. Realize each designated strict 3-paste on a globular arity by a fixed binary expression. Its lower faces have exactly the required values since they are strict. Choose the operations above for binary heads, a single input for a disc, and an empty history for an identity. For a 4-head take the unique lift of the prescribed strict value at the normal evaluations of its two interpreted faces. In a labelling, expand the input histories at the atoms and relabel all stationary operands. A switch expands to the rectangle between the two blocks, so substitution of history classes composes. For a 4-cell apply the free enriched functor with the normal evaluations as its 3-labels and the given 4-cells as its 4-labels, then conjugate by the sorting comparisons at both ends. This also composes under substitution: each switch expands with the same comparison by multiplicativity at its cut. Changing a 3-label by its sorting isomorphism acts by the endpoint conjugations on a 4-generator, by naturality on a local interchanger, and componentwise under exterior composition. Generators, structural interchangers and their inverses, whiskerings and compositions exhaust the free top cells by the path formula. Thus the two ways of substituting give identical maps on 4-cells. The same prescriptions apply to heads at converted faces.

For (i) in degree three take the **literal** common generic 2-source of the parallel words. Its 2-variable leaves have the forced lists of generating 3-steps determined by the strict value, by the leaf argument of Response 33. Each binary word executes a shuffle of those lists inside the same lower expression tree. At any binary node the stationary operand when an event executes is the value of the opposite subtree at that instant; it is determined by the prefixes already executed at its leaves. Consequently sorting the left steps before the right steps uses exactly the allowed exchanges with the required stationary labels. Recursing down the source gives the same history class for both words. Stationary branches contribute no events. For (ii) in degree three the strict identity has an empty event list. In degree four (i) uses top uniqueness at the common histories; for (ii) that lift of the strict identity is the identity of the common normal evaluation. The disc prescription returns its input, including its labelled 4-cell since its endpoints have identity sorting transports. In degrees at most two use strict evaluation. In degree five identify the two parallel 4-faces of each 5-slot of the arity. Every allowed labelling factors through this identification because its 5-cells are identities. On the resulting globular set the two 4-boundaries have the same strict value and parallel history faces, hence have identical lifts by the free calculation. The required identity exists. Higher operations and equations follow by identity extension. This gives a model $E_o(M)$ of all the binary laws. [History substitution](#researcher-response-5) · [Leaf lists with lower cuts in arbitrary rank](#researcher-response-33)

The raw reducts of the two models are weakly equivalent. Use their common cells through rank two, triples $(u,z,j:r(u)\cong z)$ at rank three, commuting pairs of 4-cells $(b,c)$ with $cj=j'b$, and identities thereafter. For a 3-head on generic labels take the comparison between the evaluated history and the column paste over their common strict value. It is the unique lift of the identity, invertible by lifting in the reverse direction and applying uniqueness. Under labelling, undo the endpoint sort, apply this comparison and transport the paste along the input isomorphisms. For a 4-head the two composites in the required square agree on generic labels by top uniqueness. On arbitrary labels the square still commutes by the endpoint conjugation formula and naturality under changing the 3-labels, verified above on the generators and the interchangers. This equips the triples with a raw action making both projections raw maps. Both lift at every prescribed boundary: lift $z$ by an atom or $u$ by its normal evaluation, solve the conjugation equation for the missing top coordinate, and use that parallel commuting pairs agree if either coordinate agrees. Hence both projections are trivial fibrations.

It follows that whenever the raw free algebra on a computad is weakly equivalent to $K_o(M)$, **all ten ordinary surfaces are conservative on that computad**. Indeed its cofibrancy gives a raw weak equivalence to the chosen surface reduct representing the isomorphism in the raw homotopy category. Interpret a surface filler there using the images of the original declarations. The globe-fibre test for the weak equivalence returns a raw filler at precisely the original faces. This criterion concerns a particular computad; it makes no strictification assumption on general weak 4-categories. [Prescribed-boundary comparison](#researcher-response-10) · [Transfer to a surface reduct](#researcher-response-32)

### Realizing the two independent interchangers

Use $A,B,I,J$ as in (5). For $I$ let $Q_I$ be the category with two objects and forward arrows indexed by $I$; let $D_A$ be the strict 2-category with two objects and forward hom $A$. Both have only the unit endohoms and empty reverse homs. Use $Q_J,D_B$ in the same way. The consecutive exterior homs of $M$ are $Q_I,1,Q_J$ and its two length-two homs are $D_A,D_B$. The remaining hom $M(0,3)$ is a local Gray-category with four objects in a square. Its four forward side homs are $A,B,A,B$ regarded as locally discrete 2-categories. Its diagonal hom is the following strict 2-category $Z$.

* Its objects are pairs $(i,j)$, and its arrows are serial words of left and right graph edges, changing the respective coordinate.
* From one word to another with the same projected paths take the paths in the doubled shuffle complex of Response 41, modulo homotopy relative to the two words. Distinct projections admit no such cell.
* Compose tracks by pasting paths. Whisker by concatenating words and compose horizontally by performing the two whiskered paths successively. The two orders give equal tracks by the squares for disjoint switches. Thus this composition is strictly functorial; its unit and associativity follow from concatenation.

The two local compositions into $Z$, one via each middle object, are cubical functors. Use the two factors as the two axis walks; for the elementary interchanger use the switch on the sheet of that middle object, with the orientation from its first serial order to its second. For two paths use the rectangle moving the entire first block across the second. Within one sheet its monotone orders differ by swaps of disjoint switches, hence define the same track. Splitting either path subdivides the rectangle, proving both composition axioms. Empty paths give identity comparisons. The side categories have only identity 2-cells, so these axioms specify the two Gray tensor functors. There is no triple of nonidentity composable local 1-arrows. All units, reverse homs and incomparable homs are as for the underlying square.

For exterior composition into this square use $D_A\times Q_J$ or $Q_I\times D_B$. Each sends the corner objects and side arrows to their namesakes, and a pair of forward local 1-arrows to its pair $(i,j)$ in $Z$. The activity of a local 2-cell in the first product is entirely in $A$, and in the second entirely in $B$. Its image on the diagonal is that single-axis path at the stationary vertex. This preserves local composition strictly, including cubical interchangers: an interchanger supported on both successive sides has an identity 2-cell on the stationary side. On the shorter intervals use the evident inclusions. The two triple composites agree on $Q_I\times 1\times Q_J$, including all pairs of forward arrows; these have the same diagonal object $(i,j)$. This constructs the required cartesian-enriched category.

Assign the computad generators to the indicated objects, arrows and single-axis changes in $K_o(M)$. The prescribed whiskered attaching faces agree by exterior composition. We verify that the resulting raw map is a weak equivalence. The exterior objects and the objects of each local hom are the same as in the interval computation of (5). All shorter intervals and the four sides give the same discrete or free path categories; on their named arrows the map is the generating assignment. For the diagonal it remains to compare each mapping space at its endpoints. Fixed hom restriction in $K_o(M)$ gives the column interpretation of the corresponding local Gray-category, since a suspended operation has only its one column. Restricting once more at its two local objects gives the ordinary strict 2-category hom with its usual compositions (all structural interchangers there are identities). Thus the target mapping space at projected paths $(p,q)$ is the nerve of the track groupoid just used for $Z$.

In the rigidification of (5) each generating square has two triangles comparing its serial routes with its diagonal. The map takes the chosen first route to the diagonal by an identity and the other by the local cubical compositor: these are the two generic triangle comparisons for column-ordered Gray composition, obtained by the unique top lift at those pastes. Along the boundary of this calculation a single edge has one serial route with trivial track group, and a corner has the empty route; fitting the original weak writings there makes no additional choice of track. The two compositions via the two middle objects use, by construction, the two different sheets. Whiskering the triangles appends the stationary words. After the edge subdivision in Response 41 this is therefore the map sending every switch edge to its own path class. It induces the identity on the fundamental groupoid, since the edges generate and the squares supply all path relations. The components of the source are aspherical by Response 41 and the groupoid nerves are 1-types, so this is an equivalence on each mapping space. This checks all the homs and proves the raw weak equivalence.

Apply the criterion to this $M$. In particular the nontrivial element $[t_{21},t_{11}^{-1}t_{12}t_{11}]$ is detected at its original 5-boundary, including when both graphs have cycles. More generally existence at any old 5-boundary on the diagonal is decided by (6); all old types of degree at least six are already raw-inhabited. If further generators first occur in degree $h>3$, Response 32 applied with $r=h-1$ also gives conservativity through degree $h+1$ with arbitrary such attachments. This settles the shared-edge family but leaves Q1 in unrestricted dimensions and the corresponding general context classification in Q2 open. ∎

On two globular arities involving both cuts it compared the projected braid actions and literal raw types for all 54 mixed hexagons. ## Researcher Response 43

**The identity-class lifting test in Known 1 fails for all eight binary surfaces, already on a point, in both kernels.** This includes the empty set of word laws, which is conservative in every context and dimension by Response 3. More precisely, in any context $X$ and rank $k\ge1$ there is an equivalence
$$
 EL_S(k;X)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \text{any two raw-parallel, surface-equal raw }k\text{-terms in }X
 \text{ are literally the same term}.                              \tag{7}
$$
For every nonempty finite context the first failing rank of $EL_S$ is **one** if $A\in S$ or $U\in S$, and **two** otherwise. It fails at every rank from the first one onward. The obstruction concerns the required surface *class* of a raw comparison. It applies even when the original boundary has raw inhabitants in both directions.

### Recording the argument of a unary disc operation

For $k>0$ write $d(a)$ for the operation on the $k$-disc with its two boundary variables as endpoints, applied to $a$ in its top slot. This is a raw $k$-term with the same literal faces as $a$. Mark every head of degree $k$ whose arity is that disc. For a raw $k$-term $s$ let $M(s)$ be the finite multiset of its marked occurrences. Each entry records the **whole raw term at that occurrence**, including the unreduced arguments. Recursively, a variable contributes nothing. A head collects the multisets of its arguments at all $k$-positions and, if marked, adds its own literal expression. Bound types are fixed parts of a constructor and are outside this traversal.

We first build a surface model to recognize a necessary condition for an identity-class comparison. Use one cell per degree below $k$. Its $k$-cells have a natural-number value. A $k$-head adds the values of the labels at its $k$-positions and adds one if marked. Between any two $k$-values the $(k+1)$-cells have a further natural number, called their grade. A $(k+1)$-head adds the grades at its $(k+1)$-positions and adds one if **either generic $k$-face** has positive value when the generic $k$-variables have value zero. Use exactly one higher cell at each parallel boundary from then on. Evaluate a head on the labelled tuple, so substitution acts by composition of evaluations. In particular the extra summand for a $(k+1)$-head is tested on its bound faces before applying the tuple. It depends only on the interpreted generic operations, hence is invariant under congruence in the head type, including types formed using conversion.

Here are the equation checks. The identity at degree $k$ has arity of height $k-1$, and a binary degree-$k$ head has two maximal positions. Neither is marked. A word of degree $k$ consequently just sums the values at its top generator occurrences. At degree $k+1$ the generic $k$-faces of a binary head or identity are words, so the extra grade summand vanishes. Words again add the input grades with precisely their strict top multiplicities. All equations (i) preserve these counts, including in the Gray theory by ordinary strict projection. For (ii), an accepted degree-$r$ head between words with equal ordinary strict values has no $r$-position. Indeed a tree with such a position projects to the unpadded ordinary total in its top dimension; its two ordinary faces are distinct. This also holds for Gray totals by the positional projection of Response 4. In particular the head is unmarked at $r=k$, and at $r=k+1$ the two generic word faces give no extra grade. Its value in either case is zero, as for the identity. Above these degrees use uniqueness at the equal boundary. This verifies the full binary laws and hence each subset. The counting method at a single rank appears in [Response 1](#researcher-response-1); the extra generic-face test is needed to constrain comparisons one rank higher.

Assign every $(k+1)$-generator of $X$ grade one at its actual interpreted boundary. Assign the lower generators any allowed values and use the unique fillers at higher declarations. For every **raw** $(k+1)$-term $e:s\to t$ in this assignment,
$$
                       \operatorname{grade}(e)=0
                 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad M(s)=M(t).             \tag{8}
$$
Prove this by induction on $e$. A variable cannot have grade zero. Write a head as $h[\rho]$. Grade zero says its two generic faces contain no marked heads and every $(k+1)$-label has grade zero. For a mark-free generic $k$-term $v$ on its arity $P$, $M(v[\rho])$ is the sum of $M(\rho_i)$ over the $k$-positions with multiplicities given by the ordinary strict $k$-chain of $v$. Indeed substitution keeps each enclosing head and its arity; all marked expressions in this traversal come from the top labels. Counts of those labels add under raw application, which is exactly the strict top-chain formula. Now interpret the $k$-positions $i$ of $P$ in the commutative monoid of multisets by $M(\rho_i)$, with singleton lower degrees and only identities higher up. Every $(k+1)$-position admits the required identity by the induction hypothesis on its label: its two $k$-variables are labelled by the literal faces of that label. The ordinary strict value of $h$ therefore has equal multiset values on its two faces. Applying the mark-free formula to the two generic faces gives (8). This proof uses the raw writings inside the multiset entries; $M$ itself is not asserted to descend to conversion.

Suppose $a,b$ are distinct parallel raw $k$-terms with $a\approx_S b$. The parallel pair $d(a),d(b)$ is also surface-equal by substitution congruence. Its multisets differ: the larger of the two whole marked expressions cannot occur as a proper subexpression of the other, and in case of equal size the two whole expressions differ. Neither orientation can have a raw comparison in the surface identity class, since every identity has grade zero. This proves the forward implication in (7); the literal identity supplies the converse. In particular exact lifting already fails at an old type whose *specified* surface cell is an identity.

### The sharp ranks on a point

Write $z$ for an iterated identity of degree $k$ on a vertex. Its two faces agree. For $U$ use $z*_{k-1}z$ and $z$ (here $z$ is the identity on its face); for $A$ use the two bracketings of three copies of $z$. These are distinct raw terms with the same literal type. They are instances of raw-parallel word laws on top-composition arities, for every $k\ge1$.

There is a pair independent of $A,U,I$ in every $k\ge2$. Over the top-composable pair of $(k-1)$-discs take the word $w$ composing the two variables. The generic head on that arity from $w$ to $w$ and the canonical $1_w$ have different arities. Clause (ii) for the reflexive word equation identifies them even for $S=\varnothing$. Label both inputs by the same identity tower on the vertex. The two resulting $k$-terms still have different outer arities and their literal faces agree. All the arities and words used here are available in either kernel, including the line of two edges at $k=2$. Apply (7). On the point there are raw comparisons in both directions between the two disc applications: take the coherence on the point at their prescribed faces. Substitution carries the examples to any chosen vertex of a nonempty context.

In degree one, for $S\subseteq\{I\}$ the raw syntax embeds through that degree. Use the actual raw objects and 1-terms of the given computad with their raw head operations, and one higher cell at every parallel boundary. At degree one the free word algebra with interchange alone distinguishes all constructor trees: take the free directed binary magma with the formal identity at each vertex and the unique higher cell at each parallel boundary. Its higher interchange equations hold, since the boundary formulas for the two layerings have the same outside faces. Thus (i) imposes only literal equality on 1-words. Clause (ii) there is just the identity on a vertex. This validates the model through degree one; all further binary equations use uniqueness at their interpreted boundaries. The identity assignment of the raw lower generators extends over every declaration, proving reflection. Objects are similarly separated for every surface. In the empty context the assertions about parallel pairs are vacuous.

Thus the additional $EL$ hypothesis on pasting contexts in Known 1 already fails at the stated ranks. For instance, for associativity alone the point satisfies inhabitation conservativity in every dimension, yet already in degree two a prescribed surface identity at an old boundary has no raw representative there. The universal binary classification in Q1 cannot be obtained by establishing those identity-class hypotheses. For the published theories the unary disc operation satisfies disc removal, and the separate exact lifting results of Responses 27–28 apply. [Conservativity of the empty and associative surfaces](#researcher-response-3) · [Associativity in every dimension](#researcher-response-16) · [Exact lifting for the published theories](#researcher-response-27) ∎

**Validation.** The two safe Agda kernels accepted comparisons in both directions at all eleven point pairs tested in ranks one through four. For each pair the checker evaluated the generic-face grade of the explicit comparison and of an identity, recorded the distinct marked raw terms at its ends, and checked a zero-grade comparison changing a surrounding unit while preserving the marked input. The exclusion of every possible identity-class comparison, including in contexts with higher declarations, is the induction (8).
