### Reading the attached hom at width three

Restrict even further to declarations whose two strict local 2-faces are crossing words at $a^3$. At this width the strict local 2-cells are freely spelled in $1,2$, the positions of the adjacent crossing. A raw return exists exactly when $212$ reaches $121$ by substring rewrites using $121\to212$ and the given pairs of crossing words. Indeed the old cubical hom has the native 3-atom with the indicated sign. The local dimension-three part of the enriched attachment adds precisely the declared 3-atoms. An exterior spatial edge path is empty on the scalar base, and a tensor with any further positive-dimensional letter enters above local dimension three. Factor a strict 3-paste into whiskered single steps. Each step already occupies all three strands, so the strand contexts are empty. At width zero even a stationary crossing diagram is the identity. The remaining whiskers give arbitrary crossing prefixes and suffixes. Distinct positions on three strands overlap, so there is no commutation of disjoint crossings in a word. Conversely each such rewrite is a strict whiskered generator. The strict-to-raw fitting argument at the end of Response 30 applies here: all lower cells are in the 2-skeleton, whose fixed-hom evaluation lifts strict identities at any pair of parallel old writings. Fit a declared step to its prescribed faces using those comparisons, and compose steps at matching writings. Only the colouring argument of that response used the four-axis bound. This proves the predicate at the *original* $Q,P$.

The reduction can effectively produce raw declarations. Here is an explicit choice of alphabet. Transport the scalar to a 2-endomorphism $w$ on $1_x *_0 1_x$ using the two point coherences between that 1-term and $1_x$. The left and right spatial unit whiskers of $a$ each compare in both directions with $w$ by 3-terms of identity strict image. On a generic 2-disc compare the whisker with the 2-variable padded at both ends by the corresponding one-dimensional unitors. This is an equal-image head on the disc. After specialization, compare its two unitor writings with the selected point coherences by heads on the point. Apply these comparisons at the two ends of the directed square to make it a 3-loop on $w *_1 w$. Whisker by $w$ at either position, using the associator and its reverse on the vertical three-disc string to meet the same literal bracketing. This gives two 3-loops at $(w *_1 w)*_1 w$, with strict values the two letters. Top composition spells any nonempty word and identity spells the empty one. Thus every input pair supplies a legal 4-declaration at literal raw faces.

### Encoding reachability at the one fixed reverse type

Take finite substring rules on an alphabet, with nonempty sides, and nonempty words $u,v$. Reachability in such systems is undecidable, already using both orientations of the relations of a finitely presented semigroup. Add two fresh markers $L,R$. Give each symbol, including each marker, a distinct number $i\ge2$ and encode it by $11\,2^i\,11$. Let $E$ concatenate these blocks. For each rule $s\to t$ adjoin $E(s)\to E(t)$ and also adjoin
$$
            212\longrightarrow E(LuR),\qquad E(LvR)\longrightarrow121 .
$$
Each encoded $2$-run has length at least two, as does every intervening $1$-run. Neither the native step nor the start step can apply within an encoded word. A match of a nonempty string of blocks must start at the final two ones immediately before a complete $2$-run and end at the first two ones immediately after a complete run. Matching therefore selects exactly aligned symbols. The marker runs occur only at the two ends, so the finish rule matches precisely the whole marked target. Starting at $212$, the first visit to $121$ is possible exactly when $u\to^*v$. Enumeration of finite raw derivations gives recursive enumerability. This proves undecidability for the specified old-boundary predicate even though each context in the construction already has other Gray failures in higher dimensions by Response 29. [Post, *Recursive Unsolvability of a Problem of Thue*](https://doi.org/10.2307/2267170) · [Higher failures at a repeatable generator](#researcher-response-29)

If all attachment rules weakly increase crossing length, or all weakly decrease it, a path between the two length-three words stays at length three. Reachability on eight words then decides the return, allowing multiple steps. For example $21\to11$ and $112\to121$ give $212\to112\to121$. The first declaration also occupies all three strands, since its two letters use both positions.

**Validation.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_three_axis.py` from the repository root. The checker compared every contextual encoded successor with direct symbol rewriting in 2,744 cases including overlaps, and compared reachability on 588 source–target instances. The mode `--rules 21:11 112:121` decides the example above. For the literal typing checks run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_three_axis.py --agda /tmp/ScalarThreeAxisReturn.agda` and `agda --safe -i src -i /tmp /tmp/ScalarThreeAxisReturn.agda`. Agda 2.8.0 checked the two positioned crossings and a composite of declarations at the words $212,112,121$. The fixed-boundary equivalence and the unbounded reduction are the arguments above. [Checker and raw term construction](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_three_axis.py)

## Researcher Response 47

**Published strict units are conservative in every dimension on every finite ordinary computad whose positive-dimensional generators all have the same dimension $n$.** Their attaching terms on the vertices may have arbitrary raw writings. This gives the same all-dimensional assertion for the binary unit surface. For published insertion the bound on every pure 3-scalar computad increases to **seven**, including all multiplicities. In particular its surface does not kill the Hopf class on two distinct 3-scalars, or the Whitehead classes involving three of them. The proof constructs maps out of the surface fixed homs that are compatible with forgetting any subset of the particles. Compatibility in unbounded arity is essential to the integral strict-unit result.

### Strictly unital path operations on a Kan complex

Let $D_0$ be the simplicial point. Inductively set $D_{k+1}=(D_k\times\Delta^1)/(\partial D_k\times\Delta^1\to\partial D_k)$, writing the quotient as a pushout along projection. Its two ends are the globular face inclusions, and projection to $D_k$ interprets identity. Realization gives the topological globe with its two hemispheres. For a Kan complex $Z$ take $Z^{D_k}$ as simplicial set of $k$-cells, with these faces and identities. A labelling of a tree $P$ has simplicial set $Z^{D_P}$, where $D_P$ is the globular sum. The inclusion of its constant tuples $Z\hookrightarrow Z^{D_P}$ is an equivalence, since $D_P$ is nonempty and contractible by successive gluings of discs along discs. The boundary map $Z^{D_k}\to Z^{\partial D_k}$ is a Kan fibration.

These cells admit simplicial operations of $\mathrm{Catt}_{su}$. To see the extension on a head of positive rank $k$, suppose first that its tree is nonlinear. For each locally maximal variable $i$, the locus of labellings on which it is an identity is the subcomplex $Z^{D_{P//i}}$ of $Z^{D_P}$. Indeed forcing that top disc to factor through its projection identifies its two faces and gives precisely the pruning quotient. Intersections are the simultaneous quotients for the selected leaves: the universal property just requires the tuple to take each selected leaf to the identity on its source. The projections for different leaves commute up to the unique reindexing fixing the other labels, also for successive discs in the same string. Their simultaneous quotient is again a tree, by pruning the selected leaves successively. Thus every intersection contains the same constant $Z$ by a monomorphic weak equivalence. Their finite union $A(P)$ does too, by gluing along monomorphisms, inductively also on intersections with the next locus. Consequently $A(P)\hookrightarrow Z^{D_P}$ is a trivial cofibration.

Induct in output rank and then in the size of the head tree at fixed rank. Use the normalized surface class of the generic boundary as index. Its faces give the map from $Z^{D_P}$ to $Z^{\partial D_k}$ using only smaller ranks. On a pruning locus use the operation with the substituted boundary on the strictly smaller pruned tree. On intersections the prescriptions agree by further pruning and substitution of identities. For a generic endomorphism use the identity on the interpreted face everywhere; this agrees on each pruning locus by the same rule in the smaller arity. On a disc use its variable or the prescribed iterated identity, as required by disc trivialization. These prescriptions also agree on its identity locus. In the remaining cases extend over $Z^{D_P}$ by the trivial cofibration and boundary fibration above. Composition of evaluations gives substitution, and equal generic types use the same choice. This simultaneously verifies the formation and equality rules: disc and endo removal hold by their prescriptions and every specialized pruning is on the indicated locus. This constructs the strict-unit operations even on cells at converted types. [Pruning and disc trivialization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.08307)

We need choices on a diagram. The following precise version will suffice. Objects are indexed by finite label sets $S$ and finitely many constraints $U$ at each $S$. Relaxing a constraint gives an inclusion, intersections of constraints give intersections of the subcomplexes, and forgetting labels restricts constraints. Suppose the Kan complexes $Z_S(U)$ satisfy these conditions and the maps to the matching objects
$$
      Z_S(U)\longrightarrow \lim_{T\subsetneq S} Z_T(U|_T)          \tag{12}
$$
are Kan fibrations, also on fixed points for subgroups of the stabilizer of $(S,U)$. The actions can be chosen to respect every such map and every relabelling. At each rank and tree size carry out the extension in increasing $|S|$, then from smaller subcomplexes to larger. Add to $A(P)$ the spaces of tuples in all proper subcomplexes at this $S$. This enlarged inclusion is still a trivial cofibration. Indeed on each new subcomplex its own pruning union is already included; adjoining the subcomplex along that union and its intersections with earlier subcomplexes is a weak equivalence by the same finite gluing induction. All intersections are computed before taking powers by the tree. The prescriptions agree there. For the target use the joint map specifying the globular boundary and the output over the matching object. It is a fibration by (12) and the globe boundary cofibration. For equivariance the same monomorphic inclusions are weak equivalences on fixed points: for a union of translates of imposed smaller subcomplexes, a fixed simplex lying in one lies in the intersection of its translates. The resulting fixed-point union has the preceding gluing proof. The joint target map is a fibration on fixed points. The equivariant lifting follows from the Kan model structure with cells $G/H\times\Delta^j$, or by its horn lifting characterization. Choose on one orbit of indices at a time. This construction also works at a single $Z$ with no constraints or forgetful maps.

For $Z=\operatorname{Sing} W$, the raw algebra on the vertices of these simplicial cell spaces represents the homotopy type $W$, compatibly on the diagram. Here is why the choices in the construction cause no change of homotopy type. The ordinary topological globe operations act on the same vertices and, by taking families, on the same simplicial cell spaces: choose on each raw head a continuous map from its globe to the topological pasting ball extending its already chosen boundary. Contractibility supplies it. This is the action by a contractible globular operad over the topological ball operad, giving the fundamental weak groupoid. The topological and chosen raw operations are joined by a parameter $t\in\Delta^1$. Induct on raw head formation. Prescribe the two endpoint actions and the common constant answer on constant tuples throughout. The inclusion of this prescription is the pushout product of $Z\hookrightarrow Z^{D_P}$ with $\partial\Delta^1\hookrightarrow\Delta^1$, a trivial cofibration. The already interpolated faces prescribe the target boundary at each parameter. Extend as above, including the smaller subcomplexes and matching objects in the diagram case. Evaluation of raw expressions uses the same parameter throughout a substitution. The algebra of paths in the cell spaces with this pointwise raw action has raw maps to both ends. Both lift globes at fixed boundaries, since the inclusion of the one end of $D_k\times\Delta^1$ together with its boundary cylinder is a trivial cofibration. This gives diagramwise weak equivalences of raw algebras. It applies also to the mapping-path replacements below, which are singular complexes of spaces: singular preserves limits and exponentiation by $\Delta^1$ here corresponds to topological paths. [Cisinski, *Batanin higher groupoids and homotopy types*](https://www.math.univ-toulouse.fr/~dcisinsk/highergr.pdf) · [Raw globular comparisons](#researcher-response-3)

### The free fixed hom on any finite set of distinct particles

Fix $n\ge3$ and standard scalar boundaries on a point. For a finite set $S$ of scalar names take the component with each name occurring once in the $n$-fold hom at the lower units. Call the published algebra in this hom $B(S)$, for either of the two published theories. Scalars with other multiplicities will be recovered by folding. Empty and singleton components are terminal by Response 35. Forgetting a name substitutes the identity scalar for it.

For $|S|\ge2$ the algebra $B(S)$ is freely presented by the following symbols at their decoded attaching types. Take each normal, nonidentity expression in the component whose outer head has **at least two occupied depth-$n$ slots**. Keep the whole expression as a single symbol. Its local rank is the global rank minus $n$. In contrast to the two-particle presentation, an argument in one such slot can itself contain several particles and can vary in higher dimensions.

To verify freeness, every leaf of a normal nonidentity expression reaches depth $n$ and every depth-$n$ slot is occupied: an unoccupied subtree has a locally maximal identity argument, contrary to pruning. Along the transports within a slot the set of names is fixed. If there is one slot, the tree is $\Sigma^n K$ and the head and tuple desuspend, by the crossing calculation of Response 35. Decode such an application as an application on $K$ to recursively decoded arguments. Decode an identity as an identity, and a split expression as its symbol. Encode by suspended operations and expansion of each symbol. The types of symbols use the decoded normal faces in strictly smaller local degree. These constructions check the incidences by induction on degree and on the expressions.

In the unit theory, expansion of a symbol introduces no identity at a locally maximal argument. In the insertion theory there is one more interface check. A nonlinear grouped outer tree $\Sigma^n K$ has branch height at least $n$ for *any* branch used for insertion: a prefix of length at most $n$ still has the nonlinear subtree $K$ below it. The tree of a split symbol has trunk height strictly less than $n$. Thus even a higher-dimensional symbol expanding to a **standard** operation fails the insertion height test at this interface. A linear outer tree already acts by disc trivialization. Inside grouped expressions the height tests both shift by $n$ and disc, identity and bound-type tests desuspend. Inside a symbol the whole expression is normal. Therefore encoding normal forms gives the indicated normal forms, and the two parsings are inverse, as in Response 35. The presentation is free in the selected published theory; a symbol may attach at a type formed there. [Normal forms and branch heights, Definitions 18–19](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.05303) · [Two-particle parsing](#researcher-response-35)

For an unordered pair of names in $S$, an observed separation in a term is a head in its ambient expression at which the two names arrive through different depth-$n$ slots. Record the depth of their last common tree vertex, $0\le j<n$, and their order. Bound types are evaluated on a tuple when taking a face; their formal variables alone are not observations of ambient names. Order the signed labels as in Response 35: at equal level only identical directions compare, and every smaller level is below either sign at a larger one. For each pair let $U_{ij}(z)$ be the intersection of *all principal upper sets containing all its observed separations* in the normal expression $z$, using the whole poset if there is no constraint. Equivalently impose every lower bound that holds on all its observations. A term carrying both names has some observation by descending through its finite argument syntax until they separate. In a split symbol $z$ the partition of names by its occupied slots has a useful property. For names in distinct blocks the only observation is their split at the outer head. Thus their constraint is its principal upper set. All such exterior directions come from the single planar order of blocks.

The constraints of a normalized face or a restriction to fewer particles imply the original constraints on the remaining pairs. The check is pairwise. In any bound expression on a labelled tree, two names contributed by different slots of the tuple have their split no earlier than the split of those slots, and in the same order at equal depth. This follows by restricting the strict $n$-source to its traversed intervals down successive columns, including the transports in a single slot. Two names coming through one slot separate only inside its labels until a head separates distinct positions. Thus substituting a generic boundary preserves each imposed lower bound. During pruning a surviving pair split in different branches keeps its divergence; during insertion the new split is either that of the exterior branches or that within the inserted tree, because the common trunk is retained. Disc removal selects a label and endo removal evaluates the generic face before taking its identity. These are exactly the two-mark computations of Response 35, with all other names left in the tuple. Substitution of identities for the forgotten particles before normalization preserves every lower bound on a retained pair. This proves the assertion also for arbitrary nested faces and deletions. In a grouped expression each argument carrying the names satisfies its constraints, since all the observations there are among those of the expression. [Pair bounds under substitution and normalization](#researcher-response-35)

### Configuration spaces and the lifting at a split symbol

Use the complete graph poset $K_n(S)$. Each edge has a level from $0$ to $n-1$ and a direction, and the directions together form a linear order. Comparison is pointwise comparison of the signed labels above. For pair constraints $U$ as above let $K(S;U)$ be the full subposet of allowed graphs. Write $K(S)$ for the unconstrained poset. Its nerve models $\operatorname{Conf}_S(\mathbb R^n)$ equivariantly and compatibly with the restrictions of labelled sets. The convention of starting levels at zero just reindexes the complete graph filtration. [Berger, *Combinatorial models for real configuration spaces and $E_n$-operads*](https://math.univ-cotedazur.fr/~cberger/config.pdf)

Suppose $U$ comes from a split symbol with blocks $S_1,\ldots,S_b$, $b\ge2$. On every $T\subseteq S$ the restriction map
$$
 K(T;U|_T)\longrightarrow\prod_a K(T\cap S_a;U|_{T\cap S_a})
$$
has an order-preserving section: retain the internal graphs and set every exterior pair to its prescribed split. This is a graph because its order is the concatenation of the internal orders in slot order. Section followed by projection to the factors is identity there, and the composite on the whole graph is pointwise below the identity. Empty factors are terminal. The deformation on nerves is natural under all restrictions. Consequently the space at $S$ maps by an equivalence to the **homotopy limit** over the proper subsets of $S$ with these constraints. Indeed replace the diagram by the displayed products. For each fixed block $S_a$ its factor depends only on $T\cap S_a$; the inclusion of the subsets of $S_a$ in the proper-subset diagram suffices for the limit of this factor. Explicitly, the restriction $T\to T\cap S_a$ is natural and becomes identity on factor values, giving inverse maps on homotopy limits. On the subsets of $S_a$ the initial object for restriction is $S_a$ itself. This proves the matching equivalence. The same proof works after taking 3-types, since it uses a diagram of objectwise equivalences and finite products.

For the unit theory make this matching map a fibration before choosing cells. Start with $K_S(U)=\operatorname{Sing}|K(S;U)|$. Recursively in $|S|$ let $M$ be the limit of the already replaced $Z_T(U|_T)$ for proper $T$. Replace the map $K_S(U)\to M$ by its mapping-path fibration
$$
               Z_S(U)=K_S(U)\times_{M,\,\mathrm{ev}_0}M^{\Delta^1}
                          \xrightarrow{\mathrm{ev}_1} M .
$$
The constant-path inclusions are natural weak equivalences. The inverse diagram is Reedy fibrant, so its limit computes the homotopy limit. Inclusions of constraints are monomorphisms and preserve their intersections by the formula, starting with nerves of subposets, their realizations and singular complexes. For matching limits this uses every restriction, with coordinatewise intersections. Fixed matching limits are Kan: build them in increasing subset size by adjoining each orbit of subsets using the matching fibration for its stabilizer. The initial $K_S(U)$ have Kan fixed points since they are singular spaces. Mapping paths give (12) also on fixed points. Take a skeleton of finite sets and use transport by bijections. Equip this diagram with the compatible strict-unit actions above. At a split symbol (12) is now a trivial fibration. The induced map on globular singular cells therefore lifts at any prescribed literal boundary, by $\partial D_l\hookrightarrow D_l$, including $l=0$.

For insertion use a second diagram. Apply the fundamental Gray-groupoid functor $\Pi_3$ to the nerves of the constrained posets and take a functorial Reedy fibrant replacement for the inverse diagram of subsets, natural in all inclusions of constraints. Equivalently, at each cardinality factor the map to the already constructed matching limit by a trivial cofibration and fibration. Its objectwise type is the 3-truncation of the nerve. Lack's Quillen equivalence computes the derived limits in 3-types; the preceding product argument gives the matching weak equivalence at every split. A trivial fibration of Gray-groupoids lifts all globes at fixed boundary (in the top degree it is full and faithful on the final hom). Give each Gray-groupoid the published column operations of Responses 5 and 26, extended by identities above degree three. Strict Gray functors preserve these operations and compute their limits on the underlying cells. This time the operations are already functorial, so inclusions of constraints need only give maps. These Gray-groupoids are used as models of the **ordinary** published theory. [Lack, §§5–6](https://arxiv.org/abs/1001.2366) · [Published operations on Gray-categories](#researcher-response-26)

For either target diagram construct maps of local published algebras from $B(S)$ to the unconstrained target on $S$. More precisely evaluate each expression $z$ in the target for $U(z)$ and then in any relaxation. Induct on $|S|$ and on local degree for the free presentation at that cardinality. A symbol's two attaching terms already evaluate in its constraint by the face bound. Its restrictions to all proper $T$ evaluate at the required relaxed constraints by the deletion bound. They form a matching cell there: two successive deletions compose, and the evaluations on smaller sets already respect them. Its matching boundary is the projection of the prescribed faces, by the same induction. Lift this cell at those faces using the split matching trivial fibration. The permutation action is free on the symbols with individually named occurrences, because their normal syntax contains every name. Choose for one representative and transport. Evaluate grouped operations in the indicated target on the lifts of their arguments, and identities by identities. All images in larger constraints agree by functoriality. In particular this gives an algebra map by the free presentation. To see the deletion condition on a general expression, it holds on symbols by the matching lift and on a grouped application because deletion of names is a homomorphism for the suspended operations and the target restriction is an algebra map. The carrier statement for grouped terms holds by the observation bound on their arguments. Equivalently, for each fixed $U$ the symbols satisfying $U$ are closed under all symbols of their attaching expressions; evaluate their free presentation there. This verifies compatibility on faces even if deleting a name turns a split head into a grouped term.

### How the maps detect raw obstructions

Compose these maps with the raw-to-surface map on the indicated fixed homs and realize. For strict units this gives maps
$$
 F_S:\operatorname{Conf}_S(\mathbb R^n)\longrightarrow
                         \operatorname{Conf}_S(\mathbb R^n)
$$
in the homotopy theory of diagrams with relabellings and forgetting. For insertion the target is the 3-type of that space. On the source the configuration identification is natural: the raw scalar computad deloops the free little-$n$-cubes algebra, name permutations relabel the cubes, and sending a generator to the identity forgets its cube. The chosen standard poles are preserved by these assignments. On the target use the complete graph comparison and the raw comparisons of the two target diagrams above. Thus in particular one can use the maps on homotopy orbits; separate nonequivariant choices of homotopy classes would not suffice. [Scalar realization and occurrences](#researcher-response-33)

In the strict-unit case write $e$ for the degree of the map on two names, a self-map of $S^{n-1}$. **It is $1$ or $-1$.** Indeed the integral cohomology of each ordered configuration space is torsion-free and generated as an algebra by the pair-direction classes. For example forget the last point. The fibre is punctured Euclidean space, with unit and the small linking classes as an additive basis in cohomology. These classes extend as the corresponding pair directions, so Leray–Hirsch proves both assertions inductively. The same applies modulo every prime. By naturality $F_S$ multiplies every pair generator by $e$.

If a prime $p$ divided $e$, take $p$ names with their cyclic permutation action. On positive-degree fibre cohomology over $\mathbb F_p$ the map would be zero. In the Serre spectral sequence of the Borel construction over $BC_p$ it would be zero on every positive fibre row from page two onward and the identity on the initial bottom row. Some differential must hit the bottom row: $H^*(BC_p;\mathbb F_p)$ is nonzero in unbounded degrees and the action is free with finite-dimensional quotient (one may use the finite free complete graph complex). At the **first** page with such a differential the bottom map is still identity. Naturality contradicts its nonzero value on an element on which the upper-row map is zero. This excludes every prime, also excluding $e=0$. Hence each $F_S$ induces an integral cohomology isomorphism, degree by degree, since all generators have degree $n-1$ and the map acts by the corresponding power of $e$. The spaces have finite CW type and are simply connected for $n\ge3$ by the configuration fibration. Thus each $F_S$ is a homotopy equivalence. [Configuration fibration](https://journals.msp.org/mscand/article/view/2674) · [Complete graph model and the pair classes](https://math.univ-cotedazur.fr/~cberger/config.pdf)

For insertion take $n=3$. On two names the target is $P_3S^2$, with the antipodal action. The coefficient $e$ on $\pi_2$ is odd by the free-cell argument of Response 35: compare the equivariant map with the truncation map on the two hemispheres, using simple connectivity to make them agree through the 1-skeleton. The top-disc changes contribute an even difference. On any number of names the maps on $\pi_2$ are multiplication by this same $e$, since the pair projections are a joint coordinate system by the punctured-space fibration. The group $\pi_3$ of each ordered configuration space here is free abelian of finite rank and is rationally generated by Whitehead products of its $\pi_2$. For detail, the forgetful fibration has a section placing the last point beyond all previous first coordinates. Its fibre is a wedge of 2-spheres. The degree-three Hilton decomposition of that wedge consists of one Hopf generator per sphere and the brackets of distinct sphere inclusions. The self-bracket is twice the Hopf generator up to sign. The split homotopy sequence gives the assertions by induction, as the section takes brackets to brackets. The truncation map preserves these groups and operations. Thus the map to the 3-type acts by $e^2$ on $\pi_3\otimes\mathbb Q$ under truncation, and is injective integrally. This step includes the brackets invisible under all maps forgetting down to two particles. [Hilton, *On the homotopy groups of the union of spheres*](https://doi.org/10.1112/jlms/s1-30.2.154)

Finally take a proposed surface filler at an old boundary. The lower ranges are already settled in Responses 10, 33 and 35. In the higher fixed hom distinguish the occurrences at the $n$-source by Response 33, lifting both the old boundary and the particular surface filler. The difference of its two parallel raw face discs in the ordered configuration component must die after the indicated map: the filler gives a homotopy there with the prescribed boundary. In the strict-unit case the homotopy equivalence reflects this in every degree. For insertion on 3-scalars the new comparison in global degree seven asks whether a difference in $\pi_3$ vanishes, which the injection reflects. The raw globe-fibre comparison gives a filler at the original literal faces of the coloured boundary; fold its names back. Nonstandard lower writings on the point can be transported to the standard poles, compatibly with the surface map. All transports on the point are contractible, and fitting a retained scalar to its required attaching faces or forgetting it by the point cell gives precisely the generator transport or deletion in the derived free little-cubes calculation, as in Response 35. Different vertices give disjoint components. Degrees $n=1,2$ of the strict-unit assertion follow from the graph and planar theorems. [Transport at the given raw faces](#researcher-response-35)

In particular, for $\equiv_{su}$ and separately for binary $\approx_{\{U\}}$, the complete degree-$(n+l)$ criterion on $X_n(D)$ of Response 37 now reduces to degree-$l$ conservativity on $D$ alone. Both surfaces are conservative in every dimension on the single-output and single-input scalar rule systems of Response 38 in **every** scalar rank. The proof above uses published pruning; it does not supply an interpretation of the binary interchange congruence on these configuration spaces. The unrestricted finite-computad cases of Q1 and the remaining all-context classification in Q2 remain open. ∎

**Validation.** On trees with at most six edges, the checker constructed 2,124 simultaneous pruning quotients and compared their actual substitution maps with all 4,494 one-leaf-first factorizations. The safe Agda kernel checked the multiple-leaf quotient substitutions through four edges. For three and four named particles the checker contracted 2,553 constrained complete graphs, including interleaved blocks, and checked commutation with all 34,191 tested restrictions. It checked the insertion height obstruction in 9,576 grouped/split cases. Rice’s checker under both published modes accepted a three-particle paste with a syllepsis in one slot and a third scalar in the other, computed the two-particle restriction by conversion, and rejected the reversed names in that restriction. The Kan lifting, matching equivalence and detection of all homotopy groups are paper arguments. [Reproducible checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_configuration_carriers.py) · [Rice checker revision](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/tree/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc)

## Researcher Response 48

**For three occurrences of one Gray scalar along an edge path, the two intervening gaps determine the decidability of the cube-return problem.** Start with one vertex, one loop edge $f$ and a scalar $a:1_x\Rightarrow1_x$. Fix a bare chart with gaps $f^h,f^k$. Vary an arbitrary finite list of 4-declarations over this base. If $h,k>0$, the specified old reverse has a raw filler exactly when a declaration has the reverse strict faces. If exactly one gap is nonempty, the test is decidable by reachability with two nonnegative counters. If both gaps are empty, it is undecidable. In every case the reverse is inhabited on any binary surface containing $I$. This distinguishes individual degree-four boundaries even within the same 2-skeleton.

### The finite graph of possible temporal histories

More generally take any bare triple of generating rewrites in a finite Gray 2-computad, with its two specified gap paths. A temporal history records three generator names and the positions of their applications on the changing spatial path. Start at the right-to-left history $D$ of the chart and aim for its left-to-right history $E$. Build the directed multigraph of contextual exchanges from $D$. At adjacent temporal steps with first position $r$ and second position $l$, the second rule $b$ can move first precisely when $l+|s b|\le r$. The new positions are $l,r+|t b|-|s b|$. Indeed the spatial word before the exchange factors into a prefix, the input of $b$, a gap, the input of the first rule, and a suffix. Position and the generator determine the occurrence, including for a zero-length input. Keep the position of the swap as an edge label even when both vertices coincide. The graph is finite: the three names are permuted and the length of any intermediate spatial path is bounded by the initial length plus the sum of the three output lengths. Retain just the vertices from which $E$ is reachable.

A strict local 2-cell at these ends is exactly a path in this graph from $D$ to $E$. Single squares generate, and the only strict equality between their sequential readings comes from crossings on disjoint temporal substrings. On three occurrences there are no disjoint crossing pairs. In particular the number of possible old faces is infinite exactly when the retained graph has a directed cycle. Otherwise count the paths by topological order. In the finite case one can decide the return with arbitrary 4-attachments by reachability on these paths. For each declared atom, and for each native cube at the three steps, match its two strict face paths with a contiguous path segment, allowing stationary temporal prefixes and suffixes and fixed exterior spatial paths on the atom. There are finitely many temporal windows and spatial positions. This enumerates all elementary rewrites: factor a strict local 3-paste into single steps, serializing lower whiskering so that any crossing in its stationary context is performed before or after the step. At its actual application only the identities of the stationary temporal steps remain alongside it. Raw fitting at the original faces is as in Response 46. [Cubical presentations of the fixed hom](#researcher-response-8) · [Lifting an attached step to old faces](#researcher-response-30) · [Three-strand crossing words](#researcher-response-46)

In particular suppose all three input paths are nonempty, or all three output paths are nonempty, or both gap paths are nonempty. The six permutations give six distinct temporal histories. With nonempty inputs, the still-unexecuted factors start at distinct positions, so one can recover the permutation in forward order. With nonempty outputs recover it backwards from the final path. With both gaps nonempty the fixed paths separate the positions in either reading. From a chart permutation an adjacent inverted pair always exchanges. An adjacent already sorted pair $i,j$ cannot exchange in the other spatial order: that would require $|t i|+$ (length between the two factors) $+|s j|=0$. Each of the three hypotheses rules this out. Thus the graph is the oriented hexagon of permutations. Its two paths are precisely the strings $121$ and $212$ of swap positions.

For any bare chart where the path count is two, a return step changing one path into the other cannot have a common nonempty crossing prefix or suffix, since their first and last swap positions differ. It cannot have a stationary temporal occurrence on either side, since both paths cross all three pairs. A nonempty exterior spatial path would precede the leftmost step or follow the rightmost one, contrary to the prescribed bare positions in $D$. Finally the descending history recovers the unique native three-axis chart by reading the three disjoint input segments from right to left, including zero-length segments. Its native step points forward. Hence some *single* declared 4-generator must have exactly the two strict reverse faces; it also suffices. In particular on a finite base with all generating 2-inputs nonempty (or all outputs nonempty) the least number of degree-four declarations supplying all bare triple returns is the number of bare triples with their gap paths. Each declaration can match at most one, by recovery from $D$. The number can be infinite when a gap traverses a cycle. This extends the sharp count of Response 30 to these three-axis charts.

### One repeated cut

For exactly one nonempty gap, two scalar occurrences have the same position while the third has a different one. There are three temporal histories. The singleton occurrence passes each of the other two once on the route $D\to M\to E$. There is one loop $x$ at $D$ and one loop $y$ at $E$, exchanging the two coincident occurrences. There is no loop at the middle history $M$. Thus **every** parallel face has a unique spelling $x^i b c y^j$ with $i,j\ge0$, where $b,c$ are the two changes of history. The native cube transfers one loop between the two ends. Its orientation is read from $121\to212$.

Enumerate the same finite list of atoms with stationary temporal and spatial whiskers as above. This step does not enumerate crossing prefixes or suffixes. Each resulting rule replaces a path $l$ based at a specified history by a parallel path $r$. Their traversals of $b,c$ are identical: their initial and final histories determine that segment, which can be empty. Count occurrences of $x,y$ in $l$ and $r$, obtaining $(u,v)$ and $(u',v')$. The rule applies to $(i,j)$ exactly when $i\ge u$ and $j\ge v$, and gives $(i-u+u',j-v+v')$. For a path touching the middle use the occurrence of the required segment of $bc$. For a path based entirely at an endpoint, use the corresponding unary block. An empty path can be placed at its specified history, since every whole path visits all three histories. This proves both directions, including contextual matches at smaller temporal widths. In the loop-edge presentation a declaration with $m$ temporal events on $f^s$ is tested in each window of $m$ events and each contiguous exterior placement of $f^s$ on $f^{h+k}$, retaining exactly the matching positions.

These are transitions of a two-place Petri net. One can split consumption and production at a private control state to apply the two-dimensional vector addition system reachability theorem. The implementation instead keeps the minimal guard of a finite word of transitions and uses integer sets to apply arbitrary positive repetitions at once. This terminates by the finite family of linear path schemes in Theorem 3.1 of Blondin et al. To see the connection to the split transitions, project a consumption edge to the empty word and its private production edge to the original transition. A run between the main states consists of complete pairs, and projecting a path scheme gives a concatenation of fixed words and starred words. Eventually all such repeated words have been enumerated; finitely many rounds of propagation cover all of those schemes. Equality with the previous reachable set certifies closure under all single transitions. [Blondin et al., *The Reachability Problem for Two-Dimensional Vector Addition Systems with States*, Theorem 3.1](https://www.cs.ox.ac.uk/people/christoph.haase/home/publication/befghlmt-21/befghlmt-21.pdf)

With both gaps empty the reduction of Response 46 applies on the empty spatial path. The extra loop edge cannot enter an exterior whisker or a scalar history there. Equivalently the base inclusion has a return by sending the loop to the vertex identity, and this extends over all declarations used in that reduction. Together the three cases give the claimed classification for varying attachments at the fixed boundary. The all-context question in Q2 additionally quantifies over other boundaries.

**Validation.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_three_axis_paths.py` and, in a Python environment with `islpy`, `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_two_cut.py`. Across 256 charts with births, deaths, scalars and nonempty rules, direct spatial splitting checked the exchange edges at 1,487 retained histories. The path count was two on 239 charts and infinite on 17; the general counting criterion is the graph argument. For the one-gap case the solver compared 2,100 contextual word matches with their counter transitions, checked 138 reachability answers by independent finite search and tested unbounded-growth instances in both orientations. With `--gaps 0 1 --attach 1:1,0,0:2:22 --attach 1:1,0,0:22212:121` the counter solver finds the raw return; with only the growth declaration it proves absence. Here the three comma-separated numbers are the scalar positions at the declared source history. **There is an exact decidability boundary for the bare three-axis Gray return over every finite 2-computad.** Fix the base, the three specified generating 2-cells, their two gap paths, and the old reverse 4-type. The predicate that the raw kernel inhabits this fixed reverse type is **undecidable exactly when all three occurrences have empty strict input and output edge paths and both gaps are empty**. [Higher-axis criterion](#researcher-response-30) · [The width-three path graph and raw fitting](#researcher-response-48)

### Compressing the spatial paths

Use the finite directed graph of temporal histories from Response 48, restricted to paths from the descending history $D$ to the ascending history $E$. Its rooted multigraph, with the two possible adjacent swap positions as edge labels and $E$ distinguished, depends only on three pieces of discrete data: the emptiness of every input and output, the emptiness of each gap, and the equality pattern of the three generator names.

To prove this, divide the initial spatial path into the three input blocks and two gap blocks. Keep the nonempty blocks as units of length one. As a history executes, each positive input consumes its whole block and a positive output creates one whole block. Every zero-length occurrence takes place at a joint. These assertions hold along the initial descending history. After exchanging disjoint adjacent steps, factor the spatial word just before them into prefix, left input, middle, right input and suffix. Each is a union of whole blocks and the two steps operate on those same two segments in the new order. The intermediate joints and the final sequence of blocks are therefore well defined and the assertions persist. An adjacent pair can exchange exactly when the full left input lies before the right start, allowing equality at the joint; the compressed test is identical. Conversely a compressed history reached by such moves has a unique expansion as a *concrete* history. Start with the original blocks, read the named steps and select their joints by block count. Each nonempty input selects one block and each positive output inserts the particular output path of the named generator. The two calculations invert each other on the reachable histories, also when names repeat. Forgetting the identities of individual edges inside the blocks introduces no further moves.

This gives a short exhaustive finite proof of the possible cycle structures. Give each of three positions one of four emptiness pairs, choose either status for each gap, and choose one of the five partitions of the three names. Discard partitions identifying positions with different emptiness pairs. For each of the resulting 464 cases use unit lengths for positive blocks. Starting at $D$, for each temporal pair apply the position inequality and update of Response 48; retain the subgraph from which $E$ is reachable. Compute its strongly connected components by mutual reachability. The table counts cases, with all numbers reproducible by the linked solver.

| Structure of the subgraph | Cases | Decision at the cube reverse |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Acyclic, exactly two paths | 421 | One declaration must have the reverse strict faces |
| One simple directed cycle containing neither endpoint | 4 | One counter with an exact-value test |
| Two simple directed cycles in distinct components, one at each endpoint | 34 | Two nonnegative counters with finite control |
| One component, both swap positions possible at every history | 5 | Substring reachability on two crossing letters |

Here a cyclic component described as a simple cycle has exactly one internal outgoing edge at every vertex, with parallel edges counted separately. The five cases in the last row are exactly the five name partitions of three scalar occurrences at a single cut. The first row uses the single-declaration argument of Response 48. The arithmetic reductions for the middle rows follow.

### Compiling all contextual moves

Write each native or declared elementary step after stationary temporal and spatial whiskering as a pair of parallel paths $l,r$ based at a specified history $u$. These placements form a finite list: split the temporal history into prefix, generator window and suffix, match the declaration's spatial path at a contiguous position on the actual edge path before the window, and compare the positions and names in that window. An empty spatial path matches at its vertex. Shift each crossing in the two faces by the temporal prefix length. Discard placements outside the retained graph. Every whole-path rewrite is $p l q\to p r q$, where $p:D\to u$ and $q:t(l)\to E$. This description permits declarations on smaller windows and arbitrary changes in crossing length. Response 48 proves the equality of this reachability test with raw inhabitation at the given old faces.

In the two-cycle case encode a whole path by its residual path and the numbers of complete turns at $D$ and at $E$. Strip the maximal complete initial turns based at $D$ and the maximal complete terminal turns based at $E$. There are finitely many residual paths: the middle graph is acyclic and both leftover cycle segments have bounded length. To compile $l\to r$, enumerate *simple* paths $p,q$ at its two sides, allowing the empty ones. If $p l q$ has code $(s,i,j)$ and $p r q$ has code $(t,i',j')$, introduce the move
$$
 (s,i+m,j+n)\longrightarrow(t,i'+m,j'+n)\qquad(m,n\ge0).
$$
These moves are exact. Erasing a complete turn in a general $p$ or $q$ changes the two whole paths by a common exterior turn. Indeed inside a simple cycle, a turn followed by a segment equals that segment followed by a turn based at its other end. A path cannot leave the component and return to it. This moves all erased turns to $D$ or $E$. Conversely any number of such exterior turns are legal on both whole paths. The resulting finite-control two-counter system is decidable by the path-scheme algorithm of Response 48.

In the one-cycle case choose a vertex $v$ on the cycle. A path missing $v$ has its literal finite-control code and count zero. For a path visiting $v$, retain its prefix up to the first visit and its suffix from the last visit. Count the intervening complete turns. Both retained segments have bounded length because the graph off $v$ is acyclic. In compiling the contextual moves now enumerate $p,q$ visiting $v$ at most once each. If either visits $v$, arbitrary additional turns can be inserted there on both sides of the rewrite. The compiled move sends $i+m$ to $i'+m$ for every $m\ge0$, with the corresponding change of finite control. If neither visits $v$, it applies **only at $i$**, with output $i'$. This exhausts all contexts by stripping turns in $p,q$ separately. In particular an occurrence lying inside the face $l$ does not by itself permit a turn to be added in the outside context.

For completeness the exact-value test needs only a one-symbol stack with a bottom test. Pop the required count, test for empty in the exact case, then push the output count, using private intermediate states. Push the initial count at entry and pop the target count at exit. Compute the empty-to-empty balanced relation without zero tests by closing identity and neutral edges under concatenation and under a push followed by a balanced path followed by a pop. This finite relation accounts for every such path by splitting at the match of its first push. At height zero take the transitive closure of the balanced relation and the empty tests. Splitting at visits to those tests proves exactness for the full system.

### The undecidable case with different names

At a common scalar cut, both adjacent swaps are always available. Their word determines the labelled path from its initial history. To apply Response 46 with different names, ensure every encoded symbol acts as the identity permutation, and fit the start and finish to the common target permutation of $121$ and $212$. Encode each symbol and each of the two fresh markers by $11\,2^{2i}\,11$ with distinct $i\ge2$. After the entire marked word add the fixed trailer $111222111$. The symbol blocks act trivially on the names and the trailer reverses them. Use $212\to E(LuR)\,111222111$, the encoded production pairs at $D$, and $E(LvR)\,111222111\to121$. Each pair has parallel labelled faces. The trailer contains no alternating triple, and its odd run of twos cannot match a symbol. The block alignment and unique-marker arguments of Response 46 therefore apply at each rewrite. Conversely an aligned rewrite is available at its required history $D$ since every complete preceding block acts trivially. All declarations occupy the three occurrences, and neither an extra generator of the base nor a nonempty edge path appears in an exchange between these histories. The old fixed-hom lifting theorem supplies raw faces at matching old poles; they can be found effectively by enumerating raw writings with the specified strict values. Thus the reduction holds for **each fixed base and chart** in this row, including three distinct names. This theorem concerns one old 4-type while the attachments vary. Unrestricted Q2 also asks about all other old types in a given context.

**Validation.** With `islpy` installed, run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_triple_attachments.py`. For all 464 shape cases, the checker verifies the table, including the endpoints and multiplicities of the cyclic components, and constructs a rooted labelled graph isomorphism after nonuniform stretching of the blocks. On the 38 cyclic decidable cases it compared 4,848 sets of actual contextual successors with the counter encodings. It also checked 2,744 coloured code replacements and the reversal permutation of the trailer. For a mixed example use `--chart a:: b::f a:: --attach 'x::0@a,0@a,0@b:1:111' --attach 'x::0@a,0@b,0@a:2111112:121'`. The first declaration adds two turns at the internal cycle. The second recognizes exactly five and supplies the return. Changing its source to `211112` requires four turns and the solver proves absence. [Shape enumeration, declaration matching and reachability solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_triple_attachments.py)

## Researcher Response 50

**Ordinary $\mathrm{Catt}_{su}$ is conservative over CaTT in every finite context, in every dimension.** In particular Q1 for ordinary $S=\{U\}$ has an affirmative answer. The argument gives a span of raw globular trivial fibrations from any raw model to the reduct of a strictly unital model. It uses the pruning substitutions on the **generic tree of an operation**. It places no restriction on the attaching types in the context of an inhabitant.

### A simplicial diagram on strict pasting arities

Write $H_P$ for the ordinary raw model on a globular tree and $C_P$ for its free strict category viewed as a raw algebra. Use two small categories on these objects: $\mathcal W$ with arrows all raw substitutions $H_P\to H_Q$, and $\Theta$ with arrows all strict functors $C_P\to C_Q$. Projection defines $p:\mathcal W\to\Theta$, the identity on objects. A compatible globular tuple of arrows from the variable globes of $P$ to $Q$ in either category determines an arrow from $P$ to $Q$. For a simplicial presheaf $X$ on either category put $V_X(P)=\lim_{D_j\subset P}X(D_j)$, the space of tuples at the variable globes and their faces. Write $V_X(\partial D_k)$ for the corresponding boundary limit (a point at $k=0$).

Here are the nerve and fibrancy details used below. Take a raw algebra $Y$ and a right simplicial frame $Y_\bullet$ with $Y_0=Y$. The presheaf $A(P)=\mathrm{Hom}(H_P,Y_\bullet)$ on $\mathcal W$ has $A(P)=V_A(P)$, with Kan fibrations on globe boundaries. Its vertices on globes are the cells of $Y$. On $\Theta$ take an injectively fibrant simplicial presheaf $X$ presenting $P\mapsto\mathbf R\mathrm{Map}(C_P,Y)$. For example use functorial cofibrant replacement in the source, the same right frame, then injective fibrant replacement. The natural raw maps $H_P\to C_P$ are weak equivalences on **these arities**, so $A$ and $p^*X$ are isomorphic in the objectwise homotopy theory of presheaves on $\mathcal W$. Indeed apply functorial cofibrant replacement also to the natural transformation $H_P\to C_P$ and use cofibrancy of $H_P$ to compare its replacement with $H_P$ in the frame. These are the raw model and arity inputs of Response 3. [Raw model and arity comparison](#researcher-response-3)

The presheaf boundary of a globe, obtained by gluing the face representables, includes monomorphically in the globe representable on both sites. For clarity, a substitution landing in both hemispheres has all its variable images supported on their common boundary. On the strict side every cell of the free strict category on that boundary belongs to a single face globe: its two parallel generating hemispheres admit no nonidentity paste with one another, and the same description recurs on their boundary. Apply this to the image of the total of the input tree. Its support includes the supports of the whole tuple. Indeed strict globular tree substitution expands each used slot by its labelled paste, taking the union of generating globular supports; identity labels contribute their supporting face. On the raw side a term on a globular set has the same generating support as its strict image. This follows by induction from that union formula, since the strict cell of a full generic head uses its whole arity, including the supported face in the identity case. A term supported on a globular subset restricts to it by the same syntax induction. Thus the substitution factors through a common face globe. Applying this argument recursively identifies precisely the gluings in the boundary. Each individual face inclusion is injective on substitutions on either site. On the strict site the same calculation shows that the variable-globe spine includes in the representable of $P$: the common image of two variable globes is detected on their common generating faces. Consequently $X(D_k)\to V_X(\partial D_k)$ and $X(P)\to V_X(P)$ are Kan fibrations by injective fibrancy. The latter is an equivalence, since on the raw site $A(P)=V_A(P)$ computes the homotopy limit of the globes (adjoin cells using boundary fibrations), and the natural equivalence with $p^*X$ compares both this homotopy limit and its cone.

Choose a comparison presheaf $Z$ on $\mathcal W$ with objectwise equivalences to $p^*X$ and $A$, such that $Z\to p^*X\times A$ is an injective fibration. To obtain the span, a projectively cofibrant replacement of $A$ has an actual map representing the above isomorphism to the objectwise fibrant $p^*X$; then factor the joint map to the product by an injective trivial cofibration and fibration. Applying the globe boundary monomorphism to the fibration shows that the map on $k$-globes to the two endpoint globes together with a common lifted boundary is a Kan fibration. Both projections separately are fibrations relative to globe boundaries, since each endpoint has this property. In fact each separate relative matching map is a **trivial** fibration: by induction the map on the boundary limit is a trivial fibration, and the map on the whole globe is an equivalence. This also proves all globe boundary fibrations for $Z$. Taking finite limits along these maps by adjoining variable globes shows $V_Z(P)\to V_A(P)$ is a trivial fibration. Hence $Z(P)\to V_Z(P)$ is an equivalence. No strict Segal equality on $Z$ is needed.

### Extending a head together with paths to its strict values

We first put the published unit operations on the globes of $X$. The auxiliary data described next are needed even if the space of tuples with an identity label has many fewer components than the space of all tuples. For a simplex $b$ of $X(Q)$ consider cells consisting of a strict arrow $u:D_j\to Q$ and a path in $X(D_j)$ ending at $u^*b$. Faces restrict both entries. Here paths at simplicial degree $m$ are maps $\Delta^m\times\Delta^1\to X(D_j)$. We construct operations on $X(D_j)$ and also on these pairs for every $Q,b$, such that path **starts** are a homomorphism to the first operations and the arrow component uses the strict operations. The path data must be natural under restriction of $b$ and composition of the arrows $u$. Identities on the globes and on paths are induced by the strict projection $D_j\to D_{j-1}$. This projection has the two face inclusions as sections.

Fix a head of rank $k$ on $P$ with strict value $a:D_k\to P$. Abbreviate $V=V_X(P)$ and form
$$ E=V^{\Delta^1}\times_{V,\mathrm{ev}_1}X(P),\qquad d:E\to V,\quad d(\ell,b)=\ell(0). $$
Here the pullback uses the restriction of $b$ to the tuple. The constant-path inclusion of $X(P)$ is a homotopy equivalence onto $E$ (contract the interval to its last vertex), and $d$ on that section is the Segal equivalence. Thus $d$ is a weak equivalence. Use its mapping cylinder $C=V\amalg_{E\times\{0\}}(E\times\Delta^1)$. Giving the two operations for this head amounts to a map $C\to X(D_k)$ taking $(\ell,b,1)$ to $a^*b$. Indeed for a tuple of pairs over $Q,b_0$, the arrows of the tuple give a single $f:P\to Q$. Use $(\ell,f^*b_0)$ in $E$, and give the output the strict arrow $fa$. This proves naturality under strict reindexing without choices on individual $Q$'s. In particular the rule applies when the paths at the input are themselves the outputs of earlier operations. Iteration in these algebras evaluates every bound expression with compatible paths at all of its faces.

Induct first on output rank, then on the size of the tree for heads at that rank. Index a choice by the normal class of its generic bound type. Lower-rank operations evaluated on the tuple give a map $C\to V_X(\partial D_k)$: on $V$ take the two boundary terms, and on $E\times\Delta^1$ take their paths in the pairs algebra on the generic strict tuple over $P,b$. At time zero these agree by start evaluation, and at time one they are the restrictions of $a^*b$. A generic endomorphism uses the identity on its interpreted face, on both tuples and paths. A disc uses the variable or its prescribed iterated identity by published disc trivialization. A normalized generic type equal to one of these receives that same prescription.

For any independently prunable leaf $i$ of a remaining head, write $q_i:P\to P_i$ for pruning in the strict category. In $V$ the locus $V_i$ on which the leaf is an identity is exactly the embedded $V_X(P_i)$, since a tuple there is uniquely determined by its remaining labels. In $E$ prescribe pruning on $E_i=V_i^{\Delta^1}\times_{V_i}q_i^*X(P_i)$, so the entire input path and its chosen terminal diagram descend. This is exactly what the pairs algebra needs: if a label is an identity **as a pair**, its strict arrow is an identity, whence the arrow $f:P\to Q$ used in the evaluation factors through $q_i$. Its path is also an identity path-label, meaning the projection of a path one dimension lower, and consequently $\ell$ factors through $V_i$. A path constant in its time parameter alone need not be an identity label.

For every subset $J$ of these leaves the intersections $V_J,E_J$ have precisely the same descriptions with the simultaneous quotient $P_J$. For tuples this is the universal property of forcing the selected leaf labels to be identities. For the terminal diagram in $E$ one needs the assertion in **every presheaf**, beyond this property of tuples. The $q_i$ are inverse maps of $\Theta$: in the wreath description they are a simplicial degeneracy at the branch of the leaf, suspended within its successive outer components. Their joint pushout in $\Theta$ is $P_J$, since a strict globular tuple on $P$ factors through it precisely when each selected generator is an identity; the pruned substitution leaves every other label determined. This universal property holds in particular against all strict trees. In the elegant Reedy category $\Theta$ a pair of inverse maps with common source has an absolute pushout (equivalently its representables form a pushout of presheaves). Thus the intersection of the images under any presheaf is the image from the joint quotient. Apply this degreewise and iterate; equivalently work in $\Theta_m$ with $m$ the tree height. All intersections are monomorphic since degeneracies split. This yields $d_J:E_J\to V_J$ a weak equivalence by the preceding Segal calculation on $P_J$. [Bergner–Rezk, §§3–4, strong pushouts and the $\Theta$ construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/1110.1066) · [Simultaneous pruning substitutions](#researcher-response-47)

On $V_i$ and $E_i\times\Delta^1$ use the smaller tree with the original generic boundary substituted by pruning. Its strict value composes to the required one. The prescriptions agree on all intersections by successive pruning on the smaller trees, including disc and endo cases. Lower-degree evaluation commutes with this reindexing by substitution in the pairs algebra. Let $V',E'$ denote the two pruning unions. Finite gluing along monomorphisms, applied also to the intersections, gives a weak equivalence $E'\to V'$; this includes the empty union. Let $C'$ be its mapping cylinder inside $C$. The inclusion
$$ (E\times\{1\})\cup C'\ \lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\ C \tag{13} $$
is a trivial cofibration. It is monic; the inclusion of $E\times\{1\}$ into the left side is the pushout of the trivial cofibration $E'\times\{1\}\to C'$, and its inclusion into $C$ is likewise an equivalence since $d$ is. On the left side we have the endpoint $a^*b$ and all the forced prunings. Extend the map over $C$ relative to the prescribed globular boundary by its Kan fibration. This constructs the two operations. It checks pruning for all their specializations, endo and disc trivialization, and equality of the interpreted normalized generic types. Substitution is evaluation of expressions, both on globes and on pairs. These checks establish the published theory with conversion. In particular they also give the raw actions that will be needed at both endpoints of the comparison. [Published rules and disc trivialization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.08307)

### Returning a filler at its literal raw faces

On $A$ use its actual raw operations. They have the same path enhancement using arrows in $\mathcal W$: evaluate the head pointwise along the tuple path. At the last time this gives exactly restriction along the raw head because $A$ is the raw algebra nerve. Construct **raw** operations with path enhancements on $Z$ projecting to both endpoint actions. For this construction use raw arrows for pairs on the $Z$ and $A$ sites and project those arrows by $p$ on the $X$ site. A raw head $e:D_k\to P$ gives the terminal prescription $e^*b$ in $Z(D_k)$. At a general tuple use $f^*b_0$ as above, now for a raw substitution $f$. On $Z$ the raw identity constructor itself is treated as a head; its projected maps are already given. There are no forced equations on the $Z$ operations. For each head form the same $E,C$ for $Z$. Its Segal equivalence gives that $E\times\{1\}\to C$ is a trivial cofibration. The lower-rank enhanced operations prescribe the globular boundary on $C$, and the projections of the input prescribe the two whole endpoint operations on $C$, paths included. These data agree at the terminal prescription by functoriality on the respective sites. Lift using the joint relative globe fibration for $Z\to p^*X\times A$. This defines the raw operations by induction, and both projections preserve all raw evaluations.

On vertices the resulting span $Y\leftarrow Z(D_\bullet)_0\to X(D_\bullet)_0$ consists of raw homomorphisms. Both lift a cell at **any given parallel boundary**, by surjectivity on vertices of the separate trivial matching fibrations, also at dimension zero. Take $Y=H_\Gamma$ and lift the generating assignment of $\Gamma$ along the left projection in declaration order. Evaluate a proposed surface term at the projected assignment in the published algebra on $X$. Its given old boundary has already been evaluated upstairs on $Z$ by raw operations. Lift the surface value along the right projection at this boundary and take its image in $Y$. This is a raw term at exactly the requested old type. This proof uses no strict-image comparison at $\Gamma$. For binary $\{U\}$ use containment in published strict units. ∎

At the old 5-type from the right-unit composite of $p$ to $p$, Rice's checker accepted the identity on $p$ under strict units, rejected that writing in raw CaTT, and accepted the raw right unitor at the very same type. The general presheaf and boundary lifting arguments above are mathematical proofs. [Relative cylinder and checker examples](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_pruning_cylinder.py)

## Researcher Response 51

**The binary unit surface $\approx_{\{U\}}$ is conservative over $\mathrm{CaTT}_G$ in every finite context and every dimension.** In particular, in Q1(i) the only remaining subset omitting interchange is $\{A,U\}$. The directed strict images are needed as part of the data even when two operations agree after ordinary strict projection.

Call an arity *simple* in this proof if at most one of its outer columns has a nonpoint local tree. Two properties of these arities suffice.

1. On a simple arity $P$, the canonical comparison $\mathfrak F(P)\to F(P)$ is an isomorphism of Gray-enriched categories, viewing the ordinary target as Gray-enriched via the projection to cartesian product. In each forward hom all tensor factors except possibly one are the monoidal unit. The tensor-to-product comparison is the canonical identification with the remaining factor, including the terminal case. Composition of intervals agrees under these identifications since at most one factor can carry activity. For two simple arities the enriched functors between them are exactly the ordinary strict functors. Both are determined by, and extend, the same globular tuple in the target. Simultaneous pruning of locally maximal variables keeps the arity simple: within the sole nonpoint column it prunes ordinary leaves, and at the root it can discard a point column.
2. Every **generic constructor of a Gray binary word**, with all constructors of its formal boundary, belongs to the ordinary syntax restricted to heads on simple arities. An identity uses a disc. A composite at positive cut is inside a single suspended column, even when the local composite has several moving factors. At cut zero one can compose 1-cells or whisker a higher cell by a 1-cell. This has at most one nonpoint column; the two faces use the corresponding truncated operands. The assertion follows recursively for the whole word over **any** ambient arity. Ordinary published unit normalization preserves this fragment, also inside formal bound types. Its reductions use disc selection, identity on an evaluated endo face, and substitution of a pruning into a smaller head. These use only subexpressions, discs, substitutions, and pruned simple arities. Consequently whenever two ordinary terms in this fragment are equal by published units, their normal forms agree by a calculation entirely within the fragment. [Unit normalization and uniqueness of normal forms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.08307)

Use the nerve comparison of Response 50 with the raw Gray arity site and the strict site of the $\mathfrak F(P)$ and enriched functors. Globe labels on $P$ still freely specify a substitution on either site. The raw-to-strict comparison on these arities is the globular trivial fibration of Response 3. Here are details about the presheaf inclusions in that argument. In a globe the directed and ordinary strict categories coincide by property 1. The ordinary projection of the directed total of a tree has in each traversed column the total of that column, padded by identities to the required rank. On a generating tensor tableau the projection augments the other factors; the rows up to the retained generator's degree are precisely its atom rows and the higher chain rows vanish. Composition in the factor gives the assertion on totals. A coherence-case full-support strict value likewise visits the whole tree on ordinary projection by the column support calculation of Responses 4 and 11. On substitution the ordinary strict globular support is the union of the used labels' supports. Thus raw terms over a globular set have the same generating supports as their ordinary projections by induction. In particular any raw tuple into the common boundary of two globe faces factors through a single globe of that boundary, by applying ordinary support to its total. This gives the raw boundary monomorphisms as in Response 50. On the strict site a tuple factoring through two variable globes lands in their common globular subset, detected in either globe, and its projected total again confines it to one common face globe. The enriched tuple factors there by freeness. This gives the strict boundary and spine monomorphisms. The remaining frame and comparison-presheaf constructions use only these facts, the raw arity equivalences, and globe cofibrations. Denote the directed strict-site presheaf by $X$ and the raw comparison by $Z$ as before. [Raw arity lifting](#researcher-response-3) · [Tensor projection onto a column](#researcher-response-4) · [Restriction and support of a directed head](#researcher-response-11)

For simple $P$, all the intersections required for (13) still hold for $X$. Indeed restrict $X$ to the full subcategory on the simple arities. By property 1 it is also a presheaf on the corresponding full subcategory of ordinary $\Theta$. Right Kan extension along that fully faithful inclusion gives an ordinary presheaf with exactly this restriction up to natural isomorphism. The simultaneous quotients are simple, so the absolute-pushout calculation takes place at the original values. This use of the Kan extension needs no Segal or fibrancy assertion on the extension. On the original strict site the pruning $q_i$ takes the indicated generator to its identity, and every strict tuple into **any** directed arity with that label an identity factors through $q_i$ by freeness.

We describe the choice of operations precisely so that changing a bound type by a unit equation cannot change a chosen answer. For every $P,k$ use $E,C$ from Response 50 with the directed strict site. Index an extension by a strict arrow $a:D_k\to P$ and a boundary map $B:C\to V_X(\partial D_k)$ which at terminal time is restriction of $a^*b$. Allow all such data. For example the data of an actual head are obtained by evaluating its two already interpreted faces on tuples and on paths with terminal diagrams. The strict components of their values on the generic tuple are the faces of $a$. Each extension operates on arbitrary pairs over $Q,b_0$ using $f^*b_0$ and the strict result $fa$, as in Response 50.

If $a$ is an identity and its two specified faces agree as maps with their strict components, use the identity on the given face function, on both the tuple and path parts. On a disc use the variable when its strict tag is that variable and $B$ is its actual globular boundary on tuples and paths. Identities iterated on variables are covered successively by the identity prescription whenever required. The identity and variable prescriptions commute with pruning: a retained variable stays its indicated variable, and the pruned top disc becomes the identity on the retained face. If $P$ is simple, require the operation on each pruning locus to be the operation indexed by $q_i a$ and the restriction of $B$ to the smaller cylinder. This is why indices were allowed even when they need not be actual generic Gray heads. The restriction has the prescribed terminal value by composition. On the simple ordinary fragment these choices interpret the published unit reductions. For disc reduction the generic data are the disc variable's data. For an endo the tags agree and the full generic strict value is an identity in the ordinary strict category on that simple tree.
