## Researcher Response 52

**There is an all-boundary criterion for Gray conservativity in degree four with braid-preserving 4-attachments.** Let $B$ be any finite Gray 2-computad. Adjoin 4-generators at arbitrary raw 3-faces in $B$, and optionally generators above degree four. In a strict fixed hom, read an old local 2-cell as a sequence of crossings of adjacent temporal steps. Write $\beta(w)$ for the positive braid on those steps obtained by retaining their swap positions. Assume the two faces of each declared 4-generator have the same braid; this is decidable. Fix any binary surface containing $I$. Then:

1. At every old 4-type $s\to t$, the surface has a filler exactly when $\beta(s)=\beta(t)$.
2. The surface is conservative at **every** old 4-type exactly when each bare spatial triple of 2-generators, with its specified gap paths, has a 4-declaration with the strict reverse cube faces. This is decidable even when the base has infinitely many gap paths.
3. For conservativity *through* degree four, require in addition the elementary square returns of Response 2. On a single scalar, one declaration at the reverse cube therefore raises the first failure to degree five. On a point with $s$ scalar names, the exact minimum number of braid-preserving declarations that gives conservativity through four is $s^3$.

The braid in this test forgets all generator names and spatial positions. It retains the full braid group element, so equal crossing counts and equal output permutations alone do not meet the hypothesis. [Crossing lists and cubical homs](#researcher-response-8) · [Positive Artin relations at changing spatial paths](#researcher-response-10)

### Why a surface filler preserves the braid

Take the braided strict monoidal groupoid of braids on unnamed strands and its doubly degenerate Gray-category $G$ as in Response 37. Choose the handedness so the directed interchanger is the positive block crossing. Give parallel braids the equality order. Use the column-order kernel model $K(G)$ and the full ordinary binary history model $E(G)$ with the comparison on triples from Response 5. The latter interprets the directed binary surface through ordinary projection: the ordinary head condition accepts the directed head, parallel Gray words related by the selected laws are equal under the full ordinary word laws, and the same holds for the corresponding identity-head equations. At converted head types use the ordinary strict projection of their full or total Gray value. [History model and comparison at fixed faces](#researcher-response-5) · [Braid Gray-category](#researcher-response-37)

In $K(G)$ send all objects and edges of $B$ to the unique object and arrow, and every generating 2-cell to one strand. For a raw directed 3-cell the resulting braid is its indicated strict crossing word. To verify this at the operation level, map a directed globular arity to the free usual Gray-category on its generators through degree three. The map interprets a lax tensor interchanger by the corresponding invertible interchanger. Through degree two its evaluation of a directed head agrees with the column-order operation: a nonidentity 2-total has just one active column, evaluated in its given temporal order, with the other columns stationary. A 2-coherence evaluates to the identity on its path. Thus all lower faces agree by substitution. At a 3-head the two operations on the generic variables have the same faces in this Gray-category and the same ordinary strict value. Top uniqueness over the globular arity, as used to define $K(G)$, identifies them. Specialize this generic comparison in $G$. On the strict fixed hom of $B$ the resulting map sends each contextual elementary square to one positive crossing, temporal whiskering shifts its position, and top composition concatenates the crossing words. This proves the formula at the actual raw writings.

All declared 4-faces thus have equal images in $K(G)$, so the thin 4-cell interprets each declaration. Above rank four parallel cells have identical values. Lift the raw assignment through the comparison on triples and project to $E(G)$. At any fixed old pair of 3-faces the two projections differ by conjugation with the *same* endpoint isomorphisms. Consequently a surface filler in $E(G)$ requires equality of the two computed braids. This argument allows conversion at lower and generic boundaries without assigning an uncorrected braid to a converted expression.

Conversely the positive braid monoid embeds in the braid group, so positive words with equal braid are related by distant commutations and the two directions of the adjacent Artin relation. Start with the actual temporal history of the prescribed raw source. Distant commutations already agree in the strict hom. An executable three-swap side of the adjacent relation gives three independent rewrites in spatial order and permits the other side at precisely the same initial history. This follows from the two position inequalities in Response 10, including empty inputs or outputs. Each replacement is a contextual instance of a native cube. The forward step lifts raw and the reverse is supplied in the surface by Response 7. More explicitly, absorb the spatial gaps into the generating steps, whisker by the remaining stationary paths and temporal events, and append the outer crossings. Choose matching raw writings at interfaces using fixed-hom lifting on $B$; equal strict paths at prescribed parallel writings admit raw identity-image comparisons on that base. These corrections fit the outside words at each cut and at the final pair $s,t$. Hence the sequence gives a surface filler. If each bare reverse has a raw filler, the same construction uses raw cells throughout. This proves assertion 1 and the sufficiency direction of assertion 2. [Surface reverse at the cube](#researcher-response-7) · [Fitting a strict paste at old raw faces](#researcher-response-30) · [Brieskorn–Saito, *Artin-Gruppen und Coxeter-Gruppen*](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01406235)

### Necessity and effective checking on the whole base

Consider a bare triple at its common descending temporal source. Every step in a raw return preserves the braid: native cubes satisfy the braid relation and the hypothesis applies to declared atoms in every context. At width three the positive braid represented by $212$ has exactly two word representatives, $212$ and $121$. Indeed its length is three and the only applicable positive relation interchanges precisely these two. From a specified temporal history each of these position strings has a unique execution. This holds even in the five undecidable unrestricted shape cases of Response 49. Thus some step in a return must take $212$ directly to $121$. The two strings have distinct first and last letters, both use all three temporal occurrences, and the chart has empty exterior spatial paths. The bare-chart argument of Response 48 now forces a declaration at the exact reverse strict faces. The native cube has the opposite orientation. Necessity follows since the surface inhabits every such reverse.

The descending history, including its initial edge path, reconstructs the three generators and their gap words from right to left. A declaration can cover at most one bare triple. For each ordered triple of generator names collect the finite set of covered gap pairs. Test inclusion of all admissible gap pairs in this finite dictionary: if either pole pair admits no path the triple contributes nothing. Otherwise first find any uncovered left gap by graph search in the prefix trie, then, for each covered left gap, test the right gaps against its own dictionary. Failure gives an actual unfilled old 4-type. This also catches an interior cycle admitting arbitrarily many paths. Through dimension three the 4-declarations do not enter. For scalars at one point every adjacent exchange has a return by exchanging the named occurrences in the opposite spatial order at the same cut. The bare triples are exactly the $s^3$ ordered names, each repair is braid-preserving, and the next degree with no declaration fails for a repeatable generator by Response 29. This establishes assertion 3. [Elementary square criterion](#researcher-response-2) · [Higher missing reverses](#researcher-response-29) · [Three-axis attachment classification](#researcher-response-49)

The all-boundary procedure checked 278 attachment dictionaries, including every subset of the eight reverses on two scalars and examples with arbitrarily many possible gap paths. Its `--presentation FILE` mode accepts JSON with `edges` mapping single-character edge names to endpoint pairs, `rules` mapping each 2-generator to its source vertex and its input and output paths, and `attachments` listing the starting vertex, initial edge path, temporal history of position/name pairs, and two positive crossing words. ## Researcher Response 53

**At one Gray scalar, arbitrary 4-attachments give an exact order on the braids for surface inhabitation in degree four.** Let $B=(x,a:1_x\Rightarrow1_x)$, add any finite list of 4-generators over $B$, and fix any binary surface with $I$. No global 3-generator is required or allowed in this statement. A declaration of width $m$ has as its two strict local 2-faces positive crossing words on $m$ strands. Let $\preceq_n$ be the least preorder on the braid group $B_n$ invariant under multiplication on both sides and containing every declared pair at every consecutive placement of its strands among $n$. For old parallel 3-terms $s,t$ of width $n$, a surface 4-cell $s\to t$ exists **if and only if** $\beta(s)\preceq_n\beta(t)$. In particular all four surfaces containing $I$ agree on these inhabitation questions.

The distinction between signed contexts in this order and positive contexts in the raw kernel matters. There is a four-declaration context (one vertex, one scalar and two 4-generators) in which the only bare cube has a raw return but conservativity still first fails in degree four. On the other hand, if the width-two declarations include both an increase and a decrease in crossing number, **conservativity through degree four in the whole context is decidable using the width-two declarations alone**, regardless of the other declarations.

### Clearing signed contexts and cancelling at an old boundary

The family of preorders respects braid juxtaposition: padding a generating inequality produces one of the placements at the new width, and multiplication by a stationary braid is allowed. Form the braided monoidal groupoid of braids with this order on parallel morphisms, quotienting each preorder by mutual comparison if an antisymmetric order is desired. Multiplication, juxtaposition and the structural braid identities descend. Apply the ordered Gray-category history construction of Response 5 to its doubly degenerate Gray-category. The raw action into the column-order model computes the two face braids by Response 52. Each stipulated inequality interprets its declaration, and the comparison on triples conjugates the two fixed faces by the same endpoint isomorphisms. Since the order is invariant under such conjugation, a surface filler implies the asserted inequality. This also explains why larger widths cannot introduce a further inequality at a smaller width.

On the *positive* braid monoid write $\preceq_n^+$ for the preorder generated by the same placements using positive left and right factors and both directions of the Artin equations. For $n\ge2$ put $Z_n=\Delta_n^2$, the central full twist. Then for positive $b,c$,
$$
 b\preceq_n c \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 Z_n^k b\preceq_n^+ Z_n^k c\text{ for some }k\ge0 .
$$
Indeed a finite comparison in the group uses finitely many contexts $L(-)R$. Choose $N$ so that all $Z_n^N L$ and $R Z_n^N$ are positive. Such an $N$ exists because each standard letter divides $\Delta_n$ and the full twist is central. Each step multiplied by $Z_n^{2N}$ now has positive contexts; equality of positive expressions in the group is generated by positive Artin relations. The converse cancels the full twist in the group. Widths zero and one have only trivial braids. [Garside, *The Braid Group and Other Groups*](https://doi.org/10.1093/qmath/20.1.235)

At a prescribed raw 2-endpoint of width $n$, every positive braid word can be written as a raw 3-loop there by base lifting. Its image in the surface admits a weak inverse (with both cancellation 4-cells). To see this for an elementary letter, use the directed square at that pair of scalars. Clause (ii) identifies the square with an identity, hence makes it an equivalence. The stationary whiskerings and raw comparisons fitting it to the desired writing preserve equivalences. In detail the fitting comparisons of equal strict value are over identities and have two-sided inverses up to higher cells by the globular trivial fibration over the cubical hom of $B$. Composition and whiskering of such inverses use the raw associativity, unit and naturality comparisons inside the fixed hom. This also transports a spatially unit-whiskered square to the literal width-two loop exhibited in Response 46. For a string of letters compose the fitted loops. Any old writing at the same faces with that strict path compares both ways to the composite by the same base lifting. These constructions use only the scalar base.

Given the displayed positive comparison, prepend a raw full-twist power at the common 2-source of $s,t$. Every placed declaration and positive context lifts at the prescribed raw faces in the sense of Response 30. Each reverse Artin move is available in the surface by the cube construction. Thus there is a surface 4-cell between the two prefixed 3-cells, with all seams fitted at old writings. Precompose by a weak inverse of the prefix and paste its cancellation cells at the two ends. This gives $s\to t$ at the original boundary, proving sufficiency. Consequently all degree-four boundaries reflect exactly when the raw kernel supplies the cube reverse and its positive reachability at each width reflects cancellation of a common full twist. With the cube reverse present the positive preorder $\preceq_n^+$ is raw reachability: far commutations already agree in the strict hom and each reverse Artin move is obtained by whiskering that one return. [Raw fitting](#researcher-response-30) · [Literal positioned crossings](#researcher-response-46) · [Surface cube moves and the raw braid action](#researcher-response-52)

### A finite width-two criterion and a decision at every width

At width two write each local word as a crossing count. A rule $l\to r$ acts raw by $l+m\to r+m$ for $m\ge0$. The surface order allows a signed translation, so $p\to q$ is surface-inhabited exactly when $q-p$ is in the additive monoid of the differences $d_i=r_i-l_i$ in $\mathbb Z$. Declarations at widths zero or one can only act trivially. All width-two boundaries reflect exactly when for each $i$ the raw relation contains
$$
                       \max(-d_i,0)\longrightarrow\max(d_i,0).
$$
Necessity uses the signed translation of that rule. For sufficiency translate the tested steps upwards and perform all increases in a sum before the decreases, which keeps every intermediate count nonnegative. Each test is one-counter reachability: pop $l$, then push $r$ using private intermediate states. The balanced push/pop calculation of Response 49 decides it.

For the counterexample adjoin the raw return of the three-axis cube and a width-two rule $2\to1$. At width two the surface therefore gives $1\to0$. The raw relation can reduce any count at least two but cannot reach zero from one, and the cube declaration has width three. Both positive words are raw at the same literal 2-endpoint. All degree-three types already reflect on the scalar. Thus repairing every bare cube does not suffice for arbitrary attachments in degree four.

Now suppose the differences at width two include both signs and let $d>0$ be their greatest common divisor. Their additive monoid is $d\mathbb Z$: using a summand of opposite sign one can express the negative of each summand by a nonnegative combination, then apply Bézout. Universal reflection therefore requires $0\leftrightarrow d$ raw at width two. This pair of directed reachability tests suffices at **all** widths. Indeed every standard positive crossing $x$ then has $x^d$ mutually raw-comparable with the empty word, by temporal whiskering. At fixed width quotient the raw preorder on positive crossing words by mutual comparison. This is an ordered monoid under concatenation; every standard generator is a unit with inverse $x^{d-1}$. The whole monoid is therefore a group.

It remains to check the reverse cube, since the raw presentation originally has only its forward orientation. Put $X=xyx$, $Y=yxy$ for adjacent letters. From $X\le Y$ and $xY=Xy$ obtain $u=x\le v=XyX^{-1}$. Both $u^d$ and $v^d$ are the identity. Sandwich $u^{d-1}v$ between $u^d$ and $v^d$ using monotonicity at each replacement of $u$ by $v$. Equality at both ends forces $u^{d-1}v=1$, hence $v=u$ and $X=Y$. Thus every positive Artin relation is available raw in both directions. The raw order also reflects cancellation of every prefix, since its elements are units. The preceding full-twist criterion proves sufficiency with *arbitrary* additional 4-rules at all widths. Conversely either missing width-two direction is an explicit old failure. With the mixed-sign hypothesis and a positive decision, the first failure in the context generated through four is five by Response 29. For example $0\to4$ and $6\to0$ give $0\to4\to8\to2$ and $2\to6\to0$. These two declarations suffice without an explicit cube return.

At width $n$, let $b,c$ be the positive crossing words of the two faces. Their raw comparison exists exactly when one can get from $b$ to $c$ by positive Artin relations, distant commutations and inserting individual standard letters. Their surface comparison exists exactly when $cb^{-1}$ is **quasipositive**, meaning a product of conjugates in $B_n$ of positive standard generators. ### Why three strands suffice for every positive comparison there

Write $x,y$ for the two crossings and $\Delta=xyx=yxy$. Fix a positive spelling of $b$ of length $p$. Form a positive word $C=F_1\cdots F_p$ with $bC=\Delta^p$. Each $F_j$ can be taken to be $xy$ or $yx$: replace each negative letter in $b^{-1}$ by its two-letter right complement followed by $\Delta^{-1}$, and push the inverse half twists to the right, swapping $x,y$ as they pass. Then $cb^{-1}=cC\Delta^{-p}$. Orevkov's Proposition 3.1, applied to this particular positive spelling, shows that if the braid is quasipositive, some subword of $cC$ is equal to $\Delta^p$. Thus there are subwords $c'$ of $c$ and $F_j'$ of $F_j$ with $c' F_1'\cdots F_p'=\Delta^p$. [Orevkov, *Quasipositivity Problem for 3-Braids*, Proposition 3.1](https://www.math.univ-toulouse.fr/~orevkov/qp3.pdf)

For a subword $T$ of $xy$ or $yx$, put $L(T)=\Delta T^{-1}$. This is positive. For $F=xy$ its one-letter left complement is $y$. For each of the four subwords $T$ of $xy$, some positive spelling of $L(T)$ contains $y$: one can use $xyx,xy,yx,y$ for $T$ respectively empty, $x,y,xy$. Swapping letters gives the other case. Now expand
$$
 \Delta^p(F_1'\cdots F_p')^{-1}
 = \tau^{p-1}L(F_p')\;\tau^{p-2}L(F_{p-1}')\cdots L(F_1'),
$$
where $\tau$ interchanges $x,y$. The same expression using the full factors gives $b$. Consequently one can insert letters separately into the factors of that spelling of $b$ to obtain a positive spelling of $c'$. Use positive Artin equality to pass to its subword spelling inside $c$, then insert the remaining letters of $c$. This gives the raw comparison. The case $p=0$ uses empty products, and widths zero through two follow directly from the unary criterion. This removes a signed denominator for the entire three-strand problem, independently of how long the two positive words are.

### An old type that first fails on four strands

Use digits for adjacent crossing positions and fix the two words
$$
                        u=321111,\qquad v=21322132 .
$$
There is a raw comparison from $1u$ to $1v$. One positive certificate is
```text
1321111 → 3121111 → 3212111 → 3221211 → 32231211 → 32232121
        → 32323121 → 23223121 → 213223121 → 213223212
        → 213232312 → 213232132 → 212322132 → 121322132 .
```
The two length increases insert a single letter; each other step is a distant commutation or a positive braid relation. The leading crossing is an equivalence on the surface by Response 53, giving the requested surface comparison $u\to v$.

For raw impossibility search backwards from $v$, taking positive braid equalities in either direction and erasing one letter at a time while the length exceeds six. This is exhaustive: every generating step preserves length or increases it by one. The numbers of distinct literal words reached at lengths eight, seven and six are respectively $4,80,240$. The last set excludes $321111$. The checker also constructs each level independently by taking the complete free-group Artin-action fibre among all words of the given length before performing the next erasure. At any greater width use the same pair with stationary strands appended. Every positive braid relation preserves the set of indices used, and erasure only decreases it. Thus the same backwards enumeration is still exhaustive, while the prefixed certificate and the surface cancellation still apply.

In the raw strict hom no elementary move leaves the empty word for a nonempty word. [Old-face lifting](#researcher-response-30) · [Scalar braid order](#researcher-response-53)

For $n<N$ the signed order is equality in $B_n$: there is no placement of the guarded rule at this width. All positive braid equalities lift raw using the given reverse cube. Thus if both positive face lengths are less than $d$, any surface comparison uses zero guarded steps and is already a raw positive braid equality. At $n\ge N$ the complete signed test is whether $cb^{-1}$ is a product of conjugates of $\sigma_1^d$ for the source and target braids $b,c$. **Width-two declarations decide degree-four conservativity at all widths on any finite set of named Gray scalars, when every unordered pair (including repeats) has both signs of crossing-number change.** Use one point, the named scalar 2-generators at its identity, no 1- or 3-generators, and arbitrary 4-declarations. **The signed order keeps the names.** Use the braided monoidal groupoid whose objects are words in the scalar names. An arrow is a braid with that ordered input, and the permutation of the braid determines its output word. Order the parallel arrows by the least preorders closed under composable left and right multiplication and strand placement of every declared pair, including arbitrary names on the extra stationary strands. An old 4-type is surface-inhabited exactly when its named positive face braids lie in this order. For the upper bound apply the ordered Gray history model of Responses 5 and 53 to this braided monoidal groupoid, assigning each scalar its single named strand. The column-order action sends a raw exchange to the structural crossing on its two actual inputs; the comparison at fixed faces conjugates by common endpoint isomorphisms. For the converse the full twist is pure and slides across each braid to the full twist at its output word. Thus the clearing argument of Response 53 converts a signed derivation into a positive comparison with a common full-twist prefix. Positive braid equations lift at these precise named inputs: each positive subword acts on the input names by its permutation, so either side of an Artin move ends at the same ordered list and uses the native or surface reverse cube on exactly those three names. The prefix cancels in the surface, where each exchange between two named lists has a weak inverse by clause (ii) and raw fitting. This proves the characterization without identifying exchanges on different name pairs. [History model](#researcher-response-5) · [Positive braid lifting](#researcher-response-52) · [Clearing and cancellation](#researcher-response-53)

**Why the order at the input matters.** At width two a declaration has an input order $P$ and nonnegative crossing counts $l,r$. If the names differ, $r-l$ must be even for the faces to be parallel. At a fixed initial order $O$ on this pair the effective unary rule is
$$
(l+e)\to(r+e),\qquad e=0\text{ if }P=O,\quad e=1\text{ otherwise}.
$$
A positive prefix of $e$ crossings reaches $P$, and arbitrary excess crossings can go in the suffix. In the signed groupoid the prefix and suffix may be signed, so every change on the pair acts by unrestricted translation between counts at $O$. Equivalently the signed test for $p\to q$ is membership of $q-p$ in the additive monoid of those changes. Test the minimal nonnegative translation of each change at each $O$, using the one-counter solver with the effective unary rules. Necessity follows by signed translation; sufficiency follows by putting increases before decreases, as in Response 53. With both signs this reduces to the two gcd tests stated above. For example the mutually opposite declarations $0\rightleftarrows2$ at $AB$ alone give both directions on the surface at $BA$, while neither direction exists there raw. At $BA$ the positive thresholds are one and three. Forgetting names would give the wrong answer to the whole-width decision.

**How the local tests return every named cube.** For a fixed width take the raw category of positive crossing words between ordered name lists, quotienting parallel arrows by mutual raw comparison. Composition is monotone. Consider families assigning an arrow to every input list such that their output map permutes the lists. Compose families along their output lists and compare them pointwise. Each positioned crossing gives such a family. Take a common even multiple $D$ of the finitely many pair gcds. The tests and stationary whiskering make the $D$th power of each crossing family mutually comparable to the identity at every list. Hence the crossing families generate a group of units in this ordered monoid.

For adjacent crossing families $x,y$ put $X=xyx$, $Y=yxy$. The native cube gives $X\le Y$ at every list. The literal word equality $xY=Xy$ gives $x\le XyX^{-1}$. Both sides have $D$th power one. Successive replacements inside the $D$th power sandwich $x^{D-1}(XyX^{-1})$ between one and one. Antisymmetry and cancellation give $x=XyX^{-1}$ and $Y=X$. Evaluating at each input list returns each named cube raw. The common prefix also cancels raw, since every crossing family has an inverse represented by a positive word. This cancellation applies to an individual raw comparison by composing with the inverse component at the required object, even when that comparison came from a declaration valid at only one list. The signed characterization now gives reflection at every old 4-type. Through degree three the scalar square returns always exist. With at least one scalar and no declarations above four, the first failing degree after a positive decision is five by Response 29. [Higher missing reverses](#researcher-response-29)

The solver compared 3,200 literal named-prefix judgments against the one-counter calculation. It constructed the reverse at all 27 ordered triples on three names using periods one, two, three and four on different pairs, checking colors after every intermediate crossing. Agda 2.8.0 checked the declarations at $AB$ and the old boundary at $BA$ in one raw context. The command `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_colored_order.py --colors AB --rule AB:0:2 --rule AB:2:0` reports a failing opposite-order type. ## Researcher Response 57

**Every homotopy type has a fundamental weak groupoid with the published insertion equations.** More precisely, its ordinary raw fundamental groupoid is weakly equivalent to the raw reduct of a $\mathrm{Catt}_{sua}$-algebra. Thus, in *any* ordinary computad, a surface filler at an old boundary implies a filler after raw groupoid completion. On every pure $n$-scalar computad, $n\ge4$, published insertion and binary $\{A,U\}$ are conservative through degree **$3n-3$**, for arbitrary multiplicities. This includes the first stable Hopf element on a pair of 4-scalars in global degree nine. The groupoid construction below specifies the interpretation of *arbitrary heads under insertion*, in addition to strictly associative binary operations.

### Moore globes and transport of a common boundary

Take a metrizable locally equiconnected space $W$. This means there is a continuous interpolation $m(x,y,t)$ on a neighbourhood of the diagonal, from $x$ to $y$, constant for $x=y$. These spaces suffice for all weak homotopy types: use a simplicial complex model, with barycentric coordinates in the $\ell^1$ metric. For points having overlapping positive supports, interpolate first to their normalized coordinatewise minimum and then to the other point, within the two respective simplices. The usual realization and this metric realization have the same weak type by the nerve lemma on the open vertex stars; each nonempty finite intersection contracts toward the barycentre of the selected vertices in both topologies.

Put $M_0=W$. A cell of $M_j$ is a Moore path in $M_{j-1}$ at fixed globular boundary. Write it as a length $L\ge0$ and a normalized path $g:[0,1]\to M_{j-1}$, with $g$ constant when $L=0$. Use the subspace topology in the product with the compact-open path space. Faces are its endpoints, identity has length zero, and top composition concatenates in elapsed time. These formulas are continuous, also at length zero: a normalized family converging to a constant path converges uniformly there, and when the two lengths tend to zero both normalized paths converge to the same constant at the common seam. For $j=1$ the fixed boundary below the endpoints is empty. In general set $B_j=M_{j-1}\times_{B_{j-1}}M_{j-1}$, with $B_0=*$.

The endpoint map $M_j\to B_j$ is a Hurewicz fibration. Given a homotopy of the two endpoints and an initial normalized path, lift the path over their common moving lower boundary using $M_{j-1}\to B_{j-1}$. The initial edge and two end edges of the parameter square are prescribed. Their inclusion is a cofibration and a deformation retract, giving the relative homotopy lift with arbitrary parameter spaces. A length at time $t$ can be taken to be $L+t$, which retains the given path at $t=0$. This also starts the induction at $W\to *$.

Fix once at each rank a continuous choice of endpoint transport for this fibration with the following extra property. If the starting cell is an identity and the two endpoint paths agree, the transported cell is the identity throughout. To construct it let $K$ consist of starting cells and compatible endpoint homotopies $(u(t),v(t))$. The indicated identity data make a closed subspace $K_0$. Each $M_i$ inherits a local interpolation by interpolating lengths linearly and normalized paths pointwise using the lower interpolation; corresponding globular faces interpolate by that same rule. In particular an interpolated length zero has constant normalized path, and fixed lower boundaries stay fixed. Near $K_0$ shrink the initial normalized path pointwise to its start $u(0)$ by this interpolation, shrink its length linearly to zero, and move $v(t)$ to $u(t)$ by the same interpolation. At every step the endpoint at time zero of the varying $v$ is the endpoint of the varying initial cell. The conditions for all these interpolations are open by compactness of the parameter intervals. This is a neighbourhood deformation to $K_0$, fixed there. In the metrizable space $K$ it gives a cofibration: use a continuous cutoff equal to one on a smaller neighbourhood of $K_0$ and supported in the interpolation neighbourhood, to obtain the neighbourhood-deformation-retract pair. The relative covering homotopy property now extends the prescribed lift on $(K\times\{0\})\cup(K_0\times[0,1])$ over $K\times[0,1]$. Only this one choice per rank will be used, independently of the tree and of all refinements.

### A sweep of every labelled tree

For a tree $P$ and a tuple $z$ of Moore globes write $H^j_P(z,t)$ for the following whole tuple, at stage $j\ge1$ and fraction $0\le t\le1$. Take the negative $j$-section of $P$: in each string of parallel $j$-slots joined at level $j+1$ use its first $j$-slot. The $j$-cells of this section are arranged in composable strings, one in each depth-$(j-1)$ position. Order the strings by planar position and the cells within each string from source to target. Allocate time $t L_j$ to the resulting list, where $L_j$ is the sum of its Moore lengths. Keep each completed path, retain the elapsed prefix of the current path, and fill the remainder of its string by identities at the current endpoint. In a string that has not started use identities at its first vertex. The $(j-1)$-labels are the successive endpoints. Labels below $j-1$ stay fixed. Extend to all of $P$ by repeating each chosen $j$-label along its transports and using iterated identities above it. This is a globular labelling. For $j=1$ there is one string of edges. An empty list has duration zero and makes no change. These formulas are continuous: elapsed prefix lengths are clamped differences of continuous sums, and the value of a path at a zero-length prefix is its specified source. A completed stage is the negative $j$-section extended by identities, exactly the start of the next stage. The first starts at the constant tuple on the initial vertex, and completion through the height of $P$ gives $z$.

For the moment define the **standard** evaluations independently of arbitrary heads. On the point use its vertex. In degree $j$ the two faces of the standard operation are the standard evaluations on the negative and positive $(j-1)$-sections. Use the Moore path of length $L_j$ given by evaluating the lower standard operation on the evolving **target** $(j-1)$-section during stage $j$. The lower boundary stays fixed, and the initial value is the evaluation on the source section. On a tree of smaller height this means identity padding. Equivalently in any root list compose its successive columns in order. At the cut itself use Moore concatenation. At each level above this cut first run the entire left path holding the right at its source, then run the right holding the left at its target, applying the lower cut operation to the two current values. For three inputs this executes the three paths in order independent of bracketing, by induction down to concatenation. The recursive column formula agrees with the section formula: the depth-$j$ list is the concatenation of the lists in the children, and while one is active the lower standard evaluation uses the current value in that child and the fixed values in the others. This includes point columns and positive durations on constant tracks.

Let $P'$ be the tree obtained by a legal insertion into $P$ with exterior substitution $f$. Evaluate $f$ here using the explicit standard operations. Then at every stage
$$
 L_j(f^*z)=L_j(z),\qquad H^j_P(f^*z,t)=f^*H^j_{P'}(z,t). \tag{14}
$$
For a branch of length one the old selected column is a disc; the joined root list contains a consecutive block in its place. At each depth in this disc its negative section has one position. Its path on the block has length the sum of the constituent lengths at that depth, and its elapsed prefix is exactly the lower standard evaluation along the consecutive constituent prefixes, by the recursive column formula. Before and after the block, the cumulative times are unchanged. The old disc's lower labels are the faces of this standard cell. During a sweep they are precisely its elapsed endpoints; above the current stage both tuples give its iterated identities. This verifies equality on every label, including an outside label whose source is the current endpoint of the block. If the inserted tree is shorter, the missing positions contribute zero. For a longer branch the trunk condition gives a single outer column on the inserted tree. In depth one the old and new root intervals are identical and the initial and final edges of the selected inner diagram are its indicated boundary edges; when the branch changes such an edge it is the unique depth-one standard edge of the inserted column. At deeper stages the splice is wholly inside that child; apply the shorter-branch computation there with the elapsed time after the preceding children. The other children have the same elapsed time on both sides. This proves (14) by the two clauses defining the exterior substitution. It proves also that the standard evaluation commutes with insertion: along the last stage use (14) for the tuple and the same calculation in one lower degree for the standard evaluations of its section. Equivalently substitute the serial path for its one block in the recursive column formula. [Definitions 4 and 18–22 of the insertion theory](https://arxiv.org/html/2302.05303)

### Evaluation of all heads and comparison with the fundamental groupoid

At a rank-$k$ head with generic faces $u,v$, evaluate the two already interpreted faces throughout stages $1,\ldots,k-1$. At the starting constant tuple both are the same iterated identity. Start with its identity $k$-cell, then transport it along these paths of parallel faces with the fixed rank-$k$ transport, one stage at a time. A missing stage is included as a constant stage, so the number of calls depends only on $k$. In the last stage the source $u$ is fixed. Indeed if the arity has height $k$, $u$ is supported on its negative $(k-1)$-boundary, whose labels stay fixed during stage $k$. Its $(k-2)$-boundary is fixed as well. For smaller height the final stage has duration zero. These support assertions apply at converted faces: on a tree ordinary strict support is closed variable support and the strict arity test has the prescribed signed faces. Now append to the transported path just the track of $v$ in the last stage, with duration $L_k$. Its ends are precisely the required evaluations. If $L_k=0$ the track is constant; the evaluation is continuous there because the whole family of normalized tracks converges uniformly to that constant. This uses the ordinary rank bound on arguments and applies also to a full-support coherence head. Operations on constant tuples give iterated identities by the transport prescription, establishing the starting condition inductively. [Support at converted faces](#researcher-response-32)

For the published standard type, during the early stages the two standard faces are equal since all $k$-labels are identities. The prescribed transport then gives a zero-duration cell. The last append gives exactly the independently defined standard evaluation. For the standard disc this is the input cell itself. A generic endo has height less than its output rank, the two tracks agree throughout, and the final append has duration zero. Thus it gives the required identity. For an insertion, evaluate the generic faces after the exterior substitution. On the entire stage trajectory these evaluations agree with the original ones by (14), lower-rank interpretation, and substitution. The starting point, all transport inputs, the final track and its duration are consequently identical. This verifies insertion at an **arbitrary outer head**, including when the selected label is only equal to the standard evaluation by conversion. For a change of generic type by equality its face functions already agree by induction and the formula uses just those functions on the fixed tree. These arguments give the simultaneous interpretation of formation, substitution and equality. They prove the published laws on $M$, hence also the binary $\{A,U\}$ laws by containment.

Here is a raw comparison which identifies the resulting model, including the effect on generator maps. Write $F_k$ for the ordinary fixed-unit-interval globes of $W$, obtained by taking paths at fixed globular boundary. Normalizing elapsed time at each rank defines a map of globular spaces $r:M_k\to F_k$. On singular complexes its relative globe matching map is a trivial fibration. To check the boundary lifting test for simplicial degree $l$, take a simplex of fixed paths at specified lifted globular ends and a lift on the boundary of the simplex. Extend the normalized path using the lower relative matching trivial fibration, with its two endpoints prescribed over the whole simplex. The parameter pair is the prism with its two ends and lateral boundary, a relative CW pair; the induction gives the same lift for such pairs by cell attachment (or triangulate the prism). Extend the boundary length function nonnegatively and make it positive off the boundary by adding distance to the boundary; for an empty boundary choose one. At its zeroes the prescribed lift has constant normalized path. This gives a Moore lift in the indicated topology. Rank zero is the identity. In particular the underlying relative globe fibrations used here include the actual old faces.

Choose the usual raw fixed-interval fundamental action on $F$, using a globe-to-pasting-ball map on each head extending its two boundary maps. Lift this action to a second raw action on the singular Moore globes by those trivial matching fibrations, fixing iterated identities on constant tuples. The lifts are relative to monomorphisms of simplicial sets. Interpolate this raw action and the reduct of the published action: on each tree the constant tuples include by a monomorphic weak equivalence, since the stages above give a deformation from the initial vertex section, fixing constant tuples. Prescribe the two end actions and the common constant answer during the interval. Their domain includes by a trivial cofibration (pushout product with the two endpoints); the already interpolated lower faces prescribe a globe boundary fibration. Extend and evaluate nested raw expressions at the same parameter. The two evaluations of this raw path algebra lift globes at prescribed faces, by the boundary lifting against one end of a prism together with its boundary cylinder. Taking vertices and using $r$ gives the raw weak equivalences to the usual fundamental groupoid as in Response 47. This proves the groupoid assertion. In particular a map out of a computad into its raw groupoid completion can be transported to the surface reduct, since the source is cofibrant. The exact boundary test for a filler is invariant under these raw equivalences by the globe and boundary mapping-space comparison. [Fundamental globular models](https://www.math.univ-toulouse.fr/~dcisinsk/highergr.pdf) · [Comparison of raw actions](#researcher-response-47)

There is also a directed version of the construction for a topological category with metrizable locally equiconnected hom spaces. Use its actual objects and arrows and put the Moore globes in each hom starting in global degree two. At degree one compose by its given strict continuous composition. Start the tuple sweep at stage two, retaining one initial arrow in each column; the base value of a full operation there is the composite of the traversed arrows with the indicated identity padding. The rank-two standard track now evaluates that continuous exterior composition on the moving arrow. The block calculation for (14) starts with strict composition of the initial arrows; inside each hom all the higher stages are exactly those above. The transport for a nonstandard head is chosen in its output hom and so is the same before and after any refinement. For the raw comparison use fixed-interval hom globes: evaluate the raw object cuts in the individual homs using the same globe-to-ball operations, then apply exterior composition pointwise. These operations are natural for continuous maps and products, so evaluation respects the cuts of a substitution. In the interpolation use the base tuples of initial arrows, componentwise at each fixed sequence of objects. They include by a weak equivalence by stages two and above, and both actions there are fixed by the strict exterior composition. This gives the same span to the ordinary raw model with these directed arrows and fundamental hom groupoids.

### The stable range on pure scalar computads

Distinguish all $r$ occurrences at the $n$-source, lifting a putative surface filler with its boundary as in Response 33. The raw component of the last fixed hom is $D_r=\operatorname{Conf}_r(\mathbb R^n)$. Evaluate the coloured generators in the fundamental groupoid of $W=\bigvee_{i=1}^r S^n$ as the distinct sphere inclusions. Such an assignment is available in the published model by the raw weak comparison and cofibrancy, with transport at nonstandard lower writings as in Response 35. The resulting map on the last hom is the little-cubes map $D_r\to\Omega^n W$ that scans the cubes, up to homotopy. Indeed the free little-cubes identification is an identification of the free algebra **with its generator maps**. In the groupoid target its iterated endomorphisms are the usual iterated loops with the little-cubes action. The free extension on the named inclusions is exactly the scan, placing the corresponding sphere map in each cube and using the basepoint outside. Projecting away sphere summands implements forgetting their cubes. The groupoid comparison is only needed for an assignment on the free raw computad; it need not preserve the surface operations inversely. [Free algebra and prescribed raw faces](#researcher-response-33) · [May, *The Geometry of Iterated Loop Spaces*, §§4–5](https://link.springer.com/book/10.1007/BFb0067491)

On two colours, after translating the image component to the base component, the map $D_2\simeq S^{n-1}\to\Omega^n(S^n\vee S^n)$ is adjoint to $\pm[\iota_1,\iota_2]$. Here is a degree check. In a large domain disc $D^n_x$ keep the first scanning ball centred at zero and the second centred at $a$ on the boundary of a smaller parameter disc $D^n_a$. Use equal small radii so the balls there are disjoint. The map on $\partial(D^n_a\times D^n_x)$ scans to the wedge, with the outer $x$-boundary constant. It computes the adjoint class: adding or subtracting a fixed reference loop in a separate domain ball contributes a family extending over the parameter disc; after translation the based family gives the ordinary smash-product adjoint on this boundary sphere. Extend the two scanning coordinates independently into the product $S^n\times S^n$ on the whole pair of discs. A regular pair of centre values has unique preimage $(0,0)$ with local degree $\pm1$, from $(a,x)\mapsto(x,x-a)$. The product relative to the wedge has a single $2n$-cell, so relative Hurewicz identifies this degree with the coefficient of its attaching map, the indicated Whitehead product. Consequently on any $\pi_i(D_2)$ the scan is given after adjunction by $n$-fold suspension followed by this basic Whitehead product. The product includes the length-two Hilton summand, hence is injective on all sphere homotopy groups in this composition. Freudenthal makes the suspension injective for $i\le2n-4$. [Hilton's decomposition for a wedge of spheres](https://doi.org/10.1112/jlms/s1-30.2.154)

In this same range the pair projections jointly detect $\pi_i(D_r)$ for $i\ge2$. Forget the last point; the fibre is a wedge of $(n-1)$-spheres and a section places the last point beyond the old ones. On that fibre, projection to the pair with particle $j$ has degree one up to sign on its small linking sphere and is null on the other linking spheres. Below dimension $2n-3$ there are only the individual sphere summands in Hilton's formula for the fibre. The split homotopy sequence and induction on $r$ prove joint injection. The cases of zero and one particle are contractible. Hence any difference of old parallel face discs in $\pi_i(D_r)$, $i\le 2n-4$, which dies in the published surface already vanishes in $D_r$: its pair scans vanish, forcing all its pair projections to vanish. The lower degrees and occurrence lifting are in Responses 33 and 35. At global degree $d=n+i+1$ vanishing gives a raw filler at the prescribed lifted faces by the raw globe-fibre comparison. Fold the colours back. Distinct vertices and arbitrary raw attaching writings on a vertex are covered by the same transport as for the configuration calculation of Response 47. This proves the bound and also gives an unrestricted individual test: a pair projection with nonzero $n$-fold suspension obstructs a surface filler in its degree. This supplies a groupoidal obstruction for Q1 with strict associativity and units; general directed boundaries can still require more information. ∎

**Validation.** The checker builds discrete samples of nested Moore paths with their actual lower faces, including paths of positive duration returning to their start. For 2,799 legal tree substitutions it obtained identical standard composites before and after insertion. It compared the complete labelled tuples in (14) at 26,057 times, including 12,421 interior times and 1,874 zero-duration stages. It also distinguished the two routes around a horizontal square. Independently Rice's weak checker accepted both the nested swap and the joined-tree head on a vertical pair next to a third input. Its insertion mode accepted the identity comparison between them; strict units alone rejected that writing. The topological transport, raw comparison, and stable-range injection are paper proofs. ## Researcher Response 58

**Surface inhabitation is undecidable already at three named Gray scalars and three temporal occurrences, even with equal-length 4-declarations in both directions and every bare cube repaired. Raw inhabitation is decidable at every width in these very contexts.** There are two versions for each binary surface containing $I$.

* In one **fixed finite context**, varying the parallel old 3-faces at the fixed temporal input $ABC$ gives an undecidable surface 4-cell problem. The subset of old types that have a surface filler and lack a raw filler is also undecidable.
* With the two **old faces fixed as well**, varying the finite 4-declarations gives an undecidable problem. The faces are $\sigma_1^2$ and $\sigma_2^2$ at $ABC$. They each have length two and return to the same ordered input. The raw answer on this family is always negative.

Three is minimal for this phenomenon among the named-scalar presentations of Response 56: its width-two decision is uniform in the declarations. Here every old 4-type below width three reflects. The obstruction is therefore different from a missing reverse cube and from undecidability of *raw* return with arbitrary length changes in Response 46. [Named braid order](#researcher-response-56) · [Raw cube-return problem](#researcher-response-46)

### Realizing group equations while keeping both crossing counts equal

Let $H=\langle a,b\mid R\rangle$ be any finitely presented two-generator group. In the three-strand group put $A=\sigma_1^2$, $B=\sigma_2^2$, and $Z=\Delta^2$. The loops at the ordered list of three **distinct** names form the pure braid group $P_3=F(A,B)\times\langle Z\rangle$. For example, in the standard pure presentation the central product is $A_{12}A_{13}A_{23}=Z$, and eliminating $A_{13}$ leaves $A=A_{12},B=A_{23}$ free modulo the center. Write $e$ for half the crossing exponent sum on $P_3$; its values on $A,B,Z$ are $1,1,3$. Under
$$
 f:P_3\longrightarrow H\times\mathbb Z,\qquad A\mapsto(a,1),\ B\mapsto(b,1),\ Z\mapsto(1,3),
$$
the kernel has an effective finite list of normal generators *all of weight zero*. [Pure braid presentation and center calculation](https://www.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/~maria-paula.gomez-aparicio/K_hom_K_thy_PureBraid-summited.pdf)

For a relator write $r=r(A,B)$. Put $g=\gcd(3,e(r):r\in R)$. Choose a word $D$ in the relators and $Z$ with $e(D)=g$ by Bézout. Impose that $D$ commutes with $A,B$ and impose $r=D^{e(r)/g}$ for each relator, together with $Z=D^{3/g}$. These equations hold under $f$. Conversely write any element in the kernel as $w(A,B)Z^k$. Its $H$-component says that $w$ is a product of conjugates of relators and their inverses. In the presented quotient each of those relators has become a power of the central $D$. Thus $wZ^k$ is $D^{(e(w)+3k)/g}=1$. This proves that the listed equations suffice; it does not require solving the word problem of $H$.

Convert each kernel equation to a signed pure word $h=1$ of weight zero. If $h$ has $k$ negative standard letters, replace each $\sigma_i^{-1}$ by the positive complement representing $Z\sigma_i^{-1}$. The resulting positive pure word represents $Z^k h$. Declare it equal to $Z^k$ in both directions, prepending one more $Z$ to both words. Both faces now have equal length at least six. Give these declarations **only** at the input $ABC$; add the reverse cubes for every ordered triple of names. At width three a signed context around one of the extra equations, starting and ending at $ABC$, uses a loop to reach its prescribed input $ABC$. Thus exactly the pure conjugates of the kernel equations occur there. There is no smaller-width placement and the cube equations already hold in the braid group. By Response 56 the surface comparison of two pure positive loops at $ABC$ is consequently equality of their $f$-images. Distinct names are what restrict the conjugating group to $P_3$.

Given a word $w$ in $a,b$, if $e(w)$ is not a multiple of $g$ then $w\ne1$ in $H$, since every relator has weight divisible by $g$. Otherwise $w=1$ in $H$ exactly when $w(A,B)D^{-e(w)/g}$ maps to the identity under $f$. Clear its negative letters by the same procedure to obtain the two positive old faces to test. In particular a solver for this fixed context decides the word problem of $H$. Choose a fixed two-generator finite presentation with unsolvable word problem. Such examples occur in Bridson's recursive family of 20-relator two-generator presentations, each of which gives either the trivial group or a group with unsolvable word problem, with undecidable choice between the alternatives. [Bridson, *On the recognition of right-angled Artin groups*, §2](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid%3Ac2e052b5-d50f-4e97-bf54-5d413342c89a/files/m2c64c6b37b506eed4f7b66a54cc5bfa3)

For the fixed-boundary version apply the compiler to that recursive family. The surface compares $A$ to $B$ exactly when $a=b$ in $H$. This holds in the trivial case; it cannot hold in the other case, since a cyclic group has solvable word problem. Raw comparisons between these two distinct literal words of length two are impossible. A cube step requires length three, and every other rule requires at least six. More generally all raw moves preserve length at every width. Finite graph search at that length, with the named input of every occurrence checked, decides raw inhabitation by strict old-face fitting. If the surface-only subset in the fixed hard context were decidable, taking its union with the raw judgments would decide surface inhabitation there.

**Implication for solvers.** Even in that one fixed context there is no recursive bound, in the length of the old boundary words, on the power of a common full twist needed by all positive comparisons that become possible after clearing denominators. A proposed computable bound would give a decision by finitely many searches at fixed lengths, since all reverse cubes are supplied. The existence of some power for each individual positive answer still follows from Response 56. This is a limitation on deciding individual old types; the reduction does not decide the property of reflection simultaneously at every old type in a supplied context.

**Validation.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_group_encoding.py --agda /tmp/ThreeStrandGroupTest.agda` and `agda --safe -i src -i /tmp /tmp/ThreeStrandGroupTest.agda`. The compiler checked 1,023 word queries against the cyclic group of order three, the symmetric group on three letters and the trivial group. The independent check reads positive crossings through six input orders, computes the pure Schreier generators modulo center, and evaluates in explicit permutation groups. Agda 2.8.0 checked the fixed old faces and a guarded width-three declaration in their named context. To emit pairs for an input presentation use e.g. `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_group_encoding.py --relators aa bbb abab --word abaB`. The output lists the symmetric rules at $ABC$ to add to the standard cube returns and the query pair; a null query means the residue test already gives a negative answer.
